## Stage 30 — Usability & Manual Overhaul ✅ (from the 2026-07-30 layman audit; 30.1/30.2 closed 2026-07-31; 30.3, 30.4, 30.6, 30.7 and 30.8 closed 2026-08-01; **30.5.5 and 30.5.8 closed 2026-08-02 — Stage 30 is now complete**)

Backlog derived from a full end-to-end pass where the manual was read first and the live app driven against it as a first-time user. Full evidence, reproductions and scoring: **[docs/UX_MANUAL_AUDIT.md](../UX_MANUAL_AUDIT.md)**.

**Framing:** the engine tested well (45/46 catalog reports run; maker-checker, GST and the full PO→GRN→stock→sale chain all correct). The failures are concentrated in (a) one POS lookup defect that blocks the flagship flow, (b) a manual that has drifted badly out of date, and (c) empty/unguided dropdowns. Order below is deliberate: 30.1–30.2 first because nothing else matters if a new user can't ring up a sale; 30.3 next because wrong docs cause wrong actions.

### 30.1 — P0: unblock the core sale flow

- [x] **30.1.1 Resolve POS SKU by code/barcode, not just internal id — closed 2026-07-31.** New `ResolveItemBySKU` (`engines/item_lookup.go`) is the one shared Item lookup: one query, deterministic `code` → `barcode` → `id` priority, soft-deleted rows excluded, typed `ErrItemNotFound`. Adopted by `GetItemGSTInfo` (so checkout *and* PurchaseOrder GST both inherit it), `validateOrderChain` (`engines/orders.go`, which had the mirror-image bug — matched only `code`, so a channel sending a barcode/id was held as an unmapped SKU) and sticker printing (`engines/stickers.go`, which printed blank name/HSN for any code-keyed run). Covered by `engines/item_lookup_test.go` — all three identifiers, a soft-deleted item, an empty SKU, and a code-vs-another-item's-barcode collision (Code must win, or a collision sells the wrong product at the wrong tax rate). **Live-verified** (scratch server :8147, real `custom_erp`): before, Code and Barcode returned "item not found"; after, all three resolve identically and a sale completes by Code end-to-end.
- [x] **30.1.2 Make HSN/GST mandatory on Item — closed 2026-07-31.** `db/migrations_stage30_1_2_item_tax_mandatory.sql` flips both `doctype_fields` rows to mandatory (tenant_default + a backfill loop over every existing tenant schema; `db/migration.sql`'s seed updated for fresh installs), so the form shows the required marker and `ValidateDocument` — the choke point every writer already runs through — refuses the save. `validateItemMasterRules` was tightened to match: HSN is now required outright (it previously only fired when a GST rate was also set, which meant "saveable now, unusable later"), and the rate must be positive, which is exactly what `GetItemGSTInfo` has always demanded at transaction time. The two layers now accept the same thing. Covered by `engines/master_data_validation_test.go`. **Live-verified**: an Item with no HSN is rejected with `GLOBAL-0001` naming the field.
  - **Follow-up worth a decision, not guessed at**: this makes a 0% (exempt/nil-rated) item unsaveable — which matches the fact that checkout has always refused to sell one, so nothing regressed. Supporting exempt goods properly needs an explicit tax-exempt flag on Item; flagged rather than slipped in by letting a 0 rate through.
- [x] **30.1.3 Prerequisites added to USER_GUIDE §4 — closed 2026-08-01.** §4 now opens with a three-item prerequisites box (a Location with Type=Store; an Item *with HSN and GST rate*, plus stock brought in by a Goods Receipt; an open cashier session) and a new **§4.0 Open the till for the shift** walking the Open/Close Session cycle. The Item half is covered properly rather than in passing: the guide now says why both tax fields are mandatory (30.1.2 made them so, and checkout cannot price an untaxed item) and that an Item starts at zero stock until a receipt posts. The whole chain is also walked end-to-end in the new §13 worked example (30.4.6).

### 30.2 — P0/P1: data-integrity and hard-error defects found live

- [x] **30.2.1 GRN can close a PO while posting zero stock — closed 2026-07-31.** Three halves, because one alone wasn't enough: (a) `db/migrations_stage30_2_1_grn_location.sql` declares `location` as a real mandatory GRN field, so the generic form renders it and `ValidateDocument` enforces it; (b) `engines.PrepareGRNReceipt` (`engines/transactional_validation.go`) defaults it from the referenced PO's `target_warehouse` (falling back to the PO's own `location`) *before* validation runs — same ordering and reasoning as `PreparePurchaseRequisition` — so an API/import caller that names a `po_id` keeps working unchanged; (c) a failed ledger post is no longer a bare `log.Printf` returning HTTP 200 "saved". The document row is already committed by then and can't be rolled back into the same transaction, so the receipt is reversed the way the domain already expresses it — status `Cancelled`, which is exactly what `fetchGRNReceivedQuantities` excludes, leaving the PO open for a real receipt — and the caller is told. Covered by `engines/grn_location_test.go` (including an assertion that the field is declared mandatory, so a missing migration fails loudly). **Live-verified**: a GRN posted through the generic API with no `location` at all took stock from 0 → 10 at the PO's warehouse; the same request previously returned 200 and posted nothing.
- [x] **30.2.2 Five missing tables + a real migration runner — closed 2026-07-31.** The audit's "no runner, no `schema_migrations` table" was half right: `public.schema_migrations` has existed since Stage 14.6, and there were in fact *three* runners — `promote.ps1`'s psql loop, `deploy/migrate.sh` (its Linux port), and CI's glob — all ordering lexicographically, none reachable from a normal dev/ops workflow, which is how the drift went unnoticed. Now: `db/migrations_stage30_2_2_integration_tables_catchup.sql` creates the five tables (tenant_default + a per-schema loop that repeats the DDL verbatim rather than `LIKE ... INCLUDING ALL`, which would have made every tenant share one `pinelabs_transactions` id sequence), and `db/migrate.go` is the single runner: migration files `//go:embed`-ed into the binary, **numeric-aware ordering** (`migrations_stage26_4_*` before `migrations_stage26_10_*`, which plain filename sort gets backwards), one transaction per file, the existing ledger table. Exposed as `erp-server -migrate` / `-migrate-status` / `-migrate-baseline`, and `promote.ps1` + `deploy/migrate.sh` now both delegate to it instead of reimplementing it. `deploy/deploy.ps1` was fixed to run migrations with the **newly uploaded** binary (`ERP_BINARY=...erp-server.new`) — with the files embedded, the old binary would apply only the old set. Startup logs a warning naming the oldest unapplied migration rather than auto-applying (two instances starting at once must not both migrate). Covered by `db/migrate_test.go` (ordering, total-order consistency, base-schema-first, and a guard that no migration contains a psql meta-command or `CONCURRENTLY`, neither of which survives the runner's per-file transaction). **Verified end-to-end**: all 66 files applied cleanly from scratch into a throwaway database in one command, a re-run was a no-op, and the startup warning correctly caught a real unapplied migration during this session.
  - `-migrate-baseline` exists for this repo's own situation: the dev DB's ledger named 13 of 68 files because the rest were applied by hand over two years. Baselining records them without executing — re-running `db/migration.sql` would have re-seeded baseline rows including the default admin credential.
- [x] **30.2.3 NULL-scan crashes — closed 2026-07-31.** Both reported crashes fixed at their queries, plus the rest of the sweep. `GetCustomerLedgerReport` (`engines/finance_reports_stage26.go`) and `GetVendorLedgerReport` (`engines/reports.go`) now COALESCE every text projection; `VerifyAuditLogChain` (`engines/logs.go`) does the same for all five text columns, not just the `checksum` that happened to be NULL (an empty string is already exactly how that loop treats a pre-checksum row, so verification semantics are unchanged). Four further unguarded sites found and fixed beyond the two reported: attendance-summary and report-performance (`engines/reports_stage26_10.go`), 3PL storage billing (`engines/wms_3pl_billing.go`), and the **payroll export** (`engines/hr.go`) — where one Attendance row missing `employee_id` would have failed the scan and taken the whole export with it. The two CRM sites the audit listed turned out to be already safe (their `WHERE` clauses exclude NULL) and were left alone rather than churned. Sales Register no longer *drops* a corrupt POSCart: it lists it with a zero total and a new `data_issue` column, because silently vanishing from a financial register is the worst of the three options. **Live-verified**: with a NULL-`invoice_number` SalesInvoice and a NULL-`checksum` audit row seeded, `customer-ledger` and `/admin/audit-logs/verify` both return 200 (were 503 and 500).
- [x] **30.2.4 Surface the useful half of every error — closed 2026-07-31.** `apiErrorBody` gained `detail` and `user_action` (both `omitempty`, so any client reading only `error` is unaffected). New `writeAPIErrorDetail` carries the engine's own `ValidationError.Message` as `detail`; `writeAPIError` delegates to it with an empty detail, so no existing call site changed signature. `writeEngineError` and the nine inline `verr.Code, verr.SubFor` sites now pass the engine message through. `user_action` comes from the catalog on **every** path including `writeAPIErrorGeneric`. A detail identical to the headline is dropped rather than shown twice, and a 5xx still withholds the raw internal message (20.6's info-leak rule is untouched — verified by a test asserting a `WIN1252` driver error never reaches the client). Frontend: `getErrorDetails` returns all three parts, `showApiError` renders them as three lines in the modal, and `showCustomAlert` now accepts a DOM node (appendChild/textContent only — never innerHTML). META-0199 needed no change once detail flowed: the engine already built "Field X value Y is not in allowed list (…)", it was simply being discarded. Covered by `internal/server/apierror_test.go`. **Live-verified**: a missing HSN now reads *"Required value is missing. Please enter HSN Code to continue."* + *"Field \"HSN Code\" (hsn_code) is required"* + *"Enter the missing value."* — where it used to read "contact administrator" with no field named.
- [x] **30.2.5 Loyalty redemption burns points without applying the discount — closed 2026-07-31.** The burn is now deferred to checkout and the discount applied automatically. "Redeem Points" checks the balance, caps against the cart total, and records an *intent* on the cart (`posRedeemPoints`) shown as its own line in the POS totals — it no longer calls the burn endpoint. `redeem_points` travels with the checkout request; `handleCheckout` rejects an over-redemption before claiming the cart (so the common case fails with no side effect), and `FinalizePOSCheckout` burns only once the sale is really going through, re-checking the balance against the ledger. Anything that can fail after the burn reverses it first via new `ReverseLoyaltyRedemption` — a compensating `Earn` row, since the ledger is append-only by design, leaving both the burn and its reversal in the customer's history. The cart clears the intent on completion, offline-queue, and approval-pending. GL: revenue is still credited at the full sale value (so the GST posting on top is unchanged and no tax judgement call was needed); the debit side splits between the payment clearing account and new account **5250 Loyalty Points Redeemed** (`db/migrations_stage30_2_5_loyalty_redemption_account.sql`). Loyalty earn now nets off the redeemed portion, which `EarnLoyaltyPoints`' own contract already asked for. Receipt and the completion dialog show the points line and the real amount to collect. Covered by `engines/loyalty_redemption_deferral_test.go`. **Live-verified**: a 210 sale redeeming 50 points returned `amount_due: 160`, moved the balance 300 → 251 (50 burned, 1 earned on the net 160), and posted a balanced entry — cash 160 / loyalty 5250 50 / revenue 210 / GST 5+5.
  - **Near miss worth recording**: the first draft used account code `5300`, which is already **Loss on Asset Disposal**. The migration's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` prevented the overwrite, but every loyalty redemption would have posted to the asset-disposal account. Caught by reading the seeded chart of accounts back after applying. Always list `gl_accounts` before claiming a new code.

### 30.3 — P1: correct the manual's false claims (do before writing anything new)

- [x] **30.3.1 The "no Edit action" claim and its delete-and-recreate workaround are gone — closed 2026-08-01.** USER_SOP §1 now documents the pencil **Edit** icon and says outright that the earlier advice was wrong and destroyed data unnecessarily; ADMIN_SOP Part D says the same. Kept as a stated correction rather than a silent deletion, because anyone working from a cached copy of the old text needs to know it was wrong. USER_GUIDE §8 (which was already right) gained the matching "you never need to delete and recreate a record" sentence so the three cannot drift apart again.
- [x] **30.3.2 ADMIN_SOP Part D rewritten — all nine rows were false, not eight — closed 2026-08-01.** Re-driving every row found the ninth had closed too: **Vendor Invoice override-pay** does have a UI path (`overrideAndPayVendorInvoice`, `public/app.js:4667`, sends `override_reason` and reports "Override submitted - routed for approval"). Part D is now a *corrections* table naming where each capability actually lives, plus a short honest list of what genuinely remains API-only (control-plane tenant provisioning; explicit-id upserts, which no screen sends by design). §B.9 documents the **Approval Rules** screen, with the curl recipe demoted to "if you need to script a bulk change" and two things it is easy to get wrong (a gap between amount bands means no gate at all; a rule needs two people to be usable). §B.1/§B.3/§B.6/§B.8 each had their own inline copy of a false claim and were corrected too.
- [x] **30.3.3 USER_GUIDE §2/§3 rewritten against the real sidebar — closed 2026-08-01.** The "menu trimming not yet built" paragraph is replaced by what actually happens (per-role hiding, per-module entitlement hiding, and 30.5.7's per-screen button gating). The §3 table is now the live structure — **twelve top-level entries**, not eight: eight module groups with flyouts plus **Dashboard, Reports, Manufacturing and PIM**, which open directly. **The audit and this item both had that wrong**, and so did the first draft of the rewrite: `Reports`/`Manufacturing`/`PIM` are `.menu-item-container:not(.has-flyout)`, so a grep for flyout children misses them. Caught by looking at a captured screenshot of the real sidebar (30.4.7) rather than by re-reading the markup. §3 also now explains the two different search boxes, which was the root of 30.3.5.
- [x] **30.3.4 USER_SOP §28 rewritten, and the source of the error deleted — closed 2026-08-01.** The eight phantom lists were real markup: `public/index.html` carried eleven hardcoded `<li>` placeholders inside `#submenu-master` that `renderSidebarSubmenu()` wipes on first render. They corresponded to no doctype that has ever existed, and §28 had plainly been written from that file rather than from the app. **The dead markup is now removed as well as the doc text**, so it cannot mislead a third time. §28 documents the real thing: 53 Master doctypes in a default tenant, grouped by module (14 groups, listed in a table), with 30.5.4's filter box and Advanced divider explained.
- [x] **30.3.5 Search described accurately, and the over-promising placeholder fixed — closed 2026-08-01.** USER_SOP §1 and USER_GUIDE §3 now distinguish the two boxes explicitly: the header box **navigates to screens and setup lists** and never looks inside records; each screen's own box filters that screen's table. The placeholder itself was the bigger problem — "Search menu, category, type or HSN..." promised a record search that has never existed — and is now **"Search screens and setup lists..."**. Fixing the app rather than only documenting the discrepancy, since the doc was describing a promise the code was making wrongly.
- [x] **30.3.6 The two-user prerequisite is now stated in all three places it is needed — closed 2026-08-01.** New **ADMIN_SOP §B.10** covers it properly: why self-approval is refused, the maker/checker table, and the trap that catches most two-person setups (both people are Store Managers but the amount lands in the HR/Admin band, so the document sits in Approvals with nobody able to decide it). Also stated as a hard prerequisite at the top of the USER_GUIDE §13 worked example and in UAT_CHECKLIST §0, which is where a tester would otherwise hit it and log a bug.
- [x] **30.3.7 All four USER_SOP "no screen" claims replaced with the real procedure — closed 2026-08-01.** §7's note became a **Prerequisite** pointing at §15B; §15 gained a real step 5 (record the receipt on Goods Receipt, and look there rather than at the PO to see what arrived); §20's note now lists the seven bin screens that exist and where they are; the closing paragraph is a correction stating that the earlier claims were wrong and that all of it is documented above. Each points at a section that now genuinely exists (30.4.1).
- [x] **30.3.8 UAT §24 rewritten from "don't test these" into twelve real test sections — closed 2026-08-01.** Every row was re-driven against the live sidebar. §24 now opens by stating plainly that it was a release-process defect, then gives §24.1–§24.10 as proper checklists for Goods Receipt, Vendor Invoice, Purchase Requisition, ASN, Sales Invoice, Putaway, wave/mobile picking, Bin Conditions, Cycle Count, and Chart of Accounts + Trial Balance (including the `JournalVoucher` row the item asked to re-check — it exists, with its own approval rule). §24.11 keeps the genuinely-generated records that have no create screen. **§24.12 is new**: the 30.5.7 permission-gated affordances, which need a non-admin login to test at all. The checklist's own §6 navigation map and §0 prerequisites were stale too and were rewritten from the live markup.

### 30.4 — Manual coverage and structure (the "SAP Help" bar)

- [x] **30.4.1 All of them documented — closed 2026-08-01.** **13 new USER_SOP sections**: §3A Offline Sync Review, §3B Offline Queue Gaps, §12A Order Management, §15A Purchase Requisitions, §15B Goods Receipt, §15C ASN, §20A Putaway, §20B Bin Conditions, §20C LPN/Cartons/Pallets, §20D Bin Replenishment, §20E Wave/Batch Picking, §20F Mobile Picking, §20G Cycle Count. **6 new ADMIN_SOP sections**: §A.7 Approval Rules, §A.8 Configuration (+ §A.8.1 Integrations), §A.9 Extension Hooks, §A.10 System Status, §A.11 Tenant Entitlements, §A.12 Tenant Usage. Numbered with letter suffixes deliberately — renumbering would have broken every cross-reference in the repo for no reader benefit. Each was written from the screen's real controls (fields and buttons extracted from `app.js`, not guessed), and each says what the screen *changes*, not just which buttons it has: Bin Conditions explains that leaving Good makes stock unsellable; Putaway explains why it refuses more than the unassigned on-hand; Tenant Entitlements explains that the module gate is the enforcement and the menu is only convenience.
- [x] **30.4.2 `docs/guides/ERROR_CODES.md` generated — closed 2026-08-01.** All **302** codes, grouped by module with a contents index, each row giving the scenario, the user message, the user action, severity and HTTP status. Produced by the new **`cmd/gendocs`** (Go, stdlib only) rather than a second Python script, so it reads the catalog map directly instead of re-parsing the spreadsheet — `server.CatalogEntries()` was added for exactly this and returns copies, leaving the map unexported. USER_GUIDE §12 gained a **§12.1 How to read an error message** (the three lines a dialog shows and what each is) and a **§12.2** table of the errors people actually hit, which is the part a user needs before a 302-row reference is any use.

Per report: id, every parameter with its type and whether it is required, the full column list with sensitive columns marked, and whether drill-down is supported. USER_GUIDE §9 now links to it instead of naming six reports, and documents drill-down and background export.
- [x] **30.4.4 `docs/guides/PERMISSION_MATRIX.md` generated — closed 2026-08-01.** **106 record types × 3 roles**, R/C/U/D per cell, read from the tenant's own `role_permissions` table (`go run ./cmd/gendocs -db ...`). Generated from the table rather than the endpoint because the endpoint is HR/Admin-only and would have needed a live session just to build a doc. States the two things the matrix would otherwise be misread on: HR/Admin is absent because it always has everything, and a missing row is a *denial* (this system fails closed).

Prerequisites blocks are attached where a step actually fails without them — §4 (the sale), §13 (the worked example), USER_SOP §7 (vendor invoice needs a posted GRN). Troubleshooting is USER_GUIDE §12.1/§12.2, keyed to real error codes and pointing at ERROR_CODES.md.

Step 9 is the point of it: a four-row table showing that stock, the sales register, the trial balance and the vendor ledger all moved **without being told**, which is the thing per-screen documentation cannot convey. Step 5 states the two-user requirement inline (30.3.6) because that is exactly where a first-timer gets stuck, and step 6 says to stop if stock did not move rather than carrying on.

Navigation is by calling each screen's own view function rather than clicking through the sidebar — the menu is permission-trimmed and flyout-driven, so a click path is exactly the fragile part. `--only` re-takes a subset, `--theme dark` captures the other theme. **Playwright is resolved from wherever it already is on the machine and is deliberately not a project dependency** (the repo has no build step and no JS dependencies; a documentation tool must not be the thing that introduces one). Wired into USER_GUIDE (sidebar, POS, PO, Inventory, reports), USER_SOP (Setup flyout, Goods Receipt, line editor) and ADMIN_SOP (Roles, Configuration). **This is also what caught 30.3.3's remaining error** — the real sidebar has twelve top-level entries, which reading the markup had got wrong twice.

### 30.5 — App easiness: dropdowns, empty states, forms

- [x] **30.5.1 Empty pickers now name the record type and link to where one is created — closed 2026-08-01.** Attached at the two shared choke points rather than per screen: the generic form's **Link** field (one site, covering every Link on every one of the 53 Setup doctypes) and **`attachTypeahead`** (a zero-match search used to close the menu silently, which is indistinguishable from "the search has not run"; it now shows a non-selectable row naming the doctype with a **create one** link). Employee's five HR `<select>`s get it via `employeePickerHint()`, since they build options as an HTML string and cannot use the Link path. New shared vocabulary — `openSetupDoctype()`, `setupLink()`, `emptyPickerHint()` — plus `.empty-state-hint`/`.empty-state-link` in the token-based CSS, so it themes for free. **Live-verified**: opening New Leave with zero Employees renders *"— No Employee records yet —"* in the select and *"No Employee records exist yet — create one first."* under it, with a working link.
- [x] **30.5.2 All 59 given a next step — closed 2026-08-01.** Each message was written against its own screen's real controls (extracted per render function, not guessed), so the guidance names the actual button: *"No payment proposals yet. Tick one or more Matched vendor invoices above, then **Create Proposal from Selected**."* Applied by a line-anchored script that fails loudly rather than silently mis-patching if a line has drifted — the two `"No purchase orders yet."` and two `"No inventory records."` pairs live on different screens and needed different guidance. **The highest-leverage one was `renderDocTable`'s**, which serves all 50+ generic record lists and said only "No records found." whether the list was empty or a search had matched nothing — two different problems with different next steps. It now distinguishes them, echoing the search term back (HTML-escaped via a new `escapeHTMLText`). Genuinely-terminal states were left terminal but made unambiguous ("No issues found — every item passed this check").
- [x] **30.5.3 Two new fieldtypes, one generic line editor, six fields converted — closed 2026-08-01.** `JSONTable` (array of row objects) and `JSONMap` (key/value object) join the six existing fieldtypes. **The column spec lives in `doctype_fields.options`, not in JavaScript** — same reasoning as 30.6.4's `auto_generated`: a doctype/field list kept in `app.js` is a second source of truth that drifts the first time anyone adds a field. Adding a line editor to a new field is now one migration row and no code. The editor is a UI layer over a **hidden input carrying the field's own name**, so `handleDynamicFormSubmit` needed no change at all and the stored value is byte-identical to what a user used to hand-type — every Go consumer (`explodeBOMComponents`, `fetchRoutingOperations`, `byProducts`, `applyReportCatalogSavedFilter`) reads exactly what it read before. Link columns render a live typeahead, which also gives them 30.5.1's affordance for free. **These fields previously had no validation of any kind**: `validateJSONTableValue` now rejects malformed JSON and names the offending line and column for a missing required value (a malformed *spec* deliberately fails open — a configuration typo must not lock a tenant out of a doctype). Every key was read off the Go struct that consumes it, not off the old label text. **Live-verified over HTTP**: a malformed `components` is refused with GLOBAL-0002, a line missing `qty` with *"Field \"Components\" line 2 is missing \"Qty per Unit\""*; in the browser the editor serialises `[{"sku":"SKU-A","qty":2},{"sku":"SKU-B","qty":3,"scrap_percent":5}]` from four typed cells. A pre-existing value the editor cannot parse is shown in a raw-JSON escape hatch rather than silently emptied.
- [x] **30.5.4 Grouped, filterable, with an Advanced divider — closed 2026-08-01.** 53 Masters now render under **14 module headings** with a filter box at the top and 15 system-internal lists behind a collapsed **Advanced (15)** divider. The advanced/everyday split is `doctype_meta.setup_advanced`, an additive column (default FALSE, so a doctype added later is everyday-visible until someone deliberately files it away — the safe direction to fail), seeded by migration and served through `/api/v1/meta/doctypes`; `GetDocTypes` returns `[]map[string]interface{}` for it. **A filter hit inside Advanced expands it automatically**, so nothing is ever hidden from a search. Two things this broke and had to fix: `applySidebarPermissions()` counts `.menu-flyout > li` to decide whether a whole flyout is empty, and the new filter row and group headings are also `<li>` — it now counts only `<li>`s carrying a `[data-view]` entry, preserving the deliberate vacuous-true "zero entries means hide"; and the submenu click-rebinding loop had to be narrowed to `.submenu-item[data-view]` or it would also fire on the Advanced toggle and navigate to a doctype named `null`. **Live-verified in a browser**: 14 groups, 38 everyday entries with Advanced collapsed, `"channel"` narrows to 5 and pulls three of them out of Advanced.
- [x] **30.5.5 Resolved — `Stores` retired, its fields folded into `Location`. Closed 2026-08-02.**
  - **User's call (2026-08-02)**, chosen from three options put to them: retire `Stores` and fold its fields into `Location`, rather than keeping it and merely scrubbing it from the menu and docs. The deciding fact was that the four fields only `Stores` had — `address`, `city`, `contact_phone`, `manager` — were **write-only by construction**: there was nowhere else in the product to record a shop's address or phone at all, so "just hide it" would have left a real gap behind a removed menu entry.
  - `db/migrations_stage30_5_5_retire_stores.sql` does it in three steps, and **the order is load-bearing**. Both `documents` and `doctype_fields` declare `REFERENCES doctype_meta(name) ON DELETE CASCADE`, so dropping the `Stores` doctype row before its documents have moved would silently cascade-delete every one of them. So: add the columns to `Location`, move the data, and only then remove the doctype — with the removal **guarded** on `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM documents WHERE doctype = 'Stores')`. A partial migration therefore leaves the doctype standing (visible, fixable) rather than taking the data with it.
  - **The data move is an `UPDATE`, not an `INSERT` + `DELETE`.** `documents.id` is the table's primary key and so is unique across *all* doctypes in the schema — which means a `Stores` row's id cannot already be taken by a `Location`, no id remapping is possible or needed, and re-pointing `doctype` in place preserves `created_by`/`created_at` and the row's history. The four fieldnames were kept byte-identical to the `Stores` ones specifically so not a single key inside a `data` blob needed rewriting. `type` (mandatory on Location) is set to `Store` via `COALESCE`, so a record that somehow already had one keeps it.
  - The new `Location` fields are all optional, so **every one of the 103 existing Location records stays valid** — `ValidateDocument` only enforces mandatory fields. `manager` is labelled "Manager" rather than Stores' "Store Manager" because a Location may be a Warehouse or the HO; the fieldname is unchanged, so no stored data moves.
  - Frontend: the `menu-stores` sidebar entry, its click handler and its `MENU_PERMISSION_MAP` row are gone, and four stale comment references to `Stores` were corrected so nobody follows a dead pointer. Both removal sites carry a "do not restore this" comment.
  - Docs: USER_SOP **§21 was repurposed from "Stores" to "Locations"** rather than deleted, so the section number stays stable and the module index still resolves; it now documents creating a Location properly and carries a note for anyone following an older printout. USER_GUIDE §3/§8/§13 and UAT_CHECKLIST §1/§17 updated — **the UAT check is now the *absence* of Stores**, so an older build fails it rather than quietly passing. `PERMISSION_MATRIX.md` regenerated (its `Stores` row is gone; `-Check` exits 0).
  - **Verified**: the dev DB held **zero** `Stores` documents, so applying the migration there exercised only the "nothing to move" path. The move itself was therefore tested separately against a real Postgres — a throwaway program built a scratch schema with the same FK/CASCADE declarations, seeded a `Stores` doctype with documents (including one that already had a `type`, plus a pre-existing `Location` so the id-uniqueness assumption was actually present), ran the real migration file against it and asserted the outcome: **15/15**, covering all four fields surviving the move, the pre-existing Location untouched, cascade cleanup of fields/grants, idempotence on re-run, and — the important one — **the guard refusing to cascade-delete an unmoved document**. Scratch schema and program removed afterwards. Plus 8 live browser checks: no `Stores` in the sidebar or the Setup list, the Stock flyout intact at 11 entries, and the Location list showing the four folded-in columns over its existing records. The 30.5.8 suite was re-run green (26/26) since this touched `app.js` again.
- ~~**30.5.5 [needs design decision] Resolve `Stores` vs `Location`.**~~ *(original text, kept for context)* `Location` already has `type: Store,Warehouse,HO` and is what every transaction uses (103 records). `Stores` has **zero Link references and zero Go references** — verified inert — yet is promoted in the sidebar and documented in USER_GUIDE §3 / USER_SOP §21 as "your shop/warehouse locations". A user who follows the manual creates their shop somewhere nothing can select it. Options: retire `Stores` and fold its fields (address/city/phone/manager) into `Location`, or keep it and remove it from the menu + docs. Needs a call before touching data.
  - **Documented rather than decided (2026-08-01, part of 30.3).** The *doc* half of this was a false claim and was fixed independently of the schema decision: USER_GUIDE §8 and USER_SOP §21 both told users to set their shop up as a `Stores` record, which nothing reads. Both now carry an explicit warning to create the shop as a **Location with Type = Store**, and name this item as the open decision. A user following the manual today no longer creates their shop somewhere nothing can select it. **The schema decision itself is still open and still needs the user's call.**
- [x] **30.5.6 Purchase Order's duplicate fields de-duplicated — closed 2026-08-01.** The remaining half was `vendor`/`vendor_id`: both mandatory, registered independently at different points in this project's history (`db/migration.sql` declared `vendor` as a Link, `db/migrations_phase3.sql` later declared `vendor_id` as Data), and **read by different consumers today** — `GetVendorLedgerReport` reads `vendor`, the payment-file and vendor-invoice paths read `vendor_id` — so neither can simply be dropped.
  - New `engines/document_mirror_fields.go` holds a doctype → mirror-pair registry and `PrepareMirroredFields`, attached at the same create/update choke point that numbers documents (and for the same reason: both halves are mandatory, so the copy must land before `ValidateDocument` runs). `FieldMeta.Mirrored` is derived from that same registry, so the generic form drops the duplicate box without a second doctype list in JavaScript.
  - **Deliberately a mirror, not a rename.** Renaming would need a data migration over every historical PO plus a sweep of every reader, for a pair whose only real cost is a confusing form. This removes the cost today and leaves the schema cleanup available later. It copies in **whichever direction has a value**, so an API caller that has always sent only `vendor_id` keeps working — the contract widens rather than narrows.
  - The `po_number`/`code` half needed only labels: both are server-issued since 30.6, but both still read "PO Number", so a record list showed two identical columns. `code` is now **"PO Number (Internal)"** and `vendor_id` **"Vendor Code (auto)"**.
  - **Live-verified over HTTP**: a PO created sending only `vendor` saved as `PO/HO/26-27/000010` and stored **both** keys; the meta endpoint reports `vendor_id` as `mirrored: true` and `vendor` as not.
- [x] **30.5.7 Create/update/delete affordances gated up front — closed 2026-08-01.** `role_permissions` has carried `allow_create`/`allow_update`/`allow_delete` since Stage 18 and the server has always enforced them; the gap was that `/api/v1/me/permissions` only ever told the client about **reads**, so the frontend had no way to know. It now returns `create`/`update`/`delete` alongside the existing `doctypes` key — as separate lists, so the existing key keeps its exact shape and an older client is unaffected. `canCreateDoctype`/`canUpdateDoctype`/`canDeleteDoctype` mirror `canReadDoctype`, and the generic list screen hides **New** and **Bulk Import** (replacing them with a **"Read-only for your role"** label carrying an explanatory tooltip) and the per-row **Edit**/**Delete** icons. The icons also gained `aria-label`s, which closes part of 30.5.8. **This is hiding, not enforcement** — the server check is untouched and remains the real gate. **Live-verified with a real Store Manager session**: `Item` is readable but not creatable (43 such record types for that role), the buttons are gone and the label is shown; `Offer`, which the role *can* create, still shows **New Offer** — so the gate is reading real grants, not blanket-hiding.
- [x] **30.5.8 Consistency sweep — closed 2026-08-02.** Add the missing typeahead on `pos-return-location`, `mkt-manifest-location`, `user-location` (siblings all have one); group the 103-entry Location typeahead by `type`; settle employee pickers on one control (`<select>` in HR vs typeahead in `asset-custodian`); ~~auto-generate the hand-typed `Attendance Code` / `Transfer Number` / `Claim Number`~~ **(done in 30.6, 2026-08-01 — along with 9 more the audit hadn't listed; `cart_number` is POS-session state, not a document number, so it stays out)**; give the icon-only row Edit/Delete buttons visible labels or aria-labels.
  - **Partly closed by 30.5.7 (2026-08-01)**: the icon-only row Edit/Delete buttons now carry `aria-label`s naming the record they act on, so that half of the sweep is done. The typeahead, Location grouping and employee-picker-consistency halves are still open.
  - **The root cause was that there was no one place to be consistent in.** All 42 picker call sites configured `attachTypeahead` themselves, so "how a Location is chosen" was decided 15 separate times — which is how the three missing ones went unnoticed. Rather than patch the three, every bespoke call site now goes through one door, **`attachLinkTypeahead(el, doctype, opts)`**, which merges in per-doctype defaults from a single `TYPEAHEAD_DOCTYPE_OPTS` table before delegating to the unchanged engine. The generic record form's Link field and a JSONTable's link column route through the same door, so they inherit every picker improvement for free — a Location Link on a Setup doctype is now grouped without anyone having edited that screen. The table is presentation only; it declares no schema, and a doctype missing from it simply takes the unadorned default.
  - **Two new opt-in options on `attachTypeahead`**, both off by default so the other ~35 pickers are untouched. `groupBy` renders a heading per distinct value — Location gets `groupBy: 'type'`. Group order is **first-appearance, not a hardcoded `Store,Warehouse,HO` list**, so a tenant that adds a fourth Location type gets it grouped with no code change; a record whose group field is missing/null/empty forms one unheaded bucket that sorts last rather than rendering a heading reading `undefined`. `showAllOnFocus` opens the menu on focus with an empty query, so a picker can be *browsed* and not only searched.
  - **The subtle part is index alignment.** `highlight()` and `pick()` address rows by index into `items`, so grouping reorders the `items` array itself rather than only the DOM, and headings/notes are deliberately **not** `.typeahead-item` — otherwise arrow-keying past a heading would select the wrong record. Verified explicitly: ArrowDown ×3 across two heading boundaries picks the 3rd *record*.
  - **Employee settled on the typeahead**, being the control ~35 other pickers already use. The five HR `<select>`s (Attendance, Leave, Payroll, Loans, Expenses) are now one shared `employeePickerField()`; `employeeOptions()`/`employeePickerHint()` are gone. Nothing is lost: `showAllOnFocus` keeps the "show me everyone without typing" affordance a dropdown had, 30.5.1's empty-state guidance actually *improves* (a static hint underneath becomes a focus-triggered dead-end row that links to where an Employee is created), the stored value is byte-identical (`code` is mandatory on Employee, so `valueFields` resolves to exactly what `e.code || e.id` sent), and validation is unaffected — `employee_id` is a Link, so a non-existent id is refused with META-0198 whichever control typed it. **Side benefit**: the whole Employee list used to be fetched on every HR tab render purely to build `<option>`s, *including for the eight tabs with no employee picker at all*, plus a parallel fetch on the Expenses screen. Both are gone.
  - **A browse that fills its page now says so** — "Showing the first 50 — type to search the rest." Without it, replacing a `<select>` that listed everyone with a menu showing 50 would let a user conclude the tenant has 50 employees.
  - **The aria-label half went wider than the item's wording.** 30.5.7 had done the row Edit/Delete pair; this pass found the other icon-only buttons had only a `title`, which is the weakest accessible-name fallback and is skipped by several screen readers. Labelled: the seven row actions in `renderDocTable` (Submit for Approval ×3, Send/Receive Subcontractor, Merge Customer, History), the Approval Rules edit/delete pair, and the five `modal-close` buttons in `index.html`. The biggest one was **`copyableCell()`** — one shared helper rendering ~30 copy chips per record list, every one of them announcing "Copy, button" with no indication of which row; it now names the value (`Copy LOC-0001`), fixed once at the helper.
  - **Verified**: 6 unit tests over the grouping helpers (run against the shipped source, not a copy) and **26 live browser checks** against a real Store Manager session — the three new pickers open grouped menus, multi-type grouping clusters correctly with the untyped row last, keyboard nav survives heading boundaries, typing-to-search is unregressed, all five employee pickers browse on focus and store the code, and every icon-only button on a record list carries a label.
  - **Incidental bug found and fixed while verifying**: `renderExecDashboard` did `document.getElementById('exec-dashboard-cards').appendChild(...)` with no null check. Reports is `DEFAULT_VIEW`, so it runs on every login, and navigating away before its fetches land detaches that container — an uncaught `TypeError` every time. Guarded the same way its own sibling `renderExecDashboardTrendChart` already guarded its container. Unrelated to this item; caught because the browser run asserts on uncaught page errors.
  - **Swept the rest of that defect class (2026-08-04)** rather than leaving it at the one instance the browser run happened to trip over. `app.js` has 352 chained `document.getElementById('x').y` dereferences, which is far too many to guard blindly and mostly harmless — the common pattern is *set `container.innerHTML`, then wire the elements you just created*, where the element provably exists. The dangerous shape is narrower: a deref **after an `await`**, of an element **this function did not create**. Scripted that filter over every one of the 216 async functions; it returned **14 sites in 8 functions**, and reading each one classified them:
    - **3 genuinely reachable, now guarded** — `submitWaveAssign`, `submitRequestRecount`, `saveReportCatalogFilter`. Each refills a form field after its request lands, so navigating away mid-request threw. The request itself had already succeeded in every case; only the convenience refill is now skipped.
    - **4 false positives** in `createASN`: the `getElementById` calls are *arguments* to the `apiFetch` call, so they evaluate before the await ever suspends. Never post-await despite matching the pattern.
    - **5 false positives** in `handleLoginSubmit`/`startMFAEnrollment`: `login-form`, `mfa-challenge-screen`, `mfa-enroll-screen` and `mfa-enroll-secret` exist only in `index.html` (0 occurrences in `app.js`), so the router cannot swap them.
    - **2 unreachable** in `submitPutaway`/`submitBinConditionTransition`: the deref sits immediately after `await showCustomAlert(...)`, and the dialog backdrop is `position: fixed` at 100vw × 100vh — the user physically cannot reach the sidebar to navigate while it is open. Left unguarded deliberately; a guard on unreachable code is noise.
    - Net: **one** genuinely severe instance (the exec dashboard, which fired on *every* login with no user action) and three minor ones. Nothing else of that shape remains.
- [x] **30.5.9 Soften the report rate limit for interactive browsing — closed 2026-07-31.** Budget raised 20 → 60/min, and the bucket is now keyed by `ip:category:session` instead of `ip:category`. The per-session half is the more important one: every user behind one NAT address — an office, a store router — used to draw down a single shared budget, so one person browsing the catalog could rate-limit their colleagues. The session key is a truncated SHA-256 of the bearer token (`sessionFingerprint`), not the resolved user id, because the limiter deliberately runs *before* authentication so an unauthenticated flood is still limited; a request with no token falls back to the original IP-only bucket. The 429 now carries a `detail` (via 30.2.4) naming the category, the limit, and the wait, instead of a bare "Too many requests".
- [x] **30.5.10 Minor — `gst_rate: 0` closed 2026-07-31; the port note was backwards.** `ComputeGSTForLines` built its rolled-up total without ever setting `GSTRate` (a per-line input), so a checkout response said 0% on a sale it had just charged 5% on. It now reports the blended effective rate actually applied (`total_tax / taxable_amount`), which equals the line rate whenever a cart is single-rate. **Live-verified**: a 5% sale now returns `gst_rate: 5`.
  - **Correction to the audit's second half**: `docs/ai_handover.md` saying **5435** is right, not stale — `environments.json`, `manage.ps1`, `promote.ps1`, `routes.go`'s fallback and all 33 test files agree on 5435. The audit's instance was on 5432 because the portable Postgres had been started with a plain `pg_ctl start` (no `-o "-p 5435"`), landing on Postgres's own default. That drift is what made `go test ./...` unrunnable this session — `db.InitDB` `log.Fatalf`s, so one unreachable port aborts a whole package binary. Fixed by restarting Postgres on 5435 as `manage.ps1` does; the doc needed no change. As a durability measure the test connection string is now overridable (`TEST_DATABASE_URL`, default unchanged at 5435) via `engines/testdb_test.go` + `internal/server/testdb_test.go`, so the suite can run against an instance on any port without editing 33 files.

- [x] **30.5.11 Retire the Dashboard landing screen — closed 2026-08-01.** User's call: *"Dashboard not required. Everything it is showing from config. So, let's skip it."* — clarified with a screenshot to mean **remove it**, not merely leave it alone. It was a welcome screen of four derived stat cards (registered doctype count, audit-log count, active tenant, a hardcoded "Operational" pill) over nine shortcut tiles into **Database Schema Design, Dynamic Labels, Prefix Configs, Extension Hooks, Activity Log, Configuration, System Status, Tenant Entitlements, Tenant Usage** — every one of which the **Settings** module already lists. So it was a second front door to configuration, owning no business data of its own. Removed: the sidebar entry (`public/index.html`), `renderDashboard()` and its router branch, click handler, `STATIC_VIEW_MENU_IDS` entry and the `.dashboard-grid`/`.dashboard-card`/`.card-content` CSS (`.dashboard-stats-row` is a *different*, still-used class — Finance, the Reports exec dashboard, System Status and Tenant Usage all render one).
  - **The landing view is now `reports`**, held in one new `DEFAULT_VIEW` constant rather than a literal repeated at each fallback. Reports is the only safe universal choice: `MENU_PERMISSION_MAP` marks it `open` (no role gate) and it has no `MENU_MODULE_MAP` entry (no entitlement gate), so it renders for every role in every tenant — and its first tab is 26.10.3's executive dashboard, i.e. real figures where the old screen showed configuration links.
  - **The stale-`localStorage` trap, which is the only way this could have shipped broken**: `restoreLastView()` replays a saved view name, and every browser that had used the app has `{"view":"dashboard"}` in `erp_nav_state`. Without a guard those users would have restored to a router branch that no longer exists — a permanently blank screen, self-inflicted on exactly the existing users, and invisible in any fresh-profile test. `saved.view === 'dashboard'` now falls through to `DEFAULT_VIEW`.
  - Docs updated in the same pass: `USER_GUIDE.md` §3 (twelve top-level entries → eleven; four flyout-less → three), `USER_SOP.md` §2 (rewritten "Where you land after signing in"), `UAT_CHECKLIST.md` (§6 preamble, §1.3, and §2 rewritten to assert the *absence* of a Dashboard so an older build fails the check), `ADMIN_SOP.md` §100 (industry switch re-renders the current screen, matching the code change from `renderView('dashboard')` to `renderView(currentView)`).

### 30.6 — Server-issued document numbers (user request, 2026-08-01)

User request, from the Purchase Orders screen: *"PO number will be auto created. I will create po sequence. Same of GRN and all other transaction."* Scope confirmed with the user before building: the ~12 doctypes whose create screens asked a human to type a number (not a blanket sweep of all 38 `document_type='Transaction'` doctypes — most of those are engine-created and already mint their own ids), and admin-configurable segments rather than a hardcoded format.

- [x] **30.6.1 Route every human-entered transaction number through the existing numbering engine — closed 2026-08-01.** `engines.GenerateSequence` (row-locked `sequence_counters` + admin-editable `prefix_configs`) has existed since Stage 1 and already numbered PurchaseRequisition, Payslip, CSV import and RFQ/PO-on-conversion. The gap was never the engine — it was that the bespoke create screens never called it. New `engines/document_numbering.go` holds a doctype → `{series key, number fields}` registry and `PrepareDocumentNumber`, attached at the one create choke point (`handlers_core_doc_engine.go`, immediately after `PreparePurchaseRequisition` and *before* `ValidateDocument`, since the fields it fills are mandatory). 12 doctypes: PurchaseOrder (PO), GRN, ASN, RFQ, VendorQuote (QTN), TransferOrder (TO), ExpenseClaim (EXP), Leave (LV), EmployeeLoan (LOAN), Grievance (GRV), ProductionOrder (PRO), Attendance (ATT).
  - **The bug this closes is not cosmetic.** The typed number was sent as the document `id`, and `documents` upserts on `ON CONFLICT (id)` — so two makers typing the same number meant the second save **silently overwrote the first**, with no error and no trace. Live-reproduced and now covered by `TestDocumentNumberIssuedByServer`.
  - **PurchaseOrder fills two fields**, `po_number` *and* `code` — both mandatory from two overlapping historical registrations (`db/migration.sql` vs `db/migrations_phase3.sql`). See 30.5.6.
  - **ASN fills only `asn_number`.** Its `po_number` field holds the *referenced PO's* number despite the name; overwriting it would have detached every ASN from its order. Regression-tested explicitly.
- [x] **30.6.2 Make `reset_frequency` actually do something — closed 2026-08-01.** It was read by `GenerateSequence` and then never used: the counter bucket was always whatever `financial_year` the caller passed, so `MONTHLY`/`NEVER` were configurable but inert. Now ANNUAL → `26-27`, MONTHLY → `26-27-07`, NEVER → one continuous series with no period segment. The visible segment and the counter bucket are derived **together** on purpose — a series that resets on a period it doesn't display re-issues last year's numbers, which (the number being the id) is a rejected save, not a cosmetic problem.
- [x] **30.6.3 Admin-configurable store segment — closed 2026-08-01.** Additive `prefix_configs.include_store` (default TRUE, so every existing series is unchanged). Off drops `HQ` from the format **and** collapses the per-store counters into one shared bucket; leaving them split while hiding the segment would hand two stores the same visible number. Exposed on the Prefix Configurations screen with a live "Next Number Looks Like" preview column. The POST field is a `*bool` so a caller that omits it keeps the stored value instead of silently reading as `false`.
- [x] **30.6.4 Read-only number fields on all 14 create forms — closed 2026-08-01.** One shared `autoNumberField()` helper (13 bespoke screens) plus a server-driven flag for the generic record form: `FieldMeta.AutoGenerated` is computed in `GetDocTypeMeta` from the same registry that assigns the number, so the generic form renders those fields read-only without a duplicate doctype list in JavaScript. Grievance's follow-up "route for HR review" call now reads the id back off the create response instead of a client value that no longer exists.
- [x] **30.6.5 Align CSV import with the same series — closed 2026-08-01.** An imported row with no `id` drew from a series named after the doctype (`PurchaseOrder`), which has no `prefix_configs` row, so it fell through to defaults and produced `PurchaseOrder/HQ/2026/000001` next to the UI's `PO/HQ/26-27/000001`. It now uses the registry's series key. Doctypes with no registered series keep the old behavior exactly.
- **Deliberately not done:** an explicit `id` in the request body is still honored as an upsert. That is this API's long-standing contract (`handleGenericDoc` resolves its id from `payload["id"]` when the path has none; `ProductContent`'s composite-id save and Stage 29.8's transition test both depend on it). Numbering it would turn an update into a duplicate document. No create *screen* sends an id any more, so the user-facing guarantee is intact; only a deliberate API caller can still choose an id.
- **Verified**: `go build` / `go vet` / `node --check` / full `go test ./... -p 1` clean, 20 new tests (`engines/document_numbering_test.go`, `internal/server/document_numbering_api_test.go`). Live over real HTTP on a throwaway port against the dev DB: two identical PO submissions returned `PO/HO/26-27/000007` and `…000008` (the exact collision that used to overwrite); stored docs carry the number on `po_number` **and** `code`; reshaping the TO series to `TRF` / `-` / pad 4 / NEVER / no-store produced `TRF-0001` on the very next save; omitting `include_store` preserved the stored `false`; `auto_generated` flags confirmed correct per doctype over the wire (PO → both fields, ASN → `asn_number` only, Item → none). All live test documents and the temporary series edit were reverted afterward.

**Verification bar for this Stage:** each item is closed only after a live re-run of the exact reproduction in `UX_MANUAL_AUDIT.md`, not just a build/vet pass. Doc items (30.3/30.4) are closed only after walking the corrected steps literally against a running server, since every defect in 30.3 is a case of a doc that was written once and never re-driven.

### 30.7 — Config sweep ("nothing hardcoded") + POS offers (user request, 2026-08-01)

User request, two parts explicitly framed as separate: *"All the configuration should be in the configuration page module to module. Nothing will be hardcoded. Analyse every module. See where it is hardcode give in config. Ex-PO edit days, GRN edit days, Pinelabs urls, Middleware url, etc etc, & If I change it should immediately change everywhere. It should be properly wired."* and *"I need offer config in pos module. It will done in erp and reflect in pos as well."*

Scope decisions taken with the user before building: **all four offer families** (core %/flat, spend thresholds, BOGO/bundles, coupon codes + customer tiers); and platform-safety limits are **exposed as settings with enforced Min/Max guardrails** rather than left in code — "do the proper actionable but it should not be hardcoded".

- [x] **30.7.1 Settings registry gains a float type + an explicit-override probe — closed 2026-08-01.** Tolerances and money thresholds (2%, ₹50, ₹21,000) had no representable type: the registry was int/bool/string/select only. Added `SettingTypeFloat` + `GetSettingFloat`/`GetSettingFloatForSchema`, and unified `Min`/`Max` to `*float64` so **one** bound pair serves both numeric types instead of a parallel set of fields (`settingBound` now takes `float64`; whole-number literals convert implicitly, so all 7 existing definitions were untouched). Also added the schema-scoped `GetSettingBoolForSchema`/`GetSettingStringForSchema` siblings, and `SettingIsOverridden` — see 30.7.3 for why that one is load-bearing rather than cosmetic.
- [x] **30.7.2 Every remaining hardcoded operational constant moved to Configuration — closed 2026-08-01.** 7 settings across 3 modules → **36 across 12**. Each Default is byte-for-byte the constant it replaced, so an untouched tenant behaves exactly as before, and each is read via `GetSetting*` **at its consuming site on every use** — never captured into a package var at startup, which is precisely what makes "change it and it changes everywhere" true without a restart. New modules: Sales & Returns, Procurement, Point of Sale, Manufacturing, HR & Payroll, CRM, Warehouse, PIM, Platform.
  - **Business policy:** sales return window (30d, both call sites — `fulfillment.go` *and* `returns.go`), vendor-invoice 3-way tolerance (2%), POS drawer variance (₹50), loyalty redemption value/point (₹1), BOM nesting depth (10), production cost variance (10%), MRP default lead time (7d), ESI wage ceiling (₹21,000 — a statutory figure that should never have needed a redeploy), CRM churn + lapsed thresholds (90d), the three WMS cycle-count tier intervals (30/60/90), WMS productivity threshold (120m), PIM bulk-edit cap (100) and thumbnail size (200px).
  - **Security:** session token TTL (24h), password-reset TTL (30m), account lockout threshold (10) and duration (15m), TOTP clock-drift tolerance (1 step).
  - **Platform (guardrailed):** default/max list page size (500/1000), per-tenant concurrency (15), CSV import batch size (500), max synchronous report rows (5000), blanket field length cap (10000). Each carries a registry `Min`/`Max` that `validateSettingValue` enforces at save time, so the value is admin-editable but a typo can't take the server down.
  - **Signature changes** where the value had no tenant in scope: `LoyaltyRedemptionValue(tenantID, points)`, `VerifyTOTPCode(tenantID, secret, code)`, and `generateThumbnail(bytes, type, maxDim)` — the last takes `maxDim` as a parameter rather than a tenantID, deliberately, so an image helper keeps no DB dependency and the caller resolves the setting.
- [x] **30.7.3 The env-var-beats-config trap, closed rather than shipped — closed 2026-08-01.** `tokenTTL()` already honored `JWT_EXPIRY_HOURS`. Naively adding a setting would have left any deployment that sets that env var with a Configuration control that renders, accepts edits, saves — and does nothing, which is strictly worse than the constant it replaced. Precedence is now **explicit admin edit → env var → registered default**, resolved via the new `SettingIsOverridden` (does a `system_settings` row actually exist, as opposed to the value merely equalling the default).
- [x] **30.7.4 PO / GRN edit windows — the user's named example, which did not exist at all — closed 2026-08-01.** There was amendment-*reason* enforcement (PURCHA-0085) and post-full-receipt blocking (PO-0252), but no time-based rule anywhere, so this is new enforcement, not a migrated constant. New `engines/document_edit_window.go` is driven by a doctype → setting-key map and attached at `ValidateTransactionalRules`' existing shared choke point (beside Stage 29.8's status-transition map) — so it covers every current and future caller of the generic doc API by construction, and adding a window for a third doctype is one map entry plus one `RegisterSetting`. Default **0 = no time limit** for both, so nothing changes for any existing tenant until an admin sets one. Orthogonal to the existing rules, not a replacement. 5 tests (`document_edit_window_test.go`).
- [x] **30.7.5 Integrations reachable from the Configuration page — closed 2026-08-01.** The user's "Pinelabs urls, Middleware url" were **already** tenant-configurable (`pinelabs_credentials` / `unicommerce_credentials`, both with `base_url`, both with working GET+POST endpoints) — the real gap was that **neither had any UI whatsoever**, so a base URL could only be changed by hand-rolling an API call. Added an `Integrations` section to the Configuration screen (Pine Labs terminals keyed by `terminal_id`, Unicommerce/middleware stores keyed by `store_code`), rendered as a synthetic module appended to the registry-driven rail because these are multi-row credential records, not scalar key/values. Deliberately **not** duplicated into the settings registry — two sources of truth for one URL is the drift this whole Stage exists to remove. Secrets are masked in the existing-entries table; save upserts, so the running workers pick a new URL up on their next call.
- [x] **30.7.6 POS offer engine, configured in the ERP and applied at the till — closed 2026-08-01.** No offers/promotions engine existed (`campaign.go` is CRM messaging); POS had a single manual "Discount %" box. `Offer` is a flat-schema Master (`db/migrations_stage30_7_pos_offers.sql`) so create/list/edit come free from the generic doctype machinery — the only new engine code is the evaluator, `engines/pos_offers.go`. One doctype covers all four families via `offer_type` (the same shape Campaign's `trigger_type` uses): **Percentage Off**, **Flat Off**, **Buy X Get Y** (cheapest qualifying units free), **Bundle Price** (most-expensive units bundled first, so adding an item can never raise the bill). Conditions AND together: `min_bill_amount`/`min_qty` (thresholds), `coupon_code` (blank = auto-apply), `customer_tier`, `valid_from`/`valid_to`, `max_discount_amount` cap, `priority`, `stackable`. 16 tests (`pos_offers_test.go`).
  - **Evaluated server-side at checkout, not trusted from the client.** `POST /api/v1/pos/offers/preview` is display-only; `handleCheckout` re-runs the same evaluator against the tenant's own `Offer` rows. So a tampered client can't invent a discount, and an offline-replayed cart is priced against the rules as they stand when it lands. Applied offers + total are persisted onto the cart (`applied_offers`/`offer_discount`) for the receipt and audit trail, the same reason `gst_breakdown` is stored rather than recomputed.
  - **A non-stackable offer ends the stack**, and the running total is floored at the bill, so no combination can drive a sale negative. Unmatched coupon codes are reported back rather than silently dropped.
  - **`module_key` is `sales`, not a new `pos` key** — caught live, not in review: POSCart/POSSession are both mapped to `sales`, and the module gate 403s any doctype whose key no plan grants, so a new key would have made every Offer request fail with SAAS-0191 on every existing tenant.
- **Verified**: `go build` / `go vet` / `node --check` clean; full `go test ./... -p 1` green (db + engines + internal/server); 21 new tests. Live over real HTTP on a throwaway port against the dev DB, including a full MFA/TOTP enrollment to obtain an HR/Admin session: **36 settings across 12 modules** returned by `GET /api/v1/admin/settings`; setting `platform.max_list_limit` to 2 changed a live list endpoint from 3 rows to 2 **in the same process with no restart**, and restoring it returned 3 (this is the "changes everywhere immediately" claim, actually demonstrated); out-of-range `999999` rejected by the guardrail with a specific message; the float type round-tripped `12.5` and rejected `abc`; an Offer created through the ERP applied at the POS preview on the very next call, with the spend threshold, coupon gating (case-insensitive), priority ordering and unmatched-code reporting all behaving as specified. All live test data was removed from the dev DB afterward.
- **Known non-blocking note:** `engines/doctype.go`'s `defaultFieldMaxLength` and `engines/import.go`'s `importBatchRows` constants were re-added by a concurrent session after their call sites had been moved to settings; both are now unused (Go permits unused consts, build is clean) and the live call sites read the settings. Worth deleting whenever that session's work settles.

### 30.8 — Project brain map (user request, 2026-08-01)

User request: *"put in through graphify and create a graphify diagram about this current erp project. Create a folder under docs with a suitable name and plot there. It will explain the entire project in a diagram. It may behave like a brain. We will append if new is added. See how to add easily if any changes done."* Built in `docs/brain/`, generated by `cmd/brainmap`.

- [x] **30.8.1 Region model over the whole tree — closed 2026-08-01.** `docs/brain/brain.map.json` is the only hand-edited file: **35 regions in 8 lobes** (Cortex/Interface, Brainstem/Kernel, Functional Lobes/Business, Peripheral Nerves/Integrations, Autonomic/Background, Hippocampus/Docs, Motor Cortex/Build, Cerebellum/Tests). Membership is by glob pattern, and **the more specific pattern wins** rather than declaration order, so appending a region can never quietly steal files from an existing one; an explicit `priority` overrides that where it must (the test suite claims `engines/pim_bulk_test.go` off PIM's `engines/pim*.go`).
- [x] **30.8.2 Coverage measured against the working tree, not the graph — closed 2026-08-01.** The generator walks the repo (with an ignore list) rather than trusting graphify's file list, because **only 246 of 345 files are parsed** — graphify has no extractor for `.sql`, JSON, PowerShell or Markdown, and `tree_sitter_sql` is not installed, so all 71 migrations contribute zero nodes. Measuring coverage against the graph would have reported 100% while silently omitting a third of the repo. Currently **100% of 345 files claimed**; an unclaimed file is named in the run output, listed in BRAIN.md §6, and fails `update-brain.ps1 -Check`. That is the mechanism that makes "we will append if new is added" self-policing rather than a promise.
- [x] **30.8.3 Declared links for boundaries no call graph can cross — closed 2026-08-01.** The first draft left the whole Cortex floating unconnected, which was *technically* accurate (the SPA is JavaScript, the server is Go, no extractor links them) and completely useless. 8 `declared_links` now state those edges by hand — HTTP/JSON, the error envelope, offline replay, runtime profile loading, outbox dispatch-by-event-name, PowerShell driving migrations and the binary — each drawn as a thick `==>` arrow and carrying a `note` saying *why* it cannot be extracted, so an asserted edge is never mistaken for a measured one.
- [x] **30.8.4 Honest confidence encoding — closed 2026-08-01.** **99% of cross-region relationships are graphify `INFERRED`**, because it resolves calls within a file exactly and calls across files by name — and a cross-region call is cross-file by definition. The first encoding (dot the edge when >50% inferred) therefore dotted literally every edge and carried no information. Now: dotted = *entirely* inferred, solid = contains at least one relationship parsed straight from source, and the legend states the global figure outright. The map's *shape* is reliable; any *single* edge is a lead to confirm with grep, exactly as `CLAUDE.md` requires.
- [x] **30.8.5 Two outputs, one generator — closed 2026-08-01.** `BRAIN.md` (7 Mermaid diagrams — lobe level, region map, the same map with the three universal hubs removed so module-to-module wiring is visible at all, and the four signal pathways — plus a region index and a detail card per region with file:line links) and `brain.html` (interactive: click a region, hover to light its wiring, filter by lobe or connection strength, search by file or symbol, table view). Self-contained, vanilla JS, no CDN, light+dark, per the repo's first principle. Stdlib-only Go, no new dependency.
- [x] **30.8.6 `update-brain.ps1`, and the Windows failure it exists for — closed 2026-08-01.** **Controlled Folder Access** (Defender ransomware protection, on by default) refuses any write under `Documents\` from a binary it does not recognise — and a freshly compiled Go binary never is. It reports that refusal as `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`, so Go says *"the system cannot find the file specified"* about a directory that plainly exists. Confirmed by isolation (writes succeed outside `Documents\`, PowerShell writes succeed inside). The wrapper generates into `%TEMP%` and copies in with PowerShell; `writeOut` in the generator detects the same case and names the cause instead of leaving the next person to debug a phantom missing directory.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...` / `go vet ./cmd/brainmap` clean. All **7 Mermaid blocks parsed with the real mermaid@11 parser** in a headless browser (not eyeballed) — all render, `<small>` survives `securityLevel: strict`. `brain.html` rendered in headless Chromium in **both** light and dark: 35 region cards, 33 links, no console errors, no NaN geometry, no horizontal body overflow. Two real defects were caught this way and fixed: Go's nil slices serialise as `null` and broke every `.length` read on page load, and the lobe-band backgrounds were painting over the connection lines (three explicit z-layers now). Categorical lobe colours validated with the dataviz palette validator — all checks pass in both modes on the adjacent pairlist, which is the right model here because lobes render in a fixed left-to-right order.
- **Deliberately not done:** no CI hook wiring `-Check` into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. It would fail the build for an unfiled *new* file, which is a docs concern, not a correctness one — flagging it in the run output and in BRAIN.md §6 is the right weight. Easy to add later if the brain starts drifting.
