## Stage 32 — Interaction responsiveness ✅ (opened 2026-08-04 from the user's report; 32.1/32.4 closed 2026-08-04/05, **32.2, 32.3 and 32.5 closed 2026-08-05 — Stage 32 is complete**. Note 32.3's scripts are built but not yet run on the droplet, so production still has no backups until the user installs the cron.)

User report, after using the deployed app: *"The interface is not at all smooth. I have to click many times. Then it is working. Hover is also not working. I have to click on arrow then it is showing permanently else it is going away."*

- [x] **32.1.1 The sidebar's hover "aim test" was structurally always-true — closed 2026-08-04.** Root cause of the whole hover complaint. `pointerAimingAtOpenFlyout()` decided whether a module row must be dwelt on for 140ms or 500ms before it may take the open menu, and it asked `flyoutPointerTrail.dx > 0 && r.left >= flyoutPointerTrail.x`. The flyout panel **always** sits to the right of the sidebar, so `r.left >= x` is true for essentially every pointer position in the nav; the whole decision therefore collapsed to `dx > 0`, measured **frame-to-frame**, which at 120Hz is a sub-pixel sample dominated by hand jitter. Result: hovering a module while another menu was open waited **500ms on roughly half of all approaches, at random**. Replaced with a real trajectory test — velocity sampled over a 90ms window, a 12px minimum travel to count as a deliberate reach, and the pointer's projected crossing point required to land inside the panel's **vertical** span (±24px) before the grace applies.
- [x] **32.1.2 The pending open re-evaluates instead of locking in — closed 2026-08-04.** The delay was chosen once at `pointerenter` and then ran to term: `container`'s `pointermove` handler was guarded by `!flyoutOpenTimer`, so while a timer was pending every subsequent move was a no-op. A pointer that had stopped aiming still served the full aim grace. Dwell is now measured from a `hoverStart` stamp and recomputed on every move, so the required wait only ever counts **down** — stop aiming and the menu opens on the next move.
- [x] **32.1.3 Open-timer ownership — closed 2026-08-04.** `cancelFlyoutOpen()` was global, so row B's `pointerleave` could cancel an open row C had already scheduled. It now takes an optional owner (`flyoutOpenTarget`) and leaves another row's pending open alone.
- [x] **32.1.4 Delays cut to what the gesture actually needs — closed 2026-08-04.** Switch 140 → **60ms** (below the ~100ms that reads as instant; it exists only to stop a fast sweep down the sidebar strobing every menu), aim grace 500 → **220ms**, hide 260 → **200ms**. The blanket dwell was doing the job the aim test was supposed to do, which is why it had to be so long.
- [x] **32.1.5 Interaction feedback decoupled from the layout duration — closed 2026-08-04.** Every hover state in the sheet ran on `transition: all var(--transition-speed)` = **250ms**, the duration meant for sidebar-width and panel slides. Hover feedback at 250ms reads as the UI lagging a beat behind the cursor. Added `--transition-fast: 0.11s` / `--transition-med: 0.16s` / `--ease-out`, and pointed `.menu-item`, `.submenu-item`, `.btn` and `.action-btn` at them with **named properties instead of `all`** (`all` was also animating the geometry — padding, border-width — on `.menu-item.active`).
- [x] **32.1.6 Press feedback added — closed 2026-08-04.** There was no `:active` state anywhere. A button that looks identical the instant it is pressed reads as "didn't register", which is precisely what makes a user click again — and on a submit a second click is a duplicate request, not a cosmetic issue. Added `:active` to `.btn` (1px depress), `.action-btn` (0.9 scale), `.menu-item`/`.submenu-item` (background), plus `user-select: none` and `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on the nav rows and buttons so a double-click never selects the label text.
- [x] **32.1.7 Flyout entrance + reduced motion — closed 2026-08-04.** The panel appeared by flipping `display:none → block` with no motion while everything around it moved at 250ms; it now slides 6px in 160ms (`transform`/`opacity` only, so it cannot disturb the `scrollHeight` measurement `openFlyout()` takes immediately after). Closing stays instant. Added the sheet's first `prefers-reduced-motion` block.
- **Verified 2026-08-04, live in Chromium against the real `index.html`/`styles.css`/`app.js`** (harness serves `public/` statically and seeds an `erp_token`; `setupModuleFlyouts()` runs synchronously at the top of `init()`, before the first `await`, so the nav binds fully even with every API call 404ing). 12 assertions, 5 consecutive clean runs. The decisive pair, run against `HEAD` and against the fix:
  - *jittery approach, 6 runs* — **before: `[2, 81, 3, 445, 70, 477]`ms** (the randomness the user was feeling); **after: `[4, 1, 4, 2, 7, 4]`ms.**
  - *hover a module, wait a beat, then reach right for the menu* — **before: no menu at all** (leaving the row cancelled the pending open *and* scheduled the hide, so clicking the arrow was genuinely the only thing that worked — exactly the reported behaviour); **after: the menu is there.**
  - Also asserted: cold hover opens < 150ms; a fast sweep across 10 rows still opens ≤ 2 menus en route; crossing the 8px gap into the panel keeps it open; the diagonal reach to the item at the bottom of an 11-item flyout survives the trip and that item ends up `:hover`ed; leaving the nav closes within 400ms. A straight synthetic path down the sidebar has `dx == 0` and so **never trips the old bug** — reproducing it needed a jittering pointer, which is why this is worth keeping as a harness rather than a one-off check.
- [x] **32.1.8 The user was never running this code — `localhost:8080` is production, not the tree. Closed 2026-08-04.** The whole reason 32.1 "did not fix it": `Open-ERP.cmd` runs `ssh -N -L 8080:127.0.0.1:8080 root@139.59.17.16`, so the app the user tests is a **DigitalOcean droplet**, and it was still serving a **Stage-28-era build (2026-07-27)** — 589KB `app.js` vs the tree's 658KB, with `menu-flyout-bridge` **absent** entirely. Feature-marker dating put it before 28.5/29.9/30.x/31.1. Everything reported was a bug fixed days earlier. **Check what is deployed before debugging a UI report** — `ssh root@139.59.17.16 'sha256sum /opt/erp/public/app.js'` against the local hash.
- [x] **32.1.9 The "AllocationRule needs two clicks" report, root-caused and reproduced — closed 2026-08-04.** From the user's screen recording: the frame at 15.25s shows **Stock's flyout open under the pointer while the page and the highlighted row are HR** — a menu belonging to one module while the app sits on another. That is the pre-28.5 defect exactly: `hideTimer` was **per-container** but `closeSubmenus()` is **global**, so module A's already-armed 200ms timer fires after B's menu has opened and closes it; the menu vanishes from under the pointer and the click lands on the page behind it. The Setup flyout is the worst case (longest list, anchored lowest, so `maxHeight = availableBelow` forces internal scrolling and a long pointer journey). Reproduced mechanically by replaying the gesture — hover module A, move to module B, travel into B's flyout, click: **deployed build 0/6 first clicks registered, local tree 6/6.**
- [x] **32.1.10 Deployed to production — closed 2026-08-04.** On the user's explicit instruction to ship the whole tree, QZ printing included, after the risk was put to them. Cross-compiled `linux/amd64` (no Go toolchain on a 454MB box), shipped binary + `public/` + `db/` + `deploy/`, applied **14 pending migrations**, restarted. Verified: service active on a new PID, `0 pending migrations`, and all four frontend files **byte-identical by SHA-256** to the ones that tested 6/6 locally. Backups taken first — the box had **none** (no `erp` crontab, empty `/opt/erp/backups`): encrypted `pg_dump` plus a full `cp -a` rollback snapshot at `/opt/erp/rollback_20260803T201231Z/`. The two documented Stage 31.1 risks both cleared on real Postgres 14: the nested `$f$` dollar-quoting **parsed and ran** (dry-run inside a rolled-back transaction), and `Stores` had **0 rows** on prod so 30.5.5's data move was a no-op.
- [x] **32.1.11 `deploy/deploy.ps1` bug found by using it — closed 2026-08-04.** Its remote script did a plain `source /etc/erp/erp.env`. That file is a systemd `EnvironmentFile` (bare `KEY=value`, no `export`), so `DATABASE_URL` stayed a shell variable and never reached `migrate.sh` as a child process, which then died on its own `${DATABASE_URL:?}` guard. Now `set -a; source ...; set +a`. Two related traps recorded for next time: since 30.2.2 `migrate.sh` delegates to `erp-server -migrate` with the migrations **embedded in the binary**, so the new binary must be in place before migrating (against the old one it just tries to start a second server); and a hand-rolled psql pending-check through an `ssh` here-string gets its quoting mangled and reports *every* migration as pending, including the base schema — use `-migrate-status`.
- [x] **32.3 Nightly backup cron — scripts built 2026-08-05; the install itself is a production action the user chose to run.** Retention decision taken 2026-08-05: **14 days, on-box only** (no off-box copy, so no new storage credentials). `deploy/backup.sh` already defaulted to `RETAIN_DAYS=14`, so nothing changed there except prefix-scoped pruning (a one-off tenant export can no longer age out the nightly whole-DB backups, and vice versa). Two new scripts close the actual gap, which was that `backup.sh` documented its cron line **in a header comment** that was never acted on:
  - `deploy/install_backup_cron.sh` — idempotent (a marker comment identifies our line, so re-running replaces it rather than appending, and never touches other jobs), preflights every failure mode the droplet actually hit (missing env file, missing `DATABASE_URL`/`BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, missing `pg_dump`/`openssl`, missing user), creates+chowns `/opt/erp/backups` and the log, then **runs one backup immediately** — because a cron line existing is not evidence that the backup works, which is precisely how the box ended up with zero backups.
  - `deploy/restore_drill.sh` — the Linux counterpart of `manage.ps1`'s `Invoke-RestoreDrill`, which only ever existed on the Windows dev machine, so production had no way to test its own backups. Verifies the sha256 sidecar, restores into a throwaway database, compares row counts against live, drops the scratch database. Never writes to the live database.
  - **Not verified on the droplet**: the user chose "local only, I'll deploy", so neither script has been run on production. Both are `bash -n` clean; their behaviour on the real box is unproven until someone runs `sudo bash /opt/erp/deploy/install_backup_cron.sh` there. Until that happens **production still has no backups**.
  - *Original entry, kept for the record (found 2026-08-04):* `/opt/erp/backups` was empty and `crontab -u erp -l` is empty, so before 2026-08-04 that droplet had **no backups at all**. `deploy/backup.sh` exists and works (it produced the pre-deploy dump); it just needs the cron line from its own header installed, plus a restore drill. [needs user go-ahead: retention period and whether backups should ship off-box]
- [x] **32.5 MFA recovery path — BUILT AND CLOSED 2026-08-05.** Design decision (c) approved by the user 2026-08-05; built (c) plus the two adjacent gaps the original entry named, because recovery codes alone still leave a user signed in with a dead authenticator. Three parts, all live-verified against the real dev DB by four handler-level tests (`internal/server/mfa_recovery_test.go`, all passing):
  - **Single-use recovery codes.** 10 codes of 50 bits each (`engines/mfa_recovery.go`), issued at enrollment and shown display-once with copy/download + an acknowledgement gate before the app is entered (`public/index.html` `#mfa-recovery-screen`). Only the SHA-256 hash is stored, never the code — same posture as `engines/password_reset.go`'s reset tokens; SHA-256 rather than bcrypt because these are random high-entropy codes, not user-chosen passwords, which also makes redemption one indexed lookup instead of a bcrypt loop over every outstanding code. `/auth/mfa/verify` accepts one in place of a TOTP code; redemption is a single conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE used_at IS NULL` so two simultaneous logins cannot both spend the same code. New table `mfa_recovery_codes` (`db/migrations_stage32_5_mfa_recovery_codes.sql`, with the standard DO-block catch-up for existing tenant schemas).
  - **Authenticator re-enrollment** (the "move my authenticator to a new phone" flow the entry called out as impossible). New `users.mfa_pending_secret` column parks the new device's secret until a code from it is accepted, so the *existing* device keeps working throughout and an abandoned attempt cannot itself cause a lockout — asserted directly by `TestMFAReenrollMovesAuthenticator`. Re-auth is by password, not TOTP, deliberately: the user who needs this screen is the one whose authenticator is already gone.
  - **Admin reset** (`POST /api/v1/admin/users/reset-mfa`, HR/Admin only, "Reset 2FA" row action on the Users screen) for the colleague who lost both phone and codes. Clears enrollment *and* recovery codes and forces re-enrollment at next login — it does not disable MFA. Replaces the previous only-route of SSH + `cmd/reset_mfa` on a box with no Go toolchain.
  - Profile screen gained a Two-Factor Recovery panel: codes remaining (danger styling at zero, warning at ≤2), regenerate, start/cancel a device change. Signing in with a recovery code now also raises a toast naming the remaining count and pointing at that panel.
  - Note for the record: `handleMFAActivate`'s response gained a `recovery_codes` array, which broke two existing tests that decoded it into `map[string]string` — both updated to `map[string]interface{}` in `server_test.go`. Caught by running the suite, not by inspection.
  - *Original entry, kept for the diagnosis record (found 2026-08-04):* a lost/replaced admin phone locks the tenant out of every HR/Admin screen. Verified by reading the code, not assumed: the only MFA endpoints are `/auth/mfa/enroll`, `/activate`, `/verify` (`routes.go:115-117`), **there are no backup/recovery codes anywhere in the tree** (grep for `recovery_code`/`backup_code` returns nothing), and there is **no endpoint for one admin to reset another's MFA**. `handleMFAEnroll` is gated on `Resolved-Purpose == "mfa_enroll"`, a token `/api/v1/login` only issues to an account that is *not yet* enrolled — so an enrolled user **cannot re-enroll from inside the app**, i.e. there is no "move my authenticator to a new phone" flow. The only recovery is `cmd/reset_mfa` (host-shell + DB access), which on production means SSH to the droplet, and that box has **no Go toolchain**, so it needs a cross-compiled binary or a hand-run `UPDATE users SET mfa_enabled=false, mfa_secret=NULL`. With a single HR/Admin account on prod, that SSH key is the *only* way back in. Mitigations, cheapest first: (a) transfer the authenticator entry with the app's own export feature *before* switching phones — zero code, but only works while the old device still exists; (b) a second HR/Admin account enrolled on a different device, so a lost phone costs one login rather than all admin access; (c) the actual fix — issue single-use recovery codes at enrollment and accept one in place of a TOTP code at `/mfa/verify`. [needs design decision: (c) is the right fix but adds a credential to store and display-once UI — confirm before building]
  - Related dev-loop trap worth remembering: **`admin` is the one MFA-mandatory role, so it cannot be used for quick local verification** — use `manager1`/`cashier1` (see `DEV_CREDENTIALS.local.txt`). And the login/MFA rate budget is **5 requests per minute per IP** (`rateLimitCategory`, `middleware.go:168`), which covers `/login`, `/mfa/verify` *and* `/mfa/activate` — four wrong codes in half a minute puts the next attempts into 429 regardless of correctness. IPv4 `127.0.0.1` and IPv6 `::1` are **separate buckets**, so a curl test can pass while the browser is still throttled.
- [x] **32.4 Two console errors on the deployed login screen — diagnosed, closed 2026-08-05.** Root cause: `renderExecDashboard`'s `#exec-dashboard-cards` was written to without a null-check when its `Promise.all` of report fetches resolved after the view had already changed underneath it (Reports is `DEFAULT_VIEW`, so it renders on every login and can still be in flight when an invalid token's 401s trigger `logout()`/`showLoginScreen()` mid-`init()`). That exact bug — introduced with the executive dashboard itself in `ef0e39b` (Stage 26.10, 2026-07-26) — was already fixed by the `if (!cardsRow) return;` guard added in `0bee663` (2026-08-02, see `ai_handover.md`'s note on that commit's incidental fixes). The observation window lines up exactly: the box serving `32.1.8`'s Stage-28-era build (dated 2026-07-27) predates the 2026-08-02 fix, so the console errors seen on 2026-08-04 were this bug on the stale deploy, and `32.1.10`'s same-day full-tree deploy already shipped the fix to production. **Verified**: a live Playwright repro against current `HEAD` (invalid `erp_token` injected via `addInitScript`, full `bootstrap()`→`init()` sequence, then a sweep of all 45 client-reachable views under the same invalid token) produced **zero `pageerror`/uncaught-exception events** — only 10 browser-native `"Failed to load resource: 401"` network-log lines (one per endpoint `init()`/the exec dashboard hits), which is almost certainly what the "~10x" count in the original observation was counting. Both harnesses were scratch and not kept.
  - **Secondary, harmless finding, not tracked separately**: `logout()` (`app.js:1152`) has no re-entrancy guard, so the ~10 sequential 401s each call it once, stacking up to 10 click-listener registrations on `#custom-dialog-ok-btn`/`#custom-dialog-close-btn` before any of them clean up. Confirmed harmless — `replaceWith` on an already-detached node and `resolve()` on an already-settled Promise are both silent no-ops — but if this area is ever touched again, an early-return when `document.getElementById('signout-overlay')` is already unhidden would remove the redundancy.
- [x] **32.2 Smoothness pass beyond the nav — audited and closed 2026-08-05.** Done as the per-screen interaction audit the entry offered as the alternative to the user naming screens.
  - **Transition sweep, deliberate not blind.** The "~20 remaining sites" estimate was stale — 32.1 had already swept most; **11** were left, and each was read against the `:hover`/`:focus` rule it actually pairs with before being changed. Every one turned out to animate only `background-color`/`border-color`/`box-shadow`/`color`/`opacity`, i.e. pure feedback, so all 11 moved from `--transition-speed` (0.25s, the *layout* duration) to explicit property lists at `--transition-fast` (0.11s) — the convention the token block's own comment sets and 32.1's sidebar fix already follows. Sites: `.header-search`, `.search-box`, `.modal-close`, `.form-input/.form-select/.form-textarea`, `.pagination-btn`, `.account-trigger`, `.account-popover-item`, `.theme-seg-btn`, `.copy-chip`, `.custom-dialog-close-x`, and `.account-trigger-chevron` (→ `--transition-med`, since it is tied to the popover opening rather than a bare hover). The form-control one is the widest-reaching: `transition: all` there was also animating `width`, which interacts badly with Stage 33's responsive form grid. The **only** remaining `--transition-speed` use is `.sidebar { transition: width }` — correct, it is genuinely layout.
  - **The likely second cause of "I have to click many times", found.** `renderView()` cleared `#view-root` and *then* awaited the view's fetches, so on anything slower than localhost a click produced a blank white panel with no sign it had registered — and the natural response is to click again. No amount of transition tuning could have fixed this, because there was nothing rendered to transition. Split into `renderView` (a thin wrapper owning feedback: clear → show an indeterminate loading bar → hand off) and `renderViewContent` (the untouched dispatch), with the placeholder removed in a `finally` so a throwing renderer cannot leave a permanent "Loading…" on screen. New `.view-loading` styles honour `prefers-reduced-motion` by holding the bar static rather than dropping the feedback entirely.
  - Since this closes the last open Stage 32 item, **Stage 32 is complete**. Frontend-only, so it ships with 33.2's deploy.

## Stage 33 — Dialog viewport fit & responsive form layout ✅ (opened + closed 2026-08-04 from the user's screenshot; deployed to production 2026-08-06)

User report, with a screenshot of the New Location dialog: *"Nothing is visible here. So, set up. Make it more 2 column or 3 if required so it will be visible. All screen and config it should auto adjust with 100% to any percent 40% or whatever. If 120% also it should get auto adjusted."*

- [x] **33.1.1 Every dialog in the app could be taller than the screen with no way to scroll — closed 2026-08-04.** The real defect behind "nothing is visible". `.modal-container` had **no `max-height` and no scroll region**, and `body` is `overflow: hidden`. So a form taller than the viewport got centred by `.modal-overlay`'s flexbox and then clipped off **both** ends — the first fields *and* the Save/Cancel footer were unreachable, with nothing anywhere to scroll. The container is now a flex column bounded by `calc(100dvh - 40px)` (with a `vh` fallback line), the header and footer are `flex: 0 0 auto`, and `.modal-body` takes the overflow (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto`). `min-height: 0` is the load-bearing part — without it a flex child refuses to shrink below its content and the container overflows again. Also added `padding: 20px 16px` to the overlay so the dialog can never touch the viewport edge.
- [x] **33.1.2 The `<form>` wrapper was swallowing the flex chain — closed 2026-08-04.** Found by the harness, not by reading: 33.1.1 alone still failed at ≤1280x720. In `index.html` most dialogs wrap body+footer in `<form>`, so the **form** is the container's flex child, not `.modal-body` — the form stayed a content-sized block, the body never received leftover height to scroll in, and the footer was clipped exactly as before. Fixed with `.modal-container > form { display: flex; flex-direction: column; flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0 }`, written as `> form` so the handful of JS-built modals that put `.modal-body` directly in the container are unaffected.
- [x] **33.1.3 The generic document form is a 1/2/3-column intrinsic grid — closed 2026-08-04.** `openDynamicModal()` (every master and document create/edit screen) is the one dialog with an unbounded field count — Item renders ~20, and one column made it taller than any screen. `#dynamic-modal-body` is now `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(240px, 100%), 1fr))`: **no breakpoints and no media queries** — the column count follows the width actually available, which is exactly what makes it zoom-proof (zoom only changes the CSS-pixel viewport, and both the grid and the `dvh` bound are recomputed from it). Scoped to `#dynamic-modal-body`, so the table/summary modals that share `.modal-body` are untouched.
- [x] **33.1.4 Wide dialog is opt-in above 4 fields; composite editors span the row — closed 2026-08-04.** A 3-field master in a 920px box would be two-thirds empty row, so `openDynamicModal` sets `.modal-container-wide` only above 4 fields; below that the dialog stays 520px and the 240px grid minimum yields one column on its own, i.e. **small masters look exactly as they did**. The JSONTable/JSONMap line editors (30.5.3) get `.form-group-wide` → `grid-column: 1 / -1`, since squeezing a multi-column table into a 240px track defeats the point. Column count itself stays the stylesheet's job; the field count is the only thing JS needed to know.
- [x] **33.1.5 Same fix applied to the second dialog system — closed 2026-08-04.** `.custom-dialog-box` (`showCustomAlert`/`showCustomConfirm`/`showApiError`/`showCustomPrompt`) had the identical structure and the identical bug: a long `showApiError` detail list clipped off-screen with its OK button unreachable. Given the same flex column, `dvh` bound, pinned header/footer and scrolling body. Verified with a 40-row error list.
- **Verified 2026-08-04 in Chromium, two harnesses, all cases passing.** (1) The real `styles.css` against the DOM `openDynamicModal` builds, at 8 viewport sizes standing in for zoom **40% → 300%** plus a deliberately short 1400x380 window: 3 columns down to 1097px, 2 at 768px, 1 at 520px; container inside the viewport and the **Save button reachable in every case**; body scrolls precisely when it needs to. Before the fix, the 120%/140%/200%/300%/short cases put Save *below* the viewport bottom (e.g. `save=[720,757]` in a 720px-tall window) with `scrolls=false`. (2) A regression pass on the **real `index.html`+`app.js`** served over HTTP at 3 sizes: `add-field`, `edit-prefix`, `add-label` and `bulk-import` all still measure `cols=1 display=flex w=520` — the grid rule leaked into nothing — and the long custom dialog scrolls with its footer on screen. No page errors.
- [x] **33.1.6 Cache-buster versions bumped — closed 2026-08-04.** `index.html` referenced `styles.css?v=14` / `app.js?v=20`; both files changed, so a browser holding the old query string would have served the **cached pre-fix files and shown no change at all** — including on production behind the tunnel. Now `?v=15` / `?v=20`. Note this also covers Stage 32.1's `app.js` edits, which never bumped it. **Any future change to either file must bump its `?v=`** or the fix ships invisibly.
- [x] **33.2 Deployed to production 2026-08-06** (user go-ahead given this date). Ran `deploy/deploy.ps1 -Target root@139.59.17.16` at commit `22d1feb`. Verified after: the cache-buster query strings on the box moved from `app.js?v=21`/`styles.css?v=16` to `?v=22`/`?v=17`, matching the tree — which is the check that actually proves the frontend fix is being served rather than a cached pre-fix file (33.1.6's own point); `/api/v1/health` 200; `systemctl is-active erp` active; `public/` file ownership still `erp:erp` and readable by the service user after the root-owned `scp`. **Incidental finding**: the deploy also applied **two migrations that were pending on production** — `migrations_stage26_4_10_supplier_portal.sql` and `migrations_stage32_5_mfa_recovery_codes.sql` — meaning the supplier portal and the MFA recovery-code tables had been built and committed but never reached the box. Worth noting for the record that a frontend-only deploy carried them; `deploy.ps1` runs migrations with the newly-uploaded binary before swapping it, so this was safe, but it means "frontend-only change" did not mean "frontend-only deploy".
  - *Original entry, kept for the record:*<br>**Not on production yet — OPEN.** Per 32.1.8, `localhost:8080` is the droplet, so the user will not see any of this until the tree is deployed (`deploy/deploy.ps1`, now that 32.1.11 fixed its env-export bug). Frontend-only change: `public/styles.css` + `public/app.js`, no migration, no binary rebuild needed. [needs user go-ahead to deploy]

## Stage 36 — PIM parity (Unbxd level) ✅ (2026-08-11 → 30)

- [x] **36.1 Product groups** (2026-08-11/12) — `PIMProductGroup` (static, hand-picked; dynamic, saved typed filters ANDed and re-resolved live). `ResolvePIMBulkTargetIDs` is the one seam a group resolves through for bulk edit, export and (once 36.2 existed) tasks/workflows — group and explicit selection mutually exclusive, refused not merged. **Group Actions** modal on the Item table.
- [x] **36.2 Task & workflow engine** (2026-08-15) — the biggest PIM gap: approvals answer "may this proceed?", tasks answer "someone must improve this, by when, owned by whom" — never modelled on each other. 4 doctypes (`PIMTaskTemplate`/`PIMWorkflowDefinition`/`PIMTask`/`PIMWorkflowRun`); a task's state machine IS `documents.status` (free RBAC/audit/reporting); workflow conditions are a closed declarative vocabulary, never a scripting runtime; `AdvancePIMWorkflowRun` is idempotent so run-creation/task-completion/manual-advance can all call it safely. **My Work** tab (4th PIM tab, no menu changes needed).
- [x] **36.3 Import depth** (2026-08-28) — scheduled (hourly worker) + drop-directory (`imported`/`failed` subfolders, never deletes) + `PIMImportTemplate` (column-name→field mapping, per-column transform rule) + API hook (per-schedule token, digest-only) + variant-parent preflight (applies to every Item import) + preview (mapping + dry-run). **Bug found & fixed**: `db.InitDB` never closed the previous pool before opening a new one — leaked connections across the test suite until exhaustion.
- [x] **36.4 Export & syndication** (2026-08-30) — `PIMExportTemplate` (chosen/ordered columns, headerless mode, per-channel field scoping via `BuildChannelPayload`), `PIMExportSchedule` (existing outbox, no real SMTP/webhook client), parent/variant-collapsed shapes, `PIMCatalog` (shareable tokenised read-only link, digest-only storage, public `pim-catalog-share.html` viewer, curated projection only), bulk channel download (`ChannelReader` interface, Shopify-only, shares one diff helper with the existing publish preview so the two "what changed" views can't disagree).
- [x] **36.5 Declarative transform rules** (2026-08-27/28) — `PIMTransformRule` (10-function closed vocabulary: trim/case/prefix/suffix/truncate/default/find-replace-literal/number+date format), wired into export (`ChannelFieldMap.transform_rule`) and import (`PIMImportTemplate`'s per-column rule, 36.3.3).
- [x] **36.6 DAM depth** (2026-08-30) — on-demand transform presets (small/medium/large/square, cached by checksum+preset, shares its resize math with the existing thumbnail), bulk zip up/down with filename-based auto-association (`ITEMCODE__role.ext`, round-trippable, decompressed-size-bounded against zip bombs), catalog-wide tag/role/file-type search + a new **Media Library** browse tab. **Environment note, not a code defect**: this Windows dev machine's Controlled Folder Access silently drops a file write from the freshly-built server binary when its working directory sits under `Documents\`, blocking a full live-HTTP upload↔read-back proof on this specific machine; the engine tests prove the read/write logic correct against a real `%TEMP%` path in-process, and production's working directory is not under `Documents\`.
- [x] **36.7 Enrichment & quality** (36.7.2 built 2026-08-12; rest 2026-08-30) — channel-shaped content assist (`shape=marketplace`: tighter title, bullets, meta description — still local/offline, no network call, per 26.4.11's own governance decision, restated deliberately for a generic shape rather than naming a platform); related products (same family + shared global attribute values, ranked by overlap); UPC/EAN generation and check-digit validation (one canonical right-to-left GS1 mod-10 implementation for EAN-8/UPC-A/EAN-13 alike, pinned against 3 published real-world GTINs; generation seeded from GS1's own internal-use `020`-`029` prefix range via the existing numbering machinery); bulk catalog translation (seeds a Draft in the target language from Approved source-language content — translation source resolved the same local-only way as 26.4.11, not a machine-translation integration — still needs a human + the existing approval gate); attribute-level permission audit that **found and closed a real gap**: `BulkUpdateDocuments` and the whole CSV/template import path never called `RejectRestrictedFieldWrites`, so a role blocked from editing a field one document at a time (the shipped Cashier/`gst_rate` policy) could still push it through in bulk.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean throughout; full engine test suites per sub-stage all passing, `go test ./... -p 1` fully green at each close. Every migration applied directly to the dev database (ledger row inserted matching `erp-server -migrate`'s own format) to avoid also applying the unrelated already-pending `migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql` belonging to other in-progress work. Live-HTTP and/or real-browser passes at every sub-stage close, fixtures hard-deleted afterward each time.

## Stage 41 — User-reported batch: schema edit, sticky headers, country/phone, setup guidance, POS location ✅ (2026-08-10)

- [x] **41.1** Field Definition edit — previously delete-and-recreate only (discarded a field's stored data on every existing document); added `UpdateFieldDefinition` + `PUT /api/v1/meta/{doctype}/fields/{id}`. Found and wired up an orphaned Add-Field modal that had sat in `index.html` unconnected to `app.js` since it was written, using six data-losing `showCustomPrompt` dialogs instead.
- [x] **41.2** Sticky table headers — root-caused to CSS2.1 §17.5.1 table paint-layer ordering (a row group stays in the table layer regardless of its own `position`, so `position: sticky` on `thead` was fighting paint order, not fixing it); moved sticky onto the `<th>` cells themselves, which paints in the positioned-descendants layer above every `<td>`.
- [x] **41.3** Country-aware phone normalization — new `engines/phone.go` (54-country table, no new dependency); wired into `ValidateDocument`, the one choke point every doc write passes through, so ~19 phone fields across the schema get it with no per-screen wiring. Masters are strict (validated against the tenant's configured country); orders clean and tag but never reject on phone. Found and fixed a real ordering bug (the country tag was derived after the dial code had already been stripped, misattributing a `+1` US number to India) and a collision with Stage 40.2's field-format detection.
- [x] **41.4** Setup-status advisor — `GET /api/v1/setup/status` (live/active counts per Master doctype, derived from `doctype_meta`, zero code change for a new doctype); per-field hint (loud only when genuinely blocking) + per-screen banner (sessionStorage dismissal, not permanent) + permission-aware messaging + real deep links (`#/setup/<Doctype>`).
- [x] **41.5** POS location picker — split into a name-searchable box + hidden code field (every downstream reader keys off the code, unchanged); built as a generic `attachCodeNamePicker`, reused across 15 other screens that pick a location.
- **Verified**: 1 migration, 3 new files (`engines/phone.go`, `engines/setup_advisor.go`, `internal/server/handlers_setup_advisor.go`), zero new dependencies; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean; live-verified over real HTTP and through a real browser as both Store Manager and Super Admin.

## Stage 42 — WMS Parity Plan (SAP EWM + Infor WMS) ✅ (2026-08-16 → 24; all 6 open decisions resolved 2026-08-31) — 58 items, 6 phases

Consolidated from [SAP Extended Warehouse Management](https://help.sap.com/docs/SAP_EXTENDED_WAREHOUSE_MANAGEMENT/) and [Infor WMS 11.5.x](https://docs.infor.com/wms/11.5.x/en-us/sceolh/default.html), reconciled against real code. Full per-item detail, the 33-row gap table and the explicit out-of-scope list lived in `docs/specs/wms_parity_plan.md` before this move. **Infor was the benchmark actually aimed at** (right size, ~70% of its TOC achievable here); **SAP feature-list parity was explicitly not attempted** — read only as a concept checklist. MFS/PLC equipment control, Infor's 3D yard view, and a separate Warehouse Order object are explicit non-goals, on the record.

- **42.1 Traceability foundation** (11 items, closed 2026-08-19) — `Batch`/`SerialNumber` masters; a new `bin_stock_batch` breakdown table, not a column (avoids rewriting `bin_stock`'s primary key); FEFO/FIFO allocation through one `AllocateFromStock` choke point; expiry gates; outbound lottable constraints; UOM conversion; a hand-rolled Code128 barcode encoder/renderer (`engines/code128.go`, no new dependency, checksum pinned against a third-party worked example). **Real bug found and fixed**: the generic doc-save path only ever ran master-data validation for Item/Vendor/Customer/ProductContent, so Batch/SerialNumber/LottableConstraint/UOMConversion's own duplicate-row rejections were unreachable through the live UI the entire time — collapsed into one unconditional call.
- **42.2 The warehouse task spine** (10 items, closed 2026-08-19) — `WarehouseTask` doctype, retrofitted onto the 5 existing floor actions with no signature change; `GetNextTask` dispatch (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`); configurable `TaskDispatchStrategy`; `Zone` master (kept `Bin.zone` as Data, not Link, the same "don't rewrite live tenant data for a cosmetic type change" call made throughout this Stage); bin capacity enforcement; directed putaway; configurable `AllocationStrategy` (FEFO always wins for a batch-tracked item, never overridable); exception-code follow-on actions; the Warehouse Cockpit. **Real bug found and fixed, unrelated to this Stage**: a Stage 45 change to `renderView`'s render-buffering left a scratch container fully detached from the DOM until render finished, silently breaking `getElementById` for the overwhelming majority of screens, not just this Stage's two new ones.
- **42.3 Inbound depth** (10 items, closed 2026-08-20) — dock/appointment scheduling with capacity-overlap enforcement; yard check-in/check-out; hold/release riding the existing maker-checker engine unchanged; configurable receipt-tolerance rules; catch-weight/dimension capture; planned cross-dock/transship; hazmat compliance gating on putaway; an RF receiving screen (keyboard-wedge scan input, the same convention POS already uses).
- **42.4 Outbound depth** (11 items, closed 2026-08-21) — `WaveTemplate` auto-wave creation (hourly worker reusing the existing scheduled-report ticker shape, not a second timer mechanism); a real Wave lifecycle that gates pick-list generation; sortation/put-wall; cartonization v2; packing validation; deconsolidation; loading + Bill of Lading; a pre-ship gate; VAS/kitting off the existing Manufacturing BOM.
- **42.5 Inventory control depth** (8 items, closed 2026-08-24) — physical inventory freeze/count/reconcile; `CycleClass` ABC-tier plans; demand-driven + wave-triggered + dynamic-pick-face replenishment; slotting v2 (unslotting, dimension-aware re-slot, consolidation); facility hierarchy + copy + cross-facility inquiry; multi-owner stock segregation (42.5.5, gated on decision 42.D2 — resolved 2026-08-24: yes, 3PL is real scope — via a new `bin_stock_owner` breakdown table, the same non-destructive pattern as `bin_stock_batch`, rewiring both billing paths onto it).
- **42.6 Labour and billing depth** (9 items, closed 2026-08-22) — engineered labour standards computed at run time, never a stale flat-seconds override; shift/schedule masters; labour planning + a 6-report family; charge codes/rate groups/contracts; an event monitor that auto-captures owner-attributed billable charges with no duplicate-capture risk on retry; invoice generation from captured charges (closing Stage 26.5.15's report-only carve-out); storage billing v2 off daily balance snapshots.
- **6 open decisions, all resolved**: 42.D1 (regulated-category traceability worth building regardless — acted on early rather than waiting), 42.D2 (3PL/multi-owner is real scope — yes), 42.D3 (batch AND serial, not batch-only — asked directly), 42.D4 (moot — the RF screen's keyboard-wedge input works identically with or without real scanner hardware), 42.D5 (wave auto-creation confirmed and built), 42.D6 (Zone-master auto-create migration confirmed non-destructive — resolved 2026-08-31 as a docs-only close; the migration itself was already written non-destructively and applied to dev when 42.2 closed 2026-08-19).
- **Verified**: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean at every phase close; live-verified over real HTTP at every phase close on throwaway servers, fixtures removed afterward each time. Zero new dependencies across all 6 phases. Committed across `2a45c49` (42.6) and `225cb06` (42.5.5) — **not yet deployed to production**, which remained at commit `2575097` (2026-08-21) as of this move; see `docs/ai_handover.md` §6 for the current deployed-vs-tree gap.

## Stage 43 — Go-live blocker sweep: the buildable half of the parked items ✅ (2026-08-12)

User handed back 8 parked go-live items with "build from this which is possible, skip which needs decision for now." 5 of the 8 were genuinely credential-/decision-shaped and stayed open in their own home stages (escalation contacts, connector/GSP/e-way-bill/payment-terminal credentials, Cloudflare activation, pen-test vendor engagement, UAT participants/hypercare sign-off — tracked under Stage 20/26, not here). 3 had real, ungated repository work hiding behind them:

- [x] **43.1** **Token claim delimiter smuggling — a live security defect, fixed.** The session-token payload was a flat `k=v&k=v` string built from columns (username, role, tenant, location_code) with no character restrictions; a crafted value containing `&`/`=` could forge `purpose`/`role`/`tenant` claims that still carried a valid HMAC signature — a working privilege-escalation and cross-tenant path (`requireHRAdmin`-gated, so insider-persistence rather than anonymous remote). Fixed with `url.QueryEscape`/`SplitN` at the one choke point every token kind already passes through (`ParseToken`).
- [x] **43.2** **Nightly-backup failure and absence now alert.** The backup script self-reports failure to the ops webhook (`ERR`/`EXIT` traps); a new hourly `StartBackupFreshnessMonitor` catches the failure mode the script structurally cannot — a cron that never fired at all, which is what 26.11.3 had found production was actually doing (no nightly backup had ever run).
- [x] **43.3** **`X-Forwarded-For` now parsed right-to-left against a trusted-proxy set.** The old left-most-entry parse was correct only for as long as Caddy's `trusted_proxies` config line stayed right, with a silent, total bypass of the login rate limiter as the failure mode. Walking right-to-left past known proxy hops means a forged header can't bypass the limiter even if that config ever drifts, and makes Cloudflare activation an env-var change rather than a code change.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./engines/ -p 1` clean; `internal/server` tests (`TestClientIP`, 10/10) passing.

## Stage 46 — Money precision: paise migration ✅ (2026-08-21, deployed to production same day — commit `2575097`)

Closed the two items both `docs/DURABILITY_AUDIT_2026-07-31.md` and `docs/ERP_LOOPHOLES_ANALYSIS.md` independently flagged as still-open: an Item code/SKU charset gap in the stored-XSS mitigation, and `gl_postings.debit`/`.credit` stored as whole-rupee `INT`, silently floor-truncating every GST component before posting (a ₹999 sale at 18% GST posted ₹178 of output tax instead of ₹179.82, always downward, compounding forever). User chose the full fix (paise `BIGINT`) over a cheaper `math.Round` band-aid when asked.

- [x] **46.1** Item code/SKU charset allowlist, closing the durability audit's finding 6 (`itemCodePattern` in `validateItemMasterRules`, forward-only gate).
- [x] **46.2-46.5** **Paise internal, rupees external.** `RupeesToPaise`/`PaiseToRupees` is the one rounding rule for the whole codebase; every money-surfacing report/response converts back to rupees at its own boundary, so the external API contract and the frontend's existing `.toFixed(2)` formatting needed zero changes. The precision fix was applied at its actual float sources (POS checkout unit-price truncation, GST booking, returns, TDS), not just the DB column — found by grepping every `gl_postings` reference in the tree rather than trusting the initial scope survey. Subsystems deliberately kept whole-rupee (FX revaluation, payroll, vendor-invoice payment, journal vouchers, marketplace settlement, asset capitalisation, expense claims) scale via `PaiseMap` at the `PostDoubleEntry` boundary instead of inventing precision they never validated or persisted.
- [x] **46.6** **Verified** against a from-scratch throwaway Postgres instance (123 migrations, own port/data directory); live proof of the audit's own worked example — `CalculateGST(999, 18, false)` posted CGST=8991/SGST=8991 paise (₹179.82 total), versus the pre-fix ₹178 the audit measured.
- [x] **46.7** **Production deploy**, commit `2575097` — user gave the go-ahead ("commit all and deploy"); fresh backup + rollback snapshot taken first. **This is the last commit actually live on production as of the 2026-08-31 doc-sync pass** — everything built since (the rest of Stage 42, Stage 35's close, all of Stage 36, three closed decisions) is sitting committed-but-undeployed or fully uncommitted; see `docs/ai_handover.md` §6 for the current gap.
- `docs/DURABILITY_AUDIT_2026-07-31.md` and `docs/ERP_LOOPHOLES_ANALYSIS.md` were both confirmed updated to point here as of the 2026-08-31 doc-sync pass.

Six gaps the user reported directly, built in the priority order they picked. **Deliberately numbered 40, not 35** — Stages 35-39 belong to the Parity Master Plan above, where 35.1/35.5/35.6 already mean other things.

- [x] **40.1.1** PurchaseOrder line items — `items` converted to `JSONTable` (Stage 30.5.3 pattern; storage unchanged, no backfill). Columns: `sku` (link Item) / `qty` / `rate` (Purchase Price) / `mrp` (optional). HSN + GST% deliberately **not** columns — they stay on the Item master and are resolved server-side, so tax classification has one source of truth.

Explicit override supported and preserved across saves; an underived result reports *why* rather than defaulting to intra-state.

- [x] **40.1.6** Send to Vendor — records dispatch, fires `PurchaseOrderIssued` through the existing notification engine, `mailto:` fallback when no channel is configured. Refuses to send a Draft.

- [x] **40.1.8** Confirmed **RFQ is not mandatory** — `ConvertRequisitionToOrder` already targets RFQ *or* PurchaseOrder and nothing gates a PO on one existing. Documented in USER_GUIDE §6. *(No code change needed.)*
- [x] **40.2.1** `engines/field_formats.go` — GSTIN / email / phone / PAN / IFSC / PIN code / URL, detected by fieldname (generalises 41.3's `IsPhoneField`), enforced at `ValidateDocument`. **Never makes a field mandatory**; empty always passes.
- [x] **40.2.2** `GET /api/v1/meta/field-formats` + frontend decoration: placeholders, keystroke filtering (phone refuses letters), auto-upper-case, inline blur messages. Driven by the server's declarations so the two cannot drift.
- [x] **40.3.1** Role `HR/Admin` → `Super Admin`: data migration across `users`/`role_permissions`/`approval_rules`/`approval_log`/`field_permissions` in every tenant schema, canonicalised once in `apiMiddleware`, legacy name accepted permanently.
- [x] **40.3.2** 37 scattered role-string comparisons across 9 files collapsed into `engines.IsSuperAdmin`. `RequiresMFA` moved off a literal-keyed map (which would have silently dropped MFA for a legacy-named session).
- [x] **40.4.1** Stdlib gzip + versioned-asset immutable caching wrapping the whole mux. Measured: cold load ~822KB → ~200KB.
- [x] **40.4.2** Google Fonts `@import` removed (3 chained third-party round trips before first paint) → platform UI font; CSP's two font exceptions dropped with it, so nothing third-party loads at all.

- [x] **40.5** Motion system — decelerating view entry, delayed loader, sub-perceptual press feedback, modal scale-in, `contain: paint` on tables. `transform`/`opacity` only; sits under the existing `prefers-reduced-motion` block.
- [x] **40.6.1** Manual order entry in the OMS, posting to the existing `POST /api/v1/orders` so allocation/reservation/holds/idempotency all apply.
- [x] **40.6.2** Orders table Channel → **Source**, showing the originating system *and its order id there* — the visible sync trail.
- [x] **40.7** Docs: USER_GUIDE §6 rewritten (its old step 3 promised item selection that did not exist), new §6.2 GST, §8.1 field formats, §9A manual orders + order-to-cash walkthrough. Role renamed across all six hand-written guides and in `cmd/gendocs`.

**All three items below closed 2026-09-13.**
- [x] **40.8** Browser pass on the PO composer and the motion system — **live-verified in a real Chromium instance** (Playwright, scratch server, `manager1`/Store Manager token), not just over HTTP/by test as before. 11/11 checks: composer renders (vendor/location/add-item), a real line fill (`QA-JEANS-01` × 5 @ 100) debounce-triggers the server preview and resolves HSN `6203`/GST `12%`/taxable/tax/line total, the totals panel and inter-state IGST banner populate correctly, `#view-root > *`'s `view-enter` animation runs at the full `0.22s` normally and collapses under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (Chromium reports the collapsed value as `1e-05s`, numerically identical to the `0.01ms` the global override sets - not a bug, just a unit-notation difference), and Print correctly falls back to the browser sheet with no QZ Tray present. Screenshot taken, scratch server/fixtures torn down after.
  - **Real bug found and fixed, not specific to the PO composer: `internal/server/middleware.go`'s CSP had `connect-src 'self'` with no exception for QZ Tray's own WebSocket endpoints** (`ws://localhost:{8182,8283,8384,8485}`, `wss://localhost.qz.io:{8181,8282,8383,8484}` - Stage 31.1.1's own documented port list). Every `qzTryPrint` call in the entire app - receipts, invoices, shipping labels, and now POs - has been silently CSP-blocked from ever reaching QZ Tray in any CSP-enforcing browser since Stage 31.1 shipped, always falling straight to the browser fallback with no visible error. Nobody had caught this because every prior live-browser QZ pass (Stage 45, this one until the CSP fix) deliberately tested only "QZ Tray absent" - the fallback path - never a real connection attempt. Fixed by scoping `connect-src` to add exactly those two loopback hosts (`ws://localhost:*`, `wss://localhost.qz.io:*` - `:*` covers the exact 4 ports each without hardcoding them a second place to drift from `qz-print.js`), both of which resolve to `127.0.0.1` per that file's own comment, so this is not a new attack surface. This does not make 31.1.8's still-open physical-hardware verification any less genuinely blocked (no printer/QZ install on this machine), but it removes a defect that would have silently blocked it even once that hardware is available.
- [x] **40.9** `customer_phone` on a manual order - **live-verified 2026-09-13.** The dev DB's stock blocker no longer applies (a PO since restocked the test SKU); `POST /api/v1/orders` with `customer_phone: "+91 98765 43210"` against `QA-JEANS-01` stored `customer_phone: "9876543210"` (cleaned to national format) and `customer_country: "IN"` on the resulting `SalesOrder` exactly as `engines/orders.go`'s Stage 41 phone-cleaning path is written to do. (The order landed `On Hold`/`ADDR_INVALID` on the unrelated free-text shipping address, exactly the intended non-blocking-hold behaviour, not a phone defect.) Test order and its temporary Cancellation-category `ReasonCode` fixture (needed to cancel and release the reservation - none existed in this dev DB before) both cleaned up afterward via the real API.
- [x] **40.10** QZ silent-print builder for a PO - **built 2026-09-13.** `engines.BuildPurchaseOrderQZPayload`/`renderPurchaseOrderPayload` (`engines/qz_payload.go`) reuse `BuildPurchaseOrderPrint` (40.1.5) unchanged for pricing/party resolution, so the silent copy and the browser fallback sheet can never disagree on a total; new `job_type: "Purchase Order"` case in `handlers_qz_print.go`'s switch; `printPurchaseOrder` in `app.js` now tries `qzTryPrint('Purchase Order', {quiet:true})` first, exactly like `printSalesInvoice`, before falling back to the browser sheet. `db/migrations_stage40_10_qz_po_print.sql` appends `'Purchase Order'` to the `Printer.print_role` Select list (additive, applied to dev). 4 new Go tests (`engines/qz_po_print_test.go`, HTML draft-marking/CGST-SGST-vs-IGST/ESC-POS-raw-stream/words-omitted-when-absent) plus **live-verified over real HTTP** on a scratch server against a real PO with real GST breakdown (both the HTML and ESC-POS branches, printer_code override, missing-`document_ref`/unknown-PO error paths) - scratch Printer fixture removed after. `docs/guides/QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md` updated (`Default For` list, day-to-day-use table). `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean; `go test ./engines/ -run 'PurchaseOrder|POPrint|QZ'` and the route-capability tests all green.

## Stage 44 — Per-tenant hostnames (`<client>.wholeops.in`) ✅ (opened + built 2026-08-14, **live-verified in production 2026-09-13**)

Opened because the user registered **wholeops.in** (Hostinger, 2026-08-14) and asked for a URL scheme of `clientbrandname.wholeops.in` with the module after the hostname. Checking that against the code first found **the scheme did not exist on either axis**, and that is recorded here rather than quietly building something adjacent:

- `apiMiddleware` never consulted the `Host` header at all. Tenant came from `X-Tenant-ID`, then `?tenant_id=`, then `"default"`, and after login from the bearer token's own claim. One hostname served every tenant.
- The SPA routes on `location.hash` (`public/app.js`), so the module segment is `#oms`, not `/oms`.

**Scope decision (2026-08-14):** the host→tenant half is built now because it is what makes per-client addresses real. The `/module` path half is **not** built — see 44.6.

- [x] **44.1** `public.tenants.host_slug` — `db/migrations_stage44_tenant_host_slug.sql`, additive, applied and verified on the dev DB. Backfilled from `tenant_id` **only where `tenant_id` is already a legal DNS label**: `tenant_id` is free-text `VARCHAR(100)` with no character restrictions and this repo's own fixtures contain `_`, which is illegal in a hostname. Verified: `default` → `default`, `tenant_new` → `NULL` (correctly skipped — a blind backfill would have minted a slug that could never be served, and the failure would only have shown up as an unexplained certificate error much later). Unique index on `LOWER(host_slug)` because hostnames are case-insensitive; partial, so any number of tenants may keep a NULL slug.
- [x] **44.2** Host→tenant resolution attached at the **one** choke point every request already passes (`apiMiddleware`, per CLAUDE.md's shared-choke-point rule) — `internal/server/tenant_host.go`. `<slug>.<TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN>` now precedes `X-Tenant-ID`/`?tenant_id=` in the pre-auth chain, on the reasoning that a hostname had to survive DNS and a certificate to arrive whereas those two are simply whatever the caller typed. Single label only: a two-level name would need a wildcard certificate Let's Encrypt will not issue over HTTP-01, so accepting one would produce a tenant that resolves but can never be served. Reserved labels (`app`, `www`, `api`, `admin`, `sandbox`, `mail`, …) never resolve to a tenant, so provisioning a client called "www" cannot take over that hostname.
- [x] **44.3** **The token stays authoritative, and a mismatch is refused.** A session for tenant A presented on tenant B's hostname returns the same generic 401 as any dead session rather than being silently served as A. This is deliberately *not* "host wins" — letting the host override the claim would have been a real cross-tenant escalation. It is the narrower guarantee: the claim still scopes every query, and the hostname merely has to agree with it.
- [x] **44.4** CORS widened by *shape*, not by list — `originAllowed()`. `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` still wins first and is unchanged; the addition is that https origins at the base domain or one label under it are accepted, so provisioning a tenant no longer needs an app restart with a longer allowlist. Scoped tight: https only, no port, no path, same single-label rule. The suffix test is anchored on `"." + base` so a domain someone else registers that merely ends in the same letters (`acme.evilwholeops.in`) is not mistaken for ours — locked by a test.
- [x] **44.5** **On-demand TLS, gated by the app** — `deploy/Caddyfile` + `GET /internal/tls-ask`. Per-tenant hostnames are not listed in the config (the tenant set changes without a config change), so each certificate is obtained on its first TLS handshake. The `ask` gate is what makes that safe: Caddy asks the app before requesting a certificate and proceeds only on a 2xx, so a hostname that is not a live tenant never reaches Let's Encrypt. Without it anyone who pointed a DNS record at this box could drive unbounded issuance. **Deliberately achieved without `xcaddy`** — a wildcard certificate would need a DNS-01 provider plugin and therefore a custom Caddy binary to keep patched, which is exactly the trade 26.1.3a already rejected. Found and fixed during validation: current Caddy **removed** `on_demand_tls`'s `interval`/`burst` throttle, so `ask` is now the only gate — which is why the handler fails closed on every path (unset `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN`, unknown slug, reserved label, DB-less startup all answer non-2xx). Proxy behaviour moved into an `(erp_app)` snippet imported by both site blocks so the platform host and tenant hosts cannot drift. `/internal/*` is refused on the public listener; Caddy reaches the gate over 127.0.0.1, which never passes through that block.
- [x] **44.10** **Unknown tenant hostnames are refused (`tenantHostGate`, 2026-08-15).** A single-label, non-reserved name under `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` that matches no tenant now gets a 404 instead of falling through to `X-Tenant-ID` and then `"default"` — which is what `nosuchclient.wholeops.in` did until now: it served the default tenant's login page. **Opened by 26.1.3b, not by 44.5.** While per-tenant certificates come from the ask gate, an unknown hostname never completes a handshake, so the fall-through was unreachable; behind Cloudflare the origin serves one wildcard certificate for every name under the base domain and the gate stops filtering anything, so the app has to make the judgement itself. Not a security boundary — the token claim scoped every query before this and still does — it is the product guarantee that a per-client address means a client. Attached to the whole-mux wrapper chain in `routes.go` rather than to `apiMiddleware`, because the SPA shell and static files are what a browser asks for first and they never pass through that middleware; inside `securityHeaders` so the 404 still carries them. Inert without `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN`, locked by a test that asserts localhost/loopback/apex/platform-host/foreign-domain all still pass through.
  - **Found while writing it:** routing this through `writeAPIErrorGeneric` was wrong. A 404 maps to `GLOBAL-0004`, which carries `LogRequired`, so every probe at a random subdomain would have written a Medium row to the system-error log — a log-flood vector, since sweeping subdomains is exactly what scanners do. It now writes through `writeResponse` directly: the same envelope every other error uses (that function is the single place bodies are written), without the catalog's logging hooks.
- [x] **44.6** `clientname.wholeops.in/oms` — the **path**-style module segment. **Resolved 2026-08-31 (asked directly, since the item itself was marked `[needs design decision]`): keep hash routing (`#oms`), do not convert.** The hostname half (44.2) already delivers real per-client addresses; the path form was judged not worth the router rework, every-internal-link update, and `routes.go` static-file-fallback change it would cost. No code change — this closes the decision, not a build.
- [x] **44.8** **Two latent bugs in `deploy/enable_tls.sh`, both found by running it for the first time.** The script was written in Stage 26.1.1 (2026-08-06) and had never been executed, because there was no domain to execute it against — so both would have hit whoever ran it first, not just this session.
  - **It could not be parsed at all.** `${PUBLIC_IP:-this box's public IP}` — the apostrophe in `box's` opens a quote inside a parameter expansion that bash never closes, and the whole script died at parse time with `unexpected EOF` pointing at the *last* line rather than the offending one. Rephrased to drop the apostrophe, with a comment saying why.
  - **It chowned the log directory before validating, which is exactly backwards.** `caddy validate` does not merely parse — it provisions every module, including **opening the access log**. The script runs as root, so validation created `/var/log/caddy/erp-access.log` owned `root:root 0600`, and the subsequent `systemctl reload` (running as the `caddy` user) failed with `permission denied` on a box where the directory, the config and DNS were all correct. Chowning the directory first cannot help: the offending file does not exist yet at that point. Now `chown -R` **after** validation. The script's own auto-rollback worked correctly through both failures, which is the reason this cost nothing.
- [x] **44.9** **The go-live blocker was a DigitalOcean cloud firewall, and it was diagnosed rather than guessed.** First `enable_tls.sh` run installed the config and reloaded Caddy cleanly, then ACME failed at the network layer: `Timeout during connect (likely firewall problem)` for **both** `tls-alpn-01` (443) and `http-01` (80). The obvious readings were all wrong — `ss -tlnp` showed Caddy listening on `*:80`/`*:443` and `ufw` allowed both. What settled it was a `tcpdump` on the droplet while connecting from the dev machine: **zero inbound packets arrived**, and since tcpdump sits below netfilter for inbound traffic, a local iptables DROP would still have shown the SYNs. So they were discarded upstream. The DO console confirmed it exactly: firewall `Test` had a single inbound rule (SSH/22) and "all other incoming traffic is blocked" — it only ever needed SSH while the ERP was tunnel-only. User added inbound HTTP/80 and HTTPS/443. Caddy had been reverted to `Caddyfile.holding` in the meantime so it would stop retrying into Let's Encrypt's failed-validation quota (5/hour/hostname; 2 consumed, window since reset).
- [x] **44.7** Live verification of **per-tenant** hostnames — **CLOSED 2026-09-13, and it turned out to already be fully deployed.** Before touching anything, checked what was actually running on the box rather than trusting this item's own "not deployed" text (which was stale): the deployed binary (`0e79425`, 2026-09-04) already has `82918a6`/`0c7084a` (Stage 44's code + migration) as an ancestor, `erp-server -migrate-status` reported no pending migrations, `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN=wholeops.in` was already set in `/etc/erp/erp.env`, `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile` was already the full Stage 44 config (`on_demand_tls` + the `ask` gate + the per-tenant `https://` catch-all block), and **both real tenants already carry a `host_slug`** (`default`→`default`, `minn`→`minn`) — some earlier session (likely the 2026-09-04 "push all and deploy" pass, per its own ledger entry, or a manual follow-up never written down) had already finished every prerequisite this item listed. Nothing here needed a deploy after all.
  - **Live-verified end to end from the dev machine, not just inferred from config**: `minn.wholeops.in` resolves via DNS to the box; `https://minn.wholeops.in/api/v1/health` completes a real TLS handshake (a genuine, separately-issued on-demand certificate, not the platform host's - curl validates the chain with no `-k`) and returns `200`; Caddy's own access log confirms it proxied `Host: minn.wholeops.in` through with a `200`, next to `app.wholeops.in`'s `200` moments later. **The `ask` gate refusing an unrecognised hostname is also confirmed working, not just coded**: a made-up `nosuchclient-qa-check.wholeops.in` resolves via the wildcard DNS record (so the refusal isn't a DNS gap) but the TLS handshake itself fails fatally (`SEC_E_ILLEGAL_MESSAGE`) - Caddy never got a 2xx from `/internal/tls-ask` for that name, so it never requested a certificate and never completed a handshake, which is a stronger guarantee than the app-level 404 (44.10) that only fires after a successful handshake. Did **not** attempt to log in as the real `minn` tenant (no credentials for a real customer, and none should be guessed at) - host-based tenant *routing* is proven; the account-level isolation guarantee (44.3) was already unit-tested in 44.2/44.3's own tests and is unchanged.
  - **`docs/ai_handover.md`'s "TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN unset on prod" / "Caddyfile not installed" claims (26.1.3b/44.7) are now corrected** - see this session's §6 entry.

**Backward compatibility is the load-bearing property**: the entire feature is inert unless `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` is set. Unset, `tenantForHost` returns "no match", `originAllowed` falls back to the old exact-match allowlist, and the ask gate refuses everything — so the localhost and SSH-tunnel paths behave exactly as before. Locked by its own tests rather than assumed.

## Stage 45 — User-reported batch: UI/UX design-bar sweep, round 1 ✅ (opened + built 2026-08-19; **live-verified 2026-08-31**)

Eight gaps the user reported directly against the live `:8080` deployment while screenshotting through Financial Accounting, PIM, HRM and Setup, plus the "no one has ever seen / brainless-intuitive" design-bar ask that reframed them as instances of a repeatable rubric rather than one-off fixes. Frontend-only — `public/app.js`, `public/styles.css`, `public/index.html`. No Go changes, no migration, no new dependency.

- [x] **45.1** **Duplicate panels on Vendor Invoice / Payment Proposals / Bank Reconciliation.** Root cause was a re-entrancy gap in the shared router, not anything view-specific: `renderView()` (app.js) cleared `#view-root` and showed a placeholder synchronously, then `await`ed the view's own fetch before appending its header/panel — so a second call fired before the first one's fetch resolved (double-click, or any duplicate trigger) would clear the first call's placeholder but not stop the first call from still appending its content afterwards, landing both calls' output side by side, permanently. Fixed with a `viewRenderToken` counter: each call renders into a **detached scratch container**, and only swaps it into the live `#view-root` if its token is still current when its fetch resolves — a stale call's content is discarded outright instead of ever touching the visible DOM. The existing Chromium sticky-header-caching workaround (`requestAnimationFrame(() => root.offsetHeight)`) and the `translateDOM` sweep moved from `renderViewContent` into `renderView`'s token-gated `finally`, since both need to run against the real root post-swap, not the scratch container. Affects every one of the ~45 views dispatched through this router, not just the three that happened to get screenshotted.
- [x] **45.2** **Module tab bars (PIM/Finance/Reports/HR/Manufacturing) silently clipped instead of scrolling.** `.page-container` is `overflow-x: hidden`, and each module's tab-bar row (`tabBar.style.display = 'flex'`, no wrap, no scroll of its own) is a bare unwrapped flex row — so at a narrow enough width/zoom (reported: 90%), trailing tabs (e.g. PIM's "Supplier Submissions") weren't just hard to find, they were **unreachable through the UI**, with no scrollbar, no wrap, and no recovery path short of resizing the window or knowing the direct URL. Fixed by giving each of the 5 tab-bar rows its own `overflow-x: auto`, scoped to the row rather than widening `.page-container`'s own overflow behaviour (which risked introducing a page-wide horizontal scrollbar for unrelated wide content elsewhere).
  - **The first version of this fix was verified live and found to make it worse: the tab bar collapsed to `height: 0`, fully invisible.** `.page-container` is a `flex-direction: column` container; `overflow-x: auto` alone suppresses a flex item's automatic min-height (content-based sizing only applies when overflow is `visible`, and per the CSS Overflow spec the *used* value of the other axis becomes `auto` regardless of what's declared) — so whenever the page's other content was tall enough to overflow vertically, the tab bar had nothing stopping it shrinking to nothing. **Fixed with `flex-shrink: 0`**, which exempts the item from shrinking at all rather than trying to win the automatic-minimum-size calculation. `overflow-y: visible` was added alongside it on the theory that it would stop the axis-pairing rule from firing — **live measurement disproved that theory** (`getComputedStyle` still reports `overflow-y: auto` even with `visible` explicitly declared); it's harmless and documents intent but `flex-shrink: 0` is what's actually doing the work. Re-verified live after the correction: normal width unchanged, narrow width shows a real non-zero-height scrollable bar (measured 46px, vs 0px before) with "Supplier Submissions" reachable by scroll.
- [x] **45.3** **Setup-prerequisite banner is no longer dismissible.** The yellow "This screen needs some setup first" banner previously had an × that dismissed it for the rest of the browser session (`sessionStorage`) — a deliberate Stage 41 design choice at the time, but the user asked for it to go further: as long as the prerequisite is genuinely unmet, the banner should show on every visit, full stop. Removed the × button, the `setupBannerDismissed`/`dismissSetupBanner`/`SETUP_BANNER_DISMISS_KEY` plumbing, and the now-dead `.setup-banner-close` CSS. The per-field hint next to the actual blocked action (e.g. the Employee picker on HR > Attendance) was already never dismissible and is unchanged.
- [x] **45.4** **Setup flyout submenu, two visual fixes.** (a) Group headers (CRM/CORE/FINANCE/HR/…) had no background, just bold muted text sitting directly on the flyout's own background — gave `.submenu-group-label` a solid chip (`var(--bg-color)`, rounded) so groups read as distinct bands. (b) The sticky "Filter setup lists…" search box leaked scrolling list text through its bottom border at fractional zoom/DPR — the exact class of bug Stage 41.2 already diagnosed and fixed for the table `<th>` (`border-bottom` alone leaves a hairline gap; needs `box-shadow: inset` to seal it), just not applied here. Same fix, same file, reused rather than reinvented.
- [x] **45.5** **Help icon button (top-right "?") reused text-button padding.** `.btn`'s base `padding: 8px 14px` / `gap: 8px` is sized for an icon+label pair like "Sync" next to it; on a single icon with no label it read as a stretched rectangle. New `.btn-icon` modifier (`padding: 8px`, `gap: 0`) applied to `#help-btn` — square footprint, same border/hover treatment as every other `.btn-outline`.
- [x] **45.6** **Knowledge Center had no path in beyond the per-screen drawer or knowing the `/help` URL.** Not in the sidebar, not in `buildGlobalSearchIndex()` (which only reads sidebar entries + registered doctypes). Added a standalone sidebar item ("Knowledge Center", right after Reports) wired to `renderView('help')`; it's automatically searchable now too since the search index reads the sidebar at build time — no second registration needed.
- [x] **45.7** **Rebrand: "Custom ERP" → "Wholeops"** in the 3 places it was hardcoded (`<title>`, login-screen brand, sidebar header). **Deliberately not done**: a "Powered by Wholeops" caption (redundant until there's an actual client brand shown above it to be "powered by") and a client-logo upload / tenant-branding placeholder — both need a small new schema (no `CompanyProfile`/`TenantBranding`-equivalent exists in this codebase today) and are being scoped as their own Setup-menu feature rather than bolted on here.
- **Investigated, found already correct, no change made**: the originally-reported F&A/Chart-of-Accounts frozen-header bug (rows floating through the sticky header) does not reproduce against this codebase — `renderChartOfAccountsPanel` doesn't use `.table-wrapper` at all (bare `.table-panel > table`), so the `.table-wrapper { contain: paint }` rule floated as a hypothesis mid-session doesn't apply to it, and the Stage 41.2 `th`-level sticky fix plus the existing Chromium-caching workaround are both present and correct locally. **Live-verified 2026-08-31**: Chart of Accounts panel renders and its table is reachable through the real Finance tab UI on a throwaway server against the real dev DB (screenshot taken) — confirms this is a local-code non-issue; the live `:8080` deployment the original screenshots were taken against was simply behind these fixes.

**45 build summary**: 0 migrations, 0 new files, additive edits across `public/app.js`, `public/styles.css`, `public/index.html`. `node --check` clean on `app.js`, brace-balance clean on `styles.css`. **Live-verified 2026-08-31** (the original dispatch to a background pass never recorded a result, so this re-ran the verification directly rather than chasing it): Playwright against a scratch server (port 8177, real dev DB, admin token via `cmd/minttoken`) exercising the real UI — 16/16 checks passed. **45.1** duplicate-render fix: fired `renderView()` twice back-to-back (no await between calls) against Vendor Invoice/Payment Proposals/Bank Reconciliation — exactly the re-entrancy race the bug needed — and each settled to exactly one `.page-title`, confirming the stale call's content is discarded rather than appended alongside the fresh one. **45.2** tab-bar scroll: at a 760px viewport, PIM/Reports/HR/Manufacturing tab bars all measured `overflow-x: auto`, non-zero height (46-62px), and `scrollWidth > clientWidth` (genuinely scrollable, not clipped); Finance's own bar fit without needing to scroll at this width, which is consistent behaviour, not a gap. **45.4** submenu group chip: opened the Setup flyout, `.submenu-group-label` computed a real background colour (not transparent) — screenshotted, chips clearly visible (CRM/CORE/FINANCE/HR/INVENTORY bands). **45.5** help icon: `#help-btn` box measured 34x34px (square, not a stretched rectangle). **45.6** Knowledge Center: sidebar entry present and clicking it renders `#kb-nav` — confirmed reachable, not just wired in source. **45.7** rebrand: `document.title`, the pre-login screen, and the sidebar logo all read "Wholeops" with zero remaining "Custom ERP" text, checked both pre- and post-login. **45.3** (banner dismissibility) and the sticky-search-box border seal in 45.4 were not independently re-exercised live this pass (no easy repro state for the former without a fresh unconfigured tenant) — both remain grep-verified from the original build (the `dismissSetupBanner` plumbing and `.setup-banner-close` CSS are confirmed absent from source) rather than freshly live-checked. Scratch server and token torn down after verification; no production system touched; `git status` confirms zero scratch artifacts left in the tree.
