## Stage 35 — OMS parity (Unicommerce/Uniware level) ✅ (≈16-20 sessions; release train R1 built 2026-08-11, rest through 2026-08-25, archived 2026-09-27)

**Release train R1 (35.1 + 35.2 + 35.3) built 2026-08-11.** Full detail below; the three structural debts R1 existed to clear are now cleared.

- [x] **35.1** Order truth: retire the POSCart channel path — **was blocking everything else in this stage**
  - **The plan's premise was partly stale and was corrected before building.** `engines/channel_orders.go`'s `ImportChannelSalesOrder` already routed Shopify + Unicommerce onto `CreateSalesOrder` (commit `efed245`, 2026-07-27), and 35.1.4's `DispatchNotification` calls were already wired into both `evaluateOrderShipmentClosure` (which `HandoverManifest` calls) and `RecordDeliveryEvent`. What was genuinely open is recorded per sub-item below.
  - [x] **35.1.1** Legacy importers retired. `ImportChannelOrder` (`engines/sourcing.go`) and `ImportUnicommerceOrder` (`unicommerce.go`) are now thin adapters onto the SalesOrder path, keeping their documented `ORDER_ALREADY_IMPORTED` replay contract so no caller breaks. **Finding: neither ever created the POSCart its own comment claimed** — they wrote reservations plus a `channel_order_mapping` row and no document at all, so every legacy channel import left stock reserved against nothing. New `channelLinesFromLooseItems`/`numericFromAny` also fix a latent bug: the old `item["qty"].(int)` assertion silently imported qty **0** for any JSON-sourced payload (`json.Unmarshal` yields `float64`).
  - [x] **35.1.2** BigCommerce + Magento wired onto the same path. **The BigCommerce webhook verified its signature, wrote an audit row, and then dropped the order on the floor** — it never created anything. A BigCommerce hook body carries only `{scope, data:{type,id}}`, so new `ImportBigCommerceOrder` reads the order back over the v2 API (order + products + shipping_addresses) and imports it; credential-gated in the 26.2.x "code-complete, inert until configured" shape, and it answers 200 on failure so a bad SKU can't cost the store its webhook subscription. The **Magento poller only counted changed orders and logged the number** — it now imports them (Magento's order search returns whole order objects, so no second round trip). Also restored the `unicommerce.order.imported` outbox event, which the 26.12.1 rewire had silently dropped.
  - [x] **35.1.3** Legacy-row decision: **no backfill**, per the plan's read-only-view preference. New registered report `orphaned-channel-orders` lists pre-35.1 mapping rows whose target document does not exist. **Fixed a real defect this exposed**: `ImportChannelSalesOrder` honoured a mapping row without checking the order still existed, so a dangling pre-35.1 row returned the phantom id and made that channel order *permanently un-importable*; the mapping insert is now `DO UPDATE` so the table converges on truth.
  - [x] **35.1.4** Verified already done (see the premise note above). No code change needed.
  - [x] **35.1.5** Regression gate green: `go test ./... -p 1` fully passing, including a rewritten `TestEngines/ShopifySyncAndSourcingRouting` that now asserts a real `SalesOrder` + SKU-translated `SalesOrderLine` instead of the retired synthetic `ORD-<channel>-<id>` identifier. New `engines/channel_intake_stage35_test.go` pins all of the above.
- [x] **35.2** The OMS Console: cross-channel order list, order detail, action bar, live exception tiles, bulk actions, global order search
  - New `engines/oms_console.go` + `internal/server/handlers_oms_console.go` + 8 routes under `/api/v1/oms/*`. **Why a backend at all**: the old screen fetched `/api/v1/doc/SalesOrder`, `/FulfillmentTask`, `/LogisticsBooking` and `/SalesInvoice` *in full* and joined them in the browser — no filter, no pagination, no ordering, and faceting is impossible on a page holding one page of rows.
  - [x] **35.2.1** Faceted, paginated list (channel / status / hold reason / location / date range / SLA age) with per-owner saved views stored as an `OMSSavedView` doctype. Facet counts deliberately exclude their own dimension, so picking a channel doesn't collapse the channel facet to one row.
  - [x] **35.2.2** Order detail in **one** call — lines with per-line status and allocation, reservations, fulfillment tasks, shipments, invoices, returns, refunds, notification log and audit trail. One endpoint rather than nine so the screen can't render in halves.
  - [x] **35.2.3** Action bar wired to the existing engines: hold / release / cancel / reallocate / switch facility / priority / split / edit.
  - [x] **35.2.4** Four live tiles, each the row count of an already-registered report (`oms-exception-queue`, `sla-breach`, `allocation-pending`, `oms-reconciliation-variance`); a failing report degrades its own tile instead of the console.
  - [x] **35.2.5** Bulk hold/release/cancel over the selection, looping the single-order engines (the `BulkDecideApproval` precedent) so every per-order guard still applies; returns per-order outcomes so a partially-applicable selection reports exactly which orders refused and why.
  - [x] **35.2.6** Global search across order id, channel order id, AWB/tracking, customer phone (normalized through the 34.4 phone engine first), customer name and SKU — a UNION of indexed lookups, no search engine.
- [x] **35.3** Order mutation surface parity: item-level hold, order edit, switch facility, priority, split
  - New `engines/order_mutations.go` + `internal/server/handlers_order_mutations.go` + 7 routes.
  - [x] **35.3.1** Item-level hold/unhold, returning the held line's stock to the pool and re-reserving on release (falling back to `Pending`, not a false `Reserved`, if the stock is gone). Holding a line deliberately does **not** drag the order header to On Hold.
  - [x] **35.3.2** Order edit (customer, phone, shipping/billing address, payment status, custom fields) re-validated through the **same** `validateOrderChain` — never a bespoke resume path. Custom fields are namespaced `custom_*` so an edit can't overwrite an engine-owned key.
  - [x] **35.3.3** Switch facility for unpicked lines only; with no explicit target it re-runs `ResolveAllocationPlan` (that is the console's Reallocate). Reserves at the destination **before** releasing at the source, so a concurrent order can't take the stock in between.
  - [x] **35.3.4** Priority/Expedite, honoured by the console's list ordering **and** by the new `PickTasksInPriorityOrder` worklist (`GET /api/v1/oms/pick-queue`) — otherwise the flag would be a label on a screen.
  - [x] **35.3.5** Order split into independent fulfilment groups via a new `SalesOrderLine.fulfillment_group`. Deliberately **not** a second SalesOrder: one order id for the customer, one parent for the invoice chain, and returns keep resolving to the order actually placed. Refuses to split every line out.
  - [x] **35.3.6** Every mutation gated through `StatusTransitionRule`, reusing Stage 29.8's existing `lookupStatusTransitionRule` rather than adding a second reader (`isCancellationBlocked`'s inline copy was consolidated onto it).

**Two bugs found and fixed while building R1, both outside its nominal scope:**
- [x] **SLA breach detection never fired.** `documents.created_at` is a bare `TIMESTAMP` holding the DB server's *local* wall clock, but `lib/pq` hands it back tagged UTC — so `GetSLABreaches`' `time.Since(created_at)` (`engines/optimization.go`) reported every open task as **330 minutes in the future** on this Asia/Calcutta deployment, and `elapsed > threshold` was false for anything younger than 5.5h + the threshold. Elapsed time is now computed in SQL (`NOW() - created_at`), which is correct in any time zone. Live proof: the console's SLA tile reads **230** where it would previously have read 0. The `TestEngines/AdvancedOptimizationAndForecasting` fixture was inserting `time.Now().UTC()`, a frame nothing else in the app writes — it only ever agreed with the buggy reader, and is now written the way real rows are.
- [x] **`.stats-grid` and `.stat-value` do not exist in `styles.css`.** The old OMS screen used both, which is why its four tiles rendered as full-width stacked bars with an unstyled number. Corrected to the real `dashboard-stats-row` / `.stat-val`. Also added a `.oms-compact-panel` modifier because `.table-panel`'s `min-height:200px` reserved a tall empty box under the search field.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean, `go test ./... -p 1` fully green, every new endpoint exercised over HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8097, stopped and released), **and a real-browser pass via Playwright** — 4 tiles, 7 facet controls, working pager, detail modal with 7 actions and all 9 sections, **zero console errors**, no horizontal overflow.

**Known limitation, stated rather than hidden:** `inventory_reservation` has **no `order_id` or line reference** (`db/migration.sql` §301), so reservations cannot be attributed to the order that made them. The detail screen matches them by the (sku, location) pairs the order's lines allocated to, and `releaseLineReservation` releases the oldest match — correct in aggregate (the same quantity returns to the same pool, which is all `computeATS` consumes) but not provably "this line's" under concurrency. **Related: nothing anywhere ever expires or sweeps `inventory_reservation`** — `expires_at` is written and indexed but never read, so every reservation is effectively permanent. Attributing reservations per line and adding a sweeper is one schema change plus one worker; it is not in Stage 35's scope but should be picked up before 35.4.
- [x] **35.3.7** [carried out of R1] Per-line reservation attribution + an expiry sweeper — **built 2026-08-12.** Additive `db/migrations_stage35_3_7_reservation_attribution.sql` adds nullable `order_id`/`line_id` plus three partial indexes; `CreateReservation` takes a variadic `ReservationAttribution` so the four order paths attribute their rows while cart holds, the manual reservation API and the load-test harness keep writing honestly unattributed ones. `releaseLineReservation` now releases **this line's** row, falling back to the old (sku, location, quantity) heuristic only for pre-migration rows. The OMS console's reservation panel matches by `order_id` first and flags each row `attributed` so the screen no longer presents an approximation as fact.
  - **The sweeper deliberately does not expire an order's reservation on time alone**, and that is the whole safety argument. `inventory.reservation_ttl_seconds` is a *cart-hold* TTL; applying it to a confirmed order would release stock out from under a live order the moment it sat in a queue longer than the TTL — a far worse bug than the leak being fixed. So: an **unattributed** hold is released when `expires_at` passes; an **attributed** one is released only when its line is gone, deleted or no longer in a stock-holding status (`Reserved`/`Allocated`/`Picking`/`Picked`/`Packed`). One statement, one snapshot, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, batched at 500 per tick, `StartReservationSweeper(ctx, 5m)`. A side benefit: it also repairs reservations orphaned by any pre-35.3.7 path that cancelled a line without releasing.
  - **Verified**: new `engines/reservation_sweeper_test.go` asserts the negative case first (a live line's reservation must survive a sweep even 48h past its nominal expiry), plus the cancelled-line release, the expired-hold release, availability arithmetic, sweep idempotence, and that releasing by line id takes the line's own row rather than an older lookalike. The test asserts per-SKU rather than on the sweep's global counts — the sweep is schema-wide by design and this suite shares one database, so global counts are not any one test's to own.
  - **Proven on real accumulated data 2026-08-13**, once the UTF-8 conversion above let the migration apply to `custom_erp`: that database held **5,395 sweepable reservations** against a total of 5,419 — i.e. 99.6% of every reservation in it was holding stock for a line that no longer wanted it. Drained to 0, with `inventory_availability.reserved < 0` still empty afterwards. **This also caught a wrong assumption in the test itself**: the sweep is batched at 500 rows per call (deliberately — one long transaction against a backlog would hold locks the live order path needs), so a single call on a real database never reaches a freshly-inserted fixture. The test now drains in a bounded loop, which is what the 5-minute worker does over successive ticks; it fails rather than hangs if the sweep ever stops converging.
- [x] **35.4** Shipping package, invoice generation from pack, gate pass, credit note — **built 2026-08-15.** New `engines/shipping_package.go` + `pack_invoice.go` + `gate_pass.go` + `cancellation_credit_note.go`, `internal/server/handlers_shipping_package.go`, 12 routes, additive `db/migrations_stage35_4_shipping_package.sql` (applied).
  - **Why the entity had to exist first.** The chain ran FulfillmentTask → LogisticsBooking, which assumes one task ships as exactly one parcel — false the moment a packer needs two boxes — and left nothing to hang an invoice off. That is the same reason 26.12.3 gave for deferring invoice-from-pack ("nothing real to generate the invoice from"); R1 built the order chain and 35.4.1 built the pack object, so the reason has expired.
  - [x] **35.4.1** `ShippingPackage` (Draft → Invoiced → Shipped, Cancelled as the exit; only Draft is mutable). Created from a **Packed** task using `packed_qty`, not `qty` — a short-picked line ships short, and invoicing the ordered quantity for a parcel that does not contain it is the most expensive mistake available here. Creation is idempotent on the task (the pack screen's "done" is exactly the button an operator double-taps). Split refuses two things rather than clamping: moving *every* unit out (that is a rename, and leaves an empty package that still looks shippable) and moving more than a line holds; duplicate SKU rows in one split request are summed **before** validation, or 5+5 passes against a holding of 8. Both halves are written in one transaction, and the source's weight/dimensions are cleared because they now describe neither box.
  - [x] **35.4.2** Invoice from a completed pack — closes 26.12.3's deferral. Invoices **the package, not the order**, so a split order's two parcels each carry their own correct tax document. Prices come from the order's own `SalesOrderLine.unit_price`, never from today's item master — re-reading `sale_price` would silently re-price a shipment mid-flight. Produces a **Draft**; posting stays an explicit finance action, matching the split `CreateSalesInvoiceFromOrder` already keeps.
    - **A real defect fell out of this, and it is fixed in the new code only.** The shared `ComputeGSTForLinesMode` is not internally consistent to the paisa: CGST and SGST are each `round2(lineTax/2)` per line and then summed, so a plain two-line 18% order totalling 900 produced **CGST+SGST = 137.30 against its own TotalTax of 137.28**, and `TaxableAmount` accumulates unrounded (stored as `762.7118644067798`). New `reconcileInvoiceAmounts` makes the document add up in both directions — components sum to the tax total exactly (the second absorbs the half-paisa, the `ConvertPostingToFunctional` technique), and taxable is derived as total − non-taxable − tax so exempt/nil/zero-rated turnover stays **out** of the filed GSTR-3B 3.1(a) figure while still reconciling. Deliberately **not** applied to the shared engine: its contract is that every existing sale-side caller stays byte-identical, and POS checkout is one of them.
    - **Also load-bearing**: `CreateSalesInvoiceFromOrder`'s existence check was widened from its own `INV-<orderID>` to *any* invoice with that `sales_order_id`. Without it, the handover cascade calls it on the Shipped transition and **every package-invoiced order would be invoiced a second time, in full**.
  - [x] **35.4.3** Invoice → label → manifest ordering enforced (Uniware's hard rule: no label before invoice). A label is what authorises a parcel to move, so this refuses rather than warns. The manifest half is a `WHERE` clause, not a post-filter, so an unlabelled parcel is never grouped and then silently dropped — it simply waits for the next manifest; the "nothing to ship" and "everything is waiting on a label" cases are now distinguished in the message, because they send an operator to completely different places. **The rule binds only a booking that has a package**: `CreateLogisticsBooking` gained a variadic package id (all six call sites unchanged, the 37.1.2 `PostingOptions` precedent) and auto-links its task's package when none is given — otherwise the guard would only apply to callers who already knew about packages, which is exactly the set that does not need guarding. A pre-35.4 or manual booking labels exactly as it did.
  - [x] **35.4.4** `GatePass` (Draft → Issued → Completed, Discarded as the exit) — create/update/issue/complete/discard/search. Hangs off the **Manifest**, not a package: a pass is issued per vehicle and a vehicle carries a manifest's worth of parcels. Carrier/location/count are taken **from** the manifest rather than trusted from the caller — the gate desk is transcribing, and a pass whose count disagrees with the manifest it references is worse than no pass at all. A vehicle number is required at *issue*, not at creation: raising the pass is paperwork, issuing it is the moment something departs, and a departure record with no vehicle cannot be reconciled later. Every transition goes through Stage 29.8's rule map (the 35.3.6 consolidation).
  - [x] **35.4.5** Credit note on cancellation-after-invoice. `CreditNote`/`PostCreditNote` already existed (Stage 20c) and are reused, not re-implemented; this works out which invoices a cancellation affects. **A Draft invoice is voided, not credited** — nothing was posted, recognised or declared, so crediting it would reverse a transaction that never happened. Wired into `CancelOrder` (variadic `actor`, four call sites unchanged) after the order is durably Cancelled, so a failure leaves the reversal visibly outstanding rather than an order credited while still live. Cancelling also voids any non-shipped package on the order — a Draft package left behind still looks bookable, and a booking against it would ship goods for an order nobody will pay for.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean and `go test ./... -p 1` fully green, including 12 new tests in `engines/shipping_package_test.go` (packed-vs-ordered quantity, idempotent creation, all four split refusals plus conservation, exact invoice reconciliation in both directions, the interstate override, both ordering refusals **and** the legacy-booking exemption, the double-invoice guard, the credit-note draft/posted/idempotent cases, and the gate-pass lifecycle). Then the whole chain over HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8093, stopped and released, fixtures removed): package → split (3+1=4, lineage recorded) → label refused → manifest refused → invoice (`635.59 + 114.41 = 750.00`, CGST `57.21` + SGST `57.20` = `114.41`) → label → manifest → gate pass (issue refused with no vehicle) → handover → package `Shipped` → credit note, idempotent on the second call.
  - **Test-suite note worth keeping**: `db.InitDB` reassigns the global pool without closing the previous one, so every call leaks up to `dbMaxIdleConns` for `ConnMaxIdleTime`. The package convention is one `InitDB` per test, which was already close enough to Postgres's `max_connections` that adding this file tipped `go test ./... -p 1` into `sorry, too many clients already` — a connection ceiling, not a logic failure. This file reuses a live pool (`spInitDB`); the other files' convention was left alone.
  - **Known limitation, stated rather than hidden**: `SalesOrder` has no state field (only free-text `shipping_address`), so inter-state cannot be reliably derived. Parsing the address would be wrong often enough to be worse than useless *on a tax document*, so the resolution order is explicit override → the order's `shipping_state`/`custom_shipping_state` if present → **default intra-state**, and the basis used is recorded on the invoice as `gst_basis` rather than left for an auditor to guess. A real `shipping_state` on SalesOrder is the fix, and belongs with 35.6's channel adapters, which are where the value would actually come from.
- [x] **35.5** Real courier integration — **code-complete 2026-08-22; live provider calls credential-gated, cf. 26.2.x**
  - [x] **35.5.1** `CourierAdapter` interface (`engines/courier.go`) covering AWB allocation, pickup, cancellation, rates and tracking-event parsing. First real adapters: Delhivery and Shiprocket (`courier_adapters.go`), following D2's documented default. Credentials reuse the AES-256-GCM `channel_credentials` store under `courier:<provider>` and are write-only through a Super-Admin route; no second plaintext credential table.
  - [x] **35.5.2** Provider-confirmed AWB allocation, pickup scheduling and cancellation. A failed provider call leaves the local booking retryable; `provider_awb` replaces the internal placeholder only after a successful response. Allocation is idempotent once a provider AWB exists. Shiprocket correctly requires its remote shipment id; Delhivery creates the shipment in the allocation call.
  - [x] **35.5.3** Real downloadable 4×6 PDF label (`GenerateShippingLabelPDF`) with a standards-checksummed Code 128-B rendered as vector bars — crisp at 203/300 DPI, no image or new dependency. It calls the existing `GenerateShippingLabel` choke point first, preserving 35.4.3's invoice-before-label guard and label-generated stamp. The existing QZ/ZPL Code128 path remains the silent thermal-print route.
  - [x] **35.5.4** Signed tracking webhook ingestion. HMAC-SHA256 is checked against the provider's encrypted `webhook_secret`; provider payloads normalize into the existing `RecordDeliveryEvent`/`RecordRTO` state machine. `CourierTrackingEvent` records provider event ids behind a unique partial index, making retries idempotent.
  - [x] **35.5.5** Rate shopping across configured adapters. A failed/unconfigured provider does not hide another provider's usable quote; serviceable results sort by landed charge then ETA and the HTTP response identifies the cheapest result.
  - [x] **35.5.6** NDR workflow (`NDRCase`): failed attempts open or refresh one active case per booking; operators can schedule a re-attempt, close the case, or escalate to the existing RTO flow. A failed delivery is no longer prematurely treated as RTO.
  - **Surface/schema**: additive migration `migrations_stage35_5_courier_integration.sql` (applied + ledger-recorded on dev only), three new engine files, one engine regression file, one handler + middleware regression file, nine OMS-gated routes. The two fixed provider webhook paths are bearer-token-exempt because couriers cannot hold a human session; they remain fail-closed behind mandatory per-tenant HMAC and a 30/min webhook budget. Zero dependencies and no secrets in documents. Live Delhivery/Shiprocket calls remain pending real account credentials.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` clean on the combined tree; focused migration-backed Stage 35.5 tests green (provider-AWB replacement + retry idempotency, cheapest-rate ordering, NDR re-attempt, tracking normalization, complete PDF/xref and vector Code128 output); `internal/server`, `internal/kb` and `db` suites green. The attempted whole-suite run hit the already-documented Postgres pool ceiling (`too many clients`) in unrelated engine tests; the Stage 35.5 suite was rerun clean after the pool settled. No real-provider live call claimed without credentials. Brain coverage 100% / 0 unclaimed.
- [x] **35.6** Channel breadth — **code-complete 2026-08-22; live calls credential/partner-contract gated per channel**
  - [x] **35.6.1** Full `OmnichannelConnector` SDK (`engines/connector_sdk.go`): credential validation, order pull, ATS push, catalogue publish, status push, error mapping and an explicit capability descriptor. Registration stays compatible with the old `ChannelConnector` publisher. Shopify/BigCommerce/Magento/Adobe Commerce are represented honestly as catalogue-capable legacy adapters; their established webhook/poller intake stays active and the scheduler does not invent unsupported operations.
  - [x] **35.6.2** Marketplace breadth: fixed-contract Amazon SP-API and Flipkart adapters plus negotiated-contract adapters for Myntra, Meesho, Ajio and Nykaa. Amazon order intake targets Orders `2026-01-01` (nested buyer/recipient/items and `paginationToken`); listings use `2021-08-01`; shipment confirmation validates order-item ids/quantities. Flipkart follows Seller API v3 shipment search, 10-SKU inventory batches, listing and dispatch endpoints. D1's default remains honest: Amazon/Flipkart are code-complete with official public contracts; the other four require the seller's private `base_url` and four negotiated paths rather than guessed endpoints.
  - [x] **35.6.3** Blinkit, Zepto and Swiggy Instamart use the same fail-closed negotiated-contract adapter. Their descriptors require location/store semantics and force no-split allocation; a missing/insufficient configured store puts the order on the existing allocation hold instead of sourcing from another node.
  - [x] **35.6.4** WooCommerce REST v3 adapter: Basic-auth order pull, stock update, order-status update and draft catalogue creation. Shopify/BigCommerce/Magento remain the existing adapters noted above.
  - [x] **35.6.5** Scheduled order/ATS worker, per-channel interval and cursor, with capability gating. Mapping rows carry external product/location ids. Before push, the maximum active channel buffer is written into the existing `inventory_availability.channel_buffer`; every quantity then goes through the existing `computeATS` formula, so sync-back never becomes a second stock truth.
  - [x] **35.6.6** Persistent per-channel mapping depth (`external_product_id`, optional `location_code`) plus `ChannelSKUException`. Missing mappings open/dedupe/increment one exception; saving a mapping resolves matching open exceptions. The OMS screen includes the live queue and mapping action.
  - [x] **35.6.7** OMS connector dashboard: active-channel last sync, lag, 24-hour runs/failures, open exceptions and capability-aware manual Pull Orders / Push ATS actions. `ChannelSyncRun` records every scheduled/manual operation.
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `migrations_stage35_6_channel_breadth.sql`, applied and ledger-recorded on dev only; it updates `tenant_default` and propagates metadata, mapping columns, permissions and indexes to existing `tenant_*` schemas. New SDK/adapter/sync engine files, focused contract + DB regression suite, one handler file, nine OMS-gated routes and one additive OMS console panel. No dependency added and no secret returned by the credential API.
  - **Verified**: official Amazon/Flipkart contracts rechecked on 2026-08-22; HTTP tests pin Amazon `2026-01-01`, Flipkart v3 and WooCommerce request/response shapes; migration-backed exception dedupe/resolution and mapping tests green. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js`, `db` and focused `internal/server` suites clean on the combined shared tree. No live seller call claimed without credentials/private partner contracts.
- [x] **35.7** Bundles, kits and virtual SKUs — **built and dev-applied 2026-08-22**
  - [x] **35.7.1** `ProductBundle` definition with an explicit `Virtual` vs `Stocked` fulfillment mode and three pricing modes: `Parent Price` (channel/manual order price allocated over components), `Fixed Price` (bundle master price), and `Component Price` (configured component unit prices). Components carry SKU, positive whole quantity and optional price weight/unit price. Generic-document validation requires active Item masters, rejects repeated/self/nested components and prevents two active definitions for one bundle SKU. This is deliberately a commercial OMS object beside manufacturing `BOM`: a BOM produces a controlled finished good through Production/VAS; a ProductBundle decides how a sellable SKU is priced, made available and picked.
  - [x] **35.7.2** Virtual bundle ATS is never stored. `ComputeSellableSKUATS` computes each component bucket through the existing `computeATS`, divides by component quantity and takes the scarcest ratio. Per-location ATS is exact; network ATS conservatively sums complete per-location bundle capacity, so stock stranded across incompatible facilities cannot form a fictitious bundle. `GetAvailableToSell` and Stage 35.6 channel inventory sync both use this seam. Stocked kits read their own assembled inventory instead of double-counting components.
  - [x] **35.7.3** `CreateSalesOrder` expands every active Virtual bundle before validation, sourcing and reservation. Warehouse lines therefore contain real component SKUs/quantities and preserve `bundle_sku`, ordered bundle quantity, component-per-bundle quantity and pricing mode. Parent/fixed prices are allocated across child lines with the final line taking the remainder; component-price mode ignores an untrusted inbound parent price. The SalesOrder retains a commercial `bundle_lines` summary and exact total. Stocked kits deliberately remain one pickable line.
  - [x] **35.7.4** Stocked-kit Assemble/Disassemble operations create immutable `BundleAssembly` evidence and apply component + kit availability changes and one `StockLedgerEntry` per movement in a **single database transaction**. Consumption checks real ATS (including reservations/holds/buffers), not merely `available`; any shortage rolls back the operation record, every stock mutation and every ledger row. Optional request keys are unique/idempotent. Virtual bundles refuse physical assembly.
  - **Surface/UI/schema**: additive multi-tenant migration `migrations_stage35_7_bundles_kits.sql` (applied + ledger-recorded on dev only), new `engines/bundles.go` and regression suite, ProductBundle validation hook, two gated API routes (OMS availability; WMS assemble/disassemble), and an OMS Bundles and Kits panel with per-location ATS, definition management and stocked-kit operations. No new dependency.
  - **Verified**: real-Postgres test covers all three pricing modes, nested-bundle refusal, derived location/network ATS, virtual order explosion/reservation and exact revenue, stocked-kit non-explosion, atomic assembly, request replay, disassembly, shortage rollback and stock ledger evidence. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js`, and `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green on the combined shared tree. Both current tenant schemas have the two doctypes; Stage 42.6 migration remains unapplied.
- [x] **35.8** Settlement/payment reconciliation — the "UniReco" gap — **built and dev-applied 2026-08-25**
  - [x] **35.8.1** Settlement file import. New `MarketplaceSettlementLine` doctype (channel, channel_order_id, settlement_batch_id, settlement_date, gross_amount, commission, shipping_fee, other_fee, tds, tcs, net_payout) imported through the **existing generic** `POST /api/v1/import/{doctype}` + `BulkImportCSV` path — no new upload endpoint, matching the "reuse `BulkImportCSV` for any new line-item CSV upload" convention.
  - [x] **35.8.2** Matching engine (`ReconcileMarketplaceSettlements`, `engines/settlement_reconciliation.go`). Each Unmatched line: validates its own row math (gross − every reported deduction must equal net payout, else `Invalid`), resolves the channel order to an internal order via the existing `channel_order_mapping` table (Stage 35.1), computes "expected" as the sum of the order's own posted `SalesInvoice.total_amount` (falling back to `SalesOrderLine.unit_price × qty` if no invoice exists yet), and either auto-posts a within-tolerance (`oms.settlement_variance_tolerance`, default ₹2) match or holds a `Variance`.
  - [x] **35.8.3** GL split, one shared poster (`postSettlementLineGL`) for both the auto-match and write-off paths: debit Cash/Bank (1100) for net payout, Marketplace Commission Expense (5200), new Marketplace Shipping & Other Fees (5210), new TDS/TCS Receivable (1310/1320); credit Accounts Receivable (1300) for the invoiced value; any gap between invoiced and reported-gross lands on new Settlement Variance Written Off (5270), on whichever side keeps the entry balanced. Proven-balanced by test (debit==credit in both the clean-match and write-off cases).
  - [x] **35.8.4** Dispute/write-off workflow: `RaiseSettlementDispute`/`ResolveSettlementDispute`/`WriteOffSettlementVariance`, gated by a mandatory `ReasonCode` of a new `Settlement` category (append-only, same precedent as the WMS-exception/cycle-count-variance category appends). Resolving a dispute takes the marketplace's corrected gross amount and re-runs the same tolerance check; within tolerance it posts and closes as `Matched`.
  - [x] **35.8.5** Reporting + UI: two registered reports (`oms-unsettled-settlements` — Shipped/Delivered channel orders with no cleared settlement line, aged against `oms.settlement_aging_days`; `oms-settlement-variance` — the open Variance/Disputed queue), 4 new OMS-gated routes (`/api/v1/oms/settlements/reconcile|{id}/dispute|{id}/resolve|{id}/write-off`), and a **Settlement Reconciliation** panel on the existing Marketplace screen (Import/View, Run Auto-Reconcile, variance queue table with Dispute/Resolve/Write Off actions).
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage35_8_settlement_reconciliation.sql` (applied to dev via a concurrent-session-safe single-file apply, not the embedded `-migrate` batch — see the ai_handover note), 4 new GL accounts, 2 new settings, 1 new engine file, 1 new handler file, 4 routes, 1 app.js panel.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js` clean; new `engines/stage35_8_9_test.go` (`TestSettlementReconcile`) covers row-math rejection, a clean auto-match with a balanced GL split, a held Variance that does **not** post, and a write-off that closes it with a balanced GL split against the full invoiced value; full `go test ./... -p 1` green on the combined shared tree.
  - **Known limitation, stated rather than hidden**: no Item-master cost/price field exists anywhere in this codebase to independently verify a marketplace's reported gross beyond comparing it to our own invoice — same documented gap `resolveSalesOrderLinePrices` already carries for RTO cost. A settlement line only ever matches at order granularity (not per-shipment/per-package), a documented simplification for this effort tier.
- [x] **35.9** Returns depth: reverse pickup, exchange/alternate item, return routing — **built and dev-applied 2026-08-25**
  - [x] **35.9.1** Reverse pickup (`ScheduleReturnReversePickup`, `engines/returns.go`). An Approved Customer Return books a real `LogisticsBooking` through the **unmodified** Stage 26.12.4 `CreateLogisticsBooking` serviceability/carrier-selection logic, tags it `shipment_direction=Reverse` + `return_request_id`, then drives it through the **unmodified** Stage 35.5 `AllocateCourierAWB`/`ScheduleCourierPickup` pair — a provider's real pickup API is called exactly the way a forward shipment already calls it. `RecordDeliveryEvent` (`engines/marketplace.go`, the same choke point the 35.5.4 tracking webhook already flows through) gained a `Reverse`-direction branch: a booking reported `Delivered` auto-calls `ReceiveReturnRequest` instead of firing the forward "Order Delivered" notification. New intermediate `ReturnRequest` status `Pickup Scheduled`, and `ReceiveReturnRequest` now accepts a manual receive from it too (the operator may physically have the parcel before the webhook catches up).
  - [x] **35.9.2** Exchange / alternate item. `ApplyReturnQC` gained an optional trailing `exchangeFor` (originalSKU → exchangeSKU) map — variadic, so every existing call site is unchanged. An exchanged line's returned stock still lands in its disposition's bucket exactly as before, but the exchange SKU's stock is deducted from the **same** `return_location`, in the **same transaction** (reusing `engines/bundles.go`'s own ATS-check-then-`StockLedgerEntry` shape), so a shortage on the exchange side rolls back the whole QC call — nothing is received without its replacement being available, and nothing is issued without the original actually coming back. That line is excluded from the refund total. **Deliberately same-value-only**: no Item-master cost/price field exists to price a different-value swap correctly (same gap as 35.8's own note), so a different-value exchange is refused with a message pointing at return + new sale instead; the exchange leg's own COGS entry reuses the returned line's own cost basis (correct for the common same-family size/colour swap this targets).
  - [x] **35.9.3** Return routing. `CreateReturnRequest`'s `return_location` is now optional: when blank **and** the request is an RTO (whose `LogisticsBooking` carries a real `destination_pincode`), it auto-resolves via `engines/sourcing.go`'s own Nearest-Pincode strategy — the same distance-proxy logic `ResolveAllocationPlan` already uses to source an order, reused here in reverse to decide where a return should land. An explicit `return_location` always wins unchanged. A Customer Return's original order is a POSCart/SalesInvoice (`resolveOriginalSale`), neither of which carries a shipping address, so that path has no pincode to route from and keeps requiring an explicit location, same as before this stage.
  - **Surface**: 1 new route (`POST /api/v1/returns/{id}/reverse-pickup`), `handleApplyReturnQC` gained an optional `exchange_for` body field, no schema migration needed (both features extend the existing JSON-only `ReturnRequest`/`LogisticsBooking` doctypes, which were never registered with `doctype_fields`).
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; new `engines/stage35_8_9_test.go` covers a same-value exchange (both stock movements, refund correctly excluded, `exchange_sku` recorded on the line) and its own insufficient-ATS rollback (asserts the *original* item's receipt was rolled back too, not just the exchange leg), plus RTO auto-routing (nearest of two candidate locations by pincode, `return_location_auto_routed` flag recorded); the pre-existing `TestEngines/ReturnsRTOQCRefund` and `TestEngines/AllocationSourcing` regression subtests both still pass unchanged; full `go test ./... -p 1` green on the combined shared tree.
  - **Known limitation, stated rather than hidden, found while building this**: the `ReturnRequest`/`RefundRequest` workflow itself (Stage 26.12.5: request → approve → receive → QC → refund) has **never had a management UI** — the OMS console only ever showed a return read-only inside an order's detail view (35.2.2's "Returns" section). All of 35.9's three sub-items are built and tested at the API layer; a full Returns management screen (the natural home for a Schedule Reverse Pickup button and an exchange-SKU picker in the QC step) is a separate, larger undertaking not attempted here — out of this item's own "3 sub-items" scope.

---

## Stage 37 — ERP core depth (Business Central level, SAP-informed) ✅ (≈16-22 sessions; built 2026-09-01, archived 2026-09-27)
- [x] **37.1** Multi-currency: rate table, dual-currency posting, realised/unrealised FX, revaluation (5 sub-items) — **COMPLETE 2026-08-16** (37.1.1/37.1.2 built 2026-08-11/12; 37.1.3-37.1.5 built 2026-08-16, which also fixed a live correctness bug 37.1.2 had shipped — see 37.1.3)
  - [x] **37.1.1** Currency master + effective-dated exchange-rate table — additive `Currency` and `ExchangeRate` Finance Masters with manual/imported source provenance, Spot/Average/Closing rate types, date windows, uniqueness guards, an INR seed and a shared direct/inverse resolver. Existing generic forms, CSV import, permissions and audit paths are reused.
  - [x] **37.1.2** Store transaction currency and functional currency on financial documents — **built 2026-08-12.**
    - **The invariant everything else rests on**: `gl_postings.debit`/`credit` keep meaning exactly what they mean today — an amount in the tenant's functional currency. Every report, trial balance, P&L, GST return and reconciliation in this codebase sums those two columns and none of them is being taught about currency, so the transaction-currency amounts arrive in **new** columns beside them (`currency`, `exchange_rate`, `transaction_debit`, `transaction_credit`, all NULL for a functional-currency posting). A single-currency tenant's rows stay byte-identical to what they are today, and so does every pre-37.1.2 row.
    - **Documents**: new `engines/currency_documents.go` stamps `currency`, `functional_currency`, `exchange_rate` and `base_<amount>` twins, attached at `ValidateDocument` — the one choke point the generic API, CSV import and every engine already pass through — so a doctype added later participates by being listed, not by someone remembering to call a function. An operator-entered rate wins over the resolved one (contract rates exist); otherwise `ResolveExchangeRate` is consulted on the **document's own date**, not today, so a back-dated invoice does not quietly pick up this morning's rate. New `finance.functional_currency` setting, default INR, with a hard INR fallback if it is ever misconfigured.
    - **A real correctness fix falls out of this**: approval slabs are written in the tenant's own currency, and `extractAmount` would have compared a USD 100 invoice against an INR slab. The three approval call sites now use `DocumentBaseAmount`, which returns the base twin and falls back to the raw amount for pre-stamp documents (correct — those were all functional-currency by definition).
    - **Postings**: `PostingOptions` gains `Currency`/`ExchangeRate`/`TransactionDebits`/`TransactionCredits` (variadic, so all 28 existing call sites needed zero changes). `ConvertPostingToFunctional` scales a foreign entry and **absorbs the rounding difference into the largest line on the short side** — without that, per-line rounding can leave the two sides a rupee apart and `PostDoubleEntry` correctly refuses a legitimate voucher as unbalanced. `postApprovedJournalVoucher` is the first real caller.
    - **Verified**: `engines/currency_documents_test.go` covers the stamp, normalisation (`usd`→`USD`), rejection of non-ISO codes, the base-amount approval comparison, the legacy fallback, balance preservation under adverse rounding, rate-1 pass-through, and a real dual-currency posting read back from `gl_postings`. Migration applied and the full suite green.
  - [x] **37.1.3** Realised FX gain/loss on settlement — **built 2026-08-16.** New `engines/currency_fx.go`; additive `db/migrations_stage37_1_fx_revaluation.sql` seeds four accounts (4200 Realised FX Gain, 4210 Unrealised FX Gain, 5600 Realised FX Loss, 5610 Unrealised FX Loss) and backfills every existing tenant schema. No new table, no new column: the revaluation state lives as engine-written JSON keys on the document.
    - **⚠️ THIS FOUND AND FIXED A LIVE CORRECTNESS BUG THAT 37.1.2 SHIPPED.** `PostSalesInvoice` read `total_amount` — which for a USD invoice is 1000, in *dollars* — and posted it straight to Accounts Receivable. **A USD 1,000 invoice at 83.00 booked a 1,000-rupee receivable instead of an 83,000 one**, so the receivable, the ageing report, the credit exposure and the trial balance were all understated by 98.8% for any tenant that used the feature 37.1.2 shipped. The posting sites now read the `base_*` twin. Same class of fix on `PayVendorInvoice`.
    - **The model, recorded once so it is not re-litigated.** A receivable is booked at the invoice-date rate and carried at that rupee value; the cash later received is worth a different number of rupees, and that difference is *realised* (4200/5600). While the invoice sits open across a period end its carrying value is stale, so revaluing it recognises the movement so far as *unrealised* (4210/5610), tracked cumulatively on the document. **At settlement the realised figure is measured against the REVALUED carrying amount, not the original one** — total P&L across every period is then exactly (cash received − amount booked), however it splits. Measuring against the original would double-count every rupee already recognised at a period end. There is deliberately no reversing entry on day one of the next period: this codebase has no automatic reversal machinery, and a cumulative carrying amount reaches the identical answer with one fewer moving part.
    - **The sign convention differs by side and that is not a bug**: when the foreign currency strengthens a receivable is worth MORE (gain) and a payable costs MORE to settle (loss). `resolveSettlementFX` is *told* which side it is on rather than inferring it, and `TestRealisedFXSignConventions` asserts both directions on the same numbers.
    - `SettlementOptions` (settlement date + the rate the bank actually gave) is **variadic**, the `PostingOptions`/`CreateLogisticsBooking` precedent, so all six existing callers of the four settlement engines needed zero changes. An operator-supplied rate beats the table — a bank confirmation states the rate it converted at, and booking a table rate instead just moves the error into the cash account where it is harder to find.
    - **`FXSettlement.PostingDate()` returns empty unless the caller named a date**, which keeps the closed-period check on Postgres's `CURRENT_DATE`. `SettlementDate` defaults to the Go process's *UTC* date, and on this Asia/Calcutta deployment that is yesterday until 05:30 local — a settlement run at 02:00 on the 1st would otherwise be refused by the previous month's just-closed period. Same lesson as the Stage 14 lockout bug and Stage 35's SLA bug: reckon a window against one clock, never a mix.
  - [x] **37.1.4** Period-end revaluation posting to configured FX accounts — **built 2026-08-16.** `RevalueOpenForeignItems` restates every open foreign-currency balance at the closing rate and books the movement to 4210/5610. New setting `finance.fx_revaluation_rate_type` (default Closing).
    - **`GET /api/v1/finance/fx-revaluation` previews and `POST` commits** — the split is the HTTP verb, not a `dry_run` flag on one POST, so a preview can never post and a retry/prefetch/double-click cannot book a second adjustment. A period-end entry a controller cannot preview before it hits the ledger will not be trusted, and this one moves numbers on the face of the P&L.
    - **What gets revalued is a closed list** (`revaluableOpenItems`): `SalesInvoice` while Approved and `VendorInvoice` while Matched/Pending Approval — balances that are actually booked in 1300/2100. Deliberately **not** every currency-bearing doctype: PurchaseOrder, SalesOrder, RFQ and PurchaseRequisition are *commitments*, and revaluing one posts a gain against a control account holding no matching balance, unbalancing the very report this exists to make correct.
    - **Idempotent by refusal, not by silent no-op**: a document already revalued on or after the as-of date is skipped *with a named reason*. "Nothing to do" and "already done" send a controller to completely different places at close. One rate lookup per currency, not per document. The as-of date is passed as the transaction date so a revaluation into a closed period is refused by the check that exists for exactly that.
  - [x] **37.1.5** Multi-currency reporting by selected rate type — **built 2026-08-16.** Three `ReportDefinition`s in `engines/currency_reports.go` (`fx-open-item-exposure`, `fx-gain-loss-register`, `trial-balance-presentation-currency`), so they inherit the generic report screen, CSV/PDF export, scheduled delivery, column masking and the role gate with **no new frontend code**. The register reads `gl_postings` directly — the postings *are* the record and a second store could only disagree with them.
    - **Known limitation, stated rather than hidden**: the presentation trial balance applies **one rate to every line**. A full IAS 21 translation uses closing for balance-sheet items, average for P&L and historical for equity, which needs an equity movement schedule and a cumulative translation reserve this codebase does not have; a half-version would look authoritative and balance to nothing. This is an honest single-rate convenience translation and says so in a `rate_type` column on every row. Real consolidation is 37.2's job.
  - **Two further pre-existing bugs found in live verification and fixed** (neither reachable from a unit test — both are on the document path):
    - **An `ExchangeRate` with no end date was unsaveable.** `ValidateExchangeRateDocument` spelled every read as `fmt.Sprintf("%v", payload[key])`, and `fmt.Sprintf("%v", nil)` is the four-character string `"<nil>"`, not `""` — so an omitted optional field arrived as a *non-empty* value that then failed its format check. An open-ended rate is how essentially every rate table is really maintained, and the message blamed a field the user had never filled in. New `currencyField` helper delegates to the existing `payloadString`.
    - **A `Currency` whose document id was not its ISO code broke every invoice that selected it.** `SalesInvoice.currency` is a `Link`, so the generic check (META-0198) resolves against `documents.id`, while `ApplyDocumentCurrency` requires a three-letter ISO code — the only value satisfying both is one where id == code. The seeded INR row happens to be keyed that way, which is why nothing had noticed. Created any other way the currency saved happily and then every invoice failed with *"Linked Currency record with ID \"USD\" does not exist"*, naming a record sitting right there. Now refused at authoring time by `ValidateCurrencyDocument` with a message naming the id to use.
  - **A defect in this stage's own first cut, caught by live verification and not by the tests**: `PostDoubleEntry` derives a missing per-account transaction amount as `functional / rate`, which is right for a plain foreign posting but *invents* a number on an FX line. A 1,000-rupee revaluation at 84.00 recorded itself as **USD 11.90**, so summing `transaction_debit` for the document reported an exposure of USD 1,011.90 against a real one of USD 1,000. `postingOptionsFor` now zeroes every account in the entry that does not genuinely carry the foreign amount — an FX gain/loss line has no foreign value *by construction*, and a revaluation moves no foreign currency at all. Pinned by `TestFXLinesCarryNoInventedForeignAmount`.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; migration applied to dev (`1 applied; 95 already up to date`, `-migrate-status` clean after); 22 new subtests in `engines/currency_fx_test.go` plus `go test ./... -p 1` **fully green**. **Live over HTTP** on a throwaway server (port 8151, stopped and released, **every fixture deleted afterwards and residue confirmed zero**): the whole AR lifecycle — post at 83 (**83,000, not 1,000** — the bug fix), revalue at Closing 84 (+1,000 to 4210, preview-then-commit, and the re-run skipped with its reason), settle at Spot 85 → cash 85,000, AR cleared at the 84,000 *carrying* amount, realised gain **1,000 not 2,000**, AR netting exactly to zero; the AP mirror paying at 85 → DR 2100 83,000 + DR 5600 **2,000 loss** = CR 1100 85,000, the opposite sign on the same numbers; both authoring-time refusals; and all three reports returning real rows including the INR→USD presentation trial balance reporting `balanced: true`. **NOT done**: no frontend surface for the two new routes (API-only so far) and no real-browser pass — the three reports do render through the existing generic report screen.
  - **Safety/verification:** additive migration `db/migrations_stage37_1_currency_foundation.sql` was syntax/execution-tested inside `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` and was **not applied as of `0f9781f`** (⚠️ no longer self-enforcing — see the migration warning at the top of this section). The new masters do not alter any existing posting or document behavior before 37.1.2. Focused validation/effective-date/direct/inverse tests plus final `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` and `go test ./... -p 1` passed.
- [x] **37.2** Multi-entity & intercompany: entity-scoped posting, mirrored entries, consolidation — **built 2026-09-01.** `LegalEntity` (Stage 17.9) existed as a Master but nothing transacted across entities before this; this tenant schema model is one-schema-per-tenant (confirmed via `engines/saas.go`'s `ProvisionTenantSchema`), so "multiple entities" here means multiple LegalEntity books inside ONE tenant's schema, not cross-tenant consolidation.
  - [x] **37.2.1** Entity-scoped posting. `PostingOptions.Entity`/`JournalVoucherOptions.Entity` (`engines/finance.go`/`journal_voucher.go`) — the exact `CostCenter`/`Department` (Stage 26.6.8) precedent, additive and variadic so none of `PostDoubleEntry`'s existing call sites changed. New nullable `gl_postings.entity` column, validated via `validateLegalEntityReferenceInSchema` (mirrors `validateCostCenterReferenceInSchema` exactly).
  - [x] **37.2.2** `IntercompanyTransaction` — a Draft→Pending Approval→Approved→Posted/Partially Posted doctype (the `JournalVoucher` maker-checker shape) whose approval posts **two independently-balanced** `PostDoubleEntry` calls, one per entity's book: FROM entity Dr 1700 (Due from Intercompany) / Cr its own chosen account; TO entity Dr its own chosen account / Cr 2500 (Due to Intercompany). Two calls, not one, because `PostingOptions.Entity` is a whole-posting dimension and the two legs need two different entities — non-atomic across one DB transaction by construction, so a mid-way failure lands in a named, retryable "Partially Posted" state (`RetryPostApprovedIntercompanyTransaction`) rather than silently losing the second leg, the same accepted shape `RetryPostApprovedJournalVoucher` already has for its own single-leg failure.
  - [x] **37.2.3** `GetIntercompanyReconciliation` (report `intercompany-reconciliation`) — per entity-pair, the IC ledger's own claim vs. each side's real 1700/2500 control-account balance (2500 read credit-normal, 1700 debit-normal — a real sign bug caught by this stage's own test before it shipped). Flags variance, the same "compare two independently-derived numbers" shape Stage 35.8's settlement reconciliation established.
  - [x] **37.2.4** `ComputeIntercompanyEliminations` — consolidation-worksheet-only (never posted to any entity's own book, the same non-invented-entity posture 37.1.5 took on not modelling full IAS 21 translation). Always eliminates the matched 1700/2500 balance; also eliminates the revenue/expense pair when the chosen accounts are actually typed Revenue/Expense (the common "internal billing" shape) — stated, not silently assumed, exactly like 37.1.5's single-rate translation.
  - [x] **37.2.5** `GetConsolidatedTrialBalance` (report `consolidated-trial-balance`) + `GetEntityTrialBalance` (report `entity-trial-balance`) — the tenant-wide trial balance before and after 37.2.4's eliminations, per account, plus a per-entity breakdown (untagged rows land under "Unassigned" so nothing silently vanishes for a tenant that never uses the dimension).
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_2_multi_entity_intercompany.sql` (applied to dev only — surgical single-file apply, `-migrate-status` still shows the unrelated pre-existing `migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql` pending, untouched). - [x] **37.3** Costing & valuation, incl. **landed cost allocation** — **built 2026-09-01.** Pre-build audit found this codebase had NO costing method anywhere: `StockLedgerEntry`/`bin_stock`/`inventory_availability` track quantity only, POS checkout's "COGS" used whatever `cost_price` the client's cart JSON sent (never verified), and `PostGRNFinanceBooking` (the intended Dr 1200/Cr 2100 GRN-receipt posting) was dead code with zero callers — meaning **nothing had ever credited 2100 GRN Suspense**, while `PayVendorInvoice`'s debit to it has been running the whole time, driving the account further into an incorrect balance on every vendor payment this system has ever posted. This closes both gaps.
  - [x] **37.3.1** Single company-wide (not per-location) moving-weighted-average unit cost per item, `item_cost` (`engines/costing.go`) — the Ind AS 2/AS 2 acceptable method, avoiding FIFO/LIFO layer-tracking this codebase has no infrastructure for. Stores CUMULATIVE received qty/value (not a live "remaining pool"): the average only moves on receipt, never on issue, so nothing needed to intercept this codebase's many stock-out paths (sales, transfers, returns, disassembly, wastage...) to stay correct.
  - [x] **37.3.2** Landed cost allocation. New `LandedCostVoucher` doctype (Draft → Applied, one-shot) references a GRN and spreads its charge lines (Freight/Customs/Duty/Insurance/Other) proportionally by each received line's own base value across `item_cost`, posting `Dr 1200 Inventory / Cr 2110 Landed Cost Clearing` (new account).
  - [x] **37.3.3** Real COGS at sale. `ResolveCOGSUnitCostPaise` wired into `FinalizePOSCheckout` (`engines/pos_checkout.go`) — a real recorded moving-average cost wins over the client-submitted `cost_price`; an item with no receipt history yet falls back to the client's own figure unchanged, so a tenant that has never used GRN-based receiving sees byte-identical behaviour to today.
  - [x] **37.3.4** `GetInventoryValuation` (report `inventory-valuation`) — real on-hand qty (`inventory_availability`, summed across locations) × the current moving-average rate, computed fresh at report time rather than tracking a parallel "current pool" that every stock-out path would need to keep in step.
  - [x] **37.3.5** Real GRN → GL posting. `RecordGRNReceiptCosting`, called from `PostGRNReceiptWithQC` (`engines/wms_receiving.go`), resolves each accepted line's ex-GST base cost from the GRN's own PO (reusing `PreviewPurchaseOrder`'s existing GST-aware taxable-amount computation, not re-derived) and posts `Dr 1200 Inventory / Cr 2100 GRN Suspense` — the missing credit side of the 2100 gap above. Best-effort/logged, never unwinding stock that has already posted, the same posture the batch/serial registration loops in that function already take.
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_3_costing_valuation.sql` (applied to dev only — surgical single-file apply, the same pre-existing unrelated `migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql` still pending, untouched). New `engines/costing.go` + `costing_test.go`, one new handler file (`internal/server/handlers_costing.go`), 2 new routes, 1 new GL account (2110 Landed Cost Clearing), 1 new report. No dependency, no dedicated frontend screen — reachable via the dedicated create/apply endpoints (the `JournalVoucher`/`IntercompanyTransaction` split), the generic DocType form for read/list, and the generic report screen.
  - **Known, stated limitations**: company-wide costing, not per-location (a location-specific transfer-cost model would need to track cost basis across every stock movement, a much larger undertaking). A landed cost voucher applied after intervening receipts/consumption spreads its charge over the item's *current* pool using the original GRN's quantity as the weight — an approximation once the pool has moved on, the same class of stated simplification 37.1.5/37.2.4 already carry. GST input credit recognition (1500) is NOT posted at GRN time (deliberately — GST law recognises input credit only against a matched tax invoice, not a PO estimate) and remains a separate, pre-existing gap in the VendorInvoice flow, not attempted here.

Every fixture hard-deleted afterward, zero residue confirmed by direct SQL count.

## Item-level closures from stages that remain open (archived 2026-09-29)

The stages below are still live in `docs/micro_checklist.md` (each retains at least one open item), but every individual item that was already fully `[x]` closed has been moved here so the live tracker holds only pending work. Fully-closed Track/Phase sub-groups moved wholesale (header included); sub-groups with a mix of open and closed items kept their header live and only the closed items moved. Item numbering is unchanged; see the live tracker for each stage's current status and remaining open items.

### Stage 20 — ERP Maturity Master Plan Execution — Track A — blocked on user input — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **20.6** Zero Real-World Production Validation — **Partially closed (2026-07-25)**: genuine *production* validation still needs real hosting/traffic (blocked on 26.1.1, Track A). What's buildable without that — real concurrent-load and messy-data validation against the actual dev Postgres — is done: `engines/scale.go`'s simulator run at 800 tx/50 workers/500 stores (0 failures, 641 TPS) and again contention-focused at 400 tx/50 workers all hitting **one** location (0 failures, confirms 24.7's row-lock holds under real contention, not just dispersed load). Messy-data pass found 2 real things, both fixed: (1) **genuine bug** — this dev Postgres cluster (including `template0`/`template1` at the time, so every future `CREATE DATABASE` inherited it — **since fixed: the two application databases were converted and `template1` recreated as UTF-8 on 2026-08-13, so a bare `CREATE DATABASE` is now born UTF8; `template0` stays WIN1252 by design, see ledger §97**) was `WIN1252`-encoded, not UTF8; any CJK/Arabic/emoji character in any text field hard-fails the INSERT with a raw Postgres error. Can't safely fix by recreating the shared dev DB outright (destructive, other sessions depend on it), so instead added `db.EnforceUTF8Encoding()` (`db/db.go`), called from `Run()`: warns loudly in dev, refuses to boot in `ENV=production` — live-verified both paths. (2) Found that any unexpected 500 leaked its raw internal error (`err.Error()`, including DB driver detail) straight to the client via `writeAPIErrorGeneric` — fixed at that one choke point to show the existing `GLOBAL-0302` catalog message instead for any 5xx, while still logging the raw detail server-side (`LogRequired=true` already covered this). The 20,000-char field / SQL-metacharacter-and-unicode / oversized-string / bogus-vendor-Link / negative-qty / idempotency-race messy-data cases were also run - all either correctly rejected or (idempotency) confirmed the real protection lives at the HTTP claim layer, not the engine layer, by design. All stress-test residue (`gl_postings`, `inventory_availability`, scratch docs) removed from the dev DB after. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
