### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.0 — Truth Freeze and Regression Fix
- [x] **26.0.1** Reconcile the new PDF against live code/docs/git state. Corrections found and logged in `specs/erp_maturity_master_plan.md` §2: the PDF's finance-regression root cause assumption and its industry-pack gap count were both stale/wrong — see 26.0.2 and Phase 26.4's intro.
- [x] **26.0.2** Root-cause fix for `TestEngines/FinanceDoubleEntryAndPOS` and `TestEngines/DocTypeValidationAndAuth` — fixed and verified clean 2026-07-24 (`go test ./... -p 1`, both packages `ok`, first time this repo's test suite has been fully green). Traced each to an actual root cause instead of assuming shared-DB debris by default: **Finance** was never DB debris — `gl_postings` is unconditionally wiped at the top of that subtest, so nothing external could be contaminating it. The real cause: Stage 24.5 added an idempotency-retry check (`V-003`, posting 500/500) *between* the balanced-entry post and the trial-balance assertions, but never updated those assertions' expected totals (1000/9000) to include it — a stale test assertion, not a bug in `engines/finance.go`. Fixed by correcting the two expected totals to 1500/9500 (`engines/engines_test.go`). **DocType** genuinely was shared-DB debris, confirmed by tracing it to its actual source: `public/profiles/agriculture.json` marks `Brand.fefo_enabled` mandatory, and some earlier Agriculture industry-profile test run applied that profile against the persistent `default` tenant schema without cleaning up afterward — that field is absent from `db/migration.sql`'s own baseline Brand fields, confirming it doesn't belong there. Fixed by having the subtest delete that one stray `doctype_fields` row before asserting, same "clear fixture before asserting" convention `GenerateSequence` already uses. Deliberately did not touch `engines/finance.go`/`engines/doctype.go` — no real bug in either.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.1 — Production-Like Environment — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **26.1.1** Production hosting decision (provider, domain, TLS, secrets store) — **decided and formalised 2026-08-06** (user: "formalise the droplet"). The provider question turned out to be already settled in practice: a DigitalOcean droplet has been serving the real build, `systemd`-supervised, Go binary bound to `127.0.0.1`, `ufw` active, nightly encrypted backups. What this pass actually found and fixed was that the *formalisation* had never happened:
  - **`ENV=production` was never set.** All three gates it controls were therefore off: the seed-admin credential hard-stop (`engines/auth.go:331`), the non-UTF8 database hard-stop (`db/db.go:88`), and removal of the `/api/v1/debug/panic` route (`internal/server/routes.go:564`). Both hard-stop preconditions were **verified before flipping it** — `SHOW server_encoding` returned `UTF8`, and the absence of the `[SECURITY]` seed-password warning in the startup journal confirmed the admin password was already rotated — because either condition failing would have made the server refuse to boot. Applied with an automatic rollback guard; verified after: service active, `/api/v1/health` 200, `debug/panic` now **404**.
  - **Caddy was serving the stock Debian welcome page.** `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile` was still the packaged default — `root * /usr/share/caddy` + `file_server`, with `reverse_proxy localhost:8080` **commented out**. The repo's own `deploy/Caddyfile` had never been installed, so Caddy had been running and doing nothing while the app was reached solely through the SSH tunnel. Replaced with a new `deploy/Caddyfile.holding` (returns a blank 404 on `:80`), stock config backed up first.
  - **Domain/TLS deliberately left open, not worked around.** Pointing Caddy at the app on plain `:80` would have been one line, and would have published the entire ERP — passwords, bearer tokens, tenant data — in cleartext. That is strictly worse than the tunnel-only posture, so it was refused; `Caddyfile.holding` documents the reasoning inline so the next session doesn't "fix" it. New `deploy/enable_tls.sh <domain> <email>` does the whole switch in one command when a domain exists: validates the hostname/email, **checks DNS actually resolves to this box before touching anything** (a premature ACME run burns Let's Encrypt's weekly failed-authorization quota and can lock out the real attempt for days), `caddy validate`s the generated config, backs up the current one, and auto-rolls-back if the reload fails.
  - `deploy/Caddyfile` rewritten as the real production config (see 26.1.3). Secrets store confirmed already correct: `/etc/erp/erp.env`, `root:erp 0640`, loaded via systemd `EnvironmentFile=`.
  - ~~**Still open, tracked here rather than as a new item:** supply a domain → run `enable_tls.sh` → set `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`. Until then production remains tunnel-only by design.~~ **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-14 — production is live at `https://app.wholeops.in`.** `wholeops.in` registered (Hostinger), delegated to Cloudflare DNS-only, `enable_tls.sh` run, `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=https://app.wholeops.in` set, `erp` restarted. **Verified externally from the dev machine, not just from the box**: valid Let's Encrypt cert (`CN=app.wholeops.in`, issuer `Let's Encrypt YE1`, expires 2026-11-12, chain verifies), `/api/v1/health` and `/api/v1/version` 200, SPA root + `app.js` + `styles.css` 200 with correct MIME types, `http://` → **308** to https, `POST /api/v1/login` returns a proper `USERAC-0021` 401 envelope (proving TLS → Caddy → Go → error catalog end-to-end), `/api/v1/debug/panic` **404** (so `ENV=production` is still in force), and all six security headers present with `Server`/`X-Powered-By` stripped. The tunnel still works and was not removed. Two blockers found and fixed on the way — see Stage 44.8 (`enable_tls.sh` bugs) and 44.7 (the DigitalOcean firewall).
- [x] **26.1.2** SLO/status-page dashboard — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. Pure frontend, no new backend route or table: a new "System Status" screen (`public/app.js`'s `renderSystemStatusView`, `Settings` sidebar flyout, HR/Admin-only same as `menu-audit-logs`) wires the existing Stage 25.8 `GET /api/v1/ops/deployment-status`/`GET /api/v1/ops/backup-status` endpoints, whose backup-status handler already computes the DR-0213 (restore drill overdue)/DR-0214 (RPO breach) warnings off the Stage 17.10 error catalog — reused as-is, not rebuilt. Screen shows: a warnings banner, quick-stat tiles (environments tracked, last backup, last restore drill), latest-deployment-per-environment table, full deployment history table, and backup/restore-drill history table — all reusing the existing `.stat-card`/`.table-panel`/`.badge-*` primitives, no new CSS. Live-verified via a throwaway-port server + Playwright (scratch HR/Admin token, real dev DB): screen renders correctly against the current empty `public.deployments`/`public.ops_run_log` tables (both warnings correctly fire, all three tables show their empty state, no console errors from the new code). `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean.
- [x] **26.1.3a** Origin/proxy hardening and rate-limiting design — **engineering scope closed 2026-08-06**. Everything achievable without a public hostname is done:
  - `deploy/Caddyfile` rewritten as the real production proxy config: strips `Server`/`X-Powered-By`, caps request bodies at 12MB *before* they reach Go (the app's own limits are 2MB global / 10MB on the PIM upload route, so this is the outer bound), health-checks the upstream, sets proxy read/write timeouts to 90s — deliberately **above** the app's own 60s `WriteTimeout` so the proxy never truncates a long report export the server would have finished — and rolls access logs at 50MB × 10.
  - `trusted_proxies static private_ranges` in the global block. This is load-bearing rather than decorative: `TRUST_PROXY=1` is set on the box, so without it a client could forge `X-Forwarded-For` and defeat the app's per-IP rate limiting entirely. With it, Caddy replaces rather than appends the header.
  - **Two genuinely missing security headers added — in the app, not the proxy.** `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and a `Permissions-Policy` denying geolocation/microphone/camera/payment/usb were absent everywhere (`internal/server/middleware.go` already set HSTS/CSP/X-Frame-Options/nosniff). Attached at the existing `securityHeaders` choke point per CLAUDE.md's shared-choke-point principle, so they hold on the SSH-tunnel path too — which never touches Caddy, and is currently the *only* access path.
  - **Deliberately NOT done: rate limiting at the Caddy layer.** Caddy has no built-in rate limiter; the community `caddy-ratelimit` module requires rebuilding Caddy with `xcaddy` and maintaining a custom binary to keep patched — a new build toolchain on the box for something the app already does (Stage 13.14: 5/min/IP on the auth category, general per-IP budget elsewhere). Rejected as a poor trade under the lightweight-first principle, and the reasoning is written into `deploy/Caddyfile` so it isn't silently revisited.
  - This closes the repository-owned work. The production activation is tracked separately below so an external prerequisite is not misreported as unfinished engineering.
- [x] **26.1.4** Tenant plan/subscription/entitlement admin screen — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. New **Tenant Entitlements** screen (`renderTenantEntitlementsView`, Settings sidebar, HR/Admin-only): a tenant picker, "Apply a Plan" buttons (one per Stage 27 `engines.ProductPackages` entry — clicking one sets that tenant's modules to exactly match, via a new `engines.ApplyPackageSelection`, extracted from `handleProvisionTenant`'s inline provisioning-time logic into a shared choke point both now call), and a per-module toggle grid (`.switch`/`.slider`, a pre-existing but previously-unused CSS component) for fine-tuning beyond a plan, reusing the pre-existing Stage 14 `GET/POST .../tenant/module-entitlement(s)` endpoints as-is. New backend surface, all additive: `GET /api/v1/admin/tenants` (tenant picker data — no tenant-listing endpoint existed at all before this), `GET /api/v1/admin/packages` (plan catalog), `POST /api/v1/admin/tenant/package` (apply-a-plan action). Live-verified via curl + a real-browser Playwright pass against a real second tenant (`tenant_new`): applied the WMS-only plan (confirmed only `wms`/`stickers`/`reports` + core modules stayed enabled, everything else correctly disabled), toggled one module individually, then restored the tenant to its original full-suite state.
- [x] **26.1.5** Tenant usage metering / tenant-health dashboard — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. New **Tenant Usage** screen (`renderTenantUsageView`, Settings sidebar, HR/Admin-only) reuses Stage 24.30's live per-tenant concurrency limiter (added a `snapshot()` accessor — the counter already existed in `internal/server/middleware.go`, just had no read path) and Stage 25.8's `tenant_limits` table, behind one new `GET /api/v1/admin/tenant-usage` endpoint that joins both plus each tenant's active-user count across every tenant in `public.tenants`. Shows: tenant count / total in-flight requests / tenants-at-cap stat tiles, and a per-tenant table (active users vs. configured `max_users`, in-flight requests vs. the fixed 15-request cap, any other configured limit) with badge color coding (green/amber/red by how close to cap). Live-verified: the endpoint's own request correctly showed up as 1 in-flight against itself (proves the counter is genuinely live, not stubbed); temporarily set a `max_users` limit on the `default` tenant to confirm the "4 / 10" badge rendering, then removed it.
- [x] **26.1.6** Tenant-scoped export/restore — **built and live-verified 2026-08-05.** Scope decision taken 2026-08-05: **on-demand export only, no per-tenant cadence** — the nightly whole-database backup already contains every tenant, so a nightly per-tenant run would only duplicate it. Extends the existing backup engine rather than adding a second one, deliberately: encryption, the sha256 sidecar and the retention rules must not drift between a whole-DB backup and a per-tenant export.
  - `deploy/backup.sh` gained `TENANT_SCHEMA=` / `--tenant <schema>`, guarding the name against a pattern *and* against actually existing (a typo would otherwise produce a silently empty dump — `pg_dump --schema` does not fail on a schema matching nothing), with prefix-scoped retention pruning so the two kinds of backup never age each other out.
  - `manage.ps1 export-tenant -TenantSchema <schema>` is the Windows/dev counterpart, reusing `Protect-BackupFile`/`Log-OpsRun` verbatim. `handlers_ops_status.go`'s history query now also admits `tenant_export`, so these show up on the existing System Status screen instead of being invisible.
  - **Live-verified on the real dev DB**: `manage.ps1 export-tenant -TenantSchema tenant_default` produced an encrypted+checksummed dump, `pg_restore -l` on the equivalent plain dump confirmed **only `tenant_default` objects are inside** (238 objects, no other schema present), and the run was recorded in `public.ops_run_log` as `tenant_export/dev/success`.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.3 — Reachability and Usability Gaps (buildable now — highest value-per-effort, wires existing backend to a screen)
Already flagged as real gaps in `project_ledger.md` §29 (Stage 21.9); formalized as build items here.
- [x] **26.3.1** GRN workbench screen — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. New **Goods Receipt (GRN)** screen (`renderGRNWorkbenchView`, `public/app.js`, Buying sidebar flyout) — the first UI GRN has ever had; it's a Transaction (not Master) doctype so it was never even reachable from the Setup submenu the generic doctype form relies on. PO Reference typeahead + "Load Items from PO" pre-fills lines from the PO's own `items` JSON (Received Qty defaulting to ordered, for the common no-variance case); manual "Add Line" for POs with no recorded items (most of them today — the PO create screen only ever saves `items: '[]'`, tracked separately as 26.3.6). Line shape matches `engines/transactional_validation.go`'s `grnReceivedLine` exactly (`sku`, `qty`, `accepted_qty`, `rejected_qty`, `rejection_reason`) so it inherits every existing server-side rule for free — GOODSR-0089/0090 accepted/rejected-qty checks and the PURCHA-0082/0084/0086/0087/0088 PO cross-checks — rather than a parallel validation path; `accepted_qty` is auto-derived as `qty − rejected_qty` client-side so it can never violate GOODSR-0089 by construction. Barcode preview: a best-effort `GET /api/v1/doc/Item/{sku}` lookup per line renders the Item's real barcode (falling back to the SKU itself on a miss, same degrade-gracefully behavior `engines.PrintStickers` already uses server-side) — not a new backend endpoint. No new backend route; posts through the existing `POST /api/v1/doc/GRN`. Two real bugs found and fixed during live verification: (1) `internal/server/handlers_core_doc_engine.go`'s GRN inventory-posting hook ran unconditionally on every save including edits (not just creates) — silently double-posting the same received qty into `inventory_availability` if a GRN were ever re-saved; gated to `id == ""` (create-only), same convention as the pre-existing Item/PIM-profile create-only check just above it in the same function. This was real but dormant (GRN had no UI at all before this screen, so nothing could trigger it) — closed before shipping a screen that could have newly triggered it, confirmed via a direct re-POST against a live-posted GRN that stock stayed at 10 instead of doubling to 20. (2) A race in `loadGRNItemsFromPO`: it fires from both the PO field's own `change` event (typeahead pick) and the explicit "Load Items from PO" button, so picking a PO and immediately clicking the button could launch two overlapping calls that duplicated every line (each resets `grnLineItems` then awaits a barcode lookup per line, so an interleaved second call's reset/pushes land on the same array the first call resumes into) — fixed with a reentrancy guard. Live-verified end-to-end via a throwaway-port server + Playwright (scratch HR/Admin token minted via a repo-internal `cmd/` program, deleted after) against a real seeded PO/Item/Vendor: loaded 1 line from a PO correctly (post-fix), posted a GRN, confirmed `inventory_availability` incremented by exactly the received qty, confirmed a same-content re-save did not double it, and confirmed the client-side rejected-qty guards and a real server-side PURCHA-0084 rejection ("PO already fully received") both surface cleanly in the form's error banner rather than a raw error. All test fixtures removed after (including two soft-deleted rows and one auto-created PIM profile that needed a direct SQL cleanup, since Approved transactional documents correctly refuse API-level deletion). `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean.
- [x] **26.3.2** Purchase Requisition entry screen + sidebar entry — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. `PurchaseRequisition` was already a fully registered doctype (fields/role-permissions/approval_rules, Stage 17.7) with a flat schema (no line items) — unlike GRN, this didn't need a bespoke workbench, just a **Purchase Requisitions** sidebar entry (Buying flyout) pointing at the existing generic doctype-table view, plus two doctype-specific row actions the generic table doesn't provide for any doctype: "Submit for Approval" (posts through the same `/api/v1/approval/submit` `submitPOForApproval`/`submitExpenseForApproval` already use) and "Convert to RFQ"/"Convert to PO" for Approved rows (calls the already-existing, already-routed `POST /api/v1/procurement/convert-requisition` / `engines.ConvertRequisitionToOrder`, which had no UI caller before this). No backend changes. Live-verified end-to-end via curl (create → submit → approve as a second user, maker-checker correctly blocked self-approval → convert to PO) and Playwright (the requisition list correctly showed the final `Converted` status).
- [x] **26.3.3** Approval Rules admin screen — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. New **Approval Rules** screen (Settings flyout) lists every amount-slab→role rule and adds a real create/edit modal (form fields, not sequential prompts, since one field is a role `<select>` sourced from `GET /api/v1/admin/roles`) plus a delete action. `GetApprovalRules`/`UpsertApprovalRule` (Stage 24.8) already existed and were already routed at `GET/POST /api/v1/approval/rules` — only missing a UI and a delete path; added `engines.DeleteApprovalRule` + a `DELETE` case on the existing handler (HR/Admin-only, same gate as the existing POST case). Live-verified via Playwright: created a scratch rule through the real modal, confirmed it appeared in the list, deleted it through the UI, confirmed removal — all against the real dev DB.
- [x] **26.3.4** WMS operations screens — audit confirmed **zero** frontend existed for any of putaway/pick-list/bin-condition-transition/cycle-count-reconcile (Stage 20 Track B.2's backend, routed since Stage 20, had sat completely unused). Built and live-verified 2026-07-24: Putaway screen, Bin Conditions screen, Cycle Count screen (reconcile action + a link into the generic doctype-table for `CycleCountLine` line entry/Bulk Import), and a "View Pick List" action added to the existing Fulfillment screen's task rows (read-only modal, reusing the `.modal-overlay` primitive already established for `viewTaxonomyHistory`). No backend/migration changes — pure frontend on top of already-working `engines/wms.go`. Found and fixed one small pre-existing gap along the way: `CycleCountLine` (a Transaction doctype) was unreachable from the Setup submenu at all (that submenu only lists Master doctypes), so Bulk Import for it had no entry point — fixed by linking directly into the generic doctype-table view instead of building a second import mechanism. Full detail: `project_ledger.md` §39.
- [x] **26.3.5** Vendor invoice override — confirmed 2026-07-24: `engines.PayVendorInvoice`'s Stage 24.11 override path (`POST /api/v1/procurement/vendor-invoice/pay` with `override_reason`, routes a MismatchHold invoice through approval instead of paying immediately) was genuinely missing a UI action — `renderVendorInvoiceActions` only ever rendered a "Match" button for MismatchHold rows. Added an "Override & Pay" button alongside it (MismatchHold only; Draft has nothing to override yet) that prompts for a mandatory reason and posts through the exact existing endpoint. No backend changes. Verified the exact HTML the row-action function now produces for a MismatchHold row directly in a live browser session (no VendorInvoice/Vendor/PO/GRN fixture chain existed in the dev DB to exercise the full flow end-to-end this pass); the endpoint itself (`override_reason` handling, pending-approval response shape) was confirmed by reading `handlers_operations.go`/`engines/vendor_invoice.go` directly rather than assumed.
- [x] **26.3.6** PO amendment screen — confirmed 2026-07-24: an Approved-but-not-yet-received PO already has a real edit path at the API level (`handleGenericDoc`'s update route already resets an Approved+approval-gated doctype to Pending Approval on edit via `ResetToPendingOnEdit`) — the gap was purely that the bespoke Purchase Orders screen (not the generic doctype-table view) had zero edit/amend action for any PO status, only "Submit for Approval" for Draft. Added an "Amend" button (all statuses except `Closed`) using this screen's own simple field set (vendor/warehouse/location/amount, prompt-based like `editPrefixConfig`) rather than the generic dynamic-modal form, which would have exposed the raw JSON `items` string as a plain text field — worse than this screen's own create form already avoids by not managing items either. Warns and requires confirmation before amending an already-Approved PO, since that will reset it to Pending Approval. No backend changes. Live-verified via curl (amend rejected with a clear field-level error for an invalid Location value, confirming the existing Link-field validation still applies; succeeded and correctly incremented the document version with a valid one) and Playwright (Amend button reachable and wired correctly); test PO reverted to its original values afterward.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.4 — PIM/PXM Maturity Sprint
Per PDF §6.1, each extends existing Stage 15/16 PIM infrastructure — no parallel PIM model. **26.4.10 built and closed 2026-08-05** (supplier portal, product decision taken that day). **26.4.1-26.4.9 built and closed 2026-07-23** — see `project_ledger.md` for the full build record. `go build`/`go vet` clean; `go test ./... -p 1` reproduces only the two pre-existing known failures (26.0.2), nothing new; every item live-verified against the real dev DB via direct API calls plus a real-browser (Playwright/Chromium) pass through the actual PIM screens — not just curl. A real pre-existing bug was found and fixed in the process: the global CSP's `img-src` had no `blob:` source, which silently blocked every PIM media gallery thumbnail/image from ever rendering in a real browser (`internal/server/middleware.go`) — invisible to any test that doesn't render a real page.
- [x] **26.4.1** Attribute groups + locale/channel attribute overrides on the existing Family/Attribute framework (Stage 15.1). New `ProductAttributeGroup` doctype; `ProductAttributeDef.group` link; `ProductAttributeValue.locale`/`.channel` optional override fields, resolved by `engines.ResolveAttributeValue` (most-specific match wins, falls back to the global blank/blank row) — wired into both `CalculateCompleteness` and `BuildChannelPayload` so overrides apply to readiness scoring *and* the actual outbound publish payload.
- [x] **26.4.2** Duplicate-item/content detection — reuse the Stage 25 master-data-validation choke point pattern (`engines/master_data_validation.go`). Added a same-family duplicate-name check for Item and a same-language duplicate-title check for ProductContent (excludes Rejected content and the document's own row). Live-verified: creating a second item/content with the same name/title in the same family/language is rejected with the offending sibling's id named in the message.
- [x] **26.4.3** Taxonomy versioning (family/attribute-set change history) — additive, audit-log-backed (reuse Stage 1.2's audit engine). No new table/trigger: `engines/pim_taxonomy.go`'s `GetTaxonomyHistory` reads the existing `audit_logs` trail (already captures every field-level change via the Stage 1.2 trigger), allowlisted to `ProductFamily`/`ProductAttributeDef`/`ProductFamilyAttribute`/`ProductAttributeGroup`. A "History" row action + read-only modal added to the generic doctype table for these 4 doctypes only.
- [x] **26.4.4** Media versioning + renditions + alt text + expiry on the existing Media/DAM library (Stage 15.2). `version_no` now genuinely increments per item+role (was hardcoded to 1); `alt_text`/`expiry_date` fields settable at upload and editable after; a real thumbnail rendition (stdlib `image`/`image/jpeg`/`image/png` decode + nearest-neighbor resize + JPEG encode, no new dependency) generated for jpg/png uploads, served via a new authenticated `/thumbnail` route — webp/gif/pdf get no thumbnail, a stated scope limit. New `media-expiry` report. Live-verified in a real browser: uploaded PNG rendered correctly as its generated JPEG thumbnail after the CSP fix above.
- [x] **26.4.5** Content workflow: owner assignment + SLA + rejected-field comments on the existing approval-gated `ProductContent` (Stage 15.1) — additive fields, not a new engine. `owner`/`sla_due_date` fields added; new `content-sla-breach` report. Rejection comments needed no new storage (`approval_log.comment` was already mandatory on reject, APPROV-0159) — new `engines.ListApprovalLog`/`GET /api/v1/approval/log` surfaces that existing data, now shown in the Workbench content panel.
- [x] **26.4.6** Bulk approval + rollback-to-prior-content-version for PIM content. `engines.BulkDecideApproval` loops the existing per-document `DecideApproval` (each call already its own atomic transaction) with per-id partial-failure reporting, wired to the ProductContent bulk-edit-bar's new Approve/Reject buttons. A new `product_content_versions` system table snapshots ProductContent on every real "Approved" decision; `RollbackProductContentVersion` restores a snapshot as Draft (never silently re-Approved). Live-verified end-to-end: approve → bad edit → restore → title correctly reverted.
- [x] **26.4.7** Channel validation packs + per-channel diff preview before publish, on the existing Channel Publishing queue (Stage 16). New `ChannelValidationRule` doctype (Min Images/Max Title Length/Required Tag) evaluated inside `CheckPublishReadiness`. Diff preview (`PreviewChannelDiff`) diffs the payload about to be sent against the last attempt's `payload_snapshot` (new JSONB column on `pim_publish_log`) — stated limit: this is not a live read-back from the platform itself, since that would need a per-connector API call this framework doesn't otherwise need. Live-verified: a Min-Images=2 rule correctly blocked publish for an item with 1 image.
- [x] **26.4.8** Marketplace error dictionary — map real connector error codes to the Stage 23 message catalog instead of raw passthrough. `classifyConnectorError` pattern-matches known platform wording (Shopify/BigCommerce/Magento) onto the existing CONN-0225/0227/0228 codes instead of every failure collapsing into CONN-0226; new `error_code` column on `pim_publish_log`, surfaced in the publish log UI. Unit-tested (`TestClassifyConnectorError`) plus confirmed the existing connector tests still pass unchanged.
- [x] **26.4.9** Search/discovery feed export (synonyms, ranking attributes, merchandising flags) — a read-only export report off existing PIM data, not a search engine. `GetSearchFeedExportCSV` exports item/title/tags/family/category/completeness-score/has-main-image as CSV via a new authenticated download button (same blob-download pattern as the existing report-export CSV).
- [x] **26.4.10** Supplier portal — **built and live-verified 2026-08-05.** Product decision taken 2026-08-05: **limited-role login, not a separate portal.** A second app would mean a second auth system, session story, deployment and set of security rules to keep in step, for what is in substance "an outside party fills in a form an internal reviewer approves" — all of which this ERP already has. So a supplier is a user whose role reaches exactly one doctype.
  - New `SupplierSubmission` doctype (module `pim`, Transaction), whose field set deliberately mirrors `ProductContent`'s so an approved submission is a field-for-field copy rather than a translation table that would drift. Plus `media_url`, the one field with no counterpart — suppliers have no DAM access, so they submit a URL for a reviewer to judge. **Not** auto-fetched on approval: a server that downloads arbitrary supplier-supplied URLs is an SSRF surface this stage does not need.
  - QC reuses the existing maker-checker engine (`approval_rules` row, HR/Admin as checker) — no bespoke review flow. Rejection comments were already mandatory there (APPROV-0159), so "why was my submission rejected" needed no new storage.
  - **Row-level scoping is what makes the limited-role shape safe**, and is the real work: doctype-level RBAC alone would let every supplier read every other supplier's submissions. New `users.supplier_code` ties a login to a Vendor; `handleGenericDoc` — the one choke point every document read and write already passes through — enforces it in three places: the list query, the single-document read, and the write path. On create `supplier_code` is **overwritten** from the session rather than validated (a supplier never picks whose name a submission is filed under); on update the **stored** code is checked, not the payload's, since the payload is attacker-controlled. A Supplier account with no vendor linked fails closed.
  - On approval the submission becomes `ProductContent` as **Draft**, never Approved — QC approval here means "this copy is acceptable to work with", not "publish it". ProductContent has its own approval gate, and short-circuiting it from an outside party's submission would let a supplier's text reach a live sales channel with no internal publish decision.
  - Frontend cost was one PIM tab ("Supplier Submissions"); suppliers reach the same doctype through the existing generic table screen, so there is no second list/table implementation either.
  - **Live-verified** by two handler-level tests against the real dev DB (`internal/server/supplier_portal_test.go`, both passing): a submission filed by supplier A while *claiming* vendor B is stored under A; B cannot list it, cannot read it by id, and cannot overwrite it by posting to its id (A's title verified unchanged); and an approved submission produces a Draft `ProductContent` carrying the supplier's title.
- [x] **26.4.11** AI content-assist — **decision taken and built 2026-08-06.** Scope decision (user): **local/offline only**, no third-party model API. New `engines/pim_content_assist.go` composes a draft (`title`/`seo_title`/`short_desc`/`long_desc`/`tags`) from the product's **own** data — Item fields plus family attribute values resolved through the existing `ResolveAttributeValue` (26.4.1), so locale/channel overrides apply here exactly as they do to completeness scoring and the outbound publish payload, rather than via a second resolution path. Deterministic templates, no network call, no API key.
  - **The four governance questions `go_live_decisions.md` §11 asks are answered by construction, not by policy.** (1) *What leaves the server*: nothing — no provider. (2) *Human-in-the-loop*: `GenerateContentSuggestion` **returns** a suggestion and has no code path that writes `ProductContent`; whoever accepts it saves an ordinary Draft, which still passes the existing Stage 15.1/26.4.5 approval gate. There is a test asserting zero `ProductContent` rows are created, so a future refactor that "helpfully" persists the draft fails the build rather than silently removing the guarantee. (3) *Audit*: every generation writes a `ContentAssistLog` row (generated text + the source fields it rested on + user + `generator: local-template-v1` — names the generator, not a model, so rows stay unambiguous if a real model is ever introduced). (4) *Prompt injection*: no prompt exists, but the related risk is real because supplier-submitted attribute values (26.4.10) can reach published copy — `sanitizeAssistInput` strips angle brackets and control characters from every value before composition, with a test asserting no angle bracket can survive.
  - **Storage follows the `ReportRunLog`/`StockLedgerEntry` precedent** — a plain row in `documents`, no bespoke table. Chosen specifically because a bespoke table needs an entry in `ProvisionTenantSchema`'s clone list, which is exactly the drift 26.11.2 caught after it had already cost every new tenant its GL/approvals/WMS tables. Read-only for HR/Admin via the generic API; `writeContentAssistLog` inserts directly.
  - **Quality caveat, stated plainly**: deterministic templates restate known attributes. They cannot write persuasive marketing copy — but they also cannot hallucinate a product feature, which is what makes this shape defensible without a review model behind it. Output is a starting point that saves retyping, not finished copy.
  - New endpoint `GET /api/v1/pim/content-assist/{itemCode}` (`moduleGate("pim")`). GET rather than POST deliberately: the call is idempotent and creates no `ProductContent` — the only write is the audit row. `db/migrations_stage26_4_11_content_assist.sql`.
  - **Verified**: new `engines/pim_content_assist_test.go` — brand-dedupe (guards "Acme Acme Cotton Shirt"), SEO length budget, provenance populated, **no `ProductContent` written**, exactly one audit row, determinism (same item → identical draft; attribute ordering comes from a map upstream, so this is a real risk), nameless-item and unknown-item both refused rather than given a SKU-shaped title, plus direct `sanitizeAssistInput`/`truncateOnWord` cases. `go build`/`go vet` clean, all tests pass. **Live-verified over real HTTP** (throwaway port 8271, scratch token via a repo-internal `cmd/` program, deleted after): returned `title: "Acme Cotton Shirt"` (brand correctly deduped, not doubled), `seo_title: "Acme Cotton Shirt | Apparel"`, provenance `["brand","category","name"]` and an honest warning that no family attributes resolved; confirmed the `ContentAssistLog` row landed attributed to the calling user; confirmed an unknown item returns **422/GLOBAL-0002** through the standard error envelope. All fixtures and scratch tooling removed after.
  - **Not built**: no frontend action yet. The endpoint is reachable but nothing in the PIM Workbench calls it — a deliberate stopping point, since wiring an "Assist" button into the content panel is a separate, small piece of work and shipping the engine + governance first is what the decision was actually about.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.5 — WMS Enterprise Maturity Sprint
Per PDF §6.2, extends Stage 20 Track B.2's Bin/putaway/pick/pack/cycle-count engines — `bin_stock` stays a breakdown of `inventory_availability`, never a second source of truth (Stage 20.17's own precedent). **26.5.1-26.5.10 built and closed 2026-07-25** (`db/migrations_stage26_5_wms_enterprise.sql`, `engines/wms_receiving.go`, `engines/wms_putaway_ext.go`, `engines/wms_picking.go`, `engines/wms_pack_count.go`, `internal/server/handlers_wms_enterprise.go`; small additive edits to `engines/wms.go`, `engines/transactional_validation.go`, `internal/server/handlers_core_doc_engine.go`, `internal/server/routes.go`). `go build`/`go vet` clean; `engines.TestWMSEnterprise` (8 subtests, real Postgres) all pass, plus the full pre-existing `go test ./... -p 1` suite still green. Live-verified: real API calls plus a real-browser (Playwright/Chromium) pass through every new/changed screen as `manager1` (Store Manager) — all render with zero console errors. A real pre-existing collision was found and fixed in the process: `ASN` already existed as a bare, never-wired stub doctype in the original `db/migration.sql` (module `Inbound`, fields `asn_number`/`po_number`/`status`/`location`, status vocabulary `Expected,Received,Cancelled`) — 26.5.1 now extends that doctype additively (new `po_id`/`vendor`/`carrier`/`tracking_number`/`expected_date`/`expected_items` fields, reusing the existing identifier/status fields) instead of building a parallel one, per this repo's own reuse-over-duplicate rule.
- [x] **26.5.1** ASN (advance shipment notice) capture before GRN — feeds 26.3.1's GRN workbench. New "ASN" sidebar screen (Buying flyout) plus a "Load Items from ASN" button on the GRN Workbench alongside the existing "Load Items from PO" one. `validateASNRules` (ASN-0271) cross-checks expected-item SKUs against the referenced PO the same way GRN's own PURCHA-0088 does.
- [x] **26.5.2** QC sampling on GRN — accept/reject/damage sub-quantities beyond today's whole-line accept. `grnReceivedLine` gains a genuine third `damaged_qty`/`damage_reason` bucket alongside the existing accepted/rejected split (GOODSR-0096/0097 validate it). The real gap was that accepted/rejected were already *captured* but never *acted on* — `PostGRNReceiptWithQC` now replaces the GRN create hook's direct `PostInventoryLedger` call: accepted qty still flows to `available` exactly as before, rejected/damaged now route to `inventory_availability.qc_hold`/`.damaged` — real ATP buckets the Stage 26.12 foundation added but nothing wrote to until now. GRN Workbench UI splits the old single "Damaged/Rejected Qty" field into separate Rejected/Damaged fields with their own required reasons.
- [x] **26.5.3** Cross-dock/flow-through putaway rule (skip bin placement when a transfer/sale is already waiting). `CheckCrossDockOpportunity` matches an open FulfillmentTask/TransferOrder need at the same location/SKU; `CrossDockPutaway` stages up to that matched qty into a synthetic `XDOCK-<location>` staging bin (still pickable via the existing `GenerateBinPickList`, no real Bin document required) instead of shelving it. New panel on the Putaway screen.
- [x] **26.5.4** LPN/carton/pallet grouping on top of `bin_stock` — additive. New `LPN` master + `bin_stock_lpn` table (a further breakdown of `bin_stock`, same non-source-of-truth precedent bin_stock itself follows). `AssignToLPN`/`GetLPNContents` + new "LPN / Cartons / Pallets" screen.
- [x] **26.5.5** Bin-to-bin replenishment min/max triggers — reuse Stage 10's replenishment-suggestion pattern, scoped to bins instead of vendor reorder. `GetBinReplenishmentSuggestions` implements the design note exactly (shortage = max_qty − current_qty; fills from `bin_type='Reserve'`-tagged bins first, falling back to any other bin with stock if none are tagged, highest-qty-first); `ExecuteBinReplenishment` is the separate action that actually moves it. New `BinReplenishmentRule` master + optional `Bin.bin_type` field + "Bin Replenishment" screen.
- [x] **26.5.6** Wave/batch pick-list grouping — extend Stage 20.18's computed pick list. `GenerateWavePickList` implements the design note exactly: aggregates each SKU's open demand across every task in a wave, allocates FIFO/oldest-stock-first across Good-condition `bin_stock` (by `updated_at`), distributes the allocated total back per task oldest-first, then sorts the consolidated pick lines zone→aisle→rack for one S-shape route. `AssignTasksToWave` tags tasks via an additive `wave_id` JSON key. New "Wave / Batch Picking" screen.
- [x] **26.5.7** Short-pick handling (partial pick → variance/backorder flag) — already fully covered by Stage 26.12.3's `ShortPickLine` (`engines/fulfillment_pickpack.go`: mandatory ReasonCode, blocks pack completion on any unresolved shortfall). This Stage's own addition is wave-level shortfall visibility: `GenerateWavePickList`'s `WaveOrderAllocation.Shortfall` surfaces a shortfall at the aggregate-wave allocation stage too, not just per-task.
- [x] **26.5.8** Cartonization at pack step — extends Stage 20.19's pack/dispatch mapping. `SuggestCartonization` is a deliberately simple qty-capacity first-fit-decreasing packer (new `CartonType` master for capacity) — doctype-agnostic, feeds a new "Pack (Suggested Cartons)" button on the Transfers screen alongside the existing manual per-line-prompt "Pack" button (both call the same existing `PackTransferOrder`).
- [x] **26.5.9** ABC cycle-count planner (auto-schedule high-velocity SKUs more often) on Stage 20.20's cycle-count engine. `GetABCCycleCountPlan` ranks SKUs by Stage 10's `CalculateSalesVelocity` into a classic 20/30/50 A/B/C Pareto split, each tier with its own recount interval and a `Due` flag off the last `Posted` count's age. A zero-velocity SKU lands in C by construction, which is why the checklist's "naive random-bin sampler... as a placeholder if true ABC isn't ready" fallback wasn't built — real velocity-weighted ABC was already ready. Read-only report, same no-auto-created-documents precedent `GetReplenishmentSuggestions` set. New panel on the Cycle Count screen.
- [x] **26.5.10** Blind-count + recount workflow + variance root-cause codes — additive fields on `CycleCountLine`. `RequestRecount` marks the original line `Recount Requested` (excluded from `ReconcileCycleCount`'s reprocessing) and creates a fresh blind `Draft` line (`recount_of` link, no counted/system qty carried over) for `SubmitRecountValue` to fill in. `SetCycleCountVarianceReason` requires an Active `ReasonCode` (new `Cycle Count Variance` category, appended not replaced so it's correct regardless of migration-application order against the concurrent Stage 26.12.3 migration) before `PostCycleCountAdjustment` will post a non-zero variance at all — a real hard gate, not just optional metadata; also closed a latent double-post gap by rejecting a retry against an already-`Posted` line. New panels on the Cycle Count screen (variance reason + retry-post, and blind recount).
- [x] **26.5.11** [P2 — tier/scope decision, don't build speculatively] Slotting/re-slotting optimizer, labor standards/productivity dashboard, RF/voice/mobile picking, 3PL multi-owner billing, robotics/conveyor/scale API integration — each needs a real warehouse-scale pilot to justify the investment. **Go-ahead given 2026-07-27** (user: "start building this") overriding the "not yet" default — scoped into 26.5.12-26.5.16 below as generic, tenant-configurable capability (same treatment the rest of Stage 26 already got, not gated on a specific named pilot customer that doesn't exist).
- [x] **26.5.12** Slotting/re-slotting optimizer — built and verified 2026-07-27. `GetSlottingSuggestions` (`engines/wms_slotting.go`) reuses 26.5.9's own `GetABCCycleCountPlan` for velocity tiering directly (no second tiering pass) and reuses 26.5.5's `bin_type` (PickFace vs Reserve) as the "distance from the pack station" proxy instead of inventing a zone-ordinal metric: a Tier A SKU with no PickFace presence is suggested into the first Active PickFace bin with room; a Tier C SKU occupying PickFace is suggested back out to Reserve. Read-only suggestion, same no-auto-move precedent as every other suggestion engine in this Stage. Registered as report `slotting-suggestions`. Verified: `TestWMSP2/SlottingSuggestions` (real Postgres) + live report-catalog check.
- [x] **26.5.13** Labor standards/productivity dashboard — built and verified 2026-07-27. New `TaskCompletionLog` doctype (plain `documents`-table log, same precedent as `StockLedgerEntry`/`ReportRunLog` — no bespoke table, no clone-list entry needed) written via `logTaskCompletion` from the three existing WMS actions that already carry a `userID`: `PutawayToBin`, `PackTransferOrder`, `PostCycleCountAdjustment` (`engines/wms.go`/`engines/transfer_orders.go`). **Documented gap**: picking isn't instrumented — `ScanPickItem` (`engines/fulfillment_pickpack.go`) has no per-user tracking today and adding it would mean changing that function's signature across every caller, out of scope for this pass. `GetLaborProductivity` aggregates tasks/hour per user per task type; registered as report `labor-productivity`. Verified: `TestWMSP2/LaborProductivityLogging`.
- [x] **26.5.14** Mobile/voice picking — built and verified 2026-07-27. New "Mobile Picking" sidebar screen (`renderMobilePickingView`, `public/app.js`) reuses the existing Wave Picking pick-list endpoint verbatim (no new picking logic) in a single-item-at-a-time card layout with Previous/Confirm&Next buttons, plus optional voice readout (`SpeechSynthesis`) and hands-free "next"/"confirm"/"previous" voice control (`SpeechRecognition`) — both browser-native (no new dependency), both feature-detected so the screen degrades to silent button-tap navigation wherever unsupported. Live-verified: renders with zero console errors as `manager1`.
- [x] **26.5.15** 3PL multi-owner billing — built and verified 2026-07-27. New `StorageBillingRate` master (per-owner per-location storage/handling rates) + additive `Bin.owner_id` field; `GetStorageBillingReport` (`engines/wms_3pl_billing.go`) computes period storage charge (current `bin_stock` snapshot × rate × day-count — a documented approximation, this codebase has no historical bin_stock balance ledger to average over) plus handling charge (`TaskCompletionLog` count at that location × rate — deliberately location-wide not owner-split, since only Putaway's reference is a bin code). Read-only report (`3pl-storage-billing`), no invoice/GL document auto-created. Verified: `TestWMSP2/StorageBillingReport`.
- [x] **26.5.16** Robotics/conveyor/scale inbound integration — built and verified 2026-07-27. New `RoboticsIntegrationCredential` master (API key) + one generic action-tagged inbound endpoint (`POST /api/v1/wms/robotics/event`, `internal/server/handlers_wms_p2.go`), authenticated via a per-tenant shared API key header (same shape as 26.7.11's CleverTap webhook). `putaway`/`pick` actions map straight onto the existing `PutawayToBin`/`ScanPickItem`; `weight_confirm` is a scale-integration audit signal (flags a mismatch, doesn't mutate stock). No vendor-specific SDK, since no specific vendor is contracted. Verified: `TestWMSP2/RoboticsEvents` (key verification, putaway, weight match/mismatch).

**26.5.12-26.5.16 build summary**: `db/migrations_stage26_5_wms_p2.sql` + `db/migrations_stage26_5_16_robotics.sql`; `engines/wms_slotting.go`, `engines/wms_productivity.go`, `engines/wms_3pl_billing.go`, `engines/wms_robotics.go`; `internal/server/handlers_wms_p2.go`; small additive edits to `engines/wms.go`/`engines/transfer_orders.go` (task-completion log hooks) and `internal/server/routes.go`. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean (new `TestWMSP2`, 4 subtests, real Postgres). Live-verified over real HTTP/browser as `manager1`; all live test fixtures removed after.
- **(info, not a build item)** A standalone WMS project (separate Go service + React frontend) was independently started against a "Universal WMS Master Blueprint" PDF before this Stage 26.5 roadmap existed. Retired 2026-07-23 — its architecture conflicted with this repo's no-new-framework/single-server rules, and this Stage already covers nearly its entire feature scope. Durable design content kept at `docs/specs/wms_master_blueprint_reference.md`; retirement rationale at `project_ledger.md`.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.6 — Finance/Tax Close Sprint — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **26.6.1** P&L / Balance Sheet reports — new `ReportDefinition` entries off existing `gl_postings`, same pattern as Trial Balance/Ageing. Built `engines/finance_reports_stage26.go` (`GetProfitAndLoss`/`GetBalanceSheet`, registered as `profit-and-loss`/`balance-sheet`); live-verified against real seeded GL data via `/reports/run/{id}` (Balance Sheet's Cash 9150 cross-checked against Cash Flow's net-change 9150 below).
- [x] **26.6.2** Cash-flow statement report — same framework, off `gl_postings`' Cash/Bank account (1100), bucketed Operating/Investing/Financing by `document_type` (heuristic categorisation, no dedicated activity-type field exists). `BankStatementLine` noted as a narrower alternative source once a tenant actually uploads bank statements. Registered as `cash-flow-statement`; live-verified.
- [x] **26.6.3** GL drill-down + vendor/customer ledger + tax-ledger reports — extends Stage 20.38's drill-down pattern. GL drill-down (`gl-drilldown`) reuses the existing `getGLAccountBook` directly (zero new query code); added `GetCustomerLedgerReport` (mirrors `GetVendorLedgerReport`) as `customer-ledger`; added `GetTaxLedgerReport` over the GST accounts (1500/2200/2201/2202) as `tax-ledger`. Live-verified.
- [x] **26.6.4** Journal voucher + reversal + recurring-journal entry — posts through the existing `PostDoubleEntry`. Built `engines/journal_voucher.go`: new `JournalVoucher` doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_6_finance_tax_close.sql`), gated through the existing maker-checker engine (seeded `approval_rules` row, every amount → HR/Admin) rather than posting on create — `DecideApproval` now calls `postApprovedJournalVoucher` on Approved (same hook shape as the existing ProductContent snapshot). `ReverseJournalVoucher` creates a new Draft voucher with swapped lines, routed through the same Submit/Approve/Post cycle rather than a second unreviewed posting path. `StartRecurringJournalWorker` (hourly ticker) spawns Draft instances from a `Recurring Template` voucher once `next_run_date` arrives. New endpoints `POST /finance/journal-voucher`/`{id}/reverse`/`recurring` (submit/approve reuse the existing generic `/approval/submit|decide`). Verified: new `TestJournalVoucherLifecycle` (validation, full approve→GL-post→reverse cycle, recurring spawn+advance, all 4 frequencies) plus live HTTP end-to-end (create→submit→approve→confirmed `Posted` in `gl_postings`→reverse→confirmed `Reversed`) on a throwaway port; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean; all live test data cleaned up after.
- [x] **26.6.5** Payment-file (bank-file) generation for a payment-proposal batch + duplicate-UTR check — extends Stage 20.27. Built `engines/payment_file.go`: `GeneratePaymentFile` builds a generic NEFT/RTGS-style bulk-upload CSV from an Executed proposal's Paid lines, resolving each invoice's Vendor for beneficiary name/account/IFSC (`bank_account_number`/`bank_ifsc`, pre-existing Vendor fields). A real UTR only exists once the bank has processed the transfer (no real bank API integration exists, same credentials-gated-later precedent as 26.2.x), so `RecordPaymentUTR` records it after the fact into a new `payment_utr_log` table with a `UNIQUE` constraint on `utr` — the duplicate-UTR check is DB-enforced, not just app-checked. New endpoints `GET .../payment-file`, `POST .../record-utr`, `GET .../utrs`. Verified: new `TestPaymentFileAndUTR` (CSV contains resolved bank details, rejects a non-Executed proposal, rejects a vendor with no bank details, duplicate UTR rejected/distinct UTRs accepted) plus live HTTP end-to-end; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
- [x] **26.6.6** Backdated-posting approval — route through the approval engine instead of a blanket period-lock rejection (Stage 17.4/24.6 already validate transaction date; this adds a signed-off override path). New `BackdatedPostingRequest` doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_6_6_backdated_posting.sql`, flat-schema, zero bespoke create/list code). `rejectIfCurrentPeriodClosed` (`engines/accounting_periods.go`) now checks for a matching Approved request before rejecting a closed-period post; `PostDoubleEntry` threads `docType`/`docID` through to it. New `JournalVoucher.RetryPostApprovedJournalVoucher` + `POST /finance/journal-voucher/{id}/retry-post` close the loop for a voucher that was Approved but blocked at posting time. Verified: new `TestBackdatedPostingApproval` (closed period blocks both the JV's auto-post and a direct `PostDoubleEntry` call with a real `FIN-0260` `ValidationError`; an approved `BackdatedPostingRequest` + retry then succeeds and posts to `gl_postings`) plus live HTTP end-to-end (real period create/close via the existing endpoints); `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
- [x] **26.6.7** Statutory audit export (structured full-GL export for a closed period) — reuses the async-export pattern (Stage 20.37): registered `statutory-gl-export` as a normal report, so `CreateReportExportJob`/`StartReportExportWorker` handle async CSV export with zero new job code. Live-verified via `/reports/run/statutory-gl-export`.
- [x] **26.6.8** Intercompany/cost-center/profit-center postings and reports — extends Stage 17.9's Department/CostCenter masters into finance postings. `PostDoubleEntry` (`engines/finance.go`) gained an additive variadic `PostingOptions{CostCenter, Department}` param (all 28 pre-existing call sites unaffected) plus additive nullable `cost_center`/`department` columns on `gl_postings`; `engines/gl_cost_center.go` mirrors `ValidateLocationReference`'s exact validate-against-active-master pattern for both. Scoped to whole-voucher, not per-line (`PostDoubleEntry` aggregates debits/credits per account_code into one row per account, so a per-line dimension isn't representable without restructuring that aggregation) — `JournalVoucher` gained optional `cost_center`/`department` fields, carried through create/reversal/recurring-spawn. New `cost-center-pl` report groups Revenue/Expense `gl_postings` by cost center. Verified: new `TestCostCenterPostings` (reference validation active/inactive/missing, a tagged voucher's `gl_postings` rows carry the cost center and the report reads it back correctly, an unregistered cost center rejects voucher creation) plus live HTTP end-to-end; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
- [x] **26.6.10** `FIN-0260` period-locked posting code — still not wired (Stage 25.9: `PostDoubleEntry` has 28 call sites across 12 files, no single choke point). **Folded into 26.6.6 above (2026-07-25)** rather than done as a separate later pass: 26.6.6 already needed to edit `rejectIfCurrentPeriodClosed` (the confirmed single choke point) to add the backdated-approval override check, so its plain `fmt.Errorf` was swapped for `&ValidationError{Code:"FIN-0260"}` in the same edit — touching that function twice in two different batches would have been pure waste. Propagates to all 28 `PostDoubleEntry` call sites for free; confirmed via `TestBackdatedPostingApproval`'s direct `PostDoubleEntry` assertion (`err.(*ValidationError).Code == "FIN-0260"`) and every pre-existing period-lock test still passing unchanged.

- [x] **26.6.11** Tax-exempt / nil-rated goods cannot be sold at all. **Brought forward 2026-08-06 from archived Stage 30.1.2's deferred follow-up** — it was flagged there but never given a live item, and `engines/master_data_validation.go:87` still points readers at "docs/micro_checklist.md Stage 30", which is now in the archive. The gap is real, not cosmetic: 30.1.2 made `hsn_code` + a **positive** `gst_rate` mandatory on Item, so a 0% item is unsaveable — and checkout has always refused to price one, which is why nothing regressed and why it went unnoticed. But exempt/nil-rated goods are ordinary in Indian retail (unbranded food grain, fresh produce, books, certain apparel below threshold), so any tenant selling them currently cannot create the Item at all. **Built 2026-08-09.** Product decision taken by the user up front: **all four treatments** (Taxable / Exempt / Nil-Rated / Zero-Rated) and **HSN stays mandatory on all four** (it belongs on the invoice whatever the rate is, and GSTR-1's nil/exempt table is itself HSN-wise), so 30.1.2's HSN guarantee is untouched.
  - **`Item.tax_treatment`** — a `Select` doctype field (`db/migrations_stage26_6_11_item_tax_treatment.sql`), **deliberately `mandatory = FALSE`**: blank/absent normalizes to `Taxable`, so every pre-existing Item and every existing CSV import template keeps validating exactly as before. Nothing is relaxed by that default — a bare `0` is still rejected; `0` only becomes valid (and required) once a non-taxable treatment is chosen explicitly, which is the point the item itself insisted on. Slotted at `display_order` 8 between `hsn_code` (7) and `gst_rate` (9, bumped from 8, with the three PIM fields bumped 9-11 → 10-12) so it sits next to the field it governs rather than after "Variant Options"; metadata-only, no document data touched.
  - **`engines/gst.go`** — the four canonical constants, `NormalizeTaxTreatment` (alias-tolerant: `nil rated`/`nil_rated`/`NILRATED` all resolve, but a typo like `Exmept` is **rejected rather than defaulted to Taxable**, which would silently tax exempt goods), and a new `GetItemTaxInfo`/`ItemTaxInfo` as the one place a transaction turns an Item into tax facts. `GetItemGSTInfo` kept as a thin two-value accessor onto it so no call site or test had to change. `GSTBreakdown` gained `ExemptAmount`/`NilRatedAmount`/`ZeroRatedAmount` (`omitempty`, so an all-taxable cart's JSON is byte-identical to before) — deliberately **not** folded into `TaxableAmount`, which is a filed figure on GSTR-3B 3.1(a).
  - **`validateItemMasterRules`** — treatment-aware, and the rate rejection moved from `MASTER-0042` to **`MASTER-0044`** ("Tax category is required for this item"), a catalog row that existed but was never wired precisely because Item had no tax-category field until now. This also fixes a small pre-existing wart: the old code raised an *HSN* headline over a *GST-rate* detail. Both directions are enforced — a Taxable item must have a positive rate, and a non-taxable one may not carry one, which is what makes the treatment trustworthy downstream.
  - **GL/returns bucketing** — the real compliance half. `GetGSTReturnSummary` reads `4100`'s net balance as the period's *taxable* value, correct only because `PostSalesGSTBooking` moves tax out of `4100` and every sale was taxable; an exempt sale moves nothing, so its turnover would have been **reported as taxable** (GSTR-3B 3.1(a) overstated, 3.1(c) understated). New `PostExemptSalesReclass` (`engines/finance.go`, called from `FinalizePOSCheckout` inside the same failure path as the other two postings — misclassified revenue is a filing problem, not a cosmetic one) moves non-taxable turnover into new accounts `4110`/`4111`/`4112`. Three accounts, not one, because the returns will not accept them merged (GSTR-1 gives nil-rated and exempt their own columns; GSTR-3B splits zero-rated 3.1(b) from exempt+nil 3.1(c)). Summary, `ReportDefinition` columns and the Finance report panel all gained the four figures; the panel's extra row hides itself when they are all zero.
  - **Scoping note:** Zero-Rated models the **LUT/bond route only** (supplied without payment of tax). Export-with-payment-then-refund needs a refund-claim workflow that does not exist here and was not invented.
  - Verified: `go build`/`go vet` clean; new `TestExemptGoodsCanBeSold` (8 subtests incl. a mixed cart's per-bucket split, the GL reclass leaving `4100` net −150, the all-taxable no-op, and the **real save path** — `ValidateDocument` then `ValidateMasterDataRules`, the same pair `handlers_core_doc_engine.go` calls — accepting an Exempt item and rejecting a typo'd one) + new `TestNormalizeTaxTreatment` + `TestItemTaxClassificationRequired` extended to 18 subtests; full `go test ./... -p 1` green. Live on a scratch `:8099`: migration applied via `erp-server -migrate`, `/api/v1/doc/Item/meta` confirms the field at order 8 with the right options and `gst_rate` at 9, and `/api/v1/reports/gst-return-summary` returns all four new keys. **Item create could not be exercised over HTTP** — it is HR/Admin-only and `admin` is MFA-mandatory — so that seam is covered by the save-path unit test above rather than by weakening a shared dev account's MFA. `gst_rate` being absent from `Item/meta` for `cashier1` is pre-existing field-permission behaviour (`migrations_stage16_field_permissions.sql`), not a regression.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.7 — CRM/Loyalty Sprint
- [x] **26.7.1** RFM (recency/frequency/monetary) segmentation — a report over existing `SalesInvoice`/`POSCart`/loyalty-ledger data, no new customer data model needed. Built `engines/crm_reports_stage26.go` (`GetRFMSegmentation`, rank-based quintile scoring off POSCart's `customer_id`, standard 5-segment labels); registered as `rfm-segmentation`. Live-verified (correctly returns "no data" on this fresh dev DB, which has no Paid POSCart carrying a customer_id yet).
- [x] **26.7.2** Voucher/coupon issuance + redemption — new doctype, reuses the loyalty ledger's earn/burn pattern (Stage 13.13d). Built `engines/voucher.go`: `Voucher` is a flat-schema Master doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_7_crm_loyalty.sql`) — create/list/edit/bulk-CSV-issuance come free from the existing generic doctype/`BulkImportCSV` machinery, zero bespoke code for those. `RedeemVoucher` is the one bespoke action: row-locked (`SELECT...FOR UPDATE`, same pattern as `DecideApproval`) so two concurrent redemptions of a `max_uses=1` voucher can't both succeed; validates expiry/max-uses/customer-restriction and computes Percentage/Flat discount capped at the cart amount. New endpoints `POST /crm/voucher/validate`(preview)/`redeem` — deliberately standalone, not deep POS-checkout integration, mirroring the existing `handleRedeemLoyaltyPoints`'s own shallow-integration shape. Verified: new `TestVoucherRedemption` (discount math both types, expiry, customer-restriction, max-uses exhaustion) plus live HTTP create→validate→redeem; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
- [x] **26.7.3** Loyalty tiering + accrual/expiry rules — additive fields on the existing loyalty ledger. Built `engines/loyalty_tiering.go`: `loyalty_tier_rules` self-service config table (same pattern as `approval_rules`, seeded Bronze/Silver/Gold/Platinum thresholds/multipliers); additive `Customer.loyalty_tier` field, recomputed (`RecomputeLoyaltyTier`) after every earn off lifetime Paid-POSCart spend; `EarnLoyaltyPoints` (`engines/loyalty.go`) now scales base points by the customer's tier multiplier and stamps the earned lot with an `expires_at` (additive column on `loyalty_point_ledger`, `loyaltyPointExpiryDays=365`). Expiry uses a documented approximation, not full FIFO lot-consumption (this ledger has no per-lot remaining-balance tracking to consume against): `StartLoyaltyExpiryWorker` (hourly) expires `min(newly-lapsed, current balance)` per customer via a normal `Burn` row tagged `reference_doctype='LoyaltyExpiry'` — deliberately *not* a new `transaction_type` value, so every existing balance query (`GetLoyaltyBalance`, loyalty-summary/points-liability/RFM reports) already treats it correctly with zero changes to any of them. Verified: new `TestLoyaltyTieringAndExpiry` (tier-rule CRUD, tier selection + earn-multiplier application end-to-end, expiry sweep capped-at-balance + idempotent-on-repeat-tick) plus live HTTP; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
- [x] **26.7.4** Campaign definition (birthday/lapsed-customer triggers) + communication log — reuses the existing CleverTap outbound integration (Stage 9.1) for delivery. Built `engines/campaign.go`: `Campaign` is a flat-schema Master doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_7_4_campaign.sql`); `StartCampaignWorker` (hourly ticker) calls the already-built-but-previously-uncalled `LogCustomerEventToCleverTap` for every customer newly matching an Active campaign's trigger — Birthday (additive `Customer.date_of_birth` field, month-day match ignoring year) or Lapsed Customer (no Paid POSCart in `lapsed_days`). Idempotent per customer per day via a check against the existing log. Communication log is the existing `clevertap_event_log`/`ListCleverTapEventLogs`, extended with an additive `campaign_id` column rather than a new log table. **Real pre-existing gap found and fixed**: `clevertap_event_log`/`clevertap_credentials` (Stage 9.1, `migration.sql` Section 36) had never actually been created in this dev DB — that section was added to the base schema file after this DB was first provisioned, and Campaign is the first feature to actually exercise `LogCleverTapEvent` against a real DB; fixed with a small catch-up migration (`db/migrations_stage26_7_4b_clevertap_tables_catchup.sql`, identical `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` definition). Verified: new `TestCampaignTriggersAndROI` (birthday match + idempotent-per-day, lapsed-customer match, a recently-active customer correctly not matching) plus live HTTP end-to-end; `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean.
- [x] **26.7.5** Fraud/staff-restriction rules + OTP redemption on loyalty burn. Built `engines/loyalty_redemption_security.go` as an opt-in front door alongside the existing immediate `/loyalty/redeem` (unchanged, still behaves exactly as before): (a) fraud/velocity — a hard daily cap (`maxLoyaltyRedemptionsPerCustomerPerDay=5`) on customer-initiated `Burn` rows; (b) staff-restriction — a new `LoyaltyRedemptionRequest` doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_7_5_fraud_otp.sql`) gated through the existing approval engine (seeded `approval_rules` row, 500+ rupee value → Store Manager; `extractAmount` gained `points_value`), with a `DecideApproval` hook (`executeApprovedLoyaltyRedemption`) that actually burns the points on Approved; (c) OTP — `InitiateSecureLoyaltyRedemption` generates a 6-digit code (hash-only stored, `loyalty_redemption_otp_challenges` table, 5-min expiry) and dispatches it via Stage 26.12.10's `DispatchNotification` (event `"Loyalty Redemption OTP"`, added to `NotificationTemplate.event`'s option list) — no new SMS/WhatsApp provider dependency, confirmed by a real local webhook receiver actually receiving the rendered code. New endpoints `POST /crm/loyalty-redemption/initiate`/`verify`. Verified: new `TestLoyaltyRedemptionSecurity` (insufficient balance, velocity cap, wrong-OTP-doesn't-burn-the-challenge, below/at-threshold routing, expired challenge) plus live HTTP end-to-end both paths (small redemption redeemed immediately after a real webhook-delivered OTP; large redemption routed to `PendingApproval`, approved by a second real user, confirmed `Redeemed` with balance reduced); `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean; all live fixtures (docs, ledger rows, challenges, webhook receiver) removed after.
