### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.10 — Reports and BI Sprint — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **26.10.1** **Stock ledger wiring** (PDF Appendix A item 7) — built and live-verified 2026-07-26. `WriteStockLedgerEntry` (`engines/inventory.go`) is now a struct-taking API (`StockLedgerEntry{ItemID, WarehouseID, Qty, VoucherType, VoucherID, IdempotencyKey, FromLocationID, ToLocationID, FromStatus, ToStatus, UserID, DeviceID}`) with the additive fields the checklist called for, plus idempotency dedupe (a second write with the same `IdempotencyKey` is a no-op — verified in `TestReportsStage26_10/StockLedgerWiredThroughPostInventoryLedger`). `PostInventoryLedger` keeps its original signature unchanged for every existing caller (manufacturing.go/manufacturing_mrp.go included — deliberately not touched, that Stage was mid-build concurrently) via a new sibling `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucher` that both share; callers with real voucher context (`wms_receiving.go`'s GRN accept path, `pos_checkout.go`'s checkout) were switched to the voucher-aware form. All six named flows are wired: **GRN** (accepted qty via `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucher`, plus direct entries for the rejected/damaged QC-split in `wms_receiving.go` with `ToStatus` set), **checkout** (`pos_checkout.go`, voucher=cart, user=cashier read from `documents.created_by`), **transfer dispatch/receive** (`transfer_orders.go`, one signed entry per line each side with `From/ToLocationID` set to the other warehouse), **putaway** (`wms.go`'s `PutawayToBin` + `wms_putaway_ext.go`'s `ExecuteBinReplenishment`, both Qty-0 pure bin moves since on_hand/available don't change), **condition transitions** (`wms.go`'s `TransitionBinStockCondition`, `From/ToStatus` = Good/Damaged/QC-Hold/RTV, Qty = whatever left/entered the Good bucket), and **cycle-count posting** (`wms.go`'s `PostCycleCountAdjustment`, now takes a `userID` param threaded from both its callers). **Known gap, deliberately not covered this pass**: `returns.go`'s `applyReturnedStockToBucket` (Returns QC disposition) and `fulfillment.go`'s ship-from-store dispatch/`ProcessReturnAnywhere` (walk-in return) also mutate `inventory_availability` directly but weren't in the checklist's own explicit six-flow list and weren't wired — a real gap for a future pass. The **Stock Ledger report** (`stock-ledger` in `engines/reports_stage26_10.go`) is registered in the existing `ReportDefinition` catalog with SKU/location/voucher_type/date-range filters and a `RunningBalance` column (cumulative, only meaningful when both SKU and location narrow to one card). Migration: `db/migrations_stage26_10_1_stock_ledger.sql` (additive `doctype_fields`, widened `voucher_type` Select options). **Verified**: `TestReportsStage26_10` (`go test ./... -p 1` green) plus live HTTP verification via a scratch server — the Report Catalog's Stock Ledger tab and the new Dashboard tab's exception cards both confirmed rendering real data over real login (`manager1`/Store Manager), screenshotted via Playwright.
- [x] **26.10.2** Attendance Summary report — deferred at Stage 20.40 for "no data model"; built 2026-07-26. **DONE.** New `attendance-summary` `ReportDefinition` (`engines/reports_stage26_10.go`, category "HR") — `GetAttendanceSummaryReport` tallies each employee's `Attendance` records by status (Present/Absent/Late/Leave/Holiday/WeeklyOff) within an optional date range, left-joined against `Employee` for name/department, with an `attendance_pct` (present ÷ expected-work-days, excluding Holiday/WeeklyOff) computed per row. No new data model needed after all — `Attendance` (Stage 13.13a) already carried everything this needs; 26.8.1's `Shift`/`ShiftAssignment` doctypes don't change what's counted, they're unrelated to why this was pickable now. No frontend work needed — the generic Report Catalog (`GET /api/v1/reports/catalog`) surfaces any registered report automatically. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean; live-verified via Playwright (Report Catalog → Attendance Summary selectable, correct 10-column table incl. From/To date params, "No rows" confirmed correct against `tenant_default`'s actual 0 Attendance records).
- [x] **26.10.3** Role-based executive dashboards (KPI cards + trend charts) — built and live-verified 2026-07-26. A new "Dashboard" tab (first/default tab in the existing Reports view, `public/app.js`'s `renderExecDashboard`) is a frontend-only layer over the `ReportDefinition` catalog exactly as scoped — no new backend endpoint, it just calls the existing `/api/v1/reports/run/{id}` for `exception-stale-approvals`/`exception-failed-syncs`/`exception-negative-stock`/`sla-breach`/`sales-register` and renders 4 KPI cards + a 7-day trend chart (plain inline-SVG bars, no charting library). Role/column masking (Stage 20.39) applies for free through the shared endpoint; the sales trend specifically detects a masked (Sensitive) `sale_total` via the response's `REPORT-0287` code and falls back to an order-count trend rather than summing a redacted `"•••"` string. Each KPI card is clickable and jumps straight into the Report Catalog tab pre-selected to that report (reuses the catalog's own `reportCatalogSelectedId` state) — live-verified via Playwright screenshot (Stale Approvals card → Report Catalog → real 3-row result).
- [x] **26.10.4** Scheduled report delivery (cron-style run + email/webhook drop) — built and tested 2026-07-26. New `ScheduledReport` doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_10_4_scheduled_reports.sql`) is a plain registered master — create/list/delete already work through the generic doc API (same precedent as `ReportFilterPreset`/`ReportExportJob`), no bespoke handler needed. `engines/scheduled_reports.go`'s `StartScheduledReportWorker` (registered in `routes.go` at 1h granularity, same shape as the other daily-granularity workers) polls Active schedules whose `next_run_date` has arrived, runs the report via the existing `RunReport`/`reportRowsToCSV` (Stage 20.37), and "delivers" by publishing a `report.scheduled_delivery` event through the existing outbox (`engines/outbox.go` — a `processOutbox` case logs the simulated email/webhook drop, matching how every other outbound integration in this codebase is simulated rather than making a real SMTP/HTTP call, deliberately avoiding an SSRF surface against arbitrary user-supplied webhook URLs). `advanceScheduleDate` jumps a missed schedule straight to the next future occurrence rather than re-delivering every missed period. **Verified**: `TestReportsStage26_10/ScheduledReportWorker` (backdated schedule → one delivery + correct advance; a second tick before the new `next_run_date` is confirmed a no-op).
- [x] **26.10.5** Exception queues (stale approvals, failed syncs, negative-stock flags) as a dashboard widget — built and live-verified 2026-07-26. Three new `ReportDefinition` entries, category "Exceptions" (`engines/reports_stage26_10.go`): `exception-stale-approvals` (`GetStaleApprovals` — cross-doctype `documents.status = 'Pending Approval'` scan past a threshold, reusing Stage 17.10's GetSLABreaches shape), `exception-failed-syncs` (`GetFailedSyncs` — `integration_event_outbox` rows with `status = 'Failed'`), and `exception-negative-stock` (`GetNegativeStockFlags` — wraps the existing `POSOfflineSyncVariance` doctype, Stage 20.13, filtered to `status = 'Open'`). These are also exactly what 26.10.3's dashboard cards call for their counts, satisfying "as a dashboard widget" directly rather than as a separate mechanism. **Verified**: `TestReportsStage26_10/ExceptionQueues` plus live HTTP/UI confirmation (screenshotted, showing real counts: 3 stale approvals, 0 failed syncs, 1 negative-stock flag).
- [x] **26.10.7** Report query-load instrumentation — built and verified 2026-07-27. New `ReportRunLog` doctype (plain `documents`-table log, same "no bespoke table" precedent `StockLedgerEntry` set) written from `RunReport`'s own single choke point (`engines/report_registry.go`) — every report execution logs report id/duration/row count/user, excluding the performance report's own runs from self-logging. `GetReportPerformance` aggregates avg/max duration + call volume per report; registered as report `report-performance`, so 26.10.6's own "once real load is measured" condition has something real to check against instead of staying permanently unevaluable. **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed along the way**: `advanceScheduleDate` (`engines/scheduled_reports.go`, Stage 26.10.4) compared a parsed date's UTC-midnight `time.Time` against `time.Now()`'s local time-of-day, which could read a genuinely-future date as "not yet past today" depending on the server's timezone offset — fixed to compare formatted calendar-date strings instead, caught by `TestReportsStage26_10/ScheduledReportWorker`.

**26.10.7 build summary**: `db/migrations_stage26_10_7_report_perf.sql`; additive edits to `engines/report_registry.go` (instrumentation call + `userID` param on `RunReport`, threaded through its 3 call sites), `engines/reports_stage26_10.go` (`writeReportRunLog`/`GetReportPerformance`/report registration), `engines/scheduled_reports.go` (the date-comparison fix above), `internal/server/handlers_report_engine.go`. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean (extended `TestReportsStage26_10` with a new `ReportPerformanceInstrumentation` subtest). Live-verified over real HTTP; all live test fixtures removed after.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.11 — Security, Scale, UAT and Go-Live — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **26.11.2** Load/soak/spike test at 1/100/1000/2000-store scale — re-run 2026-07-26 against a dedicated throwaway tenant (never the shared `default` tenant — `RunScaleSimulation` unconditionally wipes the tenant's entire `gl_postings` table with no scoping, which would have destroyed real dev data). **Found and fixed a real, serious bug in the process**: the first run failed ~60% of transactions with `relation "...accounting_periods" does not exist" — `ProvisionTenantSchema`'s (`engines/saas.go`) table-clone list had drifted badly out of sync with `tenant_default`'s actual schema (confirmed via `pg_tables`: 41 tables exist, only 23 were ever cloned into a new tenant). Fixed by adding the 18 missing tables to the clone list (`accounting_periods`, `approval_log`/`approval_rules`, `bin_stock`/`bin_stock_lpn`, `channel_credentials`, `clevertap_credentials`/`clevertap_event_log`, `loyalty_point_ledger`/`loyalty_redemption_otp_challenges`/`loyalty_tier_rules`, `payment_utr_log`, `pim_publish_log`/`pim_publish_queue`, `pos_offline_heartbeats`, `product_content_versions`, `sticker_print_log`, `tenant_limits`) plus `approval_rules`/`loyalty_tier_rules` to the data-seed list (both carry default template data, unlike the rest which are correctly-empty-for-a-new-tenant transactional data or per-tenant secrets that must never be cloned). This means **every tenant provisioned since whichever migration first added each of those tables has been silently missing GL posting, the entire maker-checker approval engine, WMS bin tracking, marketplace/CRM integrations, Loyalty, and more** — a production-blocking gap this load test happened to surface, not something specific to load/concurrency itself. After the fix: all 4 tiers (1/100/1000/2000 stores, 100 workers, 2000 transactions each) completed with 0 failures, TPS 381–466 (in line with the original Phase 5 baseline of ~456 TPS @ 100 workers), p95 latency 620ms–1020ms, p99 1.25s–1.5s; `wait_duration_ms` (24.34's new `db_pool` health stat) used directly as the queue-lag/DB-utilization figure, ranging 253s–915s cumulative pool-wait across the run. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` clean. Throwaway tenant + scratch server + scratch `cmd/` tooling all removed after.
- [x] **26.11.3** DR/restore drill in a real production-like environment — **run 2026-08-07 against the production droplet (139.59.17.16) and PASSED.** Checksum verified, `pg_restore` clean into scratch db `erp_restore_drill_20260807030948`, and all three sampled tables matched live exactly (`documents` 210/210, `users` 4/4, `gl_postings` 0/0). Scratch database dropped by the script's own `trap`, staging directory removed, production confirmed healthy afterwards (`systemctl is-active erp` → active, `/api/v1/health` → 200). The live database was never written to. Full detail in `docs/operations/restore_drill_log.md`.
  - **The drill could not have been run as documented — three blockers, all of which would otherwise have been discovered mid-incident.** (1) `restore_drill.sh` **was never deployed**: `/opt/erp/deploy/` held only `backup.sh` and `migrate.sh`, `install_backup_cron.sh` was missing, and the deployed `backup.sh` was a stale 1,527-byte copy against the repo's current 4,541 — `deploy.ps1` ships the binary and `public/`, not the `deploy/` scripts. (2) **The script's documented `sudo -u erp` invocation always fails**: production's `erp` role has `rolcreatedb=false`/`rolsuper=false` (correct least-privilege) and the script derived its admin connection from `DATABASE_URL`, so it died on `permission denied to create database`. Rather than granting the app role CREATEDB — weakening the running system to test a backup — `deploy/restore_drill.sh` now takes an optional **`DRILL_ADMIN_URL`** override for the create/drop/restore connection, falling back to the old behaviour where the app role does hold CREATEDB; its two `sed` patterns also changed `[^/]+`→`[^/]*` so socket-style URIs rewrite correctly. (3) **The superuser cannot read the backup directory** (`/opt/erp` is `drwxr-x--- erp:erp`), so the run needed the newest backup staged into `/var/lib/postgresql/drill_stage` with `BACKUP_DIR` pointed at it — and the `.sha256` sidecar's **absolute** path rewritten to a bare filename, since `sha256sum -c` resolves the path recorded inside the file and otherwise fails a perfectly good staged copy with a misleading "corrupted". The staging dance is a workaround; the proper fix (run as `root`, shell out to `sudo -u postgres psql` for admin ops) is noted as a follow-up in the drill log.
  - **⚠️ Separate and more serious finding: nightly backups were not running on production.** `/opt/erp/backups/` held exactly two files (2026-08-03 owned by `erp`, 2026-08-05 owned by `root`) that looked like manual one-offs taken around deploys. `crontab -l` was empty, `/etc/cron.d/` carried only `e2scrub_all` and `sysstat`, and there was no systemd timer — **`install_backup_cron.sh` had never been run on this droplet.** A backup that restores is only useful if a recent one exists, and `hypercare_plan.md`'s "two consecutive failed nightly backups" rollback trigger cannot fire when there is no nightly backup to fail. **RESOLVED 2026-08-07 — see 26.11.7.**
- [x] **26.11.7** Install and verify the nightly backup cron on production (user sign-off given 2026-08-07 on 26.11.3's finding). **Done — `0 2 * * *` as user `erp`, 14-day on-box retention, logging to `/var/log/erp-backup.log`.** Shipping the scripts was a prerequisite: `/opt/erp/deploy/` was missing `install_backup_cron.sh` entirely and carried a stale 1,527-byte `backup.sh` (repo: 4,541) whose retention step was also quietly wrong — `find "$BACKUP_DIR" -name '*.enc' -mtime +14 -delete`, unscoped, would have aged out on-demand tenant exports along with the nightly dumps. Both copied up; old `backup.sh` preserved on-box as `backup.sh.pre20260807.bak`.
  - **Found 2 real bugs in `install_backup_cron.sh`, both of which would have failed silently at 02:00 every night.** (1) **The cron line could never have worked as written** — the line documented in `backup.sh`'s header comment since day one used `source /etc/erp/erp.env && ... backup.sh`, but that file holds bare `KEY=value` lines with **no `export`**, and must (systemd loads the same file via `EnvironmentFile=`, which rejects an `export ` prefix). So `source` set `DATABASE_URL` as a *shell* variable, `backup.sh` ran as a **child process** and never inherited it, dying on `set DATABASE_URL (source /etc/erp/erp.env)`. Fixed by loading the env with `set -a; . file; set +a` — exports everything sourced without touching the file systemd depends on. This is the whole justification for the script's step-5 immediate run: had the header comment been pasted into a crontab by hand, as the documented procedure said, the job would have failed every night into a log nobody reads. (2) **The installer failed under its own documented `sudo` invocation** — run as root from `/root` (0700), the step-5 verification drops to `erp`, which cannot traverse that cwd, so `pg_dump` warned `could not change directory to "/root"` and the retention `find` exited non-zero, failing the run under `set -e` **even though the dump succeeded**. Cron is unaffected (jobs start in the user's home), so this was purely an install-path artifact — fixed with `cd /` at the top.
  - **Verified that cron itself can run it, not just that the line exists** — a distinct claim, since `erp`'s shell is `/usr/sbin/nologin` and cron's `PATH` is only `/usr/bin:/bin` versus the wider `sudo` environment the manual run inherits. A temporary `* * * * *` entry pointed at a scratch `BACKUP_DIR` (real backup set left untouched) fired on its own — `CRON[131799]: (erp) CMD (...)` in syslog — and produced a valid backup; temp entry and scratch dir then removed. Newest backup verified end-to-end: `sha256sum -c` → OK, and decrypt-with-`BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` piped to `pg_restore --list` yields a valid archive (`dbname: custom_erp`, 286 TOC entries), which is the only check that proves the key on the box actually decrypts what the box writes. Resting state: `/opt/erp/backups` 700 `erp:erp`, log 640 `erp:erp`, `erp`/`caddy` active, `/api/v1/health` 200; nothing restarted, no app config touched.
  - ~~**Still open: nothing alerts if the nightly backup fails.**~~ **Built 2026-08-12 — see 43.2.** The job appended to `/var/log/erp-backup.log` and that was all, so hypercare's "two consecutive failed nightly backups" trigger depended on a human looking — and an *empty* log is a distinct failure from an *error* in it (a successful run appends one line per night, so no new line means the job never ran). Both halves are now wired: `backup.sh` POSTs its own failure to `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL`, and `StartBackupFreshnessMonitor` alerts when no recent backup *exists* — the case this very item discovered, which a failing job can never self-report. Still gated on 20.2 supplying the webhook URL before anything is actually delivered. Off-box copies remain deliberately absent (no storage credentials): losing the droplet still loses the backups.
- [x] **26.11.4** Migration rehearsal (apply every migration file in order against a fresh DB, not the accumulated dev DB) — run 2026-07-26 against a genuinely fresh scratch database (not `custom_erp`), all 51 `db/*.sql` files in filename-sorted order (matching `promote.ps1`'s own `Invoke-PendingMigrations` logic exactly). **Found and fixed 3 real bugs**, confirming this check's own stated value ("would have caught the Stage 11 gap even earlier"): (1) `migrations_stage24_addendum_data_integrity.sql` referenced a `version` column not added until `migrations_stage24_security.sql`, which sorts after it alphabetically — fixed by dropping `version` from the explicit INSERT column list (the column's own `NOT NULL DEFAULT 1` backfills it correctly regardless of which file actually runs first). (2) `migrations_stage26_6_8_cost_center_postings.sql` inserted a `doctype_fields` row for `JournalVoucher` before `migrations_stage26_6_finance_tax_close.sql` (the file that actually registers `JournalVoucher` in `doctype_meta`) had run — same digits-sort-before-letters ordering trap; fixed with an idempotent `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` bootstrap insert so whichever file runs first wins, order-independent. (3) `migrations_stores_master_fields.sql` assumed a `'Stores'` `doctype_meta` row already existed (true on the accumulated dev DB, inserted by hand outside migration discipline at some point) — genuinely missing from every tracked migration file, so a from-scratch DB had no `Stores` doctype at all; fixed by adding the missing INSERT (values read back from the live dev DB) with the same idempotent guard. After all 3 fixes: all 51 files applied cleanly on a fresh DB, and the Go server booted successfully against it (`/api/v1/health` returned 200). Scratch database dropped after.

### Stage 26 — Final Leg Maturity Completion Plan (external PDF, 2026-07-23) — Phase 26.12 — OMS/Order Management Maturity Sprint
Sourced from two PDFs (`Inhouse_OMS_Master_Blueprint.pdf`, `Inhouse_OMS_Module_BRD_Pack.pdf`), reconciled against live code in `docs/specs/oms_master_blueprint_reference.md`. Unlike WMS (Stage 26.5, which already existed before its blueprint arrived), this repo had no Order-Management phase at all before this pass — see the reference doc's per-section gap table for the file:line detail behind every item below.

**Effort plan (2026-07-24, no code — planning only, all 10 items below still `[ ]`)**: sized in "sessions" (one focused, live-verified build pass — this repo's own historical unit, e.g. Stage 20 Track B.2's WMS Maturity landed in one day). **Total ≈ 7.5-9.5 sessions**, realistically **1-1.5 weeks** at this repo's demonstrated cadence (Stage 13's much larger scope shipped in about a week) — not one sitting, not a quarter. Per-item size (S/M/L) is on each item below. Recommended build order, dependency-aware rather than numbering order: **26.12.9 + 26.12.6 (foundational, no dependencies) → 26.12.1 (Order Engine — everything else hangs off this) → 26.12.2 → 26.12.3 → 26.12.5 → 26.12.4 (courier — build the internal AWB/manifest engine before any real courier API, same "code-complete, credentials pending" pattern as 26.2.1-26.2.5) → 26.12.7 + 26.12.8 last (reports/dashboards read off data the earlier items produce)**. Biggest swing factors: 26.12.1's doctype design decision, and how much of 26.12.4 is real-courier-API vs. internal-only.

**Build started 2026-07-24, closed out 2026-07-25**: all 10 items are done — see their own entries below for detail. 26.12.5 (Returns/RTO/QC/Refund), 26.12.7 (exception/reconciliation dashboard), 26.12.8 (OMS reports), and 26.12.10 (notification templates, its P2 scope decision resolved with the user to real generic webhook dispatch) were built in a follow-up pass on 2026-07-25, concurrently with a separate session's 26.12.4/Stage 26.5 work — deliberately touching disjoint files (`engines/returns.go`, `engines/notifications.go`, `engines/oms_reports.go`, `internal/server/handlers_returns.go` are new files; `engines/orders.go`/`internal/server/routes.go`/`engines/engines_test.go` got additive-only edits) to avoid collision.
- [x] **26.12.1** Order Engine — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. Design decision resolved with the user: a new `SalesOrder`/`SalesOrderLine` doctype pair (`db/migrations_stage26_12_1_order_engine.sql`, both `Transaction` doctypes, module_key `oms`), not an extension of `POSCart` — POSCart's one-status-field-per-document shape can't carry independent per-line status, which split-shipment support genuinely needs. New `engines/orders.go`: `CreateSalesOrder` runs the fixed SKU-mapping → address → payment validate chain from the design note (§12), reserving stock via the existing `CreateReservation`/`FindBestFulfillmentNode` when clean, or placing the order **On Hold** with a routable `hold_reason` (`SKU_MAPPING_FAILED`/`ADDR_INVALID`/`PAYMENT_PENDING`) when not — the same "never hard-reject, let a human/queue decide" precedent `FindBestFulfillmentNode`'s own OMNI-0247 fallback already uses, applied one layer earlier. Idempotent on `(channel, channel_order_id)` replay. **Hold engine**: `PlaceOrderHold` (manual CS-initiated hold) and `ReleaseOrderHold` (re-runs the *exact same* validate chain per the design note rather than a bespoke resume path — reserves and clears the hold if now clean, or updates `hold_reason` and stays On Hold if not). **Stage-gated cancellation matrix**: `CancelOrder` checks Stage 26.12.9's new `StatusTransitionRule` doctype first (entity=Order, to_status=Cancelled) so an admin can configure the matrix without a code change, falling back to the blueprint's own hardcoded blocklist (Shipped/Delivered/Closed/Cancelled) when no rule is configured — enforced from day one either way. Both hold and cancel require a mandatory, category-matched `ReasonCode` (26.12.9). New endpoints `POST /api/v1/orders`, `.../{id}/hold`, `.../{id}/release-hold`, `.../{id}/cancel` (`internal/server/handlers_orders.go`, `moduleGate("oms",...)`) — a dedicated entry point, not the generic doc engine, since creating an order atomically validates/sources/reserves/writes N lines, which a single-document generic create can't do. Deliberately **out of scope**: rewiring existing `ImportChannelOrder`/Shopify/BigCommerce/Unicommerce webhooks onto `SalesOrder` (they still create `POSCart` as before) — a separate follow-up, not part of this item. No dedicated frontend screen yet either — reasonable to build once 26.12.2-26.12.5 add enough for a real OMS order-management UI to show together, rather than a screen for creation alone. **Real gap found+fixed during live verification**: the release-hold endpoint originally returned a blanket `{"status":"released"}` even when the order stayed On Hold (a still-failing validate chain isn't an error) — fixed with a new `engines.GetOrderStatus` so the response reports the actual resulting `order_status`/`hold_reason`. **Verified**: extended `TestEngines` with a new `OrderEngine` subtest (happy-path reserve, idempotent replay, address/payment hold paths, hold-release after a fix, mandatory/category-checked reason codes, cancellation releasing reservations, the default blocklist blocking a Shipped cancellation, and a `StatusTransitionRule` override explicitly allowing it) — `go test ./... -p 1` green. Live-verified over real HTTP against the dev DB: full lifecycle (create → hold → release → cancel) through the real endpoints, `moduleGate("oms",...)` confirmed blocking when the module is disabled, all test data cleaned up after.
- [x] **26.12.2** Allocation/Sourcing — built and live-verified 2026-07-25. New `engines.ResolveAllocationPlan` (`engines/sourcing.go`) runs every Active `AllocationRule` (26.12.9) scoped to a channel (or the global blank-channel rules) in ascending-priority order, trying each rule's strategy until one produces a usable plan — the first to succeed wins. Five strategies beyond the pre-existing default: **Highest ATS** (the old `FindBestFulfillmentNode` logic, re-expressed over a new shared `qualifyingLocations` scan so all single-location strategies share one implementation instead of repeating it), **Nearest Pincode** (`abs(orderPincode−locationPincode)`, per the design note's own validated proxy shape — needed a new additive `pincode` field on the Location master, `db/migrations_stage26_12_2_allocation_sourcing.sql`, since none existed), **Lowest Workload** (fewest open `FulfillmentTask` rows, status not Dispatched/Rejected), **Oldest Stock** (earliest `inventory_availability.updated_at` among a location's relevant SKU rows — a documented proxy, same "proxy not a real port" caveat the design note gives Nearest Pincode, since no per-batch received-date exists), and **Split Shipment** (the only strategy allowed to spread lines across more than one location — each line sourced independently by its own highest-ATS location; every other strategy requires one location to cover every line or produces no plan at all, so split-shipment only ever happens when a rule explicitly authorizes it). A **Manual** strategy always fails to produce a plan and stops the search immediately (routes to a human, not "skip me") — configure it as the lowest-priority catch-all. Falls back to the exact pre-26.12.2 Highest-ATS/HO-fallback behavior when a tenant has configured zero `AllocationRule` rows at all (same "usable before any admin config" precedent as `isCancellationBlocked`); once at least one rule *is* configured, exhausting every rule without a plan is the **allocation-exception** case — `CreateSalesOrder`/`ReleaseOrderHold` (`engines/orders.go`) hold the order with a new `HoldAllocationFailed` (`ALLOCATION_FAILED`) code instead of erroring, mirroring `validateOrderChain`'s existing "return a routable code, don't reject" contract. No new table for the "exception queue" the checklist item calls for — the resulting On-Hold `SalesOrder` rows filtered by `hold_reason` *are* the queue, same "reuse the doctype list view" precedent 26.12.1's hold routing and 26.12.7's planned dashboard both use. `CreateSalesOrder`/`ReleaseOrderHold` were also changed to reserve **per line** (`plan.LineLocations[i]`) instead of one shared location for the whole order — the schema already supported this (`SalesOrderLine.location_code` is per-line), it just wasn't being used; `ReleaseOrderHold` now also correctly scopes re-allocation to only the lines still `Pending` rather than re-sourcing already-Reserved/Dispatched lines too (a small pre-existing correctness gap fixed as a side effect of the per-line rework, not separately scoped). Reallocation-on-reject (`fulfillment.go`'s `TransitionTaskStatus`, the design note's other §12 callout) was deliberately left untouched — its existing single DB transaction already gives it the "rollback to the old reservation on failure" property the design note asks for (a release-then-reserve sequence inside one `tx.Commit()`/`defer tx.Rollback()` boundary already rolls back both halves atomically on any failure), so there was no real gap to close there. **Verified**: new `TestEngines/AllocationSourcing` subtest — all 5 non-default strategies exercised directly via `ResolveAllocationPlan` (Nearest Pincode, Lowest Workload, Oldest Stock, and a combined Split-Shipment-plus-priority-fallthrough case where a higher-priority Highest-ATS rule correctly fails first), plus a full `CreateSalesOrder` HTTP-shaped call proving a Manual rule holds a genuinely-in-stock order with `ALLOCATION_FAILED` — `go test ./... -p 1` green (whole suite, no new failures). Live-verified over real HTTP against the dev DB (throwaway port 8237, scratch HR/Admin token minted via a repo-internal `cmd/` program, deleted after): seeded two real `Location` docs with different pincodes, created a `SalesOrder` through the real `POST /api/v1/orders` endpoint, confirmed it reserved at the nearer location and `inventory_availability.reserved` moved correctly; then seeded a channel-scoped Manual rule and confirmed a second real order on that channel went On Hold with `ALLOCATION_FAILED` despite the SKU being fully in stock. All fixtures (including one auto-created PIM profile side effect of creating a test Item) removed after. `go build`/`go vet` clean.
- [x] **26.12.3** Fulfillment Pick/Pack — built and live-verified 2026-07-25. New `engines/fulfillment_pickpack.go` (deliberately a separate file from `fulfillment.go`'s existing `CreateFulfillmentTasks`/`TransitionTaskStatus`, which are untouched — additive, not a rewrite) layers distinct scan-validated Pick and Pack stages on top of a `FulfillmentTask`'s existing `items` array via three new per-line fields (`picked_qty`/`packed_qty`/`short_qty`, additive JSON keys — no schema/table change). `ScanPickItem`/`ScanPackItem` resolve a scanned barcode (falling back to SKU/code on a miss, same degrade-gracefully convention as the 26.3.1 GRN barcode preview) and match it to the *first* task line for that SKU still short of its target — a scan for a product not on the task, or a line already fully picked/packed, is rejected naming the actual product involved (§12), not a bare "invalid scan". The blueprint's hard rule that packed quantity can never exceed picked quantity (§6) falls directly out of `ScanPackItem`'s own match condition (`packed_qty < picked_qty`) rather than being separately enforced. `ShortPickLine` marks a line's entire remaining shortfall as short in one action (§12: "no partial-short-then-more-picking modeled"), requiring a mandatory, category-matched `ReasonCode` — reused the exact Stage 26.12.1 Hold/Cancel convention, extending `ReasonCode.category`'s Select options with a new "Short Pick" value (`db/migrations_stage26_12_3_pick_pack.sql`, additive) rather than a bespoke reason field. `CompletePackTask` is the explicit, blockable completion action (§12: "any exact-qty mismatch, not just a shortfall") — hard-blocks unless *every* line has both fully resolved its pick (picked_qty+short_qty == qty) and packed exactly what was picked (packed_qty == picked_qty), only then moving the task to `Packed`. Task status otherwise stays within the FulfillmentTask doctype's own pre-existing Select vocabulary (`Pending,Picking,Packed,Dispatched,Rejected`, `db/migration.sql` — already anticipated exactly this granularity, so no status-list migration was needed): first pick or short-pick action flips `Pending`→`Picking`, and both the pick and pack stages live under `Picking` until `CompletePackTask` explicitly sets `Packed`. **Deliberately cross-checked `engines/wms.go` first per the design note's own warning, and deliberately layered alongside it rather than merging**: `GenerateBinPickList` answers "which bin do I walk to for this SKU" (a `bin_stock`-backed, warehouse-internal concern that doesn't apply to every FulfillmentTask location); this item answers "did the picker/packer actually gather and box the right units" against the task's own line items — orthogonal concerns, forcing them together would have been exactly the kind of duplication the design note flags. **Deliberately out of scope**: auto-generating an invoice from a completed pack task (the retired prototype did this, §12 "worth preserving" note) — this repo's Order Engine has no Order→Shipment→Invoice wiring yet for `SalesOrder` to generate one from; revisit once 26.12.4/26.12.5 add that chain. New endpoints (`internal/server/handlers_wms.go`, `routes.go`): `POST /api/v1/wms/pick-scan`, `.../pack-scan`, `.../short-pick`, `.../pack-complete`, same `moduleGate("wms",...)` and role-open convention as the sibling putaway/pick-list/condition-transition floor-ops routes. **Verified**: new `TestEngines/PickPack` subtest — unknown-scan rejection, not-part-of-this-task rejection naming the real product, partial pick, both distinct `CompletePackTask` block reasons exercised separately (unresolved pick shortfall vs. packed≠picked), short-pick's mandatory-reason-code enforcement, duplicate-scan rejection after a line is fully picked+short-picked, packing structurally capped at picked_qty, and a scan rejected once the task is Packed — `go test ./... -p 1` green, whole suite, no new failures. Live-verified over real HTTP against the dev DB (throwaway port 8241): a real seeded task's full pick→(blocked early completion)→short-pick→pack→complete lifecycle through the real endpoints, confirmed the final document's `picked_qty`/`short_qty`/`packed_qty`/`status` all landed exactly as expected. All fixtures removed after. `go build`/`go vet` clean.
- [x] **26.12.4** Courier/Shipment/Manifest — built and live-verified 2026-07-25. Extended `CreateLogisticsBooking` (`engines/marketplace.go`) from a bare manual carrier/tracking/charge record (hardcoded `status="Shipped"` at creation, before anything had actually shipped) into a real Shipment engine: **serviceability check** — a new `CourierServiceArea` master (Active rows with a `pincode_prefix`/blank-means-everywhere/`priority`, config-table stand-in for a "Courier Provider connector") backs `CheckCourierServiceability`, which a blank `carrier` auto-selects the top-priority match from, and an explicit `carrier` is validated against unless zero rows are configured yet (same "usable before any admin config" precedent `isCancellationBlocked`/`ResolveAllocationPlan` already use). **AWB assignment** — a locally-generated placeholder AWB number (real courier API deliberately out of scope, per this item's own "internal engine first, credentials-gated API later" note, same pattern as 26.2.1-26.2.5), status now starts at `AWB Assigned`. **Manifest grouping** — new `Manifest` doctype; `GenerateManifest(courier, location)` groups every `AWB Assigned` booking whose linked `FulfillmentTask` (`fulfillment_task_id`, additive optional field) is at that location. **Handover cascade** (design note §12's concrete split-shipment-aware closure rule) — `HandoverManifest` flips every grouped booking to `Handed Over`, dispatches its `FulfillmentTask` (reusing `TransitionTaskStatus` rather than duplicating its stock-deduction logic — the CLAUDE.md choke-point principle), then evaluates each affected `SalesOrder`: `Shipped` only once *every* `FulfillmentTask` under that order code has reached `Dispatched`, otherwise `Partially Fulfilled`. A booking's `order_id` only drives this cascade when a `SalesOrder` with that code actually exists — a manual/legacy booking (or one from the still-`POSCart`-based channel-webhook path 26.12.1 left unrewired) is simply handed over with no order-status side effect. **Tracking sync + RTO** — `RecordDeliveryEvent` (Handed Over→In-Transit→Delivered) and `RecordRTO` (courier-reported non-delivery; stock/refund handling stays 26.12.5's scope, this only captures the event+reason). **Label** — `GenerateShippingLabel` returns plain text, the same "prints text, not a real scannable symbology/PDF" scope limitation `engines/stickers.go` already documents. New endpoints under the pre-existing `moduleGate("oms", featureGate("oms_integration",...))` pair: `GET .../serviceability`, `POST .../manifest`, `.../manifest/handover`, `.../tracking`, `.../rto`, `GET .../label`. Wired `WMSLOG-0138`("AWB generation failed")/`WMSLOG-0139`("delivery status update failed") — reserved in the error catalog since Stage 25 with no call to attach them to until now. Frontend: extended the existing Marketplace screen's Logistics Bookings panel (Destination Pincode/AWB/Manifest columns, Carrier now optional/auto) and added a new Manifests panel (generate + Hand Over, via `showCustomConfirm`/`showCustomPrompt`, never a native dialog) — no new screen needed, this is the same Stage 13.7 screen. **Verified**: new `TestEngines/ShipmentManifestEngine` subtest — serviceability priority ordering + prefix fallback, auto-select and explicit-carrier-rejected paths, a full two-location split-shipment order (Partially Fulfilled after the first manifest's handover, Shipped after the second's), re-handover-refused, tracking progression + a premature-Delivered rejection, RTO + a Delivered-booking RTO rejection, label content — `go test ./... -p 1` green, whole suite. Live-verified over real HTTP (throwaway port 8253) and a real-browser Playwright pass: seeded real `CourierServiceArea` rows, booked a real shipment through the actual UI form (auto-selected courier, AWB rendered in-table), opened the label modal, generated a manifest and clicked "Hand Over" through the real confirm dialog, then independently confirmed via a manually-linked `SalesOrder`+`FulfillmentTask` pair that the handover cascade correctly deducted stock and flipped the order to `Shipped` over real HTTP. All fixtures and scratch tooling removed after. `go build`/`go vet` clean.
- [x] **26.12.5** Returns/RTO/QC/Refund — built and live-verified 2026-07-25. New `engines/returns.go` (deliberately separate from `fulfillment.go`'s `ProcessReturnAnywhere`, left untouched — that stays the POS in-store walk-in-return path; this is the OMS/e-commerce path) adds new `ReturnRequest`/`RefundRequest` Transaction doctypes (`db/migrations_stage26_12_5_returns_rto_qc_refund.sql`, module_key `oms`) and a request/approval-gated state machine: `CreateReturnRequest` (Requested) → `ApproveReturnRequest`/`RejectReturnRequest` (mandatory 'Return'-category `ReasonCode`, 26.12.9) → `ReceiveReturnRequest` (Received) → `ApplyReturnQC` (per-SKU disposition → QC Complete/Closed). Two `request_type`s share one workflow: **Customer Return** (reuses `resolveOriginalSale`'s SALESR-0129/0130/0131 window/qty checks plus a new `sumPriorReturnRequests` pool so a customer can't over-return by mixing the instant POS path and this one against the same original order) and **RTO** (requires the linked `LogisticsBooking`, 26.12.4, to already be `RecordRTO`'d, and is idempotent on `booking_id`). **QC disposition buckets** (Sellable/Damaged/Repairable/Missing/Wrong-Item/Rejected) — an explicit documented decision (design note §12's "decide explicitly, don't inherit silently"): Sellable/Damaged/Repairable all physically receive stock (into `available`/`damaged`/`qc_hold` respectively, 26.12.6's buckets) and refund in full; Missing/Wrong-Item/Rejected receive nothing and refund nothing — **per-line**, not "one QC-failed line holds the whole refund" like the retired prototype. Refund amount is always resolved from the **original order line's price** (POSCart's own stored `sale_price` for a Customer Return, `SalesOrderLine.unit_price` for an RTO), never from any return-time input — the create/QC APIs don't even accept a price field. **Refund distinct from the immediate GL post**: the inventory/COGS-side reversal posts in `ApplyReturnQC` when stock is actually received; the revenue-side reversal (debit Sales Revenue/credit Cash-Bank) only posts in a separate `ProcessRefundRequest`, gated behind its own `RefundRequest` Pending→Approved→Processed (or Rejected) lifecycle. New endpoints (`internal/server/handlers_returns.go`, `routes.go`, `moduleGate("oms",...)`): `POST /api/v1/returns`, `.../{id}/approve`, `.../reject`, `.../receive`, `.../qc`, `POST /api/v1/refunds/{id}/approve`, `.../reject`, `.../process`. **Verified**: new `TestEngines/ReturnsRTOQCRefund` subtest — happy-path Sellable refund/inventory/GL, reject-doesn't-count-against-the-pool, SALESR-0130 over-quantity, Missing (zero refund, immediate close) and Damaged (full refund, `damaged` not `available` bucket) dispositions, the full RTO path (booking-not-RTO-yet rejected, price auto-resolved from `SalesOrderLine`, idempotent replay) — `go test ./... -p 1` green, whole suite. Live-verified over real HTTP (throwaway port 8253): full Customer Return lifecycle end-to-end (1200 refund = 3 × original 400 price, inventory landed correctly, GL posted), full RTO lifecycle (order → booking → RTO → return request with auto-resolved `original_order_id`/price → QC → refund), all fixtures cleaned up after.
- [x] **26.12.6** Inventory buckets — built and live-verified 2026-07-24. Added `blocked`/`qc_hold`/`damaged`/`channel_buffer` (additive, default 0) to `tenant_default.inventory_availability` (`db/migrations_stage26_12_foundation.sql`), completing the blueprint's 7-term ATP formula. The 3-term formula (`available - reserved - safety_stock`) was independently duplicated in three places — `GetAvailableToSell` (`engines/inventory.go:255`), `CreateReservation`'s admission check (`engines/inventory.go:193`), and `FindBestFulfillmentNode`'s sourcing comparison (`engines/sourcing.go:11`) — extracted into one shared `computeATS` helper so all three read the new buckets identically and can't drift out of sync (the CLAUDE.md choke-point principle: this was a correctness requirement, not just dedup, since leaving the reservation gate on the old formula while the read-only report used the new one would let a reservation over-admit past what the report itself would call available). No new write path/endpoint added — the columns are additive infrastructure for 26.12.4/26.12.5 to populate; this item's own scope was schema + formula only. **Verified**: extended the existing `TestEngines/OmnichannelAndWMS` subtest to set all 4 buckets on a test SKU and confirm both `GetAvailableToSell` and `FindBestFulfillmentNode` reflect the reduced ATS; `go test ./... -p 1` green. Live-verified over real HTTP against the dev DB: seeded a SKU with on_hand=100 and all 4 buckets=5, confirmed `GET /api/v1/availability` returns `ats:80`, confirmed `POST /api/v1/reserve` correctly rejects a qty-85 reservation ("ATS: 80, requested: 85") and accepts qty-80. Test data cleaned up after.
- [x] **26.12.7** Exception/reconciliation dashboard — built and live-verified 2026-07-25. Rather than a new dashboard-widget mechanism, both pieces are registered as ordinary `ReportDefinition` catalog entries (`engines/oms_reports.go`), reusing the Stage 20 Track B.4 "register a function, not a new endpoint" framework — reachable via the existing `GET /api/v1/reports/catalog`/`run/{id}` with no new routes needed. **`oms-exception-queue`**: reads `integration_event_outbox`/`integration_event_log` (`engines/outbox.go`) directly for `Failed` events or `Pending` ones stuck past a 30-minute staleness window — no new retry/DLQ mechanism, matching the checklist item's own "just OMS-scoped visibility" scope (retry itself already exists via `RetryIntegrationEvent`). **`oms-reconciliation-variance`**: flags a `Shipped`/`Delivered` `SalesOrder` whose linked `LogisticsBooking`(s) (26.12.4) status doesn't actually support that claim yet (or has none at all) — a real "OMS vs courier" check over this repo's own stored data, not a synthetic channel feed. **Verified**: `TestEngines/ReturnsRTOQCRefund` exercises both report queries directly (a seeded `Failed` outbox event; a `SalesOrder` forced to `Shipped` with only an `RTO` booking) — `go test ./... -p 1` green. Live-verified over real HTTP: confirmed both ids appear in the live `/api/v1/reports/catalog` under category `OMS`.
- [x] **26.12.8** OMS reports — built and live-verified 2026-07-25. Seven new `ReportDefinition` entries (`engines/oms_reports.go`), same catalog framework as 26.12.7, category `OMS`. **Order Aging**/**Return Aging** reuse the exact `PayablesAgeingBucket` shape and 0-30/31-60/61-90/90+ bucket boundaries as the existing Payables/Receivables Ageing reports (Stage 20.33), with a matched drill-down; Return Aging deliberately omits an amount column (a return's refund total isn't known until QC completes). **SLA Breach** wraps the pre-existing `GetSLABreaches` (Stage 17.10, `engines/optimization.go`) as a catalog entry with a `threshold_minutes` param, rather than a new SLA engine, per the checklist's own "reuses Stage 17.10's SLA-breach monitor pattern" instruction. **Allocation Pending** lists `SalesOrder` rows On Hold with 26.12.2's `HoldAllocationFailed` code — the same "the hold-reason-filtered list view IS the queue" precedent 26.12.2's own build note established. **Stock Mismatch** flags every SKU/location where 26.12.6's shared `computeATS` formula is negative (over-committed past what's physically available) — a real correctness signal, not a Stock Ledger reconciliation (26.10.1's ledger wiring isn't built yet). **Reserved Stock** is a direct `inventory_availability` read for every `reserved > 0` row. **Courier Performance** aggregates `LogisticsBooking` (26.12.4) by carrier: shipment volume, delivered/RTO counts and rate, and an average Handed-Over-to-Delivered turnaround proxied from each booking's own `created_at`/`updated_at` (no separate per-status-change timestamp history exists yet). **Verified**: `TestEngines/ReturnsRTOQCRefund` exercises all 7 (a backdated `FulfillmentTask` for SLA Breach, a seeded On-Hold order for Allocation Pending, an artificially over-reserved SKU for Stock Mismatch, the RTO fixture's own `LogisticsBooking` for Courier Performance, etc.) — `go test ./... -p 1` green. One real environment quirk found+worked around during test-writing: this dev Postgres session's naive `timestamp without time zone` columns round-trip through lib/pq several hours off from Go's `time.Now()` (a pre-existing characteristic of every `created_at`-based age calculation in this codebase, not introduced here) — the SLA Breach test fixture backdates by 25 hours rather than a small margin so the assertion isn't swallowed by that skew. Live-verified over real HTTP: `courier-performance` and `return-aging` both confirmed returning correct real data against live fixtures (a real RTO showed up at 100% RTO rate for its carrier).

Role permissions follow the Location/LegalEntity precedent exactly: HR/Admin full CRUD, Store Manager read-only. **Verified**: `go build`/`go vet ./...` clean. Live-verified over real HTTP against the dev DB via a scratch HR/Admin token — created one record of each doctype through the generic `/api/v1/sequence` + `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}` flow (the same two-step the real UI's dynamic-modal form uses for any Master doctype's auto-generated code field), read all three back correctly, and confirmed a Store Manager token could read but got a 403 on create. Test records cleaned up after.

New `NotificationTemplate`/`NotificationChannelConfig` Master doctypes and a `NotificationLog` Transaction doctype (`db/migrations_stage26_12_10_notifications.sql`, module_key `oms`, no new routes — the generic doc-CRUD endpoints cover config same as `AllocationRule`/`ReasonCode`). New `engines/notifications.go`: `DispatchNotification(tenantID, event, orderID, extra)` resolves every Active template matching `event`, renders `{{placeholder}}` substitutions (deliberately flat string substitution, not `text/template`, so an admin-authored template string can't execute template actions), and fire-and-forget POSTs the rendered payload to that channel's configured webhook URL — mirroring `alerting.go`'s `SendOpsAlert` pattern (a real provider send happens on the other end of that webhook, e.g. Zapier/Make; this process never holds/forwards raw customer PII beyond order/event identifiers, same minimal-payload precedent `alerting.go` documents). An unconfigured channel or missing template never blocks the caller — both write a `NotificationLog` row (`Skipped-NoTemplate`/`Skipped-NoConfig`) so the gap stays visible. Wired into `engines/orders.go` (`CreateSalesOrder` → Order Placed/Order On Hold, `CancelOrder` → Order Cancelled) and `engines/returns.go` (Return Requested/Approved/Rejected, RTO Detected, Refund Processed). **Deliberately not wired** into `engines/marketplace.go`'s `HandoverManifest`/`RecordDeliveryEvent` (Order Shipped/Delivered) in this pass — that file was being actively edited by the concurrent 26.12.4 session; wiring those two trigger points is a small, safe follow-up. **Verified**: `TestEngines/ReturnsRTOQCRefund` covers both the `Skipped-NoTemplate` and `Skipped-NoConfig` log-only paths deterministically (fire-and-forget webhook delivery itself isn't asserted in the automated suite, to avoid goroutine-timing flakiness) — `go test ./... -p 1` green. **Live-verified over real HTTP** end-to-end: a real local webhook receiver actually received a correctly-rendered POST (`{"event":"Return Approved","channel":"Email","body":"Hi, your return request RR-... for order SO-... has been approved.",...}`) when a live `ApproveReturnRequest` call fired, through a real `NotificationChannelConfig`/`NotificationTemplate` pair created via the generic doc API — not just log-checked, actually received over the wire.

### Stage 31 — Silent printing (QZ Tray) 🚧 (opened 2026-08-03 from the user's marketplace-OMS print request; 31.1 built, live printer verification outstanding) — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **31.1.1 The signing contract, verified from QZ's source — closed 2026-08-03.** A wrongly-signed call is rejected *silently* (QZ falls back to prompting per label), so this had to be established as fact, not inferred. `qz-tray.js` hashes the request and passes the **hash** to the backend, which makes both obvious backend implementations wrong. Settled from source: `qz/auth/Certificate.java:363` → `verifier.update(getBytesUtf8(sha256Hex(data)))` with `SHA512withRSA`, over `new JSONObject(message, {"call","params","timestamp"}).toString()` (`PrintSocketClient.java:218`). So: sign the **UTF-8 bytes of the lowercase hex digest**, key order `call,params,timestamp`. Also confirmed there is **no 2.1-vs-2.2 fork** to handle — the tray does its own hashing from the raw JSON, so both client generations agree. Ports `[8181,8282,8383,8484]` secure / `[8182,8283,8384,8485]` insecure; handshake is unsigned `getVersion` → certificate → signed calls.
- [x] **31.1.2 `engines/qz_print.go` — key material and signing — closed 2026-08-03.** RSA-2048 self-signed cert, `QZ_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`/`QZ_CERTIFICATE_PATH` else auto-generated once into the OS per-user config dir — the same shape `channel_credentials.go` already uses, except it never `log.Fatalf`s: printing is optional and must not take the server down. **The sign endpoint is not a blind oracle** — it accepts exactly one SHA-256 hex digest (`^[0-9a-f]{64}$`) and nothing else, so it cannot be talked into signing an attacker-chosen document, which QZ's own reference backends allow.
- [x] **31.1.3 `Printer` Master extended rather than a new registry — closed 2026-08-03.** Six fields added (`qz_printer_name`, `print_role`, `printer_language`, `label_width_mm`, `label_height_mm`, `dpi`) via `db/migrations_stage31_1_qz_print.sql`, so the whole config screen comes free from the generic Master machinery. `print_role` is what makes it one-click — the server resolves role → printer so no operator picks one per job. Migration also creates `print_job_log` and **catches up existing tenant schemas** with a `DO` block, the failure mode Stage 30.2.2 existed to clean up. Routes share the existing `stickers` module key deliberately; a new key would 403 every existing tenant (the SAAS-0191 trap from 30.7).
- [x] **31.1.4 `public/qz-print.js` — vanilla client, no dependency — closed 2026-08-03.** ~330 lines vs the official ~150KB (which carries a promise shim, its own SHA-256 and remote-relay logic unused here). **A pure-JS SHA-256 fallback is mandatory, not defensive**: `crypto.subtle` is absent outside a secure context, and a packing bench on `http://192.168.x.x` is exactly that. Verified against Node's `crypto` — 8 vectors + 300 random-length fuzz cases, specifically across the 55/56/64-byte padding boundaries.
- [x] **31.1.5 Payloads: raw ZPL/TSPL/ESC-POS or PDF/HTML per printer — closed 2026-08-03.** `engines/qz_payload.go`. **Marketplace PDFs pass through byte-for-byte** — re-rendering a courier's label risks altering a barcode they scan — with 4x6 sizing applied as driver options instead. ERP-native shipping labels *are* generated and gained a real Code 128 AWB symbol on thermal, which `engines/marketplace.go`'s `GenerateShippingLabel` explicitly could not do ("prints text, not a real scannable symbology/PDF"). Stickers route through the existing `engines.PrintStickers`, so SKU validation, the DEVICE-0298 check and `sticker_print_log` are unchanged.
- [x] **31.1.6 UI + graceful degradation — closed 2026-08-03.** Print Setup panel on Sticker Printing: connection status, **Detect Printers** (the OS names must match verbatim — `ZDesigner ZD220-203dpi ZPL`, not "Zebra ZD220"), Test Print, and a file picker that prints any marketplace label/invoice. If QZ isn't running, `qzTryPrint` returns false and every caller falls back to the existing `@media print` sheet; non-thermal sticker printers take the same route via the server's `fallback: "browser"` reply. Nobody is blocked because the bridge is down.
- [x] **31.1.7 Rollout tooling + operator guide — closed 2026-08-03.** `cmd/qzcert` exports `override.crt` (public cert only; the private key never leaves the server) and prints the per-OS install paths. `docs/guides/QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md` covers Java/QZ install, the certificate, **Chrome's Local Network Access gate** (the step Myntra's own mail spends most of its length on, and the usual cause of "the print button does nothing"), printer-language selection, and a troubleshooting table keyed to the actual error strings.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check` clean. 7 new Go tests green — signature validates under the reproduced `Certificate.java` algorithm; **signing the raw digest bytes is asserted to fail** (the most likely future regression); non-hash input refused; deterministic signing; ZPL escaping; payload shapes. Client then run end-to-end against a **mock QZ Tray** implementing that verification with the real generated key: 15 assertions over handshake order, message shape, the exact signed string and key order, print params, plus a tampered-signature negative control.
- [x] **31.1.9 Wire one-click print into the remaining screens — closed 2026-08-05.** All three landed: **Print Label** on the logistics-booking row, **POS receipt via ESC-POS** at checkout, and **Print** on the Sales Invoice row. Two new job types (`Receipt`, `Invoice`) with server-side payload builders, plus the frontend wiring and its fallbacks. `print_role` already offered both names (the Stage 31.1 migration seeded `Shipping Label,Invoice,Sticker,Receipt,General`), so **no migration was needed** — a Printer record just needs `Default For` set.
  - **Both new documents are re-read server-side from their stored document, not taken from the browser.** `BuildReceiptPayload` reads the `POSCart`, `BuildInvoicePayload` the `SalesInvoice`. That is what makes a reprint honest — a receipt printed an hour later shows what was actually rung up — and it means a tampered client cannot print a receipt for a bill that never existed. `fetchPrintableDocument` is a deliberately separate small reader rather than a call into `PostSalesInvoice`/`FinalizePOSCheckout`: those sit inside write paths (`FOR UPDATE`, "only a Draft may be posted") that a print has no business running.
  - **Only a `Paid` cart prints a receipt** (`GLOBAL-0019`, 422). A Pending Approval cart is money not yet collected and a Failed one is a sale that did not happen; a printed receipt is what a customer walks out with, so this refuses rather than printing with a caveat. An **invoice prints at any status** on purpose — a Draft invoice is a legitimate proforma — but is stamped `DRAFT` so it cannot be mistaken for a posted one.
  - **`isESCPOS` is deliberately narrower than the existing `isRawLanguage`.** ZPL and TSPL are label languages with no concept of a continuous feed, so a receipt emitted as raw commands to a ZPL unit comes out blank. Only ESC-POS gets commands; every other language (PDF/HTML/ZPL/unset) gets rasterisable HTML through the OS driver. Do not "simplify" this back to `isRawLanguage`.
  - **`escposColumns` defaults an unset `label_width_mm` to 42 columns (80mm roll).** A 58mm till needs `label_width_mm = 58` on its Printer record or lines are laid out too wide. Every line is clipped to the column count and `escposRow` clips the *description*, never the amount — a receipt is checked on its numbers.
  - **Real bug found and fixed in the existing browser receipt**: `printPOSReceipt` never deducted `offer_discount`. Checkout returns `amount_due = sale_total - loyalty_discount - offer_discount`, so any sale with a Stage 30.7 offer applied printed a total **higher than the cash actually collected**. Now takes a 7th defaulted `offerDiscount` argument and prints Subtotal / Offer discount / Loyalty lines. The stored `applied_offers` are also named on the ESC-POS receipt, which is what the comment on `handlers_pim_pos_finance.go`'s `storedPayload["applied_offers"]` asked for in the first place.
  - **Second real bug fixed in the same area**: `#receipt-print-area` had **no on-screen `display: none`** — only the `@media print` block ever hid it — so after a POS receipt printed, its markup stayed rendered at the bottom of the live page until the next navigation. `styles.css` now hides all three print areas on screen (`#sticker-print-area` always did; the receipt one never has).
  - **`qzTryPrint` gained a `quiet` option, and the three new call sites use it.** "No Printer record is Default For Receipt" is the *normal* state of a tenant that has not set QZ up — without `quiet` a cashier would get a `DEVICE-0298` modal on every single sale before the browser sheet appeared. Quiet suppresses the payload-preparation dialog and the unmapped-printer dialog (both `console.debug`'d instead) and falls through. A failure at the tray itself stays loud in every case: that one means printing was really attempted and really failed.
  - **Print Label falls back to the on-screen label modal, not to `window.print()`** — the plain-text label is not a printable sheet, and someone whose QZ Tray is down still needs to read the AWB off the screen to write the docket. The read-only **Label** button is unchanged and still sits next to it.
  - **Verified.** `go build` / `go vet` / `node --check` clean; **`go test ./... -p 1` fully green** (including the finance-9500 test the notes warn can false-fail). **5 new Go tests** (`engines/qz_receipt_invoice_test.go`) covering column arithmetic, the amount-survives-clipping rule, ESC-POS-vs-label-language selection, the Paid-only guard, coded not-found, offer-discount arithmetic and the DRAFT stamp. Then driven **live over HTTP** on a scratch :8261 as `manager1`: all three job types role-resolved to the right printer and produced correct payloads (receipt 500.00 subtotal → 450.00 due after a 50 offer; shipping label emitted a real `^BCN` Code 128 of the AWB; invoice marked DRAFT), plus every error path (unknown job type, missing `document_ref`, missing document, unpaid cart). Finally **18/18 Playwright checks** against the real `index.html`+`app.js` with QZ absent, so every assertion exercised the fallback.
  - **Traps for whoever touches this next.** (a) The **Stage 31.1 migration was still unapplied on this dev DB** — `-migrate-status` showed it as the 1 pending item; it applied cleanly, so the nested `$f$` dollar-quoting risk 31.1.8 flags is now cleared locally as well as on prod. (b) **`Store Manager` has no read grant on `LogisticsBooking`**, so the Marketplace screen's bookings table is *empty* for `manager1` and a UI test cannot find a row there — assert on `renderLogisticsBookingActions` directly (`admin` can see it but is the MFA-mandatory account). (c) `psql` on this machine needs **flag-style args** (`-h 127.0.0.1 -p 5435 -U postgres -d custom_erp -f file.sql`); passing a `postgres://` URI as the first argument makes it silently ignore `-c`/`-f` and run nothing while still exiting 0. (d) `documents.created_by` is **FK'd to `users`** — seed with `'system'`, not an arbitrary string. (e) Both verification harnesses were scratch tooling and were not kept.
  - **Known parity point, deliberately not closed**: neither receipt rendering prints a GST breakdown, though `gst_breakdown` is stored on the cart. The browser receipt has never shown it and adding it to only the ESC-POS side would make the two disagree. A GST-compliant receipt layout is its own piece of work, not part of wiring the print path. [needs design decision if a compliant tax receipt is wanted]
