### Stage 34 — Market intelligence / competitor pricing — 34.1-34.3 — buildable now, zero new dependencies

- [x] **34.1** `CompetitorPrice` doctype + CSV ingestion — built 2026-08-07. `db/migrations_stage34_1_competitor_price.sql`: 12 fields (`code`, `our_item` Link→Item, `competitor_product`, `competitor_sku`, `platform` Select, `competitor_price`, `mrp`, `rating`, `review_count`, `observed_at` Date, `source_url`, `status`), `role_permissions` for HR/Admin (full) and Store Manager (read/create/update), and the standard backfill loop over already-provisioned tenant schemas. Zero Go code and zero JavaScript: registered as **Master** (not Transaction) deliberately, because the Setup flyout and the generic doctype table both filter on `document_type = 'Master'` — Master is what gets the generic list, the New form and the **Bulk Import** button for free, and a Transaction doctype would have been unreachable from the UI, which is exactly the trap `CycleCountLine` had to be worked around (`app.js:6505`). `our_item` is deliberately **not** mandatory so an unmatched competitor listing is still recordable. Validation is entirely `ValidateDocument`'s metadata-driven path as planned — live-verified that it rejects a missing mandatory field, an out-of-list `platform`, and a dangling `our_item` Link.
  - **Found and fixed a real bug affecting 89 doctypes, not just this one.** `GenerateCSVTemplate` (`import.go:377`) deliberately omits `id`/`status` from the header row it emits, and `ValidateDocument` (`doctype.go:314`) skips the mandatory check for those same two system fields — but `missingMandatoryColumns` (`import.go:137`) did neither, so **any** doctype declaring a mandatory `status` field rejected its own generated template with `DATAIM-0164 "missing mandatory column(s): status"`. That is 89 doctypes including Brand, Color, Customer, Vendor, Location, Employee and `CycleCountLine`, whose bulk import is the only way it is meant to be filled at all. Fixed at the shared choke point (skip `id`/`status`, matching the other two functions) rather than by making `CompetitorPrice.status` optional, so every affected doctype is repaired at once. Found by literally following the documented download-template-then-upload path instead of hand-writing a CSV that happened to include `status`.
- [x] **34.2** Price-gap report via `RegisterReport` — built 2026-08-07 as `competitor-price-gap` (Category "Sales") in `engines/market_intelligence.go`. Picks the **cheapest** current observation per SKU (best for the shopper, worst for us) and columns out our price, best competitor price, platform, gap in ₹ and %, above/at/below, observation count and source URL, with optional platform / SKU / observed-since / position filters. Live-verified over HTTP end to end: CSV import → catalog listing → run → correct cheapest-wins pick → correct gap math (599 vs 499 → ₹100, 20.04%, "Above") → platform filter changing which competitor row wins.
  - **⚠️ The item's own premise was wrong and the implementation departs from it deliberately.** 34.2 was written as "joins `CompetitorPrice` to our `Item`/price list". **There is no price list, and `Item` has no price field of any kind** — verified against `doctype_fields` (`code`/`name`/`barcode`/`weight`/`volume`/`category`/`hsn_code`/`gst_rate`/`family`/`parent_product_code`/`variant_option_values`), and the POS cashier types the sale price by hand per line (`app.js` pushes `salePrice: 0` and renders an editable input). So "our price" is resolved at report time as the **last price we actually transacted at**, newest wins across `SalesOrderLine.unit_price` and `POSCart items[].sale_price`. This needs no schema change and invents no pricing architecture — price lists per channel/customer-group with effective dates is a real feature and a real product decision, not something to slip in under a competitor-pricing item. Cost: a SKU never sold has no our-price, and reports `Position = "No price on file"` with null gap columns rather than a fabricated zero that would rank top of the gap list. **If a real price master is ever wanted, that is a new item; `competitorOurPriceCTE` is the single place to repoint.** (Also: `POSCart.items` is stored as a real JSON array by some writers and a JSON-encoded *string* by others — both shapes exist in the dev DB and both are normalised.)
- [x] **34.3** Undercut alert worker — built 2026-08-07 as `StartCompetitorUndercutWorker(ctx, 1h)` (`engines/market_intelligence.go`, started in `routes.go` alongside the other ten workers). Fires the **existing** `DispatchNotification` path with a new `Competitor Undercut` event added to `NotificationTemplate.event`'s Select options, so an admin authors the alert body in the existing Setup UI rather than the event being a magic string only Go knows. Threshold is the new `market.undercut_threshold_pct` setting, **default 0 = disabled**, so a tenant that never loads competitor prices is never alerted about them; bounded at 90 because "a competitor is 95% cheaper" is a data-entry error, not a pricing signal. De-duplicated through `public.competitor_undercut_state.last_run_at` (same single-row shape as `patch_intake_state`), so one observation alerts at most once no matter how often the worker ticks or how long the undercut persists — without that it would re-alert on the same standing undercut every hour. Reads only; never changes a price, document or configuration. `db/migrations_stage34_3_undercut_alert.sql`.

**34.1-34.3 build summary**: 2 migrations (`migrations_stage34_1_competitor_price.sql`, `migrations_stage34_3_undercut_alert.sql`), 1 new engine file (`engines/market_intelligence.go`), 1 new test file (`engines/market_intelligence_test.go`, 7 subtests), plus additive edits to `engines/import.go` (the 89-doctype choke-point fix), `engines/settings_definitions.go` (1 setting) and `internal/server/routes.go` (1 worker start). Zero new dependencies, zero frontend code, `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` all clean. Live-verified over real HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8099) as `manager1`/Store Manager; all live fixtures removed afterwards. **Note for deployment: production runs PostgreSQL 14.23 while dev runs 16.3** — the report query deliberately avoids PG16-only `pg_input_is_valid` for exactly this reason.

### Stages 35-39 — Parity Master Plan — Stage 38 — Platform & extensibility — ≈8-10 sessions — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **38.1** Public API v1 — a curated, versioned subset with a compatibility policy, not all 257 routes — **contract written 2026-08-11, first routes opened 2026-08-12 (read-only).**
  - Four endpoints, each naming its required scope explicitly: `GET /items`, `GET /items/{code}` (`items:read`), `GET /inventory?sku=` (`inventory:read`), `GET /orders/{id}/status` (`orders:read`). `publicAPIMiddleware` **panics at registration** for a route that declares no scope, so "public by accident" is not a reachable state.
  - **Every response is a curated projection, not a document dump.** The internal `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}` returns every stored field; publishing that shape would turn every future internal field into an accidental public contract. `engines/public_api_v1.go` defines the four response types — no cost, margin or supplier data on a product; availability without the held-back buckets behind it; order tracking without pricing or PII the caller did not already supply. Availability is computed through the **same `computeATS`** every internal caller uses, so a public read can never disagree with what the order path will accept. Order lookup accepts our order id *or* the caller's own channel order id, since a marketplace connector holds the latter.
  - Paging is clamped at 200 (`NormalizePublicPaging`) — an unbounded public read is the cheapest possible way to take the database down for every other tenant. `updated_since` supports incremental polling instead of re-reading the catalog.
  - Unregistered paths under the prefix answer a **JSON 404** rather than falling through to the static file server and handing an API client an HTML page. Deliberately outside the auth middleware: an unknown path has no scope to check.
  - **Read-only in this slice by choice, not by limitation** — the 38.5 idempotency spine is already in place under the same middleware, so a write endpoint joins by being curated, not by being made safe first.
  - **Verified live over HTTP**: authorised read (200 with budget headers), missing key (401), malformed key (401, generic), a narrower key hitting an endpoint it lacks the scope for (403 naming the scope), missing required parameter (422), unknown resource (404), unknown path (JSON 404).
- [x] **38.3** Rate limiting and quotas per credential — a per-minute burst window (in-process; a minute-scale check does not need durability) plus a **daily quota counted from the traffic log**, so a process restart cannot hand an integration a fresh allowance. Tenant defaults via `platform.public_api_rate_limit_per_minute`/`_daily_quota`, overridable per credential through `PUT /api/v1/admin/api-credentials/{id}/limits` (a null value clears the override, so raising a plan-wide ceiling is one setting rather than a sweep over every key). `X-RateLimit-*`/`X-Quota-*` are published on **every** response, success or rejection — an integration that can only discover its limits by being refused will keep being refused. The two 429s are distinguished in the message and in `Retry-After`, because "wait a second" and "wait until midnight" call for different client behaviour. Revocation and rotation release the in-process window. **Subtlety worth recording**: the DB seed for the daily counter excludes the outcomes that were rejected *before* consuming quota (`PublicAPIUnadmittedOutcomes`), or a credential rate-limited this morning would find its quota mysteriously smaller after a restart.
- [x] **38.4** Webhook subscriptions with HMAC signing, retry/backoff and DLQ — **built 2026-09-01, on top of 38.6 (built first, same session).** Extends `outbox.go` exactly as the backlog item names it: `WebhookSubscription` is a plain generic-doc-API doctype (the `ScheduledReport` precedent — no dedicated create/list/delete handler), and `dispatchWebhooksForEvent` (`engines/webhook.go`) is called from `processOutbox` once an outbox event is already durably committed — fanning out to one `webhook_delivery` async job (Stage 38.6's runner) per Active subscription whose `event_pattern` matches (exact match or a trailing-`*` prefix wildcard). The runner's own retry/backoff/DeadLettered handling **is** this item's DLQ — no second retry mechanism built. `idempotency_key = eventID+subscriptionID` means a re-dispatched (retried) outbox event never double-enqueues delivery to the same subscription.
  - **HMAC signing**: `X-Webhook-Signature: sha256=<hex>` is the real HMAC-SHA256 of the exact JSON body sent, computed with the subscription's own `secret` (mandatory field — a webhook is never sent unsigned) — plus `X-Webhook-Event`/`X-Webhook-Delivery` headers.
  - **SSRF guard** (`validateWebhookURL`, stdlib `net`/`net/http` only): https-only, and every resolved IP must be genuinely public — loopback/private/link-local/unspecified/multicast all refused. Checked **twice**: at document-create time (a convenience refusal) and again immediately before every delivery attempt (the real guarantee — DNS can change between the two, a create-time-only check is a classic TOCTOU bypass). Redirects are never followed (`http.ErrUseLastResponse`) — a URL that validated as public could otherwise 3xx to an internal address and reach it anyway.
  - **Sandbox side-effect-off hook** (the concrete mechanism Stage 38.7 needed): `deliverWebhook` checks `IsSandboxSchema` first and, for a sandbox tenant, simulates delivery (`{"simulated": true}`) with **no real HTTP call at all** — proven live via a canary `httptest.Server` the sandbox delivery never touched.
  - New module_key `integrations` (`is_core=false, default_enabled=true`) — genuinely cross-cutting platform infrastructure, registered in the same migration as the doctype per Stage 37.9's own lesson (checked proactively, not found live afterward).
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage38_4_webhook_subscriptions.sql` (dev only, surgical apply; registers `integrations` module + `WebhookSubscription` doctype). New `engines/webhook.go` (+`_test.go`), 4-line hook into `engines/outbox.go`'s `processOutbox`, 1 new `currency.go` dispatcher case. No new handler/route file — reachable via the generic doc API; delivery itself has no HTTP route at all, it's a job.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including the new `TestStage384WebhookSubscriptions` (validation rejects non-https/loopback/missing-secret/missing-pattern, `matchEventPattern`'s exact+wildcard cases, `dispatchWebhooksForEvent`'s Active-only/pattern-matching/idempotent-on-retry fan-out, `sendWebhookHTTP`'s real HMAC signature verified against an `httptest.Server` plus 2xx/5xx success/failure, and the sandbox no-real-HTTP-call guarantee). **Live over HTTP** (port 9270, stopped afterward): a real `WebhookSubscription` created via the generic doc API against a TEST-NET-2 address (RFC 5737, guaranteed non-routable — no real third party ever contacted), a real outbox event seeded and picked up by the live `processOutbox` ticker, a real `webhook_delivery` job enqueued and claimed by the live job-runner ticker, a genuine HTTPS POST attempted and correctly timing out (`context deadline exceeded`) with the job rescheduled — proving the full `PublishEvent → processOutbox → dispatchWebhooksForEvent → EnqueueJob → StartJobRunnerWorker → deliverWebhook` pipeline live, not just in unit tests. Retry/Cancel confirmed over the real `/api/v1/jobs/{id}` routes on that same job. Every fixture cleaned, zero residue confirmed.
- [x] **38.5** Idempotency keys on every mutating public endpoint — **required, not optional**: a retry after a timeout is the normal case for any network integration, and without a key the server cannot tell a retry from a second genuine request. The unique index on `(credential_id, idempotency_key)` *is* the lock, so two concurrent retries cannot both proceed however they are scheduled. A `request_fingerprint` (SHA-256 of method+path+body) means the same key with a **different** body is a 409, never a replay — answering a question the caller did not ask would be worse than no idempotency at all. A `5xx` is deliberately **not** stored: the claim is released so a retry after a server error is a genuine second attempt. Keys expire on `platform.public_api_idempotency_retention_hours` (default 24) and are then reclaimable. A panic releases the claim rather than wedging the caller out for the whole retention window. Exercised through direct engine tests rather than a route, since the first public slice is read-only — the guarantee has to be proven **before** the first mutating endpoint relies on it, not after.
- [x] **38.6** One general async job runner with retries, DLQ and a visibility screen — **built 2026-09-01, first of the three remaining 38.X items (built before 38.4/38.7, which both depend on it).** The foundation `micro_checklist.md`'s own §47.11.4 already named as the target every bespoke ticker in this codebase (outbox, scheduled reports, wave auto-creation, amortization, ...) should migrate onto **incrementally, later** — this stage builds the runner and its visibility screen only, and does not touch any existing ticker. A dedicated `async_jobs` table (the `integration_event_outbox` precedent, not a generic doctype — needs `SELECT...FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claiming and a status/lease taxonomy the generic doc engine's maker-checker assumptions don't fit).
  - **Claiming**: real `SELECT...FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (Pending jobs whose `next_attempt_at` has arrived, or Leased jobs whose `leased_until` has expired — a crashed worker's claim, reclaimed by the next tick) inside one transaction per tick, batch size 10, ticking every 10s (the Report Export worker's own cadence).
  - **Lease, honestly scoped**: a claim deadline (5 min), not a live heartbeat renewed mid-handler — every handler in this codebase (including both registered so far) runs synchronously to completion within one tick, so there is no genuinely long-running handler to renew a lease for yet. `UpdateJobProgress` exists for a future one (Stage 47.7.7's verifier/checkpoint/archive jobs) to call periodically; neither of today's two handlers uses it.
  - **Retry/backoff/DLQ**: exponential backoff (30s, 60s, 120s, ...) up to `max_attempts` (default 5, per-job overridable), then `DeadLettered` — that status **is** the DLQ, not a second table. `ReplayJob`/`CancelJob` mirror `RetryIntegrationEvent`'s existing precedent.
  - **Idempotency**: `EnqueueJob`'s `idempotency_key` — a partial unique index on `(job_type, idempotency_key) WHERE idempotency_key <> ''` means the same key always returns the existing job's id rather than creating a duplicate (the Stage 38.5 public-API-idempotency guarantee, applied to job enqueueing).
  - **Bounded retention**: hourly sweeper deletes terminal (Succeeded/DeadLettered/Cancelled) jobs older than `platform.job_runner_retention_days` (default 30) — the `SweepPublicAPIRuntime` precedent exactly. Payload/result also size-capped (64KB) at write time.
  - **Visibility screen**: a 4th "Async Jobs" tab on the existing Activity Log screen (`renderLogHubView`, `public/app.js`), same pane pattern as the pre-existing Integration Payloads tab — type/status/attempts/progress/last-error/updated columns, Retry on Failed/DeadLettered, Cancel on Pending/Leased.
  - **Real bug caught mid-build, before it ever reached the sweeper worker**: `StartJobRunnerRetentionSweeper`'s first draft passed the schema name straight into `SweepJobRunnerRetention(tenantID string)`, which calls `db.GetTenantSchema` internally — `GetTenantSchema("tenant_acme")` doesn't resolve (tenant ids look like `"acme"`, not `"tenant_acme"`) and silently falls back to `tenant_default`, meaning every non-default tenant's sweep would have quietly swept `tenant_default` instead of itself. Fixed by resolving `schema → tenantID` via the existing `tenantIDForSchema` helper first, the exact pattern `StartPublicAPIRuntimeSweeper` already uses for the identical reason.
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage38_6_async_job_runner.sql` (dev only, surgical apply; `async_jobs` added to `ProvisionTenantSchema`'s clone list with the `api_credentials`-style "skip if the template table doesn't exist yet" guard, so a fresh tenant provisioned between deploy and migrate still works). New `engines/jobrunner.go` (+`_test.go`), one new handler file (`internal/server/handlers_jobrunner.go`), 3 new routes, 2 new background workers (claim ticker + retention sweeper), 1 new setting (`platform.job_runner_retention_days`).
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including the new `TestStage386JobRunner` (idempotent enqueue, a real `SKIP LOCKED` claim + a succeeding handler marking Succeeded + a failing one retrying then dead-lettering at `max_attempts`, an unregistered `job_type` dead-lettering immediately rather than looping, Cancel/Replay's state-machine boundaries, `ListJobs` filtering, and the retention sweep leaving fresh/still-Pending jobs alone while deleting an aged-out terminal one). **Live over HTTP** (port 9270, stopped afterward — see 38.4's entry for the shared live pass covering both): `/api/v1/jobs` list/retry/cancel all exercised against a real job created by a real live event, and the new "Async Jobs" tab verified in a real browser via Playwright (all 4 tabs present, correct headers/empty-state, and — with 4 seeded jobs of every status — the Retry/Cancel/`-` action column rendering correctly per status with zero console errors). Every fixture cleaned, zero residue confirmed.
- [x] **38.7** Self-service sandbox tenant for integrators — **built 2026-09-01, last of the three (its webhook side-effect-off hook depends on 38.4's job handler, its expiry depends on 38.6 only indirectly via `async_jobs` being one of the tables a reset truncates).** A sandbox is a normal tenant — provisioned through the exact same `ProvisionTenantSchema` every real tenant uses, so it gets the identical fresh admin/module/RBAC baseline — flagged via two additive columns on `public.tenants` (`is_sandbox`, `sandbox_expires_at`), not a new table.
  - **Scope decision, stated rather than hidden**: deliberately does **not** seed a synthetic demo dataset (items/customers/orders) — a canned demo catalog is a real, separate, materially larger feature (representative data across every module, believable relationships between it). An integrator's whole point is testing their own integration against the Public API v1 (38.1) by creating their own records through it, same as any other fresh tenant.
  - **"Self-service" describes the integrator's experience once a sandbox exists** (their own login, their own API credentials, full use of the app/API with no path to production data) — provisioning itself stays Super-Admin-gated, the same as every other tenant-provisioning action in this codebase (`handleProvisionTenant`). A public, unauthenticated self-signup flow is a distinct, larger feature not built here.
  - **Auto-expiry, at the correct existing choke points, not a new one**: refused at login (`handleLogin`, generic auth-failure message — a caller learns their session is dead, not why, same choice the account-lockout check just above it makes) **and** on Stage 29.8's existing live session re-check (`apiMiddleware`) — so an expired sandbox locks out both new logins and any session already in progress, not just the next login. Deliberately **not** wired into `db.GetTenantSchema` itself (used by ~100+ call sites, including the admin's own reset/delete actions) — too broad a blast radius to be worth the risk versus the two narrower, purpose-built choke points actually used.
  - **Reset**: `ResetSandboxTenant` truncates exactly the set `ProvisionTenantSchema`'s own comment already documents as "per-tenant transactional data, correctly empty for a new tenant" (`documents`, `gl_postings`, `async_jobs`, `bin_stock*`, etc.) — refuses outright on any tenant not flagged `is_sandbox` (the one guard standing between this and wiping a real tenant's data), leaves `users`/config tables untouched (login keeps working), and pushes `sandbox_expires_at` back out by a fresh TTL window.
  - **Delete**: `DeleteSandboxTenant` — `DROP SCHEMA ... CASCADE` + registry row removal, the exact `is_sandbox` guard as reset. The only place in this codebase's non-test code that ever runs `DROP SCHEMA`.
  - **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage38_7_sandbox_tenants.sql` (2 columns on `public.tenants`, dev only, surgical apply). New `engines/sandbox.go` (+`_test.go`), one new handler file (`internal/server/handlers_sandbox.go`), 4 new Super-Admin-gated routes, a 2-line hook each into `handleLogin` and `apiMiddleware`'s live re-check. No frontend screen — matching every other Stage 38 admin/integrator-facing piece (38.1-38.3/5/8/9), none of which have one either; this is an admin/API-level provisioning feature, not a report/dashboard.
  - **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including the new `TestStage387SandboxTenants` (a real provisioned sandbox's flag/expiry/`Is*` helpers all agreeing, expiry flipping true once past, reset refusing a real tenant and truncating+preserving-login+extending-expiry for a real sandbox, delete refusing a real tenant and actually dropping both schema and registry row for a real sandbox, and the admin list including sandboxes but excluding real tenants). **Live over HTTP** (port 9270, stopped afterward): a real sandbox provisioned by a Super Admin and refused for a Store Manager (403); logged into with its own fresh one-time admin password, correctly requiring MFA enrollment exactly like any other Super Admin account (no sandbox-specific security downgrade); reset and confirmed; expiry forced into the past and login correctly refused with the generic failure message; reset/delete both confirmed refused against the real `default` tenant; finally deleted, with both its schema and registry row confirmed gone via direct SQL. Every fixture cleaned, zero residue confirmed.

**Stage 38 is now fully closed** (38.1-38.9, every item `[x]`) except the explicitly-deferred **38.2d** (OAuth2 client-credentials grant — stays open by design, not needed unless a real integration asks for standards-based token exchange over the working opaque-key path).
- [x] **38.8** OpenAPI generated from `routes.go` — the input to 39's API reference — **built 2026-08-12.** The public routes live in one declarative table (`internal/server/routes_public_api_v1.go`) read by **both** the registration loop and the generator, so a spec generated from a separately maintained list — wrong the first time someone adds a route and forgets — is not possible. Schemas come from reflecting the declared Go response types, so renaming a json tag changes the published contract in the same commit. OpenAPI 3.0.3, stdlib only (`encoding/json` + `reflect`), no spec library vendored. Written to `docs/specs/openapi_public_v1.json` by `cmd/gendocs` and served live at `GET /api/v1/admin/public-api/openapi.json` (Super Admin — an unauthenticated endpoint enumerating every route is a gift to anyone probing the deployment). **One trap found and handled**: a type whose shape depends on its example value (the paged envelope's `interface{} Data`) must **not** be cached as a named component — two endpoints returning `PublicPage` carry different `Data`, and one named schema could only describe one of them while silently mis-documenting the other. Those are inlined per endpoint.
- [x] **38.9** Per-credential API traffic audit/observability — an append-only `api_request_log` row per public call, written from a `defer` so a rejection, a panic and a normal response are all recorded by the same path. **Metadata only** — no request or response body — so support and capacity questions can be answered without the log becoming a copy of customer payloads. Readable at `GET /api/v1/admin/api-credentials/{id}/traffic` and `/api/v1/admin/api-traffic`, both behind the existing Super Admin gate: an integration key can never read or change its own limits or history. Retention via `platform.public_api_log_retention_days` (default 30), swept hourly. Logging never fails a request that already succeeded — a write failure goes to `LogSystemError` and the response stands.
  - **Live-verified end to end** on a throwaway UTF-8 database and server (port 9101, both destroyed afterwards): issue key → authorised read with budget headers → scope denial naming the scope → pin the limit to 3/min → 3× 200 then 429 with `Retry-After: 58` → pin a daily quota → quota-exceeded 429 with a midnight reset → clear the overrides → read the traffic log back showing all six calls with status, outcome, duration and key prefix.

### Stages 35-39 — Parity Master Plan — Stage 39 — Knowledge Center — ≈7-9 sessions ⏳ (39.1-39.17 + 39.13's 10 handbooks + 39.8/39.9/39.10/39.14/39.15 all closed 2026-09-03; only 39.18 remains, deliberately deferred as its own reviewed unit) — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **39.1** Minimal Markdown renderer in Go — **built 2026-08-11**, stdlib only: headings with stable duplicate-safe anchors, paragraphs, ordered/unordered lists, tables, fenced code, links, emphasis, images and GitHub-style admonitions. Raw HTML is escaped and URL schemes are allowlisted (`http`, `https`, `mailto`, relative) so article text cannot inject script/data URLs. Tests cover the full vocabulary, unsafe links/raw HTML, malformed emphasis, invalid UTF-8 and unclosed code fences.
**39.2-39.7 built as one unit 2026-08-12**, as planned: the generator, the shell and the access model had to land together or the content would be reachable before it was gated.

- [x] **39.2** `cmd/genkb` — renders `docs/kb/**/*.md` → HTML fragments + `index.json` + `search.json`, nav built from frontmatter. **Output goes to `internal/kb/content/`, not `public/help/`, and that is the access model.** The app serves `public/` straight off disk with `http.FileServer`, so anything written there is readable by anyone who can reach the host — which directly contradicts 39.6. The generated tree is embedded with `go:embed` instead and only reachable through a handler that decided to let you. It also means the Knowledge Center ships wherever the binary ships: no deploy-script change, no second artifact to forget. Frontmatter is a deliberately tiny YAML subset (scalars, booleans, ints, one-line lists) parsed in ~40 lines rather than taking a YAML dependency for a header that holds eight keys. The build **fails** on an article with no `section` — that article could not appear in the sidebar, and shipping a page nobody can navigate to is worse than not shipping it — and on duplicate slugs across the tree, because a slug is a permanent link people bookmark and must not change when a file moves between folders.
- [x] **39.3** Client-side search over a prebuilt inverted index — ~35 lines of vanilla JS over a `search.json` built at generation time. Terms from the title are indexed twice so a title match outranks a body match; stopwords are dropped (they match everything and rank nothing); queries match by **prefix**, so typing "reserv" finds "reservation" rather than a search box that feels broken mid-word. No Lunr, no Algolia, no scan over article bodies per keystroke.
- [x] **39.4** `/help` route + shell — sidebar grouped by section in a fixed reading order, live search, breadcrumbs, an on-page table of contents from the renderer's own heading anchors (no second slug implementation), prev/next following the sidebar order, and `/help/<slug>` as a real bookmarkable URL that resolves ahead of any saved view. All styling uses existing CSS tokens, so the Knowledge Center follows the app's light/dark theme with no palette of its own.
  - **Found and fixed a latent bug this was the first URL to hit**: `index.html` referenced its assets **relatively** (`styles.css`, `app.js`), so any two-segment SPA URL resolved them against the wrong directory and got the SPA shell back — the browser then refused every stylesheet and script on MIME grounds and the app never booted. Single-segment product prefixes like `/wms` never exposed it; `/help/<article>` does. Fixed at the source by making the five asset references absolute, which also hardens `/wms/<anything>` and every future deep link.
- [x] **39.5** In-app contextual help — a `?` in the header opens a drawer over the current screen (built on the existing `.modal-overlay` primitives, so no second dialog system) showing the article(s) mapped to that screen. The mapping comes from each article's own `screens:` frontmatter via the index's `screen_map`, so there is no second mapping file to drift. A screen with no article says so plainly rather than showing an arbitrary one — a wrong article is worse than an honest gap.
- [x] **39.6** Access model: authenticated by default, per-article `public: true` for the API/integration subset. **Structural, not a check that can be forgotten**: there is no copy under `public/` for a file server to hand out. Three authenticated endpoints serve the whole centre; three `publicRoutes`-listed endpoints serve **only** articles whose frontmatter opts in. The public index and public search index are **rebuilt narrowed**, not filtered in the browser — an index that lists an internal article's title has already leaked it, whatever the client is told to render. Requesting an internal article through the public door returns the same 404 an unknown slug gets, because "that exists but is internal" is itself information.
- [x] **39.7** `docs/kb/update-kb.ps1` with `-Check` — regenerates in memory, compares against the committed output, and exits non-zero naming every stale, missing or **orphaned** file (an article deleted from source whose HTML is still being served). Uses the mandatory `%TEMP%`-then-copy write path for the same Controlled Folder Access reason as `update-brain.ps1`. `WriteTo` deletes orphans rather than merging, so a deleted article stops being served.
  - **Verified**: `internal/kb/build_test.go` covers nav/search/screen-map generation, every refusal case, duplicate slugs, stale/orphan detection, that `WriteTo` clears drift, that the embedded tree is readable, that the public subset is strictly narrower, that every listed slug resolves to a body, and that a path-traversal slug is refused. Plus a **real-browser pass**: deep link opens the right article, sidebar renders 4 sections, breadcrumb, prev/next, 4-entry TOC, admonition styling, prefix search finds and opens an article, the drawer opens on a mapped screen and closes clean, no horizontal overflow, **zero console errors**. Seed content: 6 articles (2 Getting Started, 2 public integration references, 1 module handbook, 1 troubleshooting) — the 39.11-39.18 content items remain open.
- [x] **39.8** Drift guards: stale `last_verified`, dangling screen/endpoint/error-code references, unmapped screens — **built 2026-09-03**, `internal/kb/drift.go`. Reads the real sources as plain text rather than importing them (`internal/server` already imports `internal/kb`, so the reverse would be a cycle) - `public/app.js` for the `view === 'x'` dispatch chain, `internal/server/routes.go` + `routes_public_api_v1.go` (both the literal `http.HandleFunc` calls and the `Method:`/`Path:` struct-literal table) for registered routes, `internal/server/error_catalog_generated.go` for real codes. A cited error code only warns if its prefix is a real catalog prefix (e.g. `POSOFF`, `INVENT`) and the full code doesn't exist - an unrelated hyphenated token like `ISO-8601` is never a false positive because `ISO` was never a catalog prefix to begin with. A cited endpoint is checked against a Go-1.22-mux-aware pattern match (`{id}` → one segment, `{path...}` → the rest). `last_verified` stale after 180 days (a working default, not derived from anything else). Unmapped screens are reported as one aggregate line, not one per screen - with ~54 real screens and content still incremental, per-screen spam would bury the signal. All findings are non-fatal `[warn]` lines alongside Build()'s existing per-article warnings (not folded into `-check`'s exit code), since this is a softer content-quality guard, not 39.7's build-reproducibility gate. **Verified**: 6 new tests in `internal/kb/drift_test.go` (stale date, dangling screen, dangling endpoint incl. wildcard match, dangling error code incl. the ISO-8601 non-finding, the unmapped-screens summary, and graceful degradation when a source file is missing) all pass; `go build`/`go vet ./...` clean. **Run for real against the live repo, not just fixtures**: found a genuine pre-existing bug on the first run - `docs/kb/reference/public-api-idempotency.md` illustrated its idempotency-key contract with `POST /api/public/v1/orders`, but the entire public API v1 surface is GET-only (confirmed by reading `routes_public_api_v1.go`'s route table and `openapi_public_v1.go` - no mutating public route exists anywhere yet, though the idempotency *middleware* itself is real and generic, enforced for any non-GET method regardless of endpoint). Fixed by adding a `[!NOTE]` marking the example illustrative rather than currently callable, and re-verified `last_verified`. The other standing finding (14 of 54 screens still unmapped) is real and expected - it is 39.13's own remaining scope, not a bug. **Bugfix found in real use, not fixture-only**: the endpoint guard originally required an HTTP method prefix on every registered route string, so the 10 routes this codebase registers with none at all (Go's mux then matches every method) - including the generic doc API, /api/v1/doc/{doctype}, the single most-cited endpoint shape in these handbooks - were silently absent from its comparison set and every citation of one came back a false matches no registered route. Caught by 39.13.2 citing POST /api/v1/doc/GRN (a real, correct citation - GRN is a real doctype, confirmed against db/migrations_phase3.sql) and flagged anyway. Fixed by treating an empty captured method as a wildcard in both the regex and
outeExists; locked by a new TestDriftGuardsMethodlessRouteIsAWildcard.
- [x] **39.9** "Was this helpful?" feedback to a doctype, surfaced as a registered report — **built 2026-09-03**. `HelpArticleFeedback` (`db/migrations_stage39_9_help_feedback.sql`) is an ordinary generic document - `article`/`helpful`/`comment` fields, `module_key = 'core'` (always-on, since the Knowledge Center is authenticated-by-default platform functionality, not an optional module) - so submission goes straight through the existing `POST /api/v1/doc/{doctype}` generic-document API with **zero new Go handler code**. `engines/help_feedback.go` aggregates it into `GetHelpFeedbackSummary` (per-article Yes/No counts + helpful % + last-feedback date, percentage computed in Go to sidestep a divide-by-zero in SQL); `engines/help_feedback_report.go` registers it as report `help-article-feedback` ("Knowledge Center Feedback", category Admin) via the existing `RegisterReport` framework - no new report endpoint either. Frontend: two Yes/No buttons in the article footer (`renderHelpArticle` in `public/app.js`), deduped per browser via `localStorage` (a UX nicety against re-asking on reload, not a server-side one-vote rule - this is authoring signal, not a ballot). Role access mirrors the `LottableConstraint` pattern: HR/Admin (Super Admin) gets full CRUD, Store Manager/Cashier get create-only (an article's raw comments are for an author to read via the report/admin view, not for browsing) - Supplier deliberately excluded, since `handleGenericDoc`'s row-level supplier scoping expects a `vendor`-shaped field this doctype has none of. **Verified live end-to-end**, not just by code review: started a scratch dev Postgres + server (port 8099, `JWT_SECRET` scratch value, stopped after), applied the migration for real (confirmed `doctype_meta`/`doctype_fields`/`role_permissions` rows via `psql`), minted a Store Manager token and posted 3 feedback docs over real HTTP, then ran `GET /api/v1/reports/run/help-article-feedback` as Super Admin and got back correctly aggregated rows (`pos-operations`: 1 helpful/1 not/50%; `inventory-wms-operations`: 1 helpful/0 not/100%) - the exact counts the 3 POSTs should produce. `go build`/`go vet ./...` clean, `node --check public/app.js` clean, `styles.css` brace-balance clean.
- [x] **39.10** Release notes generated from the ledger's stage sections — **built 2026-09-03**. `cmd/gendocs/release_notes.go` adds a fourth generated Knowledge Center article alongside 39.16/39.17's three (error codes, report catalog, country/phone rules), following the exact same pattern: parse the real source mechanically, never hand-write a summary that can drift. `ledgerEntryPattern` matches every real `## N. Title (date, type)` Stage heading in `docs/project_ledger.md` - sections 1-3 (genesis/architecture/rollout-phases) are numbered headings too but carry no trailing `(date, type)`, so they're excluded by the pattern itself with no special-casing needed. The excerpt per entry is that section's own first paragraph (not a rewritten summary, and not a fragile first-*sentence* cut - a paragraph is more robust than trying to split on `. ` when the prose is full of abbreviations and inline code), grouped under a date heading whenever the date changes from the previous entry. Written to `docs/kb/reference/release-notes.md`, order 25 (between the existing generated report-catalog at 20 and country-phone-rules at 30). Wired into `cmd/gendocs`'s existing run, skipped with its own `[warn]` (not a failure) if the ledger can't be read, since the other three generators have no such external file dependency. **Verified**: ran `go run ./cmd/gendocs` for real (scratch output dir) - produced 70 correctly date-grouped entries ending exactly at the Stage 28/2026-07-27 archive boundary, spot-checked against the ledger by eye. **Found the committed `REPORT_CATALOG.md`/`ERROR_CODES.md` were themselves stale** (last regenerated 2026-08-09, predating this session - 47 registered reports vs the current 91, an unrelated pre-existing drift this run surfaced and fixed as part of the same regeneration pass) and refreshed all four generated KB articles plus both `docs/guides/` copies and `openapi_public_v1.json` together, since one `gendocs` run produces all of them and letting the others stay stale while only adding release notes would have been its own new drift. `go build`/`go vet ./...` clean, `pwsh docs/kb/update-kb.ps1` clean (27 articles).
**39.11 + 39.12 + 39.16 + 39.17 were authored as one pass on 2026-08-17**, taking the Centre from its 6 seed articles to **23**. Two enabling code changes came first, because every article in this pass cross-references others and the seed content had already got this wrong:
  - **Cross-article links were dangling, and nothing caught it.** `first-login.md` linked `first-order.md`, which rendered as a *relative* href — from `/help/first-login` the browser resolved it to `/help/first-order.md`, a slug that does not exist. `error-codes.md` pointed at `../../guides/ERROR_CODES.md`, which is not reachable from inside the app at all. Fixed at the renderer, not by hand-writing `/help/<slug>` in every article: `articleURL` in `markdown.go` rewrites any scheme-less, host-less `*.md` link to `/help/<basename>`, mirroring `slugFromPath`. Sources stay readable as plain Markdown on disk, and an author cannot get the app's URL shape wrong because they never type it. Fragment preserved; `http(s)` links to some other project's `.md` left alone; the rewrite runs *after* `safeURL`, so it cannot smuggle a scheme past it (pinned by test). `app.js` additionally intercepts in-body `/help/` clicks so a cross-reference is as fast as a sidebar click, leaving modified clicks alone so ctrl/middle-click still opens a tab.
  - **`HeadingSlug` exported** from `internal/kb`, because the generators below write their own contents lists and have to predict the anchor the renderer will emit. A second implementation would have produced a table of contents whose links silently stop resolving — GitHub's anchor rule and this renderer's differ on runs of punctuation (`data-import--excel-upload` vs `data-import-excel-upload`), which is exactly how that goes unnoticed.

Each journey is written as a day — what you do first, what you do when something is wrong, what you check before you go home — not as a feature list.

- [x] **39.17** Content: troubleshooting index keyed by error code — **the "half-generated" half is now generated into the Centre itself.** `cmd/gendocs` gained `-kb-out` and writes three articles as *Markdown into `docs/kb/`* rather than HTML into `internal/kb/content/`: `error-code-reference` (all 302 codes), `report-catalog` (every registered report) and `country-phone-rules`. Deliberately Markdown, so `genkb` stays the only thing that renders an article and these get the same anchors, search index and access model as a hand-written one. On top of that, the hand-written `troubleshooting-index` keys **~70 symptoms** to the codes they produce across 11 areas — because the reference answers "what does this code mean" and the question people actually arrive with is "this happened, now what". Every code cited was read out of the live catalog, not recalled.

Two mechanical checks over the built output: **zero dangling cross-references** (all 20 distinct `/help/` targets resolve to a real slug) and **zero invalid screen ids** (all 40 declared `screens:` values match a real `renderView` id). Live over HTTP on a throwaway server (port 9142, stopped afterwards): authenticated index returns 23 articles in the intended sidebar order, four new articles fetch 200 with correct titles/TOCs, rendered hrefs are the rewritten `/help/<slug>` form, and **the public index is still narrowed to the 2 opted-in articles** — the 39.6 access model holds at 23 articles as it did at 6.

- [x] **39.14** Content: integration guides per connector and per courier, with the 26.4.8 error dictionary inline — **written 2026-09-03**, two articles: `docs/kb/module-handbooks/channel-connectors.md` (Shopify/BigCommerce/Magento/Amazon/Flipkart/private-contract channels + WooCommerce, shared readiness→queue→worker→connector→log/retry pipeline, credential storage) and `courier-integrations.md` (Delhivery/Shiprocket, shared model, webhook verification/manifest/RTO). **The 26.4.8 error dictionary is inline in the connectors article as its own named section**, sourced from `classifyConnectorError` (`engines/connector.go`) and its test - the real platform-wording→code mapping (`CONN-0225` rate-limit/throttle, `CONN-0227` blank/required field, `CONN-0228` duplicate/already-exists, `CONN-0226` fallback), plus the circuit-breaker/rate-limiter direct `CONN-0225` path and the omnichannel SDK's 401/403→`CONN-0224` mapping. **Real gaps found and documented**: channel-connector credential-save/drift-check endpoints have zero UI callers outside Pine Labs/Unicommerce; the entire real Stage 35.5 courier engine (Delhivery/Shiprocket credentials, real AWB/pickup/cancel/rate-shop, NDR resolve, PDF label, reverse pickup) has zero UI callers - the Marketplace screen's Logistics panel only drives the older manual/placeholder-AWB flow, confirmed by the engine's own code comment stating a real courier API call is deliberately out of scope there; and Shiprocket's `AllocateAWB` needs a `remote_shipment_id` this codebase has no order-creation call to obtain. **Verified**: `pwsh docs/kb/update-kb.ps1` clean (35 articles) - the only drift-guard finding against either article is the same accepted class of false positive already documented for 39.13.2 (a `{start,submit-count,...}`-style comma-list of route suffixes inside a `[!WARNING]` block, which the endpoint-citation regex was never meant to parse as one URL).
- [x] **39.15** Content: admin & operations, migrated from `docs/operations/` — **written 2026-09-03**, new `Admin & Operations` KB section: `docs/kb/admin-operations/backup-and-restore.md` (order 10) and `incident-response.md` (order 20). **Scope decision, made after reading all 9 files in `docs/operations/` (line counts checked first, four read in full)**: only `backup_restore.md` and `incident_runbook.md` are genuinely "how an admin operates this system" content, so only those two became KB articles. `connector_live_verification.md` is channel-connector setup, not general ops - left as 39.14's source material instead of duplicated here. `hardening_roadmap.md` is a closed, dated (2026-07-12) security-audit record from Stage 12-13; `go_live_decisions.md`, `hypercare_plan.md`, `pentest_scope.md`, `uat_run_sheet.md` and `restore_drill_log.md` are historical/planning/audit-trail engineering documents, not recurring admin tasks - none were migrated, and none were deleted from `docs/operations/`, which stays the authoritative engineering record (the same "written from, not duplicating" relationship 39.11/39.12 have with `USER_GUIDE.md`). Content is adapted for an in-app admin audience, not copied verbatim - e.g. the source's placeholder escalation-contacts table is described as something each deployment fills in for itself rather than reproduced as literal `_TBD_` cells that would read as live, and the two articles cross-link each other (`[Backup & Restore](backup-and-restore.md)` / `[Incident Response & Alerting](incident-response.md)`) where the rollback and restore-drill procedures meet. **Verified**: `pwsh docs/kb/update-kb.ps1` clean (29 articles, up from 27), zero new 39.8 drift-guard findings against either article - the `system-status` screen id and every `manage.ps1`/`deploy/` command citation checked clean.

### 🛑 Production deploy blocker (discovered 2026-09-13) — fix before any future deploy of `main` — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **SB-012** `TRUST_PROXY=1` but `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` is empty on prod's `/etc/erp/erp.env`. Mechanical fix, no ambiguity: set it to `127.0.0.1/32`, matching Caddy's own `trusted_proxies static private_ranges` (Caddy is the only thing that reaches the app over loopback — `deploy/Caddyfile`, Stage 26.1.3a). Safe to do any time, not gated on a product decision. — **fixed and verified live 2026-09-13**, user-approved: backed up `/etc/erp/erp.env` (timestamped `.bak` alongside it on the box) before appending `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS=127.0.0.1/32`; `systemctl restart erp.service` came back `active` with a clean boot log (no crash loop); confirmed `http://127.0.0.1:8080/` on the box and `https://app.wholeops.in/` externally both `200` afterward. No behavioral change on the currently-running `0e79425` binary — `internal/server/middleware.go`'s `defaultTrustedProxyCIDRs` already includes `127.0.0.0/8` unconditionally, so this only widens the boundary redundantly (127.0.0.1/32 ⊂ 127.0.0.0/8); its entire purpose is to satisfy `security_baseline.go`'s explicit-config-required startup gate on the *next* deploy, which the running `0e79425` build predates and therefore doesn't check yet.
- [x] **Deploy auto-rollback (2026-09-16), so this can never repeat unattended.** The 2026-09-13 rollback above was done by hand over SSH because `deploy.ps1` had no rollback logic at all — a failed deploy just left the box crash-looping until someone noticed. New `deploy/remote_deploy.sh` (shipped fresh every deploy, invoked by `deploy.ps1`): snapshots `erp-server`/`public/` to `.prev` before the swap, restarts, polls `systemctl is-active` + a real HTTP probe against `http://127.0.0.1:8080/` for up to 15s, and **automatically restores the `.prev` snapshot and restarts** if the new build never comes up healthy — no human step required. `deploy.ps1` now surfaces one of `DEPLOY-OK` / `DEPLOY-ROLLED-BACK` / `DEPLOY-ROLLBACK-FAILED` rather than trusting a bare exit code. `bash -n` and the PowerShell parser both pass; **not yet exercised against a real box** (SB-021 still blocks any deploy of `main` from reaching the health-check step at all) — verify the rollback path for real the next time a deploy is attempted, once SB-021 is decided. Full detail in `deploy/README.md`'s Part F and `project_ledger.md`.
