### Stage 47 — Deep persona audit remediation: trustworthy, lightweight, self-operable ERP — Release train 47.R0 — conditional stop-ship controls (build first) — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **47.2 Server-authoritative POS quote, price, tax, discount and cost** — closes A-02/A-38; reuses Stage 37.6's effective-dated price-list resolver rather than creating another price store. **Built and green 2026-09-05/06.** The hole in one sentence: the ONLY source of a POS line's sale price and cost price was the browser (`public/app.js` pushed every cart line as `{salePrice: 0, costPrice: 0}` and let the cashier type both), and the discount-approval gate keyed on a third, unrelated, equally client-supplied `discount_pct`. New engine `engines/pos_quote.go` (`ResolvePOSQuote`) is now the only thing that decides what a line costs; `db/migrations_stage47_2_server_authoritative_pricing.sql` adds `Item.sale_price`/`mrp`/`standard_cost`, `Customer.price_list_code`, and the `POSPriceOverride`/`POSCostingGap` evidence doctypes.
  - [x] **47.2.1** `ResolvePOSQuote` takes item/location/customer/channel/qty/date/currency/tax-basis/coupons and returns a quote whose `quote_version` is an HMAC digest of its own priced content, keyed with the server signing secret — both the rule version and the tamper-evident identity, in one field. Price precedence: approved `POSPriceOverride` → `Customer.price_list_code` → `pos.default_price_list` → `Item.sale_price` → `Item.mrp`. Reuses Stage 37.6.4's `ResolvePriceForSKU`, which had **no production caller at all** before this stage. New `POST /api/v1/pos/quote`.
  - [x] **47.2.2** `cost_price` is gone from the checkout request, the stored cart, the response and the cashier UI. Cost resolves server-side only (`ResolveQuoteUnitCostPaise`: moving-average cost → `Item.standard_cost` → zero); a line with no cost basis posts **no COGS leg** and records a `POSCostingGap` instead of letting the till invent a number. `cost_total` is withheld from any role the existing cost/margin policy excludes (`engines.RoleMaySeeCost`, reading 47.1.3's own `sensitive_fields.go` rules rather than inventing a second answer to the same question). `PostSalesFinanceBooking`'s `costPrice <= 0` guard relaxed to `< 0` for this, with the COGS leg skipped entirely at zero rather than posting an empty voucher.
  - [x] **47.2.3** `POST /api/v1/pos/price-override` — capability-gated (`pos.price_override`, held by Administrator + Store Supervisor), mandatory reason and location, threshold from `approval_rules` doctype `POSPriceOverride`, immutable `POSPriceOverride` evidence carrying reference/override price, effective discount, actor, approver and quote version. Consumed by the sale it priced, so a replayed cart cannot inherit it. **The A-02 bypass itself is closed by the gate now keying on `max(client discount_pct, quote.ManualDiscountPct)`** — the server-measured deviation from master price — so declaring a discount honestly and hiding it in the unit price produce the identical requirement. Residual case (a line nothing on the server prices, in assisted mode) routes to the lowest configured POSCart slab via new `engines.LowestApproverRoleForDoctype`; a tenant with no slab configured is unaffected.
  - [x] **47.2.4** Checkout **always** re-resolves the quote server-side. A submitted `quote_version` that no longer matches returns `409 price_changed` with the new figures; the POS reprices the screen and requires an explicit confirmation (`accept_price_change`) — never silently the stale price and never silently the new one.
  - [x] **47.2.5** `internal/server/pos_pricing_stage47_2_test.go` — zero/near-zero/absurd price, client cost, false discount, contract-vs-default price-list precedence, expired price list, changed location/currency/tax-basis/qty (each must change the version), concurrent reprice → stale quote → confirm, unauthorized override (Cashier → 403, nothing written), over-threshold override → SALESP-0123 + Pending Approval that prices nothing, strict-mode refusal. **The A-02 red-team test now passes and was promoted out of the `stage47redteam` build tag** per 47.0.1's closure note (helpers moved to an untagged `stage47_redteam_helpers_shared_test.go` so a promoted test compiles on the default path).
  - **Acceptance: met.** One authoritative quote drives receipt, GST, revenue, tender and approval; the stored cart carries the server's own resolved prices with a `price_source` per line; Cashier receives no cost/margin field. **Known limitation, deliberate:** `pos.pricing_mode` defaults to `assisted`, which still accepts an operator-typed price for an item the tenant has never priced (and flags that cart for approval). `strict` refuses it outright and is the end state — assisted is the default only because `Item.sale_price` did not exist before this stage, so every existing tenant prices at the till and strict-by-default would stop all of them selling on the next deploy.

- [x] **47.3 Exactly-once atomic sale/checkout** — closes A-03; this is an inventory+finance invariant, not a UI debounce. **Built and green 2026-09-06.** What was actually wrong: the pre-existing "idempotency guard" claimed the POSCart **row**, but everything after it ran as five independently-committed transactions (availability + stock ledger, loyalty burn, revenue/COGS, GST, exempt reclass), unwound by hand-written compensations that could themselves fail and only log. A crash between any two left a sale genuinely half-posted, and a retry under a **new** cart number sailed past the guard entirely.
  - [x] **47.3.1** `engines/command_idempotency.go` + `tenant_default.command_idempotency`. Claimed before any mutation (the INSERT is the lock), completed **inside the sale's own transaction** — which is what makes taking over an expired lease provably safe rather than a gamble: an abandoned InProgress claim proves nothing was committed. Outcomes: acquired / replay (original response returned verbatim, INT-0222 semantics) / in-progress / payload-mismatch (409) / retry-after-failure. Key namespaced by user + command. When the client supplies its own key, `cart_number` is excluded from the request digest, so a retry that mints a fresh cart number is recognised as the same command — the exact gap the cart-row guard had. Settled claims age out after 7 days on the reservation sweeper's existing tick (no second worker).
  - [x] **47.3.2** `FinalizePOSCheckout` is now ONE transaction: cart row locked `FOR UPDATE`, availability decrement, loyalty burn, revenue/COGS, GST, exempt reclass, cart → Paid, override consumption, idempotency completion and the **outbox event** all commit together. Required splitting six engines into `…Tx` forms (`PostDoubleEntryTx`, `PostSalesFinanceBookingTx`, `PostSalesGSTBookingTx`, `PostExemptSalesReclassTx`, `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucherTx`, `RedeemLoyaltyPointsTx`); every existing caller keeps the original function, now a wrapper that opens and commits a transaction. Deliberately still post-commit, with reasons recorded in the code: stock-ledger rows (append-only + idempotency-keyed), loyalty earn, offline-sync variance, costing gaps — all additive records that must never undo a completed sale.
  - [x] **47.3.3** `engines/pos_payment.go` — `Initiated → Authorized → Posted` plus `Failed`/`Voided`, as one `paymentStateTransitions` map so no two call sites can disagree. `authorize_only` on `/checkout` reserves stock and stops; `POST /api/v1/pos/payment/confirm` (provider reference doubles as the provider idempotency handle) and `/void` finish it. **No DB transaction is held across the network** — that is the whole reason the posting waits for the authorization instead of racing it. Reservation expiry needed no new mechanism: Stage 35.3.7's sweeper already reclaims a TTL'd hold.
  - [x] **47.3.4** Deterministic lock order (cart row → availability rows sorted by SKU → loyalty ledger), applied in `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucherTx` and mirrored by `AuthorizePOSSale`'s reservation order. New `InsufficientStockError` type + `IsRetryableDBConflict` so a **business shortage** (INVENT-0101, 422) and a **retryable serialization/deadlock conflict** (409, "nothing was charged, try again") are different answers — reporting either as the other tells the cashier a lie about their own inventory.
  - [x] **47.3.5** `internal/server/pos_atomic_checkout_stage47_3_test.go` — clean sale reconciles; a failure injected after the stock decrement leaves availability untouched and posts nothing (and retrying compounds nothing); 100 identical retries under one key post exactly one sale (the per-session 60/min rate limiter is counted as a refusal rather than a failure, documented in the test); 8 concurrent tills against 10 units sell exactly what stock allows, with no negative availability and no 500s; key reuse with a different payload is refused; authorize → void releases the hold and posts nothing; 5 concurrent confirms of one authorization post exactly one sale. **The A-03 red-team test now passes and was promoted out of the build tag**, with its sanity check rewritten — it used to *require* availability to have dropped after a failed attempt, because that was the bug it was freezing.
  - [x] **47.3.6** `engines/pos_sale_reconciliation.go` + the `pos-sale-reconciliation` report (Finance). One row per sale comparing cart qty/value against stock ledger, revenue, COGS, tax, tender and loyalty, plus whether the outbox event exists, with a verdict. Deliberately independent reads rather than a join (a join lets one missing side hide another) and deliberately reports **Balanced** rather than only exceptions, so an empty exception list can be told apart from a report that never ran. Used by the 47.3.5 tests as the shared definition of "balanced", so a test cannot pass while the operator's own report calls the same sale broken.
  - **Acceptance: met.** Every injected failure produces zero result or one complete result; no failed/retried cart double-decrements stock; entries balance and reconcile to the receipt — proven by the report, not asserted. **Note:** a pre-existing test (`TestCheckoutToForecastIntegration`) needed idempotency-row cleanup added; it pins a fixed cart number across runs, so its second run was correctly refused as a duplicate. That is the mechanism working, not a bug.

- [x] **47.4 One atomic, replay-safe return/refund/exchange model and operator surface** — closes A-04 and Stage 35.9's stated missing management UI. **Built and green 2026-09-06.** Stage 35.9 already had the right *shape* (one `ReturnRequest` aggregate, a real state machine, prices from the original sale); what it lacked was the four properties that make it safe, plus any UI at all. All four are now in `engines/returns_atomic.go`, and the workflow endpoints — which had **zero caller anywhere in `app.js`** — finally have one.
  - [x] **47.4.1** The legacy path is retired twice over: `POST /api/v1/fulfillment/return` is a hard **410** naming its replacement, and `engines.ProcessReturnAnywhere` itself is a coded refusal, because it was an exported engine function as well as a route. Its `sumPriorReturns` helper is deleted. Why it could not be repaired in place: it took price and cost from the caller, incremented stock with an unlocked upsert, committed, and only then posted two GL reversals with **no idempotency key at all**; its "already returned" pool came from one `SalesReturn` document keyed `RET-<orderID>` whose repeat INSERT conflicted and whose error the HTTP handler discarded — so the recorded total never advanced past the first call while stock and GL for every later call still went through. Four calls of 3 against a sale of 10 returned 12.
  - [x] **47.4.2** Eligible price and cost basis come from the immutable original sale lines. The request shape carries no price at all, so there is nowhere for a client to put one. **A real regression 47.2 introduced was caught here by 47.4.7's own test**: `resolveOriginalSaleLinePrices` read `cost_price` from the stored cart, which 47.2.2 had removed — so every returned line resolved to a zero cost basis and silently posted no COGS reversal. Cost is now resolved through the same `ResolveQuoteUnitCostPaise` the sale used.
  - [x] **47.4.3** The cumulative-quantity check runs **inside** a transaction holding the original sale's own document row `FOR UPDATE` (`assertReturnEligibleTx`) — the natural serialization point: every return against one sale contends on exactly one row, returns against different sales never contend. Plus a tenant-scoped idempotency key through the *same* `command_idempotency` mechanism 47.3.1 built for checkout, completed inside the return's transaction, and stored on the document as its own evidence identity.
  - [x] **47.4.4** Both QC GL legs moved **inside** the QC transaction (they ran after `tx.Commit()` — the A-03 shape applied to returns) and both gained real posting keys (the COGS leg had none). `ProcessRefundRequest` is now one transaction — row-locked status re-check, cash leg, refund status, return closure — because it previously posted the GL and saved the status separately, leaving cash credited against a refund still marked `Approved` and therefore immediately processable again. **New: output-GST reversal** (`PostReturnGSTReversalTx`), which nothing did before — a refund debited 4100 for the full tax-inclusive amount and left the payable accounts untouched, so a tenant kept reporting output tax on goods that had come back. That is a filing error, not a cosmetic one.
  - [x] **47.4.5** Receipt return, partial return, same-value exchange, damaged/quarantine/reject dispositions and supervisor approval already existed. The two genuinely missing ones are built: **No Receipt** (no original bill; priced from the item master and labelled as such, never presented as if it came from a receipt) and **Goodwill** (accepts a return past the window — and *only* the window: cumulative eligibility still holds, because "we will take this back as a gesture" is not "you may return more than you bought"). Both require a written reason, record the authoriser from the session rather than the request body, and are gated on a new `returns.exception` capability held by Administrator + Store Supervisor. Gated in the handler rather than by route, deliberately: the exceptions are two *actions* on a route that must stay open to ordinary returns, and splitting them onto their own route would duplicate the handler (`engines.RoleHasCapability` reads the same templates `checkRouteCapability` does).
  - [x] **47.4.6** The management UI, which Stage 35.9's own entry recorded as missing. Two surfaces: the **POS return panel rebuilt** — the clerk types the bill number and the server says what is still returnable and at what price, instead of the old form that asked the clerk to type SKU, quantity, sale price *and* cost price; and a new **Returns screen** (sidebar, under Sales) listing every return with per-row actions derived from the aggregate's own state machine, plus the inspection dialog (per-line disposition + optional exchange) and the two-step refund (approve, then pay). New `GET /api/v1/returns/eligibility` is what both render from — cumulative quantities and a plain-English reason per line, so a refusal can be repeated to the customer at the counter.
  - [x] **47.4.7** `internal/server/returns_stage47_4_test.go` — legacy endpoint retired (410 + names its replacement); prices come from the sale not the caller; 6 repeated submissions raise one return and report the replay; 6 concurrent clerks against 4 sold units accept exactly 2 with no 500s and no over-claim; return-window boundary with an explained refusal; eligibility reflects prior claims; inspection posts stock + COGS + tax atomically and reconciles; 4 concurrent refund processings pay exactly once. **The A-04 red-team test now passes and was promoted out of the build tag** — the closure taken was the audit's first option (remove the legacy path), so the four replayed calls are refused four times over, which is stronger than the "at most 10 of 10" the test demanded; the assertion still holds it to that. Two pre-existing tests updated for real reasons, recorded inline.
  - **Acceptance: met.** Total returned quantity cannot exceed original eligibility under concurrency (proven, not asserted); stock/refund/GL/tax/evidence reconcile via `engines.ReconcileReturn`, which the tests and the operator share; no duplicate-evidence error is ignored. **Known limitation, unchanged from Stage 35.9 and still true:** an RTO-sourced line has no cost basis (`SalesOrderLine` carries only `unit_price`), so its inventory-side reversal posts zero — and an exchange reuses the returned line's cost as the exchange leg's basis, correct for the same-family swap this feature targets.

- [x] **47.5 Mixed-owner 3PL isolation across the inventory lifecycle—or enforced de-scope** — closes A-05. **Closed 2026-09-09 by the second of the two closures the item offered: "the system makes the unsupported configuration impossible."** Decision taken by the user on evidence, not preference — the entire development database held **two `bin_stock_owner` rows and both were the A-05 red-team fixture itself** (`OWNER-A-TEST`/`OWNER-B-TEST` in `WH-OWN-TEST`, one at qty 0). No tenant has ever segregated real stock by owner, so building owner-awareness through receive → allocate → pick → pack → ship → adjust → count → bill would have been a multi-session feature for zero users, while the *claim* of isolation stood unproven in the meantime. The claim was the risk; the missing feature was not.
  - [x] **47.5.1** `db/migrations_stage47_5_single_owner_guard.sql` + `engines/wms_single_owner.go`. **The guard is structural, not procedural**: a `warehouse_owner` table whose PRIMARY KEY *is* `location_code`, so a second owner for a warehouse cannot be stored. `AssertSingleOwnerForLocation` is called from `RecordOwnerStock` — the one API in this codebase that can put a second owner in a building — rather than repeated per call site. An **unassigned** warehouse adopts its first owner (so a tenant that never thought about ownership is unaffected; the rule blocks the *second* owner, not the first), and reassignment is refused while the incumbent still holds stock, or the guard would be cosmetic. New setting `wms.stock_ownership_mode` defaults to `single_owner`; `mixed_unsupported` exists so a real 3PL pilot can opt in **knowingly** — it is labelled unsupported because allocation and picking genuinely are owner-blind. New routes `POST /api/v1/wms/owner/assign-warehouse` and `GET /api/v1/wms/owner/mixed-locations`.
    **The migration deliberately does not "fix" pre-existing mixed data.** It backfills `warehouse_owner` only for locations already holding exactly one owner, and for genuinely mixed ones it `RAISE WARNING`s and leaves them alone. Aborting would block every later migration on such a database; silently picking a winner would re-attribute somebody's stock without being asked. Naming them is the only honest option, and `ListMixedOwnerLocations` surfaces the same list through the API. **Verified on the dev DB: it correctly detected `WH-OWN-TEST`, refused to guess, and warned.** This matters because production could not be inspected from here — the migration is safe there regardless of what it finds.
    **Claim removed** from `docs/ERP_BLUEPRINT.md`: "multi-owner (3PL) stock segregation and billing" → single-owner-per-warehouse attribution, with mixed-owner isolation explicitly *not* claimed.
  - [x] **47.5.2–47.5.6** — **not built, by decision.** These are the "if supported" branch (owner mandatory through every transition, owner on `SalesOrder`/allocation demand, removal of every owner-agnostic fallback in sourcing/availability/wave/picking, FK+uniqueness constraints, owner reconciliation with an exception queue, adversarial two-owner lifecycle). They remain the real thing if a 3PL pilot ever signs; the guard above is what makes their absence safe rather than merely undocumented.
  - **Acceptance: met**, via the second limb. **The A-05 red-team test now passes and was promoted out of the `stage47redteam` build tag** — rewritten to assert the refusal directly, which is a *stronger* statement than the original "allocation stayed within one owner's holding" (that could have passed by luck on a quiet bin). It also checks the refusal is a coded `ValidationError` naming the incumbent owner, that nothing partial was written, and that only one owner ends up at the location. `engines/wms_single_owner_test.go` covers the guard's own behaviour: first-owner adoption, same-owner top-ups allowed, second owner refused, takeover-while-stocked refused, the unsupported mode really disabling the guard, and mixed locations being reported once it does.
    **One pre-existing test needed updating for a real reason:** Stage 42.5.5's `MultiOwnerStockSegregation` sub-test asserts one bin holding two owners — precisely the configuration now guarded. It opts into `mixed_unsupported` explicitly, which is what a real 3PL pilot would also have to do; its billing maths is unchanged and still covered.

- [x] **47.7 Audit evidence, queryability and bounded legal retention** — closes A-07/A-30. **All of 47.7.1–47.7.8 built and green 2026-09-12.** Model chosen by the user 2026-09-09: **independently signed events + checkpoints**, not a serialized tenant chain.
  **Why that model, concretely.** What existed was the worst of both: `engines/logs.go` hashed each row's checksum from the *previous* row's — chain semantics — while deliberately not locking, and said so: *"Worst case under real concurrency is two rows briefly chaining from the same parent, not a broken tamper-evidence guarantee."* The second half of that sentence was wrong, and it is exactly the failure 47.7.2 names: sibling rows are what made the verifier report a break on perfectly clean data. **A verifier that cries wolf under normal load is worse than no verifier**, because it trains an operator to ignore it. Taking the lock instead would have serialized every audit write per tenant — on a table that takes a row on nearly every request — and Stage 47.3 had just finished making checkout deliberately concurrent. A per-tenant chain would have put a global serialization point back on every sale.
  - [x] **47.7.1** Evidence shape extended to what the item names: `entity_type`, `entity_id`, `correlation_id` (tying a row to the request *and* to 47.3's command-idempotency record), plus `signature` and `sig_version`. Trusted time is the row's own `created_at`, signed.
  - [x] **47.7.2** `engines/audit_evidence.go`. Each row signed over its **own** content with a length-prefixed canonical encoding (so `a|bc` and `ab|c` cannot collide), with **no dependency on any other row** — concurrency stops being a correctness question. The one thing per-row signatures cannot catch is **deletion**: remove a row and every survivor still verifies. Checkpoints close that — a digest over the ordered signatures in a definite `seq` range, so a missing row changes both the count and the digest. Checkpoints chain to each other (there are few, written by one job, so it costs nothing and gives the series a single verifiable head). Same shape as AWS QLDB digests and Certificate Transparency signed tree heads.
  - [x] **47.7.3** `WriteAuditEvidenceTx` writes evidence **inside the caller's transaction**, so evidence and mutation commit together or neither does — proven by a test that rolls back and asserts no row survives. This is the half the chain model could not do: reading "the last row" inside a business transaction is precisely what would deadlock or serialize against other in-flight sales.
  - [x] **47.7.4** **345,808 of 383,810 rows (90.1%, matching the audit's own ≈90% estimate) carry no checksum.** The tempting move — backfill them so the verifier reports 100% — is refused explicitly: a signature computed today proves only that the row exists today, and asserting otherwise converts a known gap into a false assurance an auditor would rely on. Instead: one boundary checkpoint seals the legacy range (their content is fixed **from the seal forward**), and every verification returns a `coverage_statement` saying in words that those rows predate the scheme and are **deliberately not counted as verified**. A test asserts that negative.
  - [x] **47.7.5** Four indexes, for the four access patterns the API actually issues: newest-first, by actor, by entity, by correlation. **No speculative index** — the item says so, and every index is a write cost on a table that takes a row on nearly every request.
  - [x] **47.7.6** Retention/legal-hold schema (`audit_retention_policy` versioned, `audit_legal_hold` scoped by entity/actor and outranking retention, shipped default 365 days hot with auto-deletion OFF) plus the archive itself, built 2026-09-12: `engines/audit_archive.go` — AES-256-GCM-encrypted, gzip-compressed archive files (`AUDIT_ARCHIVE_KEY`/`AUDIT_ARCHIVE_DIR` env vars, same key-management pattern as `channel_credentials.go`); one archive == exactly one checkpoint window, never partial; a held row (legal hold) blocks the *whole* window rather than fragmenting it. **Manifest-then-delete is literal**: the file is written and self-verified (re-decrypt, re-decompress, re-check the digest) *before* the archive row is recorded and the source rows deleted, all inside one transaction. Checkpoint verification (`verifyCheckpoints` in `audit_evidence.go`) is archive-aware: an archived window is verified against its archive file instead of the (now-empty) live table, so a legitimate archive is never mistaken for a deletion attack — the archive redirect still catches the archive file itself being deleted, corrupted or swapped. `GET/POST /api/v1/admin/audit-logs/archive(s)` + `/{id}`, `/{id}/export`, `/{id}/restore`; `StartAuditArchiveScheduler` runs daily on Stage 38.6's runner.
    **Incident during build (2026-09-11), fixed same-day, recorded so it isn't re-derived**: an early test version sealed a "boundary checkpoint" over all pre-existing history to isolate its own rows, then called the (correctly tenant-wide) `RunAuditArchive` with retention disabled — which archived that boundary checkpoint too, into `AUDIT_ARCHIVE_DIR=t.TempDir()`, deleted by Go at test exit. `tenant_default.audit_logs` dropped from ~389,000 rows to 334, unrecoverably (no usable backup — nearest was 2 months stale, and restoring it would have rolled back every other session's dev data). Root-caused and closed with four independent fixes, each verified by its own test: (1) tests now run against a freshly provisioned, single-use scratch tenant schema, never `tenant_default`; (2) `RunAuditArchive` refuses `archive_after_days` below a 30-day floor (`minArchiveAfterDays`) as a coded error, so a misconfigured policy can't reproduce this in production either; (3) `maxRowsPerArchiveWindow` (50,000, overridable only via an explicit `allow_large_windows` flag on the manual trigger) caps how large a window the *scheduled* pass will process unattended; (4) `ensureArchiveDirReady` refuses a non-absolute, non-existent or unwritable `AUDIT_ARCHIVE_DIR` before anything is read, rather than discovering it mid-pass. **Two related security gaps found in peer review, also fixed**: a checkpoint's `archive_id` isn't covered by its own `signCheckpoint` signature, so a DB-write attacker could repoint it at an unrelated archive (closed by an explicit checkpoint-id match check) or — the sharper version — at its *own* previously-restored archive after deleting the live rows again (closed by having `RestoreAuditArchive` **delete** the archive manifest row, inside the same transaction as restoring the rows and logging the restore itself as a signed audit event, rather than flagging it with a mutable, unsigned `restored_at` column — so the repoint is refused by `audit_checkpoints.archive_id`'s own foreign key, not merely by an app-level check that a second UPDATE could bypass). 21 orphaned `audit_archives` rows (dead references to the deleted temp files) remain in `tenant_default`, left for a deliberate operator cleanup rather than an agent-run `DELETE` against the shared dev DB.
    13 tests in `engines/audit_archive_test.go`, all green in isolation: the full write→verify→query→export→restore drill; legal hold blocking a whole window (and respecting scope — a non-matching hold does not block); archive-file tamper detected via the checkpoint redirect; the repoint-after-restore attack failing on the foreign key; the oversized-window ceiling (and its override); the archive-dir safety check (relative path refused; unwritable directory skipped where the OS/filesystem cannot make that genuine).
  - [x] **47.7.7** Checkpoint and verification run as jobs on **Stage 38.6's durable runner** (retries, DLQ, visibility screen already there) rather than a bespoke ticker — 47.11.4 names every bespoke ticker as something to migrate *onto* that runner, so adding one here would move the wrong way. `StartAuditEvidenceScheduler` only *enqueues*, hourly, idempotent per tenant-hour. **47.7.7's subtle half is implemented**: `AuditVerificationOverdue` alerts on verification that did not *happen*, not only on verification that *failed* — absence of a failure is not evidence of success, and a stopped verifier is exactly the state an administrator-level attacker would engineer.
  - [x] **47.7.8** `engines/audit_evidence_test.go` — 24 concurrent writers all verify (the false-positive case that drove the model choice); direct SQL tamper detected; a row deleted from inside a sealed window detected *by the checkpoint*; legacy rows sealed-not-fabricated; key rotation **not** mistaken for tampering; overdue-verification silence detected; evidence rolled back with its transaction. **A real bug was found by these tests and fixed**: Postgres `TIMESTAMP` stores microseconds while `time.Now()` carries nanoseconds, so the value signed differed from the value stored and *every freshly written row failed its own signature*. Caught because the tamper test reported a row as tampered before anything had touched it.
  - **Acceptance: met.** Coverage and limitations are explicit and machine-readable; concurrent clean data verifies; tamper and deletion are both detected (live table or archive, whichever is authoritative for that window); the measured query patterns are indexed; the archive/restore drill is built and tested. The conditional-claim register (`engines/environment.go`) is updated rather than cleared — the legacy coverage boundary is a permanent factual limitation of this data set and an auditor must be told, not left to discover it. **Not yet done:** the 47.7.6 migration (`db/migrations_stage47_7_6_audit_archive.sql`) and the three new Go files are uncommitted, deliberately kept separate from `7f5fadf`'s Stage 47.5/47.7-core/48/49 commit while the incident above was being fixed; the 21 orphaned `audit_archives` rows in `tenant_default` (dev only) are unreviewed cleanup, not yet done.

### Stage 48 — Documentation architecture, governance, controlled migration and deletion — Phase 48.D1 — Governance, ownership and one authority per question

- [x] **48.1 Documentation standard and machine-enforced lifecycle** — foundation built and verified 2026-09-07. Governance rules are explicitly drafted; their creation and offline enforcement do not self-approve business/legal/release claims. Legacy living files have provisional owners and rebuild dispositions. Legacy metadata has now been repaired and strict enforcement enabled; accountable owner acceptance remains distinct from provisional ownership.

  - [x] **48.1.2** Required front matter by type: stable `doc_id`, title/type/status, owner/approvers, audience, applies-to/support scope, authority, confidentiality, last-verified/review-by, supersedes/by and generated source. Git remains version history; human version/effective date only where released/signed policy/legal/customer material needs it.
  - [x] **48.1.3 Ownership RACI and cadence.** Product owns BRD/PRD/scope; process owners/BA own module/process requirements; architect owns architecture/ADRs; data owner owns MDM; security/privacy/legal own their controls; QA owns test/evidence; SRE owns runbooks; documentation+module owners own help. AI/developer may draft but cannot self-approve business/legal/audit/UAT/compliance claims.
  - [x] **48.1.4 Authority matrix.** Why=Vision/BRD; intended behavior=PRD/requirements; shipped/supported=capability register + release evidence; planned=roadmap/backlog; API=OpenAPI; design=architecture/ADR; operation=runbook; implementation=implementation guides; user task=KB; what happened=immutable assurance record; legal claim=qualified control evidence.
  - [x] **48.1.5 Naming/link/style/confidentiality standard.** Lowercase kebab-case, ISO dates only for immutable records, no spaces/uppercase for new paths, no workstation-specific file links/usernames/local ports/secrets, one H1, business language first, public/customer/internal/restricted/secret-never-in-docs classes.
  - [x] **48.1.6 Lightweight `doclint` in warning mode** using existing Go/PowerShell only: metadata/status/owner, naming convention (lowercase kebab-case per 48.1.5), duplicate IDs, local files/anchors, KB slug resolution, non-portable links, unregistered/orphaned files, stale review, generated drift, superseded replacement, secret patterns and size budgets (ordinary article ≤120 KiB raw, split by task if larger; KB index ≤250 KiB).
  - [x] **48.1.7 Generated document register** showing every active document's owner/status/authority/review/replacement and every record's commit/release/date. Directory READMEs are navigation, not parallel truth.
  - [x] **48.1.8 Build the question/audience-based docs portal** (the `docs/README.md` rebuild) as the navigation entry point into the registered document set, keyed by reader question rather than folder structure.
  - **Acceptance:** 100% of living normative/procedure/template docs are registered and owned or explicitly queued for rebuild; zero ambiguous authority category; lint can run offline with no new dependency/service.

Database roles/extensions deliberately NOT included — they are deployment state, not source, and belong to 49.7.4.*

Verified live on a scratch instance. Audited the rest: no pprof anywhere, `/api/v1/debug/panic` already registered only when `ENV != production` (24.16), no demo seeder route.*

Remove it or convert it to governed break-glass in 49.14. — *Scanner in `internal/securityscan/bypass.go`, reviewed allowlist in `bypass_test.go` (kept next to the scanner, not as a doc, so it is executed rather than aspirational). Tree is genuinely clean: **zero** magic identity comparisons, **zero** impersonation/back-door paths, one governed kill switch (`ERP_DISABLE_EXTERNAL_SIDE_EFFECTS`, 47.0.5). The 12 block-severity hits are all the four shipped bootstrap credentials and the code that exists to refuse them. "Permissive fallback" is deliberately left to code review and 49.10.6 — it is a shape, not a string, and a regex for it would produce noise that gets the whole scanner suppressed.*
  - [x] **49.1.5 Secure tenant provisioning/deprovisioning.** Cryptographically random one-time bootstrap, mandatory rotation/MFA, atomic registry+schema setup, least-privilege DB ownership, expiry/reset controls, evidence, backup/retention/legal-hold-aware deletion and proof no old tenant route/worker/session remains. — *2026-09-06: `engines/tenant_lifecycle.go` (state machine + evidence + residue proof), `db/migrations_stage49_1_5_tenant_lifecycle.sql` (lifecycle/bootstrap/retention columns on `public.tenants` + append-only `public.tenant_lifecycle_events`), `cmd/tenantctl` (the operator surface — deliberately a CLI, not a route: nothing inside the product should be able to reach `DROP SCHEMA`, and this adds one CLI command to 49.1.1's inventory instead of an internet-reachable path). **Four real gaps found and closed in the existing provisioning path**, not just new features on top: (a) the registry INSERT and `CREATE SCHEMA` ran as two autocommitted statements *ahead* of the clone/seed transaction, so a mid-provisioning failure left a registry row resolving to a half-built schema — a tenant that authenticates and then 500s; all of it is now one transaction (PostgreSQL DDL is transactional), with a test that forces the failure and asserts the registry row is gone; (b) the one-time admin password never expired and nothing recorded whether it had ever been rotated — it now expires (72h, `TENANT_BOOTSTRAP_TTL_HOURS`) and `/login` refuses it afterwards with the same generic failure as a wrong password, until an operator reissues one with `tenantctl rotate-bootstrap`; (c) re-running provisioning over an existing tenant silently reset a rotated, in-use admin password via step 5's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (the R-01 hazard, in Go rather than SQL) — refused now, but only once the credential has actually been rotated, so an interrupted first attempt is still recoverable; (d) there was no reversible state between "serving" and "DROP SCHEMA CASCADE" — `suspended` and `deprovision_requested` now cut off **sessions already in flight**, not merely new logins, via a tenant gate in `ResolveLiveUserState` (same ~30s SLO as the existing live user-state check). **Rotation detection needs no flag**: bcrypt salts every hash, so "the admin's stored hash is still byte for byte the one we issued" is proof it was never changed — a rotation through raw SQL is caught the same as one through the app. **Purge is gated four ways** (must be deprovisioned first, no legal hold, retention elapsed, backup reference supplied), each refusal itself recorded, and it **proves** removal afterwards (`VerifyTenantResidue`: registry row, schema, hostname mapping, any `public.*` table carrying a `tenant_id` — discovered from `information_schema` rather than hardcoded, so a table added by a later stage is covered — and this process's own caches). MFA was already mandatory for the provisioned admin's role (`RequiresMFA`), and a test now pins that rather than assuming it. **Least-privilege DB ownership is reported, not fixed**: `tenantctl db-privilege` names the connected role and raises a finding if the app is a superuser/CREATEROLE/CREATEDB — creating and granting roles is deployment state and belongs to 49.7.4; this makes the gap visible instead of assumed. 5 test functions in `engines/tenant_lifecycle_test.go`; ADMIN_GUIDE §C.6 is the operator procedure end to end.*

Deliberately scoped to what is derivable from source: OS/service/proxy/database posture and public reachability are host state, and belong to 49.7.8 and 49.1.7 respectively — this item does not claim them.*
  - [x] **49.1.7 Outside-in verification** from an untrusted network: only declared ports/hosts/routes/assets respond; unknown tenant hosts, direct origin, alternate methods, encoded paths and internal/admin interfaces fail safely. — *2026-09-08: new `cmd/edgecheck` (reusable, not a one-off script) run from the developer's own machine over the real internet against `https://app.wholeops.in` and the box's public IP - a genuine outside vantage point, not the SSH tunnel and not `127.0.0.1` (the distinction 2026-08-14's handover entry already learned the hard way). 27 PASS, 1 SKIP (unknown-tenant-host - `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` isn't live in prod; Stage 44 is built but not deployed), 1 real FAIL: the static asset server answered `TRACE`/`PUT`/`DELETE` with `200` and the file body, because `http.Handle("/", fs)` carries no method in Go's ServeMux syntax and `http.FileServer` never checks `r.Method` beyond special-casing HEAD - confirmed live against production (`TRACE /app.js`, `PUT /styles.css`), not just reasoned about. Fixed in this tree: `onlyReadMethods` in `internal/server/static_fileserver.go` wraps the file server, refusing anything but GET/HEAD with 405 before it runs; `routes.go`'s one registration site updated; verified on a local scratch instance and locked by `TestOnlyReadMethodsRejectsEverythingButGetAndHead`. Recorded as R-10 in `docs/security/risk_register.md`, staying OPEN until deployed - the checklist item is done (the verification ran and the gap it found is fixed in source), the production exposure is not. Direct origin (port 8080), Postgres (5432) and four other common service ports all correctly timed out from outside; declared ports 22/80/443 all open; internal/tls-ask and five encoded-traversal variants all 404; security headers and no-server-fingerprint both hold. Full writeup: `docs/security/outside_in_verification_2026-09-08.md`. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -p 1` all green; `docs/security/attack_surface.json` regenerated (11->12 CLI commands, 6->8 outbound call sites, for `cmd/edgecheck` itself).*
  - **Acceptance:** a generated surface diff accompanies every release; all reachable surfaces are classified, owned and tested; a clean production boot is impossible with a known-insecure baseline; unsupported surfaces are technically unavailable, not merely undocumented.

Two real bugs: (1) `deploy.ps1` scp'd the new frontend directly over the live `public/` before `remote_deploy.sh`'s own pre-deploy snapshot ran, so a rollback "restored" a snapshot that was already the new release — fixed by staging to `public.new` and moving the swap/snapshot/rollback inside `remote_deploy.sh`, mirroring how the binary already worked; (2) a bare `sudo systemctl restart erp` under `set -e` either killed the whole script with no rollback attempted, or — worse — reported `DEPLOY-OK` while the OLD process was still the one answering (a real false success). Fixed both restart call sites with `|| true` plus a commit-aware `wait_healthy` (`/api/v1/health` now echoes `git_commit`; the initial activation check verifies it matches the deploy's own commit, not just a bare 200). After both fixes: 12/12 `release_consistent` true across three separate full mock-harness runs. `deploy/README.md` updated.
  - **AUD-01 (stored XSS) fixed.** `copyableCell` (`public/app.js`) now escapes `displayValue` with the existing `escapeHTMLText` unconditionally instead of trusting it — the one choke point every record-list/table cell in the app renders through, so this closes the exact `renderDocTable`/generic-list vector the audit reproduced plus four other call sites that had the identical unescaped shape but weren't the one named (`internal:` two call sites that pre-escaped their own `displayValue` before calling this were simplified to stop double-escaping through the new choke point). The `status`-badge cell in the same generic table function had its own separate unescaped injection, fixed alongside it. Verified with a standalone script reproducing the audit's exact payload shape (`<img src=x onerror=...>`) against the fixed function: renders as inert text, not markup, in every code path (empty-raw branch, main branch, and the status badge). `node --check public/app.js` clean.
  - **AUD-02 (fresh migration failure) fixed at the root cause, not by renaming the already-in-flight migration file.** `db/migrate.go`'s `compareMigrationNames` compared a sub-item's digit (`_6_` in `migrations_stage47_7_6_audit_archive.sql`) against its dependency's next letter (`_a...` in `migrations_stage47_7_audit_evidence.sql`) as a raw byte — every digit sorts below every letter in ASCII, so a numbered sub-item always sorted before the unnumbered "foundation" file it depends on, regardless of which one actually had to run first. Fixed the general rule (a digit continuing where the other side has moved to text means that side is the deeper/later sub-item), not just this one pair — this shape recurs across `db/*.sql` (26.4, 26.5, 26.6, 26.7, 26.9, 26.12, 29, 37.1, 42.1, 42.5). **Live-verified, not just reasoned about**: reproduced the exact fresh-install failure against a genuinely empty scratch database first (`relation "tenant_default.audit_checkpoints" does not exist`), then re-ran against a freshly recreated empty database with the fix — all 161 embedded migrations applied cleanly in one pass, re-running was a correct no-op (`0 applied, 161 already up to date`), `-migrate-status` reported clean. New pinned test case in `db/migrate_test.go`. `go build`/`go vet` clean repo-wide.
  - **AUD-05 (failed package-disable reported as success) fixed.** `ApplyPackageSelection` (`engines/modules.go`) returned `nil` even when its retry loop broke on "no progress" with modules still in `toDisable`. Now returns a named error listing exactly which module(s) could not be disabled; `handleSetTenantPackage` (`internal/server/handlers_integrations_admin.go`) already surfaces this as a 422 to the operator, so no handler change was needed. The tenant-provisioning call site (`handleProvisionTenant`) deliberately still ignores this error — an existing, documented, reasoned decision ("a package-application hiccup shouldn't fail the whole request" during provisioning), not touched. **Real regression test added** (`engines/modules_test.go`), reproducing the failure the same way the audit's own "fixture trigger" did — a temporary Postgres trigger that unconditionally refuses to disable one module — because this codebase's one real module dependency (`rfq`→`procurement`) is always bundled together in every product package, so an ordinary client selection can never organically reach the stuck state either (the same honest limitation the audit itself recorded). Confirmed the test fails against the pre-fix code and passes against the fix (`git stash` A/B). Full state snapshot/restore and trigger cleanup via `defer`; confirmed zero residue in the shared dev database afterward.
- [x] **50.8** Remediate AUD-06–09: quota operational errors, Store role-template coverage, programmatic form labels and focus restoration. Keep each fix separate from unfinished feature scope. — *2026-09-17: AUD-06/AUD-07 fixed and verified (BLD-008/009). 2026-09-18: AUD-08/AUD-09 live-verified (BLD-010/011) against a disposable scratch server with a real Chromium install (found at `the workstation-local node_modules Playwright package`, contradicting the prior session's "none cached" note); two real gaps found and fixed in the process (post-save focus fell to `<body>` after the list re-render destroyed the just-restored trigger; Escape did not close the dialog at all) — see `project_ledger.md` and the build checklist's BLD-010/011 entries for full detail. The OTHER dialog system (Add/Edit Field config, CSV import) is unaffected and stays open as a deliberate, separately-scoped follow-up.*
  - **AUD-06 fixed.** `CheckTenantLimit` (`engines/tenant_limits.go`) treated any lookup error — not just "no row" — as "no limit configured", silently authorizing unlimited usage exactly when the check was broken. `errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows)` now distinguishes the two; the one caller falls back to 500 for a real operational failure instead of 422. 3 new tests, A/B-confirmed against pre-fix code on 3 fresh databases.
  - **AUD-07 fixed.** `knownModules` (`engines/role_templates.go`) was missing `"Store"` (the shipped `Stores` doctype's module), so Administrator/Auditor silently excluded it. One-line fix + a new test resolving real `TemplateGrants`, not just the module-name-set comparison. Green on 3 fresh databases.
  - **AUD-08/AUD-09 fixed in code, not yet live-verified.** The shared dynamic form builder (`public/app.js`) now threads a stable `dyn-field-<doctype>-<fieldname>` id through every field's `<label for=...>` and its control (Select/Link/Number/text branches). `openDynamicModal`/`closeDynamicModal` gained focus capture/restore, falling back to `#view-root` (new `tabindex="-1"`) if the trigger is gone. `node --check` clean; no live Chromium/accessibility-tree or keyboard-task pass performed this session (no cached Playwright install found) — remains the concrete next step for this item. Only this one dialog system is covered; other per-modal `classList.add('open')` pairs elsewhere in `app.js` are a separate follow-up.

### Stage 51 — User-reported production defects: Master id/code invariant, GRN navigation, industry lock, Jewellery Item depth (opened 2026-09-23) — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **51.1** Fix the Master-record id≠code invariant regression. Root cause: `handleGenericDoc`'s POST path never set a new Master doctype record's `id` equal to its `code` (only doctypes in `documentNumberSeriesByDoctype` got `id` populated, via `PrepareDocumentNumber`) — so every Link field that validates by `id` (e.g. `PurchaseOrder.vendor`) rejected a just-created Vendor/Item/Customer/Employee forever, even though the record existed. `db/migrations_stage24_addendum_data_integrity.sql`'s own comment documents id=code as the assumed invariant its Link-field conversions depended on. Fixed once at the shared choke point (`internal/server/handlers_core_doc_engine.go`, "Master record id = code invariant" block): on create, if `document_type='Master'` and payload has a non-empty `code` but no explicit `id`, set `id = code` before the rest of the save pipeline runs. Covers every Master doctype and every caller (UI, CSV import, API), not just Vendor. **Live-verified** on a disposable scratch instance (fresh Postgres, cross-compiled binary, real HTTP calls): new Vendor's stored id now equals its code; a PO referencing that vendor now saves successfully; a PO referencing a genuinely nonexistent vendor code still correctly fails (`META-0198`) — negative control intact. Pre-existing production Vendor/Item/etc. records created before this fix (their `id` is still a stray UUID) are **not** backfilled by this change — see 51.1a.
- [x] **51.2** Fix the GRN "create Item master" quick-create dead end. This app deliberately never uses browser history for in-app navigation (see `saveNavState`'s own comment), and `openSetupDoctype` (used by every typeahead's empty-state "create one" link and every setup hint across the app, not just GRN) navigated to the new master's list with no memory of where it was launched from. Fixed generically: a new `quickCreateReturn` global captures `{forDoctype, view, label}` from the origin screen; `renderDocTableView` shows a "← Back to X" link when relevant; `handleDynamicFormSubmit` auto-returns to the origin screen after a successful **create** (not edit); `renderViewContent` invalidates the stored target the instant navigation strays anywhere else, so a later unrelated save can never redirect somewhere stale. Fixes this for every doctype reachable via this shortcut, not GRN→Item only. **Live-verified** in a real headless-Chromium session: GRN → Item quick-create → "← Back to Goods Receipt" shown → create Item → landed back on the GRN workbench automatically; separately confirmed the stale-state guard does NOT fire (abandon the GRN shortcut, create an unrelated Vendor normally elsewhere, correctly stays on the Vendor list).
- [x] **51.3** Industry-profile lock. **Decision (user, 2026-09-22): explicit override action.** Built: new singleton table `industry_lock` (per-tenant-schema, `id` always 1 — `db/migrations_stage51_3_industry_lock.sql`, cloned for new tenants via `engines.ProvisionTenantSchema`); `engines.GetIndustryLock`/`SwitchIndustryProfile` (now takes `actingUser, isOverride, overrideReason`) record who set it and bump `override_count` on an override rather than resetting it. `handleSwitchIndustry` refuses a second switch with 409 unless `override:true` + a non-empty `override_reason` are supplied (still gated by the pre-existing `requireHRAdmin`/`IsSuperAdmin` check, not a second role mechanism); new `GET /api/v1/admin/industry/lock` (`handleGetIndustryLock`) lets the UI show current lock state. Frontend: the industry `<select>` (literally labelled "Select Industry" — confirmed this is the exact control the user meant) disables itself and shows an "Override" button once locked, reading real server state (`applyIndustryLockUI`) instead of the old client-only `localStorage` memory; the override flow prompts for a reason and retries with it on a 409. **Verified**: new engine-level test `TestIndustryLockAndOverride` (own throwaway tenant schema, not the shared dev tenant, to avoid the doctype_fields-pollution risk a real `SwitchIndustryProfile` call carries — see its own comment) and handler-level `TestHandleSwitchIndustryRefusesWhenLocked` (409/422/GET paths) both pass; `go build`/`go vet` clean; route added to `route_capabilities.go` and `docs/security/attack_surface.json` regenerated (`go run ./cmd/surfacescan`, copied in via the `%TEMP%` workaround) so `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent`/`TestEveryAPIMiddlewareRouteIsClassified` stay green.
- [x] **51.4** Wired Vendor and Item into the existing Prefix Configurations system. Turned out the numbering *engine* already covered them — `GenerateSequence` falls back to sane defaults (`prefix = doc_type`) when no `prefix_configs` row exists, and the frontend's `/api/v1/sequence` pre-fetch for any Master doctype's empty `code` field already used it — confirmed live (Item saved as `Item/HQ/2026/000002` with zero code changes needed). The actual gap was narrower than scoped: the admin screen only lists/edits rows that already exist (`editPrefixConfig` returns early if not found), so Vendor/Item's prefix/padding were invisible and uneditable. Fixed: `db/migrations_stage51_4_vendor_item_numbering.sql` seeds explicit rows for both (prefix identical to the fallback default, so no already-issued code's shape changes); added a genuinely reusable "+ New Series" button + `addPrefixConfig()` to the Prefix Configurations screen (reuses `editPrefixConfig`'s existing sequential-prompt flow and the already-generic upsert endpoint) so **any** future doctype needing a series is now editable without a manual DB seed. `go build`/`node --check` clean; numbering/prefix engine tests still green.
- [x] **51.5** Jewellery Item Master attributes + Design ID / Combination ID (SKU) generation. **Decision (user, 2026-09-22): standard jewellery attribute set.** Added to `Item` via `public/profiles/jewelry.json`'s existing additive overlay mechanism (module/document_type set to Item's real current values, `Inventory`/`Master`, so the overlay is a no-op on those two columns — checked first, since an industry switch overwrites them unconditionally and Item is used far outside PIM): metal type, purity/karat, gross weight, net weight, stone type, stone weight, making-charge type & value, size. Design ID / Combination ID turned out to be a wiring gap, not new infrastructure, once traced fully: `engines.GenerateVariantCode` (parent code + attribute map → child code) already existed with **zero callers anywhere**, and `ValidateItemVariantUniqueness` already groups sibling variants by the existing `parent_product_code`/`variant_option_values` fields — not `family`/ProductFamily, a separate, orthogonal concept (attribute-completeness policy). New `engines.PrepareItemVariantCode` (Item create, no explicit `code`) bridges `family` → `parent_product_code` when the latter is blank (so ProductFamily, already auto-numbered like any Master doctype, serves as the real "Design ID" record), merges a small fixed set of variant-differentiating fields (`metal_type`, `purity_karat`, `stone_type`, `size` — deliberately excluding continuous/measured fields like weight) into `variant_option_values`, and calls `GenerateVariantCode` for the Combination ID/SKU. Fixed a real latent bug in `GenerateVariantCode` while making it live for the first time: its no-pattern fallback iterated a Go map directly, which is iteration-order-randomized — the same attribute set could render as a differently-ordered code between calls; now sorted. Frontend: Item's `code` field skips the `/api/v1/sequence` pre-fill when `family` is set, so the server generates the combination code instead (`public/app.js`). Also seeded `ProductFamily` a friendlier "Design" prefix via `db/migrations_stage51_5_design_numbering.sql` (51.4's same mechanism). **Verified**: `TestGenerateVariantCodeIsDeterministic`/`TestGenerateVariantCodeCustomPattern`/`TestPrepareItemVariantCodeFromFamily`/`TestPrepareItemVariantCodeExplicitOptionsWinOverFields`/`TestPrepareItemVariantCodeNoOpCases` all pass (pure unit tests, no DB); `go build`/`node --check` clean.
- [x] **51.6** Automatic barcode generation at GRN. **Decision (user, 2026-09-22): fully automatic.** New `engines.EnsureItemBarcodes(tenantID, items)`, called from the GRN receipt-posting success path in `handlers_core_doc_engine.go` (after `PostGRNReceiptWithQC` succeeds, so a barcode failure never blocks or reverses a receipt that already posted stock — best-effort by design, logged via `LogSystemError` on failure). For each distinct SKU in the receipt with no existing `barcode`, generates one via the existing `GenerateEANBarcode` (previously reachable only through a standalone manual PIM action, zero automatic callers) and writes it onto the Item record; an Item that already has a barcode, or doesn't exist, is left alone; idempotent across repeated calls/duplicate SKUs in the same receipt. Print-on-receipt (physical printer trigger) was explicitly left out of this pass pending a scope confirmation, per the original plan's own caution about wiring a printer into a posting transaction — barcode *generation* is what was decided as fully automatic; printing still uses the existing manual sticker/label path. **Verified**: `TestEnsureItemBarcodesGeneratesOnlyWhenMissing` (own throwaway tenant schema) covers generate-when-missing, preserve-existing, unknown-SKU-is-a-no-op, and idempotency across a second call; `go build` clean.
- [x] **51.9** Corrected the Stage 51.5 Jewellery Item field set against real client data, and prepared/ran the client's own PO&Inventory.xlsx (`minn` tenant) master-data migration. User supplied the client's real workbook (`Downloads\MyBusiness\IT Solution\ERP\Bulk Upload\PO&Inventory.xlsx`) and asked for ERP's own bulk-import template, not molding the ERP to the client's format, with our own generated Design ID/SKU. **Finding: 51.5's field set was wrong for this real business.** It assumed fine/gold jewellery (metal_type, purity_karat, gross/net weight, making charge); the client's actual catalog (206 fully-attributed `PRODUCT_MASTER` rows, verified via a two-pass openpyxl formula+value read) is fashion/imitation jewellery — Category, Sub_Category, Type, Stone, Color, Polish, Brand, Size, no karat/weight data at all. Fixed `public/profiles/jewelry.json`: added `sub_category`/`type`/`brand`(Link→Brand)/`color`/`polish`, and changed every attribute field (old and new) from `mandatory:true` to `false` — a single "Jewellery" profile now serves either business model instead of blocking one of them. Extended `itemVariantAttributeFields` (`engines/pim.go`) to also recognize `color`/`polish` as variant-differentiating attributes (fine jewellery differs by metal+purity, fashion jewellery by color+polish/plating) — new `TestPrepareItemVariantCodeFashionJewelleryAttributes` locks this in. **Second finding: 51.5's Design ID generation is unreachable from bulk import.** `PrepareItemVariantCode` is only wired into the single-record HTTP save path (`handleGenericDoc`); `engines/import.go`'s `BulkImportCSV`/`importBatch` never calls it, and separately has no equivalent of 51.1's "Master id=code" bridge either — a bulk-imported Master row with a blank `id` gets an unrelated, independently-sequenced id from `GenerateSequence`, disconnected from its `code`. **Not fixed in the shared import engine this pass** (flagged as a real, reusable gap for a future session — every future bulk import of a Master doctype or a family-linked Item hits it, not just this one); worked around for this one-time migration by pre-computing `id`/`code` explicitly for every row in the transform script below, in the exact format `GenerateSequence`/`GenerateVariantCode` would themselves produce. **Also found**: `engines/pim_import_template.go`'s `pimVariantParentPreflight` (Stage 36.3.5) requires `parent_product_code` to be the id of an existing **Item**, not a ProductFamily — this codebase has two coexisting, never-unified variant mechanisms (`family`/ProductFamily = attribute-completeness policy; `parent_product_code` = a self-referential Item-to-Item parent/child model). Setting `parent_product_code` to a Design code would have failed every bulk-imported Item; worked around by setting `family` only and leaving `parent_product_code` unset (duplicate-combination protection for this migrated batch comes from having already deduped in the transform, not from that live mechanism). **Deliverable** (`Downloads\...\Bulk Upload\migration_output\`): `Brand.csv` (7, using the client's own clean short codes SIYY/THOF/etc.), `Vendor.csv` (7, ERP-sequential codes — the old V001-V007 weren't meaningful enough to keep), `ProductFamily.csv` (21 Designs, grouped by Category+Sub_Category+Type+Brand — mirrors the client's own proven `HELPER_Design_FP` formula), `Item.csv` (34 real Combination/SKUs, grouped by Design+Color+Polish+Size — mirrors the client's own `HELPER_Variant_FP`; Stone is design-level, not a combination differentiator, matching their own formula). **206 raw rows collapsed to 34 real SKUs** (172 exact duplicates) — independently corroborated, not a transform bug: the client's own spreadsheet already flags every one of those exact rows `⚠️ DUPLICATE ROW` in its own Status column (verified by direct inspection of several). HSN 7117 / GST 3% applied to every item (user decision, standard imitation-jewellery classification); fresh GS1-valid EAN-13 barcodes generated per item (client's own barcode-like numbers were inconsistent/questionable, not reused). 35 rows with no attributes at all and the 172 duplicates are logged separately (`skipped_no_attributes.csv`, `skipped_duplicates.csv`), not silently dropped; `sku_crosswalk.csv` maps every legacy SKU to its new Design/Item code for the historical-transaction-replay phase this pass deliberately excluded (user decision: master data only, not historical GRN/PO replay). `seed_counters.sql` (idempotent, safe to re-run) must be run against the real tenant schema immediately after these CSVs are imported and before any new live Vendor/Item/ProductFamily/barcode is created, or the next live-generated one collides with this pre-allocated batch. **Not yet imported anywhere** — these are reviewable CSVs for the user to check and upload themselves via the ERP's existing Bulk Import screens, per the standing "no release should risk client data" principle (stated by the user this same session) — nothing was written to any tenant, dev or production, as part of this item.

### Stage 52 — Category-based sticker/label printing module (opened + built 2026-09-27) — closed items (see docs/micro_checklist.md for this section's remaining open item(s))
- [x] **52.1** `StickerTemplate` doctype (`db/migrations_stage52_sticker_templates.sql`) — `code`/`name`/`categories` (plain comma-separated Data field, matched case-insensitively against `Item.category`; `Item.category` has no master/taxonomy behind it — `db/migration.sql:345` — so there was nothing to Link to)/`is_default`/`label_width_mm`/`label_height_mm`/`elements` (JSONTable, no column spec so a static-text element's extra `text` field doesn't fail the shape check)/`status`. Reuses the existing `stickers` module (already default-enabled, bundled under `pim`/`wms`) rather than a new module key. `engines.ResolveStickerTemplate` (`engines/stickers.go`) matches an item's category to an Active template, falls back to the single `is_default` template if none match, and returns `nil` — not an error — when neither exists, which is the backward-compat guarantee: a tenant that never configures a template sees byte-identical behavior to before this Stage.
- [x] **52.2** Generic document-source resolver (`engines/sticker_document_source.go`, new file) — `ResolveDocumentStickerLines(tenantID, sourceDoctype, sourceDocID)` normalizes a GRN's `received_items` (prints against `accepted_qty` = `qty - rejected_qty - damaged_qty` or an explicit override, the same derivation `wms_receiving.go` posts against; two lines of the same SKU merge only when they share a batch, so a batch-tracked item received across two lots keeps its lots separate on the printed stickers) or a TransferOrder's `items` into a common `{SKU, Qty, BatchNo, ExpiryDate}` shape. Adding a third source (PO/SO) later is one more `case`, not a redesign. `PrintStickersForDocument` resolves lines, optionally filters to a caller-supplied SKU selection (this is what makes "print the whole GRN" and "print one line" the same code path — one-by-one is just a length-1 selection), applies per-SKU copy overrides (defaulting to the line's own accepted/transfer qty), delegates into the same `resolveAndLogSticker` helper the pre-existing manual-scan `PrintStickers` uses (factored out of it, zero behavior change to that function), and returns labels **sorted by resolved category** so a mixed-category GRN prints as separable per-category stacks. `PreviewDocumentStickerLines` is the read-only precursor (no `sticker_print_log` write) backing the frontend's review table. New `GET /api/v1/stickers/preview` route; `POST /api/v1/stickers/print` and the QZ silent-print payload endpoint both extended with optional `source_doctype`/`source_doc_id`/`copies_override` fields rather than a parallel endpoint. `sticker_print_log` gained additive nullable `source_doctype`/`source_doc_id`/`template_id` columns for the audit trail.
- [x] **52.3** Generalized ZPL rendering (`engines/qz_payload.go`) — `BuildStickerPayload` now takes the full `QZPrinter` (was just the language string) and, per label, emits the **untouched pre-Stage-52 hardcoded 3-line layout** when no template resolved, or walks a template's `elements` converting mm positions to dots at the printer's own DPI (`Printer.dpi` existed as a field but was dead code before this Stage — everything hardcoded 203dpi; now actually read, still defaulting to 203 when unset so every existing printer record behaves identically). Text elements use ZPL's own `^FB` (Field Block) command for width-based clipping/justification rather than an approximate character-count truncation — the printer firmware wraps to the box width itself. A label produced by `PrintStickersForDocument` carries its own resolved `Qty` (the document line's accepted/transfer quantity), which takes precedence per-label over the manual flow's flat `copies` parameter.
- [x] **52.4** Frontend (`public/app.js`, `public/styles.css`) — restructured the single "Sticker Printing" screen into a Print/Templates tab bar (reusing `renderHRView`'s existing tab-bar convention, not inventing a new one). **Print tab**: new "Print from Transaction" panel — a module `<select>` (`BULK_STICKER_SOURCE_DOCTYPES`, currently GRN + Transfer Order) plus `attachLinkTypeahead` (the existing single-door document picker, reused exactly as GRN's own PO-reference field already does) to pick the specific document; loading it calls the new preview endpoint and renders a review table (checkbox per line, editable copy count, resolved category + template name shown per row) with both a per-row **Print** button (one line) and a **Print Selected** button (the whole document, respecting unchecked rows) — both flow through the existing QZ-silent-then-browser-fallback path. `renderPrintSheet` (the browser @media print fallback) now renders a template-driven label as absolutely-positioned mm-sized `<div>`s when a template resolved, falling back to the original fixed markup otherwise, and marks the last copy of each category run with a page-break class so a mixed-category batch tears apart as separate stacks. **Templates tab** (new): a list of `StickerTemplate` documents plus a from-scratch drag-and-drop designer — the first pointer-drag UI in this app (no existing canvas/floor-plan editor to pattern-match against), built as plain `mousedown`/`mousemove`/`mouseup` on positioned `<div>`s, no library. A field palette adds a positioned element bound to a label field (SKU/Name/Barcode/HSN/Category/Batch/Expiry/Qty/Source Doc) or free static text; the canvas renders through the exact same `renderStickerElementHTML` function the live print sheet uses, so there is nothing to keep in sync between "what you designed" and "what prints." Save goes through the plain generic `/api/v1/doc/StickerTemplate` CRUD route — no new backend handler needed for that part. Found and fixed live during Playwright verification: the side property panel's X/Y/W/H inputs didn't refresh after a drag/resize ended (data was correct, display was stale) — fixed by re-rendering the panel on `mouseup`.
- [x] **52.5** Testing: `engines/sticker_document_source_test.go` (new) covers GRN accepted-qty derivation incl. partial rejection/damage and same-SKU-different-batch splitting, TransferOrder line parsing, unsupported-doctype error, category-match/default-fallback/nil-fallback template resolution, and an end-to-end `PrintStickersForDocument` run asserting category-sorted output, correct per-line qty, copy overrides, and the sticker_print_log audit trail; `engines/qz_print_test.go` gained a template-driven-ZPL test pinning the mm-to-dots conversion and `^FB` field-block emission, plus updated the two pre-existing `BuildStickerPayload` call sites for its new `QZPrinter`-taking signature. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, and the full `go test ./... -p 1` suite are clean except the two pre-existing `role_templates_test.go` failures already tracked under 51.8 (confirmed unrelated — that file was already modified by a concurrent session before this Stage started). `docs/security/attack_surface.json` regenerated for the new `GET /api/v1/stickers/preview` route (`go run ./cmd/surfacescan`, copied in via the `%TEMP%` workaround) so `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` stays green. **Live-verified** end-to-end on a disposable scratch instance (fresh build on a scratch port, real HTTP calls, no physical thermal printer available so the browser-fallback path was exercised): created two category templates + a mixed-category GRN via the API, confirmed `/api/v1/stickers/preview` and `/api/v1/stickers/print` resolve categories/templates/qty/sort order correctly, confirmed the audit trail records `source_doctype`/`source_doc_id`/`template_id`; then a full real-Chromium (Playwright) pass over the actual UI — both tabs render, the transaction picker loads and previews a real GRN, the Templates list and designer canvas render, a field added via the palette drags correctly (data and visuals both), and the template saves and reappears in the list — zero console errors throughout.
